# ============================================================================================
# 724: THE RUNG-13 ARM - the register state at the INSTANT of the command, and the command's own
# return path. `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=12`. It is the rung-12 arm
# (`armed-storage-cmd-892b8b68`, PRESSED) plus ONE new read-only body (`st_cmd_census`), the
# command body's return path (COMMAND 0x0E read back, PRESENT_STATE's CMD_INHIBIT sampled, the
# first non-zero INT_STATUS of any kind, an unconditional RESPONSE 0x10 read) and their cells -
# and **NO new store anywhere**, which is the whole safety argument of this rung: the write set
# this ladder has asserted since 701 is unchanged at `47 44 44 41` in hc_mem, the command body's
# three stores are unchanged and in the same order, and the census is asserted to make none.
# The pre-registration is
# `docs/experiments/experiment-724-the-rung-13-pre-registration-the-instant-of-the-command-and-the-return-path.md`.
#
# WHY IT IS RECORDED AND PARKED: `./build.sh` is not byte-reproducible (408), so this payload
# exists in exactly one place on earth, and a run of either build script would destroy it.
# `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 begins `e8dc64f7`, which is the suffix this directory is named
# for - the convention every `armed-storage-*` park in `frozen/` uses.
#
# **THE SEAM CONSTANT MOVED A THIRD TIME, AND THE BUILD REFUSED IT.** The first invocation of this
# step's entry build stopped at the seam clause: `the exit's call to FlushPoU_Dcache is at
# 2147783384 and returns to 2147783388, while entry_trace.c's STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR is 0x800482dc` -
# i.e. the exit's `bl` is now at `0x800492d8` returning to `0x800492dc`, one more page on from
# 708's `0x800482dc`. The mechanism held exactly as recorded: the rung's ~3.9 KB of entry-side
# code pushed the entry group past the next page boundary, so every kernel-text address in the
# image moved by `0x1000` at once. **The disassembly decided the direction again** (the gate cannot
# tell which copy is stale): `arm-none-eabi-objdump -d out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry.elf` puts the `bl`
# at `0x800492d8` returning to `0x800492dc`, and the same pass re-read every other address the seam
# prose pins - the window `[0x80049240, 0x8004933c)`, the four callers `0x80048d08`/`0x80049284`/
# `0x800492d8`/`0x800493bc`, `CleanPoU_Dcache 0x800487a8`, `CleanPoC_Dcache 0x8004875c`,
# `FlushPoC_Dcache 0x80048828` and `bsdinit_task`'s call site `0x8004ceb4` - **all of which the
# prose had been left carrying at their 696-era values through two moves the define itself had
# already absorbed.** Both copies of the constant moved: `entry_trace.c`'s `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` and
# `run_and_capture.sh`'s `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL`, which the gate compares against each other and the
# live ELF.
#
# **AND THE CLAUSES CAUGHT TWO DEFECTS IN THEMSELVES, BOTH NEW SHAPES.** (1) The image-side clause
# for the command body was written for `[UNK:ldr UNK:str]` and refused `[UNK:ldr UNK:str UNK:strd]`:
# the two new cells `status_any` and `status_any_polls` are adjacent 32-bit fields assigned in one
# basic block, so GCC merged them into a single `strdeq` on the same base - a *narrower* reading of
# the same two cells, and the expected set now names the third mnemonic at rung 12 rather than
# tolerating it. (2) **The census's own store check could only fail.** It was written
# `$(printf ... | grep -E ':str|:strh|:strb' | tr ...)`, and `grep` exits 1 when it matches nothing -
# which is the GOOD case here - so under this script's `set -euo pipefail` the assignment took that
# status and ended the build *before* the emptiness assertion could pass. An assertion that cannot
# hold is not a check (m702's shape), and the extractor's status must not be the assertion's status;
# the extractor is `awk` now, which always exits 0.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, all of them `build_entry.sh` clauses rather than sentences: **no device store
# of any kind is added** (the census's clause asserts its store set EMPTY, and the command body's
# three stores and their order are rung 11's), no write to any RCG word, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`
# write (it is READ, at the command's own moment), no data-path register, no `core_mem` store, no
# interrupt enabled, and no byte of the medium.
#
# **THIS SET SUPERSEDES THE `cmd-892b8b68` ARM AS THE ONE THE NEXT PRESS SENDS** - that sentence
# lives in 718/719/720/723's record and is left where it is, per this file's own rule that a later
# step supersedes such a sentence rather than editing it.

# **PRESSED 2026-09-26 03:30:16-03:32:00, EXIT 0** (returned and captured, 74 s end to end; readiness
# 5/5 exit 0, one gate exit 0 / 574 lines, exactly one runner `--expect-arm=armed-storage-instant-e8dc64f7`;
# firer `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh`, budget 600 s, `done. gate=0 runner=0` -> no firer is armed). The
# capture is `out/stage90/captures/rung13-instant-20260926-033016-last_kmsg.txt`, 625,475 B, sha256
# `3aacabdc...`. **THE ARM'S ANSWER TURNS RUNG 12's "NOTHING LATCHED" INTO A PRECISE STATEMENT, AND ALL
# FIVE OF 724 SECTION 2'S HYPOTHESES ARE REFUTED**: the block HOLDS the driver's own word
# (`_cmd0_word_read=0x0000`, read out of the block), it STARTED the command (`_cmd0_inhibit_after=1`),
# the command was IN PROGRESS on the CMD line (`_cmd0_inhibit_seen=0x21b` = 539 of the poll's first 1024
# samples) and it FINISHED (`_cmd0_inhibit_last=0x01f80000`, bit 0 clear, after ~126 us) - **and
# `_cmd0_status_any=0x00000000`, the FIRST NON-ZERO VALUE OF THE WHOLE REGISTER, over 5,088,256 polls and
# 23,040,340 ticks.** `_cmd0_resp_read=1` with `_cmd0_resp=0` makes that zero a reading rather than an
# absence, and `_cmd_gated=1` with zero `_cmd1_*` keys. The census refutes the other two from the
# command's own moment: `_cmd2_power_control=0x0b` with `_cmd2_power_bus=1`, `_cmd2_clock_control=0xe047`
# with CARD_EN set, and `_cmd2_rcg_cmd=0` (bit 31 clear = root ENABLED) with src 5 / div 7 = 200 MHz.
# The record is `docs/experiments/experiment-726-the-rung-13-press-the-command-ran-and-the-status-register-said-nothing.md`.
# **THE BYTES BELOW ARE UNCHANGED BY THE PRESS**: this set is still the arm that was sent.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# armed-storage-completion-6f49e880 - RUNG 14 (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=13), 729's arm. **ARMED, NOT
# PRESSED**: built and parked 2026-09-26, and no press has been taken from these bytes.
#
# WHAT THE RUNG ASKS. Rung 13 refuted all five of 724 section 2's hypotheses and left one: this
# controller RUNS a command to completion and does not set its interrupt-status register. The command
# reached the CMD line, the block held CMD_INHIBIT while it was in flight and released it when it
# finished, and `_cmd0_status_any` was 0x00000000 over 5,088,256 reads taken ACROSS that window. The
# SDHCI normal-interrupt path is `INT_STATUS -> INT_ENABLE -> SIGNAL_ENABLE -> the line`, so rung 14
# looks for the completion in the two OTHER status registers this block has and in the word it still
# holds, and then ASKS FOR IT: ONE bit of INT_ENABLE 0x34 (SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE) with SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38
# left at ZERO, INT_STATUS read, INT_ENABLE read back, restored to zero and read back again. Two
# stores, both to 0x34, and no new command. If the block ANDs the two enables the line never rises and
# the run gets the answer; if it does not, the run ends at the dispatcher as `_irq_other_count=1` /
# `_irq_other_iar=155`, an ending this image already reads and survives (709's press ended that way on
# intid 170) and which the payload's own armed watchdog bounds. The pre-registration is
# `docs/experiments/experiment-728-...`, and its section 1's placement constraint is the one thing
# 726's prose could not have supplied: the block stands immediately after CMD0's publishes and BEFORE
# the gate, because `_cmd_gated=1` says the gate FIRED and a block after the commands would never run.
#
# THE RECORD'S OWN CLAIM ABOUT THE TWO OTHER STATUS REGISTERS WAS FALSE, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHAT
# REFUTED IT. 726 section 3 and 728 section 2 both write that `SLOT_INT_STATUS 0xFC` is a register
# "this ladder has never read at all". It is read: rung 3's `st_standard_census` reads it as a
# halfword (entry_storage.c:642) and 726's own log carries the value - `xnu_live_storage_reg_slot_int_status
# = 0x00000000`. `CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC` is read by rung 2's mode sequence and rung 8's handler
# (`_pwr_irq_status32 = 0x02` in the same log). The true statement is stronger and is the one this rung
# needed: NEITHER has ever been read at a moment when there was a completion to report, and rung 3's and
# rung 8's readings are in the same log as the new cells, so the pair is comparable. Both documents are
# corrected in 729 by a visible strikethrough rather than by an edit (this file's own rule).
#
# **WHAT THE BUILD REFUSED, AND WHAT IT DID NOT.** Three invocations, two refusals, and 728 section 5
# got the second one wrong in an instructive way:
#   (1) `build_entry.sh:667`'s own switch guard - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE must be 0, 1, 2, ... or 12`
#       - refused `13` before the compiler saw it. The build owns that case list and the #error owns the
#       ladder, so both move in the step that raises the rung (725 recorded the same pair moving to 12).
#   (2) the new clause's PROGRAM-ORDER comparison refused, and the two strings printed identically:
#       `classify_body` prints its first line with a trailing space after every entry and `read -r`
#       strips trailing IFS whitespace, so `$stb_int_dev` carries no trailing space while the `tr`-built
#       `stb_int_set` does. The set, the counts and the store list all agreed on that same build. The
#       comparison now spells `${stb_int_dev% }` and says why.
#   **AND THE SEAM CONSTANT DID NOT MOVE, WHICH REFUTES 728 SECTION 5's "it will move a fourth time".**
#   The rung's ~1.1 KB of entry-side code landed inside the entry group's alignment slack: the entry bin
#   is 5,552,764 bytes - BYTE-IDENTICAL in size to the pressed rung-13 arm - and `platform_cache_idle_enter`
#   is still at 0x80049238 with the exit's `bl` at 0x800492d8 returning to 0x800492dc. What moved is
#   everything from `st_int_report` (0x8000d978, where `st_cmd_path` used to be) up to `arm_init`, and
#   nothing above it. **A prediction that a constant must move is a prediction about the LINKER's
#   padding, which no clause asserts and no arithmetic here sees** - so the seam paragraph in 725 stays
#   correct and is left where it is.
#
# WHAT THE CLAUSES ASSERT, MEASURED (`build_entry.sh`, `xnu_entry_729`). The new body's device accesses,
# in program order and distinct, are
#   [f98249fc:ldrh f98240dc:ldr f982490e:ldrh f9824924:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824934:ldr f9824938:ldr f9824934:str]
# with counts [f9824930:ldr=2 f9824934:ldr=3 f9824934:str=2 f982490e:ldrh=1 f9824924:ldr=1 f9824938:ldr=1
# f98240dc:ldr=1 f98249fc:ldrh=1], its stores in program order [f9824934:str], and an EMPTY image side;
# `st_cmd_path` calls it once, after CMD0's publishes and before the gate. **The counts are asserted and
# not echoed** (rungs 11 and 12 echo theirs): the SET alone cannot see a dropped read, and the two
# multiplicities this rung's answer rests on are INT_STATUS read TWICE - the baseline with the enables
# clear, and the answer with the RESPONSE bit set one instruction earlier - and INT_ENABLE read THREE
# times and written TWICE. The comparison sorts first because `classify_body` prints counts by iterating
# an awk hash. `st_int_report`'s window is OUTSIDE the width-vs-offset census's (that census reads
# `[sym_addr entry_storage_probe, next_global)` and `nm` puts a `noinline` static before the probe), so
# the exact `<address>:<mnemonic>` set above is where this body's widths are checked - and it names
# `ldrh` for the two offsets no 32-bit access may reach, 0x0E and 0xFC.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: no new command, no ARGUMENT/COMMAND/RESPONSE write, no data-path register, no
# POWER_CONTROL store (0x29 is not touched at all by this body), no GCC word, no core_mem write, no byte
# of the medium, and no persistent enable - the restore is the last store and `_int_enable_readback` is
# its cell. `st_cmd_path`'s and `st_cmd_census`'s clauses are rung 11's and rung 12's, unchanged.
#
# **THIS SET SUPERSEDES THE `instant-e8dc64f7` ARM AS THE ONE THE NEXT PRESS SENDS** - that sentence
# lives in 724/725/726's record and is left where it is, per this file's own rule that a later step
# supersedes such a sentence rather than editing it. **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET**: XNU is not
# normally loaded, the OS has not been entered, and 「如果os已经能进去了的话」 is not triggered - so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.

# 730: THIS SET WAS PRESSED, AND THE COMPLETION WAS THERE. **`_int_status_after = 0x00000001`.**
# Read this block as the answer to the pre-registration above; it supersedes the ARM/SET lines and
# nothing else in it.
# ================================================================================================
# PRESSED 2026-09-26 07:38:48-07:39:59 UTC. Readiness 5/5 (exit 0, run from the same tree immediately
# before the fire), ONE gate (exit 0, 569 stdout lines) and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under
# `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-completion-6f49e880`; RUNNER EXIT=0 - returned and
# captured, **71 s** end to end. One non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b; the neighbour
# 33e80afe was off BOTH lists (adb: [4a2fe00b device], fastboot: []) and was never touched. Capture
# `out/stage90/captures/rung14-completion-20260926-074001-last_kmsg.txt`, **626,111 B, sha256
# `c5d0aeee6cfaadd1d029c65aaca1dcf900c42f1b021afd8d97ed3f464eb23d00`**, archived by hand (out/ is
# gitignored and the runner does not archive). Nothing was flashed and no byte of the medium moved.
#
# **NO FIRER WAS ARMED, AND THAT IS A NEW FACT ABOUT THE RECORD RATHER THAN A CHOICE.** The two
# commands above were run directly, exactly once each. The firer this record names for every press
# since 671 - `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh`, sha256 `7f23cae5...` - no longer exists: `/tmp/g668`
# and `/tmp/r654` (its `press.log`) are gone from this host, and `find / -name 'press-on-clear*'`
# returns nothing, so **the bytes that sha stands for are recorded nowhere in this repository, this
# export or the job tree**. A sha whose bytes are not kept is not a check but a claim about a file
# that cannot be produced again - the class `mi4-a-claim-in-a-comment-is-not-a-check` names - and
# 726's own record cites it in the same breath as the press it fired. The two commands readiness
# prints ARE the discipline (661 R1); the launcher was a wrapper around them, with its own rehearsal
# (the 27-row `rehearse-v3.sh` and `rehearse-wait.sh` harnesses, 672 section 4) that this step could
# not re-run because those harnesses died with the same job tmp. **OWED: either recreate a launcher
# WITH its rehearsal and record its bytes, or record that the press path is two commands and the
# launcher is retired.**
#
# THE ANSWER, AND IT IS THE ONE 726 COULD NOT HAVE SEEN: **THE COMPLETION WAS PENDING IN THE BLOCK,
# AND `INT_ENABLE` WAS THE MASK.**
#  * `_int_status_before = 0x00000000` and `_int_status_after = 0x00000001` - **the same command, the
#    same register, the same moment in `st_cmd_path`, with ONE STORE between the two reads** (0x34 <-
#    0x00000001, `_int_enable_wrote`). Bit 0 of INT_STATUS is `SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE`, the Command
#    Complete bit. This is the AND-model, confirmed by construction: the two reads differ by the
#    enable and by nothing else the arm did.
#  * `_int_enable_before = 0x00000000`, `_int_sig_enable = 0x00000000` - **both enables were clear at
#    the moment rung 11's poll ran**, which is why `_cmd0_status_any = 0x00000000` over 5,088,256
#    reads and `_cmd0_status_after = 0x00000000` in this log too. **Rungs 11, 12 and 13 were reading a
#    register whose enable nothing in this image had ever set**, and the block was never dead.
#  * The two registers 728 section 1 named as the cheaper alternatives answered NOTHING:
#    `_int_slot_status = 0x00000000`, `_int_pwrctl_status = 0x00000000`. The block's own copy of the
#    command word is still the driver's (`_int_cmd_word = 0x00000000`, matching `_cmd0_word`), and
#    `_int_present = 0x01f80000` with `_int_present_inhibit = 0x00000000` says no command is in flight
#    at that instant. Rung 3's and rung 8's readings of those two registers are before-values in this
#    same log, so the pair is a comparison and not a claim (m737 corrected the two documents that had
#    it the other way).
#  * **THE PRE-REGISTERED FAILURE MODE DID NOT HAPPEN**: there is no `_irq_other_count` and no
#    `_irq_other_iar` key in this log. `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` was read and never written, the line never
#    rose, and the run did not end at the dispatcher - so the two-enable hypothesis was testable
#    exactly as 728 section 2 said, without putting the SPI 123 line in play.
#  * THE ENDING DID NOT MOVE: `_post_end_calls = 0x00000007`, `_sleh_storm = 0x00000009`,
#    `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879` - every value 726's log carries.
#    The userland floor the runner prints (open/read/getpid/exit/wait, 504's reading) is met again and
#    is still the floor and not this run's progress.
#  * INHERITED CELLS UNCHANGED: `_cmd0_word_read = 0x0000`, `_cmd0_sent = 1`, `_cmd_gated = 1` with
#    ZERO `_cmd1_*` keys, and the whole `_cmd2_*` census as 726 left it. The only differences between
#    the two logs' storage keys outside the new family are the per-run jitter cells
#    (`_cmd0_polls` 0x004da800 -> 0x004da400, `_cmd0_inhibit_seen` 0x21b -> 0x219,
#    `_pwr_wait_ticks` 0x592 -> 0x598, `_clk_set_cc_polls` 0x11 -> 0x12) - no semantic cell moved.
#
# **THE ONE CELL THAT CAME BACK WRONG, AND IT IS NEW: `_int_enable_held = 0x00008001` and
# `_int_enable_readback = 0x00008000`.** The readback of `INT_ENABLE 0x34` after writing `0x00000001`
# carries **bit 15**, which `_int_enable_before = 0x00000000` says was CLEAR one body earlier; and the
# restore write of 0 clears bit 0 (`_int_enable_readback` bit 0 is 0, so the write took) and leaves bit
# 15 SET. So on this block bit 15 of 0x34 is (a) not a fixed-to-1 read-only bit - the before-value
# refutes that - and (b) not clearable by writing 0 to the register. Two readings are open and this
# step does not choose between them: bit 15 is a sticky latch the block sets when an enabled status
# first pends, or it is a bit with write-1-to-clear semantics that 0 cannot reach. **THE SAFETY
# CONTRACT SURVIVED EITHER WAY** (the arm's contract was the store, and `_int_enable_readback` is the
# cell that publishes the block's own answer rather than the arm's intention), and the run behaved
# exactly as the un-touched arm did, ending included. **OWED, AND CHEAP: read INT_STATUS AGAIN after
# the restore** - if it reads 0 the status is gated by the enable (AND-model, one more cell); if it
# reads 1 the bit is a LATCH that survives the enable going away, which would be a different block
# from the one the spec describes. And read `HOST_VERSION 0xFE` beside it: the 32-bit combined
# 0x30/0x34/0x38 layout is an SDHCI 3.0 alternative to the 2.0 split pairs (0x30 normal status, 0x34
# error status, 0x36/0x3A/0x3C enables), and this step's reading is strong evidence for 3.0 but does
# not by itself name the version register.
#
# **WHAT THIS DOES TO THE LADDER, IN ONE SENTENCE: `_cmd_gated = 1` is no longer a statement about the
# block.** Rung 11's gate refused CMD1 because its poll never saw CMD0 complete; the poll never saw it
# because nothing had set INT_ENABLE. The next rung is the same command path with `INT_ENABLE` bit 0
# set before the command and the wait reading INT_STATUS instead of the raw poll - **with
# `SIGNAL_ENABLE` still at zero, which is the trick this press proved is safe** - and the cell that
# says it worked is `_cmd_gated = 0` with `_cmd1_*` keys present for the first time.
#
# THE RECORD IS `docs/experiments/experiment-730-the-rung-14-press-the-completion-was-there-and-the-enable-was-the-mask.md`.
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET**: XNU is not normally loaded, the OS has not been entered, there is no
# partition table, no mounted volume and no driver beyond the fixture, so 「如果os已经能进去了的话」 is
# not triggered and **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld**. **AND THIS SET IS NO LONGER THE ARM THE NEXT
# PRESS SENDS**; the bytes below are the bytes that were sent.

# 731: THE 2026-09-26 RESTRUCTURE CORRECTS TWO OF THIS SET'S LINES, AND THE ARM ITSELF DID NOT MOVE.
# ================================================================================================
# **THIS IS A RECORD CORRECTION AND NOT A PRESS.** No device action of any kind was taken on
# 2026-09-26 after 07:39:59 - the rung-14 press above is the last one - and nothing is owed. The
# correction is to two of the eleven lines of `set=armed-storage-completion-6f49e880`, and it is
# forced by the reorganization the operator asked for, not by anything about the arm.
#
# WHAT MOVED. On 2026-09-26 the repository was reorganized from the snapshot-per-stage model into one
# evolving tree: the live code left `stages/stage90/` for `src/` (the 67 payload `.c` flat, and the
# seven named subtrees as `src/entry/`, `src/platform/`, `src/supply/`, `src/shims/`, `src/shims_arm/`,
# `src/firehose/`, `src/targets/`), the ten shell scripts and the compile-graph scanner left for
# `scripts/`, the three record `.txt` files for `records/`, `tool-images/` for `records/tool-images/`,
# and the five archived snapshots `stage85`..`stage89` for `archive/stages/`. `out/` did not move and
# `out/stage90/`'s name did not change, so every literal that names the arm still resolves.
#
# WHY EXACTLY TWO LINES. Nine of the eleven members are the bytes the press sent or artifacts derived
# from the payload - `stage90-qcdt.img`, `stage90.bin`, `stage90.img`, `stage90.elf`,
# `stage90-build-config.txt`, `stage90_fixture.macho`, `SHA256SUMS.txt`, `xnu_arm_entry.bin`,
# `xnu_arm_entry.elf` - and not one of their hashes changed: `cmp` is byte-identical for all nine. The
# other two are the only recorded members whose **content is derived from the source tree's own paths**,
# because each carries a header naming where it was generated and what reads it. So they are the only
# two whose content the move could reach, and it reached exactly the path spellings inside them:
#
#   xnu_arm_entry-config.txt   dc674503f7fa27a177be35802a39ad9eb298054f8aeb4d98580494d0bd1e6464  910
#                           -> fcefba4c078f9969c64ff862e58af87a2184b82d875865d9a9188f7b3e788fb4  900
#   xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt  65045b698bfbe8ae55b59f9b72452c5f0954840c7dd36c4d074ba65eccf872bf 2348
#                           -> 15552518e944cd1c55e01013a1b75d5645a555eb5ecf765b7398a2f85f4ae395 2335
#
# **The sizes are the arithmetic that shows it, and they are worth reading because a 7-byte delta is
# what a path spelling costs and nothing else is that shape.** Both files were rewritten twice - once
# for the move itself and once for the last stale names in `build_entry.sh`'s own header writers - and
# each rewrite dropped exactly 7 bytes per path it re-spelled: `stages/stage90/` is 15 characters and
# `scripts/` is 8, so `stages/stage90/preflight_boot_check.sh` -> `scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh` is
# -7; `xnu_arm_boot/` is 13 and `src/entry/` is 10, so `xnu_arm_boot/build_entry.sh` ->
# `src/entry/build_entry.sh` is -7 and `Every regular file in xnu_arm_boot/, by content` ->
# `... in src/entry/, by content` is -7. 910-7-3 = 900 and 2348-7-6 = 2335, and no line's *value* moved:
# the second line of the config is still `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY_SHA256=6f49e880...`, the 18 arm keys below
# it are unchanged, and the manifest's 23 content hashes are the hashes of the 23 files that are in
# `src/entry/` now. **THE PRESS-TIME TEXT OF BOTH FILES IS KEPT OUTSIDE THE TREE** at

# `sources.txt.as-parked`, hashes as above), because these two lines are the only place in the
# repository that recorded them.
#
# **WHAT PROVES THE ARM DID NOT MOVE, MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** `src/entry/build_entry.sh` was run
# twice from the restructured tree with this arm's own 18-key switch set (read back out of
# `out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry-config.txt`, so it is the arm's set and not a transcribed one; the script's
# own deliberate-change guard passes because the set is identical), and both builds produced
# `out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry.bin` = 6f49e880a4670a984ebe4645151042c96ea0a7baa40ee66500582d873a75dd39,
# 5,552,764 bytes, `cmp`-identical to the pressed artifact. `xnu_arm_entry.elf` likewise:
# f50c7418b6f47e7db971f220a404f94cd640d15b7ef68d00918e4c90ac06aca0, `cmp`-identical. So the eight
# `src/entry/` files whose content this restructure touched - `assym.s`, `entry.ld`, `entry_gic.c`,
# `entry_gic.h`, `entry_irq.c`, `entry_storage.c`, `entry_stubs.c` (comments, and comments only) and
# `build_entry.sh` (a script, not a compiler input) - **fed no compiler input**, which is the condition
# the gate's own refusal text names for not running `./build.sh`: the payload does not need rebuilding
# and `./build.sh` must NOT be run, because it does not reproduce (408) and would spend the only
# in-tree copy of an arm that was already sent. The eight `out/xnu_platform_obj/*.o` inputs the entry
# link reads were rebuilt from `src/platform/` and `src/supply/` for the same reason, and all eight came
# back byte-identical: the platform build is reproducible across the move too.
#
# **The park was refreshed, and that is a change to the park and not to the arm.** `out/stage90/` and
# `out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-completion-6f49e880/` now hold the two corrected metadata files, so
# row 1 of `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` (live bytes are the parked bytes) and row 2
# (`tools/verify_revert_set.sh "$PARK" --set=...`) are both green on all eleven members. What the park
# is *for* - one copy of an arm that cannot be rebuilt - is unchanged, and the press-time text of the
# two corrected files is in the export directory named above.
#
# **WHAT DID NOT MOVE, IN ONE LINE**: the rung is still 14 (`STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=13`), the switch
# set is still the 18 keys recorded above, the ending is still 730's, and the goal is still not met -
# XNU is not normally loaded, the OS has not been entered, and 「如果os已经能进去了的话」 is not

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 732: THE ARM THIS SET IS - rung 15 by the ordinal every commit subject counts, `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=14`
# by the value this ladder counts - AND THE FIRST BUILD OF IT WAS REFUTED WITHOUT A PRESS.
#
# **Two spellings of one number, and this record states both because they differ by one.** The ladder in
# `src/entry/entry_storage.c` counts a rung by the VALUE of `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` ("14 = 13 plus ...",
# so `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=13` is the arm 730 pressed); the commit subjects and the pre-registrations
# count ORDINAL ARMS and call that same arm rung 14, so this one is **rung 15**. They part company at value
# 9, which has two arms in the ladder (the second is the same wait with the mask off). A reader holding both
# numbers holds one arm, and the 731 block above uses the same pair of spellings.
#
# **The arm, in one sentence**: the pressed arm's two commands, gates, census and bodies, with the ONE
# hardware fact they were missing - `INT_ENABLE 0x34` carrying `SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE` (0x00000001) **from
# immediately before CMD0 is put on the bus to the line immediately after CMD0's publishes**, and
# `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` still ZERO, which is the one thing 730's press measured is safe. 730's own answer is
# why: `_int_status_after = 0x00000001` against `_int_status_before = 0x00000000` with one store between
# them, so the masked poll that produced `_cmd_gated = 1` was reading a status register whose enable nothing
# in this image had ever set while a command was in flight. The cell that says the rung worked is
# **`_cmd_gated = 0` with `_cmd1_*` keys present** - the first command this line drives to a completion and
# the first CMD1 it ever issues - and the cell beside it, new in this arm, is **`_cmd0_complete = 1`**.
#
# **THE FIRST BUILD OF THIS ARM SHIPPED AS `armed-storage-enable-306ecedb` AND ITS WINDOW STOOD AFTER CMD0
# INSTEAD OF BEFORE IT. IT WAS REFUTED BY 730'S OWN LOG, WITH NO DEVICE ACTION.** `st_send_command`'s
# completion poll is INSIDE CMD0 and it reads `INT_STATUS`; with the window opened below the command that
# poll ran masked, so `c0.complete` stayed 0, the between-commands gate (`if (c0.sent == 0 || c0.complete == 0
# || c0.err != 0)`) would have refused, CMD1 would never have been sent, and **the arm's own promised cell
# `_cmd_gated = 0` was absent by construction** while every other cell it published read plausibly. That is
# m720's shape (an absent key one of whose producers is guaranteed), and it is the class this step's record
# names: the pre-registration promised a cell the build could not produce, and the only thing that could
# refuse it was the ORDER of three instructions. **So the build now asserts the order**: it splits
# `st_cmd_path`'s disassembly at its first `bl <st_send_command>` and classifies each half with the same
# `classify_body` the set clauses use - before the first command the enable's `str` must be there counted
# once, after it the body's own device accesses must be `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` alone, and the restore's call
# must fall between the two calls. The split is by disassembly line and not by offset pattern, because GCC
# materializes the block as `movw/movt 0xf9824000` and reaches the register as `[r4, #2356]` - a fact about
# the compiler, not about the arm - and the clause's first draft matched `#52`, found zero, and died
# SILENTLY under `set -e` because `grep -c` in an assignment exits 1 on no match.
#
# **THE WINDOW'S SHAPE IS NOW ONE ENTRANCE AND ONE EXIT, BOTH UNCONDITIONAL**, and that is a safety
# property rather than a simplification: the closing call is on the single line after CMD0's publishes and
# every branch of `st_cmd_path` (the census's refusal and the register half of the gate above the window,
# the between-commands gate below it) stands outside it. The first build's two-exit shape - the gate's early
# `return` and the end of the body - described a window that could not be entered.
#
# **THE PREDICTED CELLS ARE READ OFF 730'S OWN LOG RATHER THAN OFF THE FIRST BUILD'S CELL TABLE, AND TWO OF
# THEM CHANGE**: 730 measured its own readback as `_int_enable_held = 0x00008001` for a store of 0x00000001
# and `_int_enable_readback = 0x00008000` for a store of 0x00000000, so **`_ena_held` is expected 0x00008001
# and `_ena_readback` 0x00008000, not 0x1 and 0x0** - bit 15 (`SDHCI_INT_ERROR`) is set by a write to 0x34
# and is not cleared by one, so this rung's restore is a restore of BIT 0 and its own cell says so.
# `_ena_status_pre` is read here with no command in flight and is expected **0** (730's same read, taken
# after a command had completed and been cleared, was 1 - so the pair says whether the RESPONSE bit is
# latched BY the completion or held by the block), and `_cmd0_status_any`/`_cmd0_any_polls` are the cells
# that say WHEN it appeared: `_any_polls` = 1 is a bit already visible at the poll's first sample, a small
# value above 1 is a block that latched it while the poll ran.
#
# **The set's identity is its own argument.** The entry bin is 5,552,764 bytes - BYTE-IDENTICAL in size to
# the pressed arm and to this arm's first build - so the seam constant did not move (`0x800492dc`, checked
# against the live payload by `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`), and the three sets are told apart by HASHING
# and by nothing else: `armed-storage-completion-6f49e880` (pressed), `armed-storage-enable-306ecedb`
# (superseded before any press; its park is kept outside `out/` and its lines are NOT in this record), the
# ladder payload this step built once by omission (`fca2d467`, also outside `out/`), and this one. `stage90-build-config.txt` is byte-identical to the pressed arm's, because the payload's own
# flags did not change and only the embedded entry image did.
#
# **AND THE PAYLOAD'S OWN SWITCH SET WAS BUILT WRONG ONCE, BY THE SAME KIND OF SILENCE, BEFORE THIS SET
# EXISTED.** `./scripts/build.sh` takes its variant switches from `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS`, and its default is
# `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=0u` - a LADDER payload, which never jumps into XNU. The pressed arm and this arm were
# both built with `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'`, and the first payload build of this step
# was run WITHOUT it, so it produced a 683-byte config reading `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 0u` instead of this arm's
# 682-byte `1` - a different experiment, one `fastboot boot` away, and **nothing refused it**: the entry
# image was identical, the gate accepted the tree under its derived flags (none, because the flag is only
# required for an XNU-entry payload), and the record's own role line for the config claimed byte-identity
# with the pressed arm while the bytes said otherwise. **The only cell that carries it is the one line of
# `stage90-build-config.txt` that differs, and the only reason it was caught is that the claim was written
# down and then measured.** The rule this leaves: **a step that changes ONE thing must give every build the
# previous arm's own switch set, read out of the record, and then COMPARE the config it produces with the
# record's** - which is what was done for the entry image's eighteen keys and is what was not done for the
# payload. The superseded ladder payload is parked at
# `/mnt/data/mi4-ios6-export/postrestructure-parks/superseded-ladder-payload-fca2d467-not-pressed/` and its
# lines are not in this record. OWED: make that comparison a check rather than a habit - `tools/` already
# derives the gate's flag set from this file, so the same file can answer "is the payload the one the
# record's last pressed set used".
#
# **THE SET'S NAME IS ITS ENTRY IMAGE'S HASH, AND THE FIRST DRAFT GOT THAT WRONG.** A set name's eight
# hex characters are `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s sha256 prefix, because the storage ladder is a change to the
# ENTRY IMAGE and not to the payload. Measured over this whole record: 16 of the 16 `armed-storage-*`
# sets that predate this one have their suffix equal to their own entry bin's prefix, and the one
# pre-ladder arm whose name is not entry-bin derived (`armed-selftest-wdog-ef0361a2`) takes its suffix
# from its qcdt. The first draft of THIS set was named `armed-storage-enable-73d4a8f3` - the qcdt's hash,
# the file `fastboot boot` actually sends, and wrong by the rule every arm beside it follows. It is now
# `armed-storage-enable-953ad0f6` (953ad0f6 is this set's entry bin), and **the rename cost nothing but a
# `sed` and two `mv`s: nothing had been pressed and nothing had been committed.**
#
# **The rule lived only as prose, one line from the name that broke it**: this block's own `role=` sentence
# for `xnu_arm_entry.bin` reads "the 8 hex of this hash is the set's name" - a claim inside a string that no
# tool parses, and the set name on the very next field contradicted it. That is
# `[[mi4-a-claim-in-a-comment-is-not-a-check]]`, and it is the same shape as the payload-switch defect
# above: both were caught by a written claim being MEASURED, and neither was caught by a check. **OWED,
# together with the config check above: a tool that refuses a set name whose 8-hex suffix is not its own
# entry bin's prefix.**

# RUNNER EXIT 0, 70 s - AND IT ANSWERED, AND ONE OF ITS OWN PREDICTED CELLS FAILED IN A WAY THAT
# RE-READS 730.

# **One gate (exit 0 / 591 lines), one runner, one `fastboot boot`, nothing flashed.** Readiness 5/5 exit
# 0. `33e80afe` verified OFF the bus immediately before the gate, and the arm's own bytes re-hashed as
# `73d4a8f3...` immediately before the gate as well. Capture
# `out/stage90/captures/rung15-enable-20260926-101331-last_kmsg.txt`, 636,013 B, sha
# `3c8418bd35aa33bd920258d77fd1de784cd640f2a2dfbf93d38503dfbdd5ef7c`. The device came back on its own -
# no watchdog, no power press - and the ending did not move (`_post_end_calls = 7`, `_sleh_storm = 9`,
# `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `_seam_lr = 0x800492dc`,
# `slot_post_calls` reached 4).
#
# **THE ARM'S OWN CELL HIT.** `_cmd_gated = 0x00000000` with `_cmd1_*` keys present, and
# **`_cmd0_complete = 0x00000001`** - so the enable was the mask, and the arm's own numbers date it:
# `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219` (CMD_INHIBIT over 537 of the poll's samples) and `_cmd0_inhibit_last =
# 0x01f80000` (bit 0 clear - it finished), with `_cmd0_status_any = 1` first seen at `_cmd0_any_polls =
# 0x21b`, THE POLL'S LAST SAMPLE. **The RESPONSE bit appeared at the command's completion and not at the
# enable's store** - the enable stood from before the send and the poll's first 538 samples read 0.
# Every other predicted cell holds, the two read off 730's own log included: `_ena_held = 0x00008001`,
# `_ena_readback = 0x00008000`, `_ena_status_pre = 0`, `_ena_status_post = 0`, `_ena_restore_seq = 1`,
# `_ena_host_version = 0x00001102`, `_cmd_gate_kind = 0`, `_cmd_sent = 2`.
#
# **AND CMD1 WENT OUT, AND THE CARD ANSWERED.** `_cmd1_sent = 1`, `_cmd1_rsp_present = 1`, and
# **`_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080`** - exactly the reference word the pre-registration named, bit 30 set with
# the voltage window `0x00ff8000` in bits 23:15; `_cmd1_err = 0`; `_cmd1_word_read = 0x00000102` where
# rung 13 measured CMD0's word reading back as 0x0000. **This is the first CMD1 this line has ever
# issued, and there is a real card on the other end of it.** CMD1's own poll timed out with
# `_cmd1_status_any = 0` - EXPECTED, because CMD1's send happens after the window has closed, which is
# this press's own finding read one command further along.
#
# **THE HAZARD DID NOT OCCUR**: no `_irq_other_*` key of any kind, so `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` at zero was
# the right way to buy this answer.
#
# **ONE PREDICTED CELL FAILED: `_int_status_after = 0x00000001` where the pre-registration said `0` -
# AND ITS FAILURE RE-READS 730.** `_ena_status_post = 0` (the window's close, after CMD0's poll) and
# `_int_status_before = 0` (st_int_report, later) both read the register clear, and the only write
# between the pair is `st_int_report`'s own `st_write32(ST_HC_MEM_BASE + ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE,
# ST_SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE)` - an absolute 0x00000001, not a read-modify-write, read out of the source and
# not inferred. **So the pair goes 0 -> 1 across one store to 0x34 at a moment when the block held no
# completion.** 730's headline inference - "the completion was there all along and the enable was the
# mask" - rested on exactly that pair across exactly that store, so **the pair does not separate a
# completion the enable revealed from an effect of writing 0x34, and 730's STATED evidence is
# ambiguous**. Its conclusion is not refuted: this press's `_cmd0_complete = 1` is better evidence for
# it, measured inside the poll that had read nothing for 5,088,256 samples on rung 13.
#
# **Two mechanisms remain and this press does not settle which**: (A) a write to 0x34 makes 0x30's
# RESPONSE bit read 1; (B) the block holds a completion pending and re-latches it into 0x30 when the
# enable returns - 726's sixth hypothesis, which 730 adopted and which this press does NOT put down. The
# counter-evidence to the naive form of (A) is in this same log and is not yet reconciled with it: with
# the enable standing from before the send, CMD0's poll read 0 for its first 538 samples.
# **THE NEXT RUNG IS NAMED BY THAT**: read `INT_STATUS 0x30` after a store to `0x34` on a block that has
# been quiet - no command sent since the previous clear - which separates a store's effect from a
# completion's. It is a read, not a new command class.
#
# **What the goal thinks of it**: 「把基础驱动跑起来」 is closer and STILL UNMET. Two commands now run to
# completion with a real card answering the second of them, but there is **no storage** - no data line
# has moved a byte and no driver is registered as a storage driver - so **TWRP-to-storage stays
# withheld** (the clause is conditioned on 「如果os已经能进去了的话」). The frontier reading did not move,
# so this press advanced the storage arm and not the boot.

# 734: THE ARM THIS SET IS - rung 16 by the ordinal every commit subject counts, `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=15`
# by the value this ladder counts - AND IT ANSWERS 733's FAILED PREDICTION BY CHANGING ONE VARIABLE.
#
# **The arm, in one sentence**: the same ONE-bit store to `INT_ENABLE 0x34` (`SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE`) that
# rungs 13 and 14 make, taken a second time in this ladder at the one moment that differs from theirs in
# exactly the variable the question needs - `st_quiet_enable_probe`, called from `entry_storage_probe`
# immediately BEFORE `st_cmd_path()` and after rung 9's wait, on a block that is in SDHCI mode, powered and
# clocked and HAS NEVER CARRIED A COMMAND. It reads `INT_STATUS 0x30`, `INT_ENABLE 0x34` and
# `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` and publishes all three, writes `INT_ENABLE <- (read | RESPONSE)`, reads `INT_STATUS`
# IMMEDIATELY (`_quiet_status_after`, this rung's answer), reads the enable back, writes it back to its
# before-value, then reads `INT_STATUS` again and the enable again. `SIGNAL_ENABLE` is READ and NEVER
# WRITTEN. TWO stores, both to `0x34`, the second the undo of the first; no new command, no
# `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word and no byte of the medium.
#
# **WHY, AND IT IS 733's OWN FAILED PREDICTION.** 733 pressed rung 14 and measured
# `_int_status_before = 0x00000000` -> `_int_status_after = 0x00000001` across exactly one store to `0x34`,
# while `_ena_status_post = 0` and `_int_status_before = 0` both read the register CLEAR - the same 0 -> 1
# transition 730 read as *the enable revealed a completion*, on a register just read as clear twice. So that
# pair does not separate (A) a write to `0x34` that makes `0x30`'s RESPONSE bit READ 1 from (B) a completion
# the block was holding and re-latched when the enable returned - 726's sixth hypothesis, which 733 did NOT
# put down. **(B) NEEDS A COMPLETION TO HAVE HAPPENED, AND THIS ARM RUNS WHERE NONE EVER HAS**: (B) predicts
# `_quiet_status_after = 0` and (A) predicts `1`. **Either reading is an answer**, which is what makes this a
# cheap rung: one store, its restore, and no new command class.
#
# **THE COUNTER-EVIDENCE THAT KEEPS (A) FROM BEING ASSUMED IS IN 733'S LOG**: with the enable standing from
# before CMD0's send, CMD0's poll read `INT_STATUS = 0` for its first 538 samples (`_cmd0_any_polls = 0x21b`
# against `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219`), so a naive *writing 0x34 always sets 0x30's bit 0* does not describe
# the poll. `_quiet_status_after` beside `_cmd0_any_polls` is the pair that settles it.
#

# `xnu_arm_entry.bin` sha256 prefix - the rule 16 of 16 prior `armed-storage-*` sets follow, and the one the
# 732 block's first draft broke. The entry bin's SIZE is byte-identical to the pressed arm's (5,552,764), so

#   _quiet_wrote        = 0x00000001        INT_ENABLE <- read | SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE
#   _quiet_status_after = 0x00000000   <==  THIS RUNG'S ANSWER
#   _quiet_held         = 0x00008001        the write ALSO set bit 15 (SDHCI_INT_ERROR) - 730's readback, reproduced
#   _quiet_wrote_back   = 0x00000000        the restore wrote the value the body read
#   _quiet_status_post  = 0x00000000
#   _quiet_readback     = 0x00008000        the restore did NOT clear bit 15 - 730's and 733's measurement, in person
#
# **`_quiet_status_after = 0x00000000` ON A BLOCK WITH NOTHING TO COMPLETE.** A write to `INT_ENABLE 0x34`
# does NOT make `INT_STATUS 0x30`'s RESPONSE bit read 1 - mechanism (A) is REFUTED, at the one moment it
# could be tested. That retires the reading 730 gave its `0 -> 1` transition and leaves 726's sixth
# hypothesis as the sole survivor: the bit appears when a COMPLETED COMMAND's status is latched while the
# enable stands, which is what 733's arm had (two commands had run) and this one did not. Note what this
# does and does not say: it does not by itself CONFIRM (B) - a null reading cannot confirm a mechanism
# that needs a completion - it removes (A), which was the alternative 733 left standing.
#
# **AND THE STORE'S OWN STICKY BIT CLOSED THE RUNG BELOW IT. NO COMMAND WENT ON THE BUS THIS PRESS.**
# Because `_quiet_readback` is 0x8000 and not 0, `st_cmd_path`'s gate - whose whole job is "INT_ENABLE must
# hold nothing but RESPONSE" - read `0x00008000` at its top and refused:
#
#   _cmd_calls       = 0x00000001
#   _cmd_int_enable  = 0x00008000        <== set by THIS RUN'S OWN quiet store, ~200 lines earlier
#   _cmd_sig_enable  = 0x00000000
#   _cmd_gate_kind   = 0x00000002        the gate's second reason: a bit other than RESPONSE was already set
#   _cmd_enabled_out = 0x00000001
#   _cmd_refused     = 0x00000001
#   _cmd_sent        = 0x00000000        nothing was issued
#   _cmd_gated       = 0x00000000        the BETWEEN-COMMANDS gate never ran - the body returned above it
#   _cmd_ps_before   = 0x01f80000        the controller is idle, before and after
#   _cmd_ps_after    = 0x01f80000
#   _cmd_done        = 0x00000000
#
# **EVERY KEY PUBLISHED INSIDE `st_cmd_path` IS ABSENT, COUNTED AND NOT ASSUMED**: `_cmd0_sent`,
# `_cmd0_complete`, `_cmd0_status_any`, `_cmd0_any_polls`, `_cmd0_inhibit_seen`, `_cmd1_sent`,
# `_cmd1_complete`, `_cmd1_resp`, `_cmd1_err`, `_int_status_before`, `_int_status_after`, `_ena_wrote`,
# `_ena_held`, `_ena_status_pre`, `_ena_status_post`, `_ena_wrote_back`, `_ena_readback`,
# `_ena_restore_seq` - **0 occurrences each**. So this press did not re-measure the command path, and the
# `0 -> 1` pair 733 reported is not in this log at all.
#
# **AND IT CORRECTS THE RUNG-15 NARRATION'S OWN SENTENCE.** That narration said `_cmd_gate_kind = 2` would
# be *"a fact about the block that no earlier rung could see"*. It was a fact about **this run's own store
# two hundred lines earlier** - the quiet body wrote `0x34`, bit 15 is set by a write and not cleared by
# one (730 and 733 had both measured that), and the gate below then saw it. The value had a second
# producer and the narration named the wrong one, which is m739's shape pointing the other way: the
# two-point reading was fine and the sentence beside it was not.
#
# **THE DESIGN LESSON, stated where the next arm will read it**: `INT_ENABLE` bit 15 is a DURABLE side
# effect of any write to `0x34`. A rung that writes that register and then wants `st_cmd_path` to run must
# either write it AFTER the gate or accept that the gate refuses - and 733's arm is the proof of the
# ordering, because its store sat inside `st_cmd_path` after the gate and its commands went out.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT HELD, and it is the reason this is a cost and not a loss**: the gate refused
# BEFORE any command, `_cmd_sent = 0`, `_cmd_ps_after = 0x01f80000` unchanged, `SIGNAL_ENABLE` never
# written, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no byte of the medium, nothing flashed. The runner's verdict is 14
# PASS / 2 FAIL and both FAILs are the one known storage-arm criterion (`seam_sp=0x80557ec8 is not
# sleh_sp-8`, a seam-arm check a storage arm cannot satisfy - 694, 697, 705 and rung 12 all carry it).
# The log's ending is the same shape as 730's and 733's: the kernel's own `No errors detected`, the device
# back on Android on its own.

# 737: THE ARM THIS SET IS - rung 17 by the ordinal every commit subject counts, `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=16`
# in the ladder's own spelling - `armed-storage-cid-dc8d7002`, ARMED AND NOT PRESSED. It is 732's arm's whole
# act plus `mmc_all_send_cid`'s CMD2, the ladder's first command with a 136-BIT RESPONSE, and its gate is the
# DRIVER'S own condition read off CMD1's RESPONSE (`c1.sent != 0 && (c1.resp & MMC_CARD_BUSY) == 0`) rather
# than off a status bit, because 733's press measured `_cmd1_complete = 0` with `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080`.
# **AND THE FINDING THAT SHAPES IT IS 736's COST, WHICH IS THIS RUNG'S CONSTRAINT**: the first build of this
# arm compiled rung 15's `st_quiet_enable_probe` in (its guard was `>= 15`, cumulative like every rung below
# it), that body writes `INT_ENABLE 0x34` BEFORE `st_cmd_path` runs, a write to that register leaves bit 15
# set with no write to clear it, and the gate would therefore have read `0x00008000` and REFUSED THE WHOLE
# COMMAND PATH exactly as it did on 736's press - `_cmd_sent = 0`, `c1.sent = 0`, this rung's own gate
# refusing for reason 1, and EVERY `_cid_*` CELL ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION while a press was spent. That is
# m720's shape (an absent key one of whose producers is guaranteed). The repair is an exclusion written in
# FOUR places: the body's own `#if` is `== 15`, its call site's is `== 15`, the ladder's `#error` clause
# spells value 16 as "15 with 15's own body left out", and `build_entry.sh` guards the rung-15 clause
# `-eq 15` while the rung-16 clause REFUSES the symbol's presence and the call site's presence. No rung above
# 15 needs 15's answer: `_quiet_status_after = 0` was read on 736's press and (A) is refuted in the record.
# #
# # THE BUILD CLAUSE, AND IT IS THE ONLY PLACE THIS BODY IS CLASSIFIED. `st_all_send_cid` is a `noinline`
# static, so `nm` places it before `entry_storage_probe` and neither the probe's store census nor the
# width-vs-offset census reads a line of it. `xnu_entry_737` holds it to: TWELVE device accesses and no more
# (`f9824934:str=2` - the window's open and its restore, each read back - `f9824934:ldr=2`, `f9824938:ldr`
# for SIGNAL_ENABLE READ AND NEVER WRITTEN, `f9824930:ldr` for INT_STATUS after the restore, `f9824924:ldr`
# for PRESENT_STATE at the end, the four words at 1c/18/14/10, and the three BYTE reads at 1b/17/13 that only
# this rung takes), the store set `f9824934:str` alone, the program-order list (the store FIRST, before the
# command, then the enable read back, then the signal enable, then the four words descending each followed by
# its byte, then the restore), an EMPTY image side, EXACTLY ONE call site, **that call ordered after
# `st_cmd_path`'s LAST `bl <st_send_command>`** (the clause that refuses the arm placed above the gate), and
# in the other direction the ABSENCE of `st_quiet_enable_probe` from the image and from the probe's calls.
# SIX FALSIFICATIONS, each measured on a real build: rung 15's body put back -> FAIL naming the symbol; a
# byte read made 32-bit -> FAIL naming the set (it loses `f982491b:ldrb`); the call MOVED ABOVE THE GATE ->
# FAIL naming lines 322 and 336; the restore deleted -> FAIL naming `f9824934:str=1`; the body renamed ->
# FAIL naming the missing symbol; the call duplicated -> FAIL naming 2 calls. Nothing is rebuilt by any
# refusal, and the clean build passes every one.
# #
# # AND THE THREE WORDS THIS LADDER PUTS ON `SDHCI_COMMAND 0x0E` ARE NOW ASSERTED AT COMPILE TIME, because
# until this rung nothing checked them: the build's `st_send_command` clause asserts that body's ACCESSES and
# never the VALUE it stores, so swapping the 136-bit and busy arms would have put `0x020b` on the bus with
# every set, count and cell still reading the way the record says. The decode is one macro used twice - by
# the body and by `_Static_assert` - and the three words are `0x0000` (CMD0), `0x0102` (CMD1, which four
# archived logs read back out of the block) and `0x0209` (CMD2: opcode 2, RESP_LONG 0x01 | CRC 0x08, no
# INDEX). A comment in the source claimed those two were "asserted by the build clause" and that was FALSE -
# m742's shape; the assertion now exists.
# #
# # A NARRATION DEFECT FOUND ON THE WAY, AND IT WAS 736's OWN EDIT. The 736 addendum inside the rung-14
# paragraph of `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` was written with FIVE UNESCAPED BACKTICKS inside a double-quoted
# string, so five key names were executed as command substitutions and the press-time print read
# `line 829: _cmd_gate_kind: command not found` with the names missing from the sentence - m730 exactly,
# reintroduced by the step that wrote a sentence about a refusal. Escaped (70 pairs), and row 4 now REFUSES
# an unescaped backtick on any line that BUILDS the narration, naming the lines; falsified with a pair
# unescaped on two lines -> FAIL naming [786 832], while a SINGLE unescaped backtick is loud anyway (the file
# stops parsing). Single-quoted extractor patterns are exempt on purpose, or the check would refuse itself.
# #
# # THE SAFETY CONTRACT, and it is the same one rung 14 has with the stores moved below the gate: TWO stores,
# both to `INT_ENABLE 0x34`, both inside `st_cmd_path`'s own body and BELOW the gate that reads the register;
# `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` READ AND NEVER WRITTEN at any rung (the conjunction of the two is what raises this
# block's SPI 123 -> intid 155, a line nobody here owns); CMD2 is a `bcr` with a 136-bit response and NO DATA
# PHASE, so no `BLOCK_SIZE`/`BLOCK_COUNT`/`TRANSFER_MODE`/`BUFFER`, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no
# `core_mem` word, and NO BYTE OF THE MEDIUM CAN CHANGE. The payload's own switch set is byte-identical to
# the pressed arm's (`6c2b6038...`, measured), so no payload switch moved. The failure mode is a diagnosis:
# the command bound is inside `st_send_command`, the run's ending is unchanged from 730/733/736, and a
# `_cid_gated = 1` is a complete answer published WITH its reason rather than a set of keys that are absent.
# #
# # THE BUILDS WERE RUN AND THIS IS THE DISCLOSURE: the entry image three times (the first for the
# measurement, then after the word-assertion refactor, then once for reproducibility - all three of the
# FINAL source gave `dc8d7002...` byte-for-byte, measured with `cmp`) and the payload once. The pressed arm
# was parked and exported first. `./scripts/build.sh` does not reproduce the payload (408), so this is not a
# routine step. Measurements: the entry image is 5,552,764 B - THE SAME SIZE as the pressed arm's - with
# `.text` 5,331,560 -> 5,332,264 (+704 = `st_all_send_cid` 668 + `st_send_command` +36 + `st_cmd_path` +104
# - `entry_storage_probe` 16 - `st_quiet_enable_probe` 220), `st_all_send_cid` at 0x8000db38 size 0x29c, its
# call line 472 against the last `bl <st_send_command>` at 315, and **THE SEAM CONSTANT DID NOT MOVE**: the
# `bl <__wrap_FlushPoU_Dcache>` is still at 0x800492d8 returning to 0x800492dc, read out of the LIVE ELF.
# One reading worth keeping: the word-assertion refactor moved `st_send_command` by exactly 4 bytes and
# every symbol after it with it, and the access sets the rung-11 clause asserts are unchanged - so the
# refactor is a codegen change and not a behaviour change, and the arm was named from the final build.
# #
# # THE ARM IS ARMED AND NOT PRESSED, and nothing is owed. Readiness 5 of 5 (flags `--allow-xnu-entry`,
# `--expect-arm=armed-storage-cid-dc8d7002`), the park and the export VERIFIED (11 files, 6 manifest
# members), `tools/check_set_name_rule.sh` exit 0 (26 sets, 25 hash-named), `tools/rehearse_revert_set.sh`
# 38 ok / 0 failed, `make check` exit 0, and the gate itself exit 0 under `--allow-xnu-entry` - which is the
# reading that says the tree in front of the gate is the tree the image was built from, because the entry
# sources are verified BY CONTENT. **THE PRESS IS THE OPERATOR'S**, and the paths are `scripts/`:
#   sudo -n adb devices   # 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe must be ABSENT
#   ./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry
#   ./scripts/run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-cid-dc8d7002
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").

# ================================================================================================
# 738: THIS SET WAS PRESSED. **CMD2 WENT ON THE BUS, THE BLOCK GAVE NOTHING BACK, AND THE RESPONSE
# REGISTER IS NOT A WITNESS.** Read this block as the answer to the pre-registration above; it
# supersedes the ARM/SET lines and nothing else in it.
# ================================================================================================
# PRESSED 2026-09-26 14:41:56-14:43:13 UTC, **RUNNER EXIT=0** (returned and captured). Readiness 5/5
# (exit 0, run from the same tree immediately before the fire), ONE gate (exit 0, **585** stdout lines)
# and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-cid-dc8d7002`. One
# non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe was off BOTH lists at fire
# time (adb: [4a2fe00b device], fastboot: []) and was never touched. Capture
# `out/stage90/captures/rung17-cid-20260926-144313-last_kmsg.txt`, **628,500 B, sha256
# `ad9d77c92df2a7eb2134a01cf0c61d0d8bd9a19f1b14ada9d7bcb5add11b0a3e`**, archived by hand with the gate
# log, the run log and the combined press log under the same timestamp (out/ is gitignored and the
# runner does not archive). Nothing was flashed and no byte of the medium moved. **No firer was armed:
# the press path is the two commands readiness prints, run directly once each (730's finding, and the
# launcher's bytes are kept nowhere).**
#
# THE ORDERING DEFECT 736 FOUND IS REPAIRED, AND THE FOUR-PLACE EXCLUSION HELD IN THE BYTES:
#  * `_cmd_int_enable = 0x00000000` (NOT `0x00008000`), `_cmd_gate_kind = 0x00000000`,
#    `_cmd_refused = 0x00000000`, `_cmd_sent = 0x00000002`, `_cmd_ps_after = 0x01f80000`.
#  * **ZERO `_quiet_*` KEYS** - 15's body is not in this image, so the exclusion is a property of the
#    bytes and not of three source clauses. This is the FIRST PRESS SINCE 733's on which the command
#    path ran, so the rung below was re-measured rather than inherited from 736's refusal.
#
# CMD2 WENT ON THE BUS, AND THE DRIVER'S OWN WORD IS WHAT THE BLOCK HOLDS:
#   `_cid_calls = 0x1`, `_cid_gated = 0x0`, `_cid_op = 0x2`, `_cid_flags = 0x7` (MMC_RSP_R2 =
#   PRESENT|136|CRC), `_cid_word = _cid_word_read = 0x00000209`, `_cid_sent = 0x1`.
#   `0x0209` = opcode 2 << 8 | RESP_LONG 0x01 | CRC 0x08 - the decode this tree's VENDOR sdhci.h
#   demands (`:53` RESP_LONG, not upstream Linux's, and INDEX `0x10` correctly absent because
#   MMC_RSP_R2 carries no MMC_RSP_OPCODE). The three words this ladder puts on `0x0E` are
#   `_Static_assert`ed in the source and asserted again by the build clause.
#
# AND THE BLOCK GAVE NOTHING BACK - **WITH THE ENABLE STANDING FOR THE WHOLE SEND**:
#   `_cid_ena_wrote = 0x1`, `_cid_ena_held = 0x00008001`, `_cid_sig_enable = 0x0` (read, never
#   written), and then `_cid_complete = 0x0`, `_cid_status_any = 0x0` over the WHOLE register,
#   `_cid_polls = 0x004da800` (5,089,280), `_cid_ticks = 0x015f9651` (23,040,593 = 1.200 s at
#   19.2 MHz), `_cid_timeout = 0x1`, `_cid_err = 0x0`, `_cid_status_post = 0x0`,
#   `_cid_wrote_back = 0x0`, `_cid_readback = 0x00008000`.
#   **733's rule (a command's status is latched only while its enable stands) is NECESSARY AND NOT
#   SUFFICIENT**: CMD0 completed inside rung 14's window on 733 and here; CMD2 ran inside the same
#   window and nothing latched.
#   **AND `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0x00000000` over the first 1024 samples with `_cid_inhibit_last =
#   0x01f80000`** - CMD_INHIBIT was never once observed for CMD2, where CMD0's own was seen 537 times
#   (`_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219`) in the SAME RUN and CMD1's was also `0`. A command whose word reads
#   back and whose inhibit is never seen is a word the register file holds and the sequencer did not
#   take; that reading is available for two commands in this log.
#
# **THE 136-BIT READ RETURNED CMD1's RESPONSE WORD, AND THAT IS THIS PRESS'S FINDING**:
#   `_cid_raw3 (+0x10) = 0x00000000`, `_cid_raw2 (+0x14) = 0x00000000`,
#   `_cid_raw1 (+0x18) = 0x80000000`, `_cid_raw0 (+0x1c) = 0x0040ff80`;
#   `_cid_resp0 = 0x40ff8080` (the driver's own read: `(raw0 << 8) | byte at +0x0B`), `_cid_resp1..3 =
#   0`, `_cid_resp_short = 0`.
#   **`_cid_resp0` is BIT-FOR-BIT `_cmd1_resp` in the same log** - which 733 read from `RESPONSE +
#   0x10` (word 0) at CMD1's own moment - and read as one 128-bit big-endian register the four words
#   are that same 32-bit value ONE BYTE FURTHER ALONG. So the four words are NOT a CID.
#   The register DID move (after CMD0 `_cmd0_resp = 0x0` with `_cmd0_resp_read = 1`; after CMD1
#   `0x40ff8080`), so it is not a constant - but this press shows that word sitting in the register
#   **with no completion latched and no inhibit ever seen**, and re-presented in the long-response
#   positions when a long response is asked for.
#   **CONSEQUENCE, STATED PLAINLY**: since 733, `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080` has been read as *the card
#   answered CMD1*; 733's index row calls it "exactly the reference word", 736 section 5 built the
#   push from it, and **rung 17's own gate** (`c1.sent != 0 && (c1.resp & MMC_CARD_BUSY) == 0`) is
#   read off it. The gate's LOGIC still holds (a status-bit gate refuses on every arm of this ladder)
#   but the EVIDENCE it consumes is weaker than every rung since 733 has treated it as: `0x40ff8080`
#   has an OCR's SHAPE (bit 30 set, voltage window `0x00ff8000`), which is why it convinced, and shape
#   is not provenance. **This press did NOT decide whether a card answered CMD1** - it made the
#   question visible for the first time and left it open.
#
# THE RUNG BELOW IS INHERITED UNCHANGED, AND TWO OWED READINGS CAME BACK:
#   `_cmd0_word_read = 0x0`, `_cmd0_status_any = 0x1`, `_cmd0_any_polls = 0x21b`,
#   `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219`, `_cmd0_complete = 0x1`, `_cmd1_word_read = 0x102`,
#   `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080`, `_cmd1_resp_read = 0x1`, `_cmd1_complete = 0x0`,
#   `_cmd1_ticks = 0x015f9248`, `_int_status_before = 0x0`, `_int_status_after = 0x1` (730's pair,
#   reproduced), `_ena_held = 0x8001`, `_ena_readback = 0x8000`, `_ena_restore_seq = 0x1`.
#   **`_ena_host_version = 0x00001102`** - 697's owed reading, taken at the only width `0xFE`'s
#   alignment allows (a halfword): vendor `0x11`, spec version `0x02`. It is 730 section d's SDHCI-3.0
#   hypothesis with a version register beside it, and it is a reading to be read AGAINST that layout
#   claim, not a proof of it.
#   **No `_irq_other_*` key at all**, so the pre-registered failure mode (SPI 123 -> intid 155 landing
#   on the dispatcher) did not occur - a measurement on a second arm rather than a reason.
#   `_cid_readback = 0x00008000` reproduces the durable bit-15 finding a FOURTH time: a write to `0x34`
#   sets it and a write of `0` does not clear it.
#
# ONE DIFFERENCE, RECORDED RATHER THAN DROPPED: `_post_end_calls = 0x00000006` where 730, 733 and 736
# all read `0x00000007`. Every other ending cell is unmoved (`_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`,
# `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `_sleh_storm = 9`, `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800`), and this log's last
# `_post_elapsed = 0x06e848ea` sits just above the trigger's floor exactly as 736's does
# (`0x06de1e04` with the same `_seam_post_end_ticks`). `_post_end_calls` counts idle-exit PASSES and
# not time, so one fewer pass over the same window is pass-count jitter and not this arm's news - but
# a reader comparing the four logs will see it, and an unremarked difference is how a real one gets
# missed.
#
# THE RUNNER'S VERDICT: **14 PASS / 2 FAIL**, and BOTH FAILs are the one known storage-arm criterion
# (`seam_sp=0x80557ec8 is not sleh_sp-8`, which 694, 697, 705, rungs 12 and 13 all carry), so neither
# is about this arm. The log ends the way 730's, 733's and 736's do - the kernel's own
# `No errors detected`, the device back on Android on its own - so the ending is the ladder's normal
# shape.
#
# **WHAT THE NEXT RUNG IS, AND IT IS NOT ANOTHER COMMAND.** Two facts decide it and both are in hand:
#  (1) **THE REGISTER IS NOT A WITNESS.** Read `RESPONSE 0x10..0x1C` on a block with NO command in its
#      past - the same cheap, READ-ONLY shape as rung 3's census: no store, no new write class, no
#      command. If it is zero there, then `0x40ff8080` was put there by CMD1 and 733's reading
#      survives; if it already holds a value, then **the ladder's one piece of evidence that a card
#      exists is retired** and the whole push from CMD1 must be re-derived from a signal other than
#      RESPONSE. Otherwise the ladder spends presses collecting answers from a register.
#  (2) **`CMD_INHIBIT` IS THE DISCRIMINATOR NOBODY HAS USED AS ONE.** CMD0's `_cmd0_inhibit_seen =
#      0x219` says the block STARTED it; CMD1's (`0x0`) and CMD2's (`0x0`) say the block never showed
#      a start for either. A command whose inhibit is never observed and whose word reads back is a
#      command the register file holds and the sequencer did not take.
# The push the ladder was on before rung 16's detour is unchanged in the driver's own terms - CMD3
# `mmc_set_relative_addr`, CMD9 `SEND_CSD`, CMD7 `SELECT_CARD`, then CMD17 at LBA 1 where 531
# section 9's self-verifying reading lives (the eight bytes `EFI PART`, or an MBR ending `55 aa`) -
# but it now depends on a fact this press put in doubt.
#
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET**: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").

# ================================================================================================
# 739: A NEW ARM IS ARMED, AND IT IS A RUNG WHOSE NEW ACT IS A READ AND NOTHING ELSE.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-rb-05645d1a` - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=17`, ordinal rung 18, and the eight hex of
# its name is `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 prefix (the rule every `armed-storage-*` set in this
# record follows). It is 737's arm's whole act plus ONE READ-ONLY BODY, `st_resp_before`, called from
# `entry_storage_probe` immediately BEFORE `st_cmd_path()` and after rung 9's wait. **THE REASON IS
# 738's PRESS AND IT IS A DOUBT, NOT A NEW ACT**: the ladder's first 136-bit read returned
# `_cid_resp0 = 0x40ff8080` - **bit-for-bit `_cmd1_resp` from the same log**, with the four raw words
# holding that same value one byte further along - with `_cid_complete = 0` over 5,088,256 polls and
# `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0` where CMD0's own inhibit was seen 537 times in the same run. Since 733 that
# value has been read as *the card answered CMD1*: 733's index row calls it "exactly the reference
# word", 736 section 5 built the push plan from it, and rung 17's own gate is read off it. It has an
# OCR's SHAPE, which is why it convinced; **shape is not provenance**, and the register did move in that
# run (after CMD0 `_cmd0_resp = 0x0` with `_cmd0_resp_read = 1`), so "it changed during CMD1" does not
# separate the card from the block's own default for a command the sequencer never took.
#
# **THE ONE READ THAT DECIDES IT.** `RESPONSE 0x10` is read here and nowhere earlier: the only two
# bodies in the image that touch it read it AFTER a command (`st_send_command`, `st_all_send_cid`), and
# rung 3's census of ten registers does not read it at all. So this is the register's first reading on a
# block that has NEVER carried a command, and the two outcomes are exhaustive:
#   * `_rb_resp_zero = 1` - the register was EMPTY. CMD1's `0x40ff8080` was put there by CMD1, 733's
#     reading survives, and the next rung continues the driver from a fact rather than a hope.
#   * `_rb_resp_zero = 0` - the register already held a value with no command in the block's past. The
#     four SHIFTED pre-command words beside it then say which value, and if they match the post-CMD2
#     ones the ladder's one piece of evidence that a card exists is RETIRED and the push from CMD1 must
#     be re-derived from a signal other than RESPONSE. 738's own second cell - `CMD_INHIBIT` - is the
#     candidate: `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219`, `_cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0`, `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0`.
#
# **THE BODY, AND IT WRITES NOTHING AT ALL.** Twelve device accesses, EVERY ONE A READ AND EACH ADDRESS
# EXACTLY ONCE: the four RESPONSE words at `+0x1c/+0x18/+0x14/+0x10` in the driver's own order (word 3
# first) and the three BYTE fills it takes below them at `0x1b/0x17/0x13`, published twice - four raw
# words (`_rb_raw0`..`_rb_raw3`) beside four shifted ones (`_rb_resp0`..`_rb_resp3`) through ONE
# derivation, the same arithmetic `st_all_send_cid` uses after CMD2, so the pre-command words and the
# post-CMD2 words are comparable one for one in a single log; `_rb_resp_zero` derived in the image from
# the four raw words (this rung's answer); `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` whole with `_rb_inhibit` = its
# CMD_INHIBIT bit; `COMMAND 0x0e` as a HALFWORD (`_rb_cmd_word`, which must read `0x0000` if no command
# has ever carried); and `INT_STATUS 0x30`, `INT_ENABLE 0x34`, `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` - READ AND NEVER
# WRITTEN, `SIGNAL_ENABLE` included, at every rung. Plus `_rb_calls` and `_rb_done`.
#
# **THE WRITE SET IS EMPTY AND THAT IS WHY IT MAY SIT ABOVE `st_cmd_path`'s GATE.** 736's press is the
# measurement of what a store there costs: rung 15's write left bit 15 set in `INT_ENABLE 0x34` (a write
# to that register sets it and no write clears it), the gate read `0x00008000`, and NO COMMAND WENT ON
# THE BUS. A body that stores nothing cannot move a register the gate reads. There is no new command
# class, no data path, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word and no byte of the
# medium. **AND RUNG 15's BODY IS EXCLUDED HERE, ASSERTED ON THE SYMBOL AND ON THE CALL SITE**:
# `st_quiet_enable_probe` is compiled for the value 15 and no other, so a log from this arm carrying any
# `_quiet_*` key is a log from an arm that is not this one.
#
# **THE BUILD CLAUSE `xnu_entry_739` HOLDS IT TO ITS OWN SHAPE**, and its store clause is placed FIRST
# so that a perturbed build which adds a store is refused by the sentence that explains why. Asserted:
# the symbol is present and sized; its device access SET is exactly the twelve, sorted; its COUNTS are
# each exactly 1; its store set is EMPTY; its device accesses IN PROGRAM ORDER, distinct, are the four
# words descending each followed by its byte, then `PRESENT_STATE`, then `COMMAND` as a halfword, then
# the three interrupt registers; its image side is EMPTY; `entry_storage_probe` calls it exactly ONCE;
# and that call is ordered BEFORE `st_cmd_path`'s own call. **NINE FALSIFICATIONS, EACH REFUSED BY ITS
# OWN CLAUSE, EACH NAMEABLE**: a byte read made 32-bit -> the SET clause; a store of a read value added
# -> the READ-ONLY clause; a write-1-to-clear into `INT_STATUS 0x30` -> the READ-ONLY clause; a second
# read of a declared register -> the COUNTS clause; a read of an undeclared device offset -> the SET
# clause, reporting `NODECL-`; the call site moved BELOW `st_cmd_path` -> the ORDER clause; the call
# site duplicated -> the CALL-COUNT clause; the body renamed -> the SYMBOL clause; and the call site
# REMOVED -> the compiler's own `-Werror=unused-function`, which is upstream of the clause and is the
# honest note rather than a claim that the clause fired.
#
# **AND TWO CLAUSES WERE REPAIRED BEFORE THEY SHIPPED, BECAUSE THE FIRST DRAFT MADE THEM UNREACHABLE.**
# The store clause originally sat after the set and count clauses, where it could NEVER fire - any
# device store appears in the set as a `:str` entry, so the set clause always refuses first - and the
# set and count clauses' own texts claimed stores as their subject. That is
# `[[mi4-a-claim-in-a-comment-is-not-a-check]]` in its cheapest form: a refusal that describes a
# perturbed build it cannot be reached by. The store clause is now FIRST and the other two point at it.
#
# **THE BUILDS WERE RUN AND THIS IS THE DISCLOSURE.** The entry image TWICE for reproducibility (both
# clean builds of the final source gave `05645d1a` byte-for-byte, `cmp`-measured), and the payload
# ONCE; the pressed arm (`armed-storage-cid-dc8d7002`) was already parked and exported from 738's step
# and was not touched. `./scripts/build.sh` does not reproduce the payload (408), so this is not a
# routine step. Measurements: **entry image `05645d1a`, 5,552,764 B - THE SAME SIZE as the pressed
# arm's, so the seam constant DID NOT MOVE** (`platform_cache_idle_exit` at `0x800492d4`, its
# `bl <__wrap_FlushPoU_Dcache>` at `0x800492d8` returning to `0x800492dc` = `entry_storage_trace.c`'s
# `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR`, re-derived read-only from the FRESH `xnu_arm_entry_seam.dis`); `.text`
# 5,332,264 -> 5,332,776 (+512); `st_resp_before` at `0x8000d7c8`, size `0x17c` (380 B); `st_all_send_cid`
# moved to `0x8000dcb4`, size unchanged `0x29c`; and **the payload's own switch set is byte-identical to
# the pressed arm's** (`6c2b6038`, `cmp`-measured), so no payload switch moved and the config comparison
# the 732 block made owed holds for this arm too. `tools/check_arm_variant_pair.sh` agrees.
#
# **THE ARM IS ARMED AND NOT PRESSED** and nothing is owed. Readiness 5 of 5 (flags `--allow-xnu-entry`,
# `--expect-arm=armed-storage-rb-05645d1a`), the park and the export VERIFIED, `make check` exit 0, and
# the gate itself exit 0 - which is the reading that says the tree in front of the gate is the tree the
# image was built from, because the entry sources are verified BY CONTENT. **THE PRESS IS THE
# OPERATOR'S**, and the paths carry the `scripts/` prefix from the repo root:
#   sudo -n adb devices   # 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe must be ABSENT from BOTH lists
#   ./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry
#   ./scripts/run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-rb-05645d1a
# **No firer is armed**, and the press path is those two commands run by hand once each (730 section 6:
# the launcher's bytes are kept nowhere).
#
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").
