# 740: THIS SET WAS PRESSED. **THE RESPONSE REGISTER WAS EMPTY BEFORE ANY COMMAND, AND THE LADDER'S
# EVIDENCE SURVIVES.** Read this block as the answer to the pre-registration above; it supersedes the
# ARM/PRESSED lines and nothing else in it.
# ================================================================================================
# PRESSED 2026-09-26 15:58:25-15:59:37 UTC, **RUNNER EXIT=0** (returned and captured, 72 s end to end
# from the gate log's own mtime to the captured log's; the runner's own line is "the device came back
# 29s after this run called `fastboot boot`"). Readiness 5/5 (exit 0, run from the same tree immediately
# before the fire), ONE gate (exit 0, **594** stdout lines) and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under
# `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-rb-05645d1a`. One non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial
# 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe was off BOTH lists at fire time (adb: [4a2fe00b device], fastboot: [])
# and was never touched; no stray runner, gate or watcher was running. Capture
# `out/stage90/captures/rung18-rb-20260926-155937-last_kmsg.txt`, **642,375 B, sha256
# `eb641e07223b74cad7651b841017fce399f517de890a517775bb5bde47769201`**, archived by hand with the gate log,
# the run log and the combined press log under the same timestamp (out/ is gitignored and the runner does
# not archive). Nothing was flashed and no byte of the medium moved. **No firer was armed: the press path
# is the two commands readiness prints, run directly once each (730's finding).**
#
# THE ANSWER: **`_rb_resp_zero = 0x00000001` - `RESPONSE 0x10..0x1C` held NOTHING AT ALL before any
# command had ever been on the block's bus.** All four raw words read zero (`_rb_raw0` `+0x1C`,
# `_rb_raw1` `+0x18`, `_rb_raw2` `+0x14`, `_rb_raw3` `+0x10`), all four driver-derived words read zero,
# and `_rb_calls = _rb_done = 0x1` says the body ran. **So the `0x40ff8080` the SAME log reads after CMD2
# is not a reset default that was sitting in the register file**: this log carries the whole movement,
# `0 -> 0 -> 0x40ff8080` - zero before any command, `_cmd0_resp = 0x0` with `_cmd0_resp_read = 0x1` after
# CMD0, `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080` with `_cmd1_resp_read = 0x1` after CMD1 - so a command MOVED this
# register. **733's reading of `0x40ff8080` as CMD1's response survives, and 738's doubt about the
# ladder's one piece of evidence that a card exists is RETIRED**, which is exactly what the
# pre-registration said this outcome would buy. This is 738 section 4's own sentence discharged: "a read of
# `RESPONSE` on a block with no command in its past would settle it".
#
# THE OTHER TWELVE PRE-COMMAND CELLS CAME BACK AS PRE-REGISTERED, one for one:
#   `_rb_present    = 0x01f80000`   the controller idle, no command in flight (739 predicted exactly this)
#   `_rb_inhibit    = 0x00000000`   CMD_INHIBIT clear at the pre-command moment
#   `_rb_cmd_word   = 0x00000000`   the block's own copy of the last command word: nothing has carried
#   `_rb_int_status = 0x00000000`
#   `_rb_int_enable = 0x00000000`   <== **BIT 15 CLEAR**: nothing above the gate has written 0x34
#   `_rb_sig_enable = 0x00000000`   read and never written, at every rung
# `_rb_int_enable = 0` IS THE SAFETY READING AND IT IS A READING RATHER THAN A PROMISE. 736's press is the
# measurement of what a store above `st_cmd_path`'s gate costs - rung 15's write set bit 15 of
# `INT_ENABLE 0x34`, the gate read `0x00008000` and NO command went on the bus. This rung's body stores
# nothing, bit 15 is clear at the pre-command moment, and the gate below read `_cmd_int_enable = 0x0` and
# let the command path run (`_cmd_sent = 0x2`). **The read-only design is verified in the bytes of a real
# run, not only by the build clause `xnu_entry_739`.**
#
# AND THE ORDERING THE BUILD CLAUSE ASSERTS IS VISIBLE IN THE LOG'S OWN LINE NUMBERS: the twelve `_rb_*`
# cells are at lines 8673-8689 and `_cmd_calls` is at 8690, so the read-only body published all twelve and
# the command path was entered immediately after. Had the call been placed below `st_cmd_path`, every
# `_rb_*` value would have been a statement about a block that had already answered three commands and
# `_rb_resp_zero` could not have meant what its name says.
#
# THE EXCLUSION HELD AND THE RUNG BELOW IS INHERITED UNCHANGED:
#   `_cmd_int_enable = 0x00000000`   NOT 0x00008000
#   `_cmd_gate_kind  = 0x00000000`   gate 1 did not fire
#   `_cmd_refused    = 0x00000000`
#   `_cmd_sent       = 0x00000002`   two commands, not zero
#   `_cmd_ps_after   = 0x01f80000`   the controller idle, unchanged
#   `_quiet_*` keys  = 0 occurrences  rung 15's body is not in this image
# `_cid_*` reproduced 738 one for one: `_cid_word = _cid_word_read = 0x0209`, `_cid_op = 0x2`,
# `_cid_flags = 0x7`, `_cid_sent = 0x1`, `_cid_gated = 0x0`, `_cid_complete = 0x0`,
# `_cid_status_any = 0x0`, `_cid_err = 0x0`, `_cid_polls = 0x4da800`,
# `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0x0`, `_cid_raw0 = 0x0040ff80`, `_cid_raw1 = 0x80000000`,
# `_cid_raw2 = _cid_raw3 = 0x0`, `_cid_resp0 = 0x40ff8080`, `_cid_resp1..3 = 0x0`. `_cmd*` likewise:
# `_cmd0_status_any = _cmd0_complete = 0x1`, `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219`, `_cmd0_polls = 0x21a`,
# `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080`. **One difference was looked for and is NOT one**:
# `_cmd1_inhibit_last = 0x00f80000` against `0x01f80000` for CMD0 and CMD2 - but 738's own log reads
# `0x00f80000` in that cell, so it is a reproduced property of the CMD1 window and not this arm's news
# (738 section 5's discipline: an unremarked difference between two logs is how a real one gets missed).
#
# THE ENDING IS UNMOVED AND THE PRE-REGISTERED FAILURE MODE DID NOT HAPPEN A FOURTH TIME:
# `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `_seam_lr = 0x800492dc`,
# `_sleh_storm = 9`, `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800`, `_post_end_calls = 0x6` (738's own value), the seam's
# four words the clean pair (`_seam_a0 = _seam_b0 = 0x800b5648`, `_seam_a1 = _seam_b1 = 0x8047fb04`),
# the kernel's own `No errors detected`, and **0 occurrences of any `_irq_other_*` key** - SIGNAL_ENABLE
# was read and never written, the SPI 123 -> intid 155 line never rose, and the run did not end at the
# dispatcher. The runner's verdict is **14 PASS / 2 FAIL**, and both FAILs are the one known storage-arm
# criterion (`seam_sp=0x80557ec8 is not sleh_sp-8`, which 694, 697, 705, rungs 12, 13, 16 and 17 all
# carry), so neither is about this arm.
#
# WHAT THE NEXT RUNG IS, NOW THAT BOTH OF 738 section 7's DECIDING FACTS ARE IN HAND: the register IS a
# witness, so the push from CMD1 does NOT have to be re-derived; and **`CMD_INHIBIT` is the one thing
# still unexplained about CMD1 and CMD2** - not as a doubt about the response word, which is retired, but
# as the next question of its own: a command whose word reads back and whose response register moved, and
# whose inhibit was never observed, where CMD0's own inhibit was seen 537 times in the same window. CMD2's
# 136-bit read still returned CMD1's own word rather than a CID, so the CID is not in hand and the
# driver's continuation is the next rung: CMD3 `mmc_set_relative_addr`, CMD9 `SEND_CSD`, CMD7
# `SELECT_CARD`, then CMD17 at LBA 1, where 531 section 9's self-verifying reading lives (the eight bytes
# `EFI PART`, or an MBR ending `55 aa`) - **carrying the inhibit reading beside the response reading**.
#
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").

# ================================================================================================
# 741: A NEW ARM IS ARMED, AND IT IS THE FIRST RUNG WHOSE COMMAND CARRIES A NON-ZERO ARGUMENT.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-rca-c333d09c` - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=18`, ordinal rung 19, and the eight hex of
# its name is `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 prefix (the rule every `armed-storage-*` set in this
# record follows). It is 739's arm's whole act - the same two commands, the same three gates, the same
# census, the same bodies INCLUDING `st_resp_before`'s pre-command read - plus the driver's OWN NEXT
# STATEMENT: `mmc.c:1409`'s `mmc_set_relative_addr(card)`, transcribed from `mmc_ops.c:194-210`.
#
# **THE REASON IS 740's ANSWER, WHICH IS AN ANSWER AND NOT A DOUBT.** 740 pressed rung 18 and read
# `_rb_resp_zero = 0x00000001`: `RESPONSE 0x10..0x1C` held NOTHING AT ALL before any command had ever
# been on the block's bus, and the SAME log carries the whole movement `0 -> 0 -> 0x40ff8080`. So the
# `0x40ff8080` this ladder has read since 733 as *the card answered CMD1* was put there by CMD1, 733's
# reading survives, and **738's doubt about the ladder's one piece of evidence that a card exists is
# RETIRED**. What 740 left is the next question, and it is the driver's own continuation: CMD2's 136-bit
# read returned CMD1's own word rather than a 128-bit CID, so the CID is not in hand, and `mmc_attach_mmc`
# continues with CMD3 `mmc_set_relative_addr`, CMD9 `SEND_CSD`, CMD7 `SELECT_CARD`, then CMD17 at LBA 1.
#
# **WHAT THE COMMAND IS.** opcode `MMC_SET_RELATIVE_ADDR` 3 (`mmc.h:32`), argument `card->rca << 16` with
# `card->rca = 1` assigned eight lines earlier at `mmc.c:1400`, so the argument is **`0x00010000` - THE
# FIRST NON-ZERO ARGUMENT THIS LADDER HAS EVER PUT ON THE BUS** - and flags `MMC_RSP_R1 | MMC_CMD_AC` =
# `MMC_RSP_PRESENT | MMC_RSP_CRC | MMC_RSP_OPCODE`, which decodes under THIS TREE'S VENDOR ORDERING
# (`sdhci.h:53` has `RESP_LONG 0x01` where upstream has `RESP_SHORT 0x02`) to `RESP_SHORT 0x02 | CRC 0x08
# | INDEX 0x10` and the word **`0x031A`**. **That word is the ladder's first `INDEX` BIT**, and the bit is
# R1's own property rather than decoration: an R1 response carries the opcode back, so this is the first
# word here that is not `opcode << 8 | small_flags`. All three constants are `_Static_assert`ed in the
# source (`ST_SDHCI_CMD_WORD(ST_CMD_OP_SET_RELATIVE_ADDR, ST_MMC_RSP_R1) == 0x031Au` and
# `ST_MMC_RCA_1 == (1u << 16)`), because the build's `st_send_command` clause asserts that body's
# ACCESSES and never the value it stores.
#
# **THE NEW CELL IS A PAIR, WHICH IS WHAT RUNG 18's METHOD MAKES POSSIBLE.** `st_set_relative_addr` reads
# `RESPONSE 0x10` through the DRIVER'S OWN WORD-0 DERIVATION - `(readl(RESPONSE + 0x1C) << 8) |
# readb(RESPONSE + 0x1B)` - immediately BEFORE the one-bit window opens and again immediately AFTER the
# command's publishes, so `_rca_resp_pre` and `_rca_resp_post` are ONE ARITHMETIC TAKEN AT TWO TIMES and
# **`_rca_resp_moved` is this rung's answer**: 1 says CMD3 MOVED the register, 0 says it did not. The
# three times are comparable in one log (`_rb_resp0` before any command, `_cid_resp0` after CMD2,
# `_rca_resp_pre`/`_post` around CMD3) because they are one derivation and not three that agree.
# **`_rca_resp_is_arg` names the alternative producer**: 1 iff the word after CMD3 equals the ARGUMENT
# `0x00010000`, so a register echoing what was written to `ARGUMENT 0x08` is a published reading rather
# than a coincidence a reader has to think of. **And the SHAPE test is in the log too**: `_rca_state`
# (`(resp >> 9) & 0xF`, `CURRENT_STATE`), `_rca_ready` (bit 8) and `_rca_illegal` (bit 22) are the R1
# fields, published beside the raw `_rca_resp` - because CMD1's answer `0x40ff8080` has an OCR's shape
# and an R1 CARD STATUS does not, so a word that comes back as `0x40ff8080` again is the register not
# answering CMD3 at all.
#
# **THE INHIBIT TRIPLET COMES WITH IT, SO ONE LOG HOLDS FOUR COMMANDS' OWN INHIBIT READINGS.**
# `_rca_inhibit_after`, `_rca_inhibit_seen` and `_rca_inhibit_last` are the same three cells rung 12
# added, read for CMD3 as they are for CMD0, CMD1 and CMD2 - which is 740 section 6's next question made
# answerable rather than asked: `_cmd0_inhibit_seen` was `0x219` while `_cmd1_inhibit_seen` and
# `_cid_inhibit_seen` were both ZERO, and a CMD3 whose inhibit is seen beside a CMD3 whose register moved
# is a pair that separates *the sequencer took it* from *the register file answered*.
#
# **THE GATE IS THE ONE READING RUNG 17's BODY NOW RETURNS.** `mmc.c` puts `mmc_set_relative_addr`
# immediately after `mmc_all_send_cid` with no condition beyond `!mmc_host_is_spi(host)`, so the driver
# itself does not gate CMD3 on a completion - which this arm cannot read anyway, the latch being the
# thing this whole line of rungs is about. So the gate is the weakest one that is still a reading: **CMD2
# was SENT**. `st_all_send_cid` now returns the field it publishes as `_cid_sent`, so the gate and the
# cell are ONE READING WITH TWO CONSUMERS and cannot disagree - and it is deliberately NOT the CMD2
# response, because CMD2's response is the reading 740 has just re-established rather than a new
# condition. `st_send_command` still carries the driver's own two per-command guards (the bounded wait
# for `SDHCI_CMD_INHIBIT` to clear, and the refusal to issue when the write-1-to-clear left a command bit
# latched), so nothing here can put a command on a bus another command still holds.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE EVERY RUNG SINCE 13 CARRIES.** TWO stores, both to
# `INT_ENABLE 0x34`: the one-bit window `(read | SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE)` taken immediately before CMD3 is
# put on the bus - because 733's press measured that a command's status bit is latched only while its
# enable stands (CMD0 completed inside rung 14's window; CMD1, sent with it closed, was answered and
# never latched) - and its restore on ONE unconditional line immediately after the publishes. The value
# written back is the value the gate read, not a constant, because bit 15 (`SDHCI_INT_ERROR`) rides along
# with any write to that register and no write clears it, so by this point the register reads
# `0x00008000` and the restore is a PARTIAL one (730, 733, 736, 738 and 740 all measured that pair).
# **`SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` is READ AND NEVER WRITTEN, at every rung** - it is the register whose conjunction
# with `INT_ENABLE` raises this block's SPI 123 -> intid 155, a line nobody here owns. No new command
# class beyond CMD3, no data path, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word, and no byte
# of the medium. The failure mode is a DIAGNOSIS and not a lost device: the 1.2 s command bound is inside
# `st_send_command`, and a delivery on that line would end the run at the dispatcher as `_irq_other_count
# = 1` / `iat = 155`, an ending this image already reads and survives (709 ended that way on intid 170).
#
# **THE BUILD CLAUSE `xnu_entry_741` HOLDS THE BODY TO ITS OWN SHAPE**, and its clauses refuse in this
# order: the SYMBOL is present (an unreferenced static is dropped, and a build without it publishes every
# `_rca_*` cell as absent while the record claims the rung - m720's shape); it has a SIZE; its device
# access SET is exactly `f982491b:ldrb f982491c:ldr f9824924:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824934:ldr f9824934:str
# f9824938:ldr`; its COUNTS are exactly `f982491b:ldrb=2 f982491c:ldr=2 f9824924:ldr=1 f9824930:ldr=1
# f9824934:ldr=2 f9824934:str=2 f9824938:ldr=1`; its STORES are exactly `f9824934:str`; its device
# accesses IN PROGRAM ORDER, distinct, are `f982491c:ldr f982491b:ldrb f9824934:str f9824934:ldr
# f9824938:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824924:ldr` - **the FRESHNESS BASELINE FIRST, before any store**; its image
# side is EMPTY; `st_cmd_path` calls it exactly ONCE; and that call is ordered AFTER `st_all_send_cid` and
# after the command path's last `bl <st_send_command>`. The four `st_send_command` call sites are
# `[st_cmd_path 2, st_all_send_cid 1, st_set_relative_addr 1]`.
#
# **WHAT THE CLAUSE DOES NOT ASSERT, SAID SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR IT**: the ARGUMENT and the WORD. A
# body's memory accesses and its `bl` sites carry no immediate, so the opcode, the flags and
# `0x00010000` are carried by the source's `_Static_assert`s and by the RUN's own `_rca_op`, `_rca_flags`,
# `_rca_arg` and `_rca_word` cells. A perturbation that changed the argument to zero would build, and
# this record would then carry `_rca_arg = 0x00000000` beside a document that says `0x00010000` - a
# disagreement between the log and the record, and not one this build can see.
#
# **ELEVEN FALSIFICATIONS, AND TWO OF THEM WERE REFUSED BY SOMETHING OTHER THAN THE CLAUSE THEY WERE
# WRITTEN FOR - WHICH IS THE FINDING, NOT THE VERDICT.** Perturbations 1 to 6 and 10 refuse exactly as
# intended: a byte read made 32-bit at `0x18` -> the SET clause, reporting `NODECL-f982-2328:ldr`; the
# post half taken from the pre half -> the COUNTS clause; the window's restore store removed -> the
# COUNTS clause, `f9824934:str=1`; a write-1-to-clear into `INT_STATUS 0x30` -> the SET clause,
# `f9824930:str`; a store to `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` -> the SET clause, `f9824938:str`; the baseline moved
# below the window store -> the PROGRAM ORDER clause; a fifth `st_send_command` call inside the body ->
# the four-call-site clause, `[2, 1, 2]`. **The call site duplicated** -> the CALL-COUNT clause. **The
# call site removed** -> the compiler's own `-Werror=unused-function`, which is upstream of the clause
# and is the honest note rather than a claim that the clause fired. **THE CALL SITE MOVED ABOVE
# `st_all_send_cid`** -> the SYMBOL clause, and that is the interesting one: moving the whole gated block
# above the gate makes `cid_sent` provably 0 there, so the branch - and the call - is DEAD, the static is
# dropped, and the ORDER clause's own subject is never built. A LIVE call placed above CMD2 (perturbation
# 11) is what reaches it, and it is refused there: *"st_cmd_path calls st_set_relative_addr on
# disassembly line 447 and st_all_send_cid on line 474: CMD3 must come AFTER CMD2."*
#
# **AND THE TWO THAT WERE REFUSED BY SOMETHING ELSE ARE THE HARNESS'S DEFECT, RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN
# DROPPED.** The batch asserted that a perturbation's anchor was PRESENT (`count >= 1`) and replaced the
# FIRST occurrence, so the first run of perturbations 3 and 4 edited bodies other than the one under
# test: `st_set_relative_addr`'s restore anchor occurs three times in the source and the first is in
# `st_quiet_enable_probe`, a body compiled for the value 15 and NO OTHER - so perturbation 4's
# `BUILT-OK` was a statement about DEAD CODE - and perturbation 3's first-occurrence edit landed in
# `st_all_send_cid` and was refused by THAT body's clause. Re-run with `count == 1` asserted on every
# edit, both are refused by the rung-19 clause as cell 3 and cell 4 above. The class is m714's point (2)
# - *a mutation that cannot reach its target has tested nothing* - one level earlier: there the wrong
# clause fired, here the wrong BYTES were edited.
#
# **THE BUILDS WERE RUN AND THIS IS THE DISCLOSURE.** The entry image FOUR times (the rung-19 build, two
# reproducibility builds of the final source giving `c333d09c` byte-for-byte, and one after the
# falsification batch restored the source) and the payload TWICE. **The first payload build of this arm
# was made WITHOUT `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'`** - `./scripts/build.sh` takes its
# variant switches from that variable and its default leaves `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY` at `0u`, where every
# `armed-storage-*` arm carries `1` - and the result was a payload with the entry path switched off and a
# **682-byte config that differed from the arm's at line 5**, caught by `cmp` against the parked pressed
# arm and rebuilt with the flag (`6c2b6038`, `cmp`-measured, byte-identical). **This is the same mistake
# the rung-15 step made and recorded, so it is now a known shape and still not a refusal** - the gate's
# flags are derived from the arm and nothing in the tree compares a fresh payload's config to the arm's
# own record line. The other rung-19 measurements: **entry image `c333d09c`, 5,552,764 B - THE SAME SIZE
# as the pressed arm's, so the seam constant DID NOT MOVE** (`platform_cache_idle_exit` at `0x800492d4`,
# its `bl <__wrap_FlushPoU_Dcache>` at `0x800492d8` returning to `0x800492dc`, re-derived read-only from
# the fresh disassembly); `.text` 5,332,776 -> 5,333,960 (+1,184); `st_set_relative_addr` at
# `0x8000df60`, size `0x2d8` (728 B); `st_all_send_cid` at `0x8000dcc0`, size `0x2a0`; `st_cmd_path` at
# `0x8000e238`, size `0x788`; `st_send_command` at `0x8000cff8`, size `0x320`; `st_resp_before` at
# `0x8000d7d4`, size `0x17c`. `tools/check_arm_variant_pair.sh` agrees.
#
# **THE ARM IS ARMED AND NOT PRESSED** and nothing is owed. Readiness 5 of 5 (flags `--allow-xnu-entry`,
# `--expect-arm=armed-storage-rca-c333d09c`), `tools/rehearse_live_path.sh` **39 ok / 0 failed, exit 0**
# (the runner's own state machine against stubs - no device), the park and the export VERIFIED (11 files
# each), `make check` exit 0, and the gate itself exit 0 - which is the reading that says the tree in front of the gate
# is the tree the image was built from, because the entry sources are verified BY CONTENT. **THE PRESS IS
# THE OPERATOR'S**, and the paths carry the `scripts/` prefix from the repo root:
#   sudo -n adb devices   # 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe must be ABSENT from BOTH lists
#   ./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry
#   ./scripts/run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-rca-c333d09c
# **No firer is armed**, and the press path is those two commands run by hand once each (730 section 6:
# the launcher's bytes are kept nowhere).
#
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").

# ================================================================================================
# 742: THE RUNG-19 ARM WAS PRESSED, AND IT ANSWERED IN TWO CELLS THIS LADDER HAD NEVER SEEN.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-rca-c333d09c` - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=18`, ordinal rung 19 - was PRESSED on
# 2026-09-26, **EXIT 0**, 18:08:10-18:09:22 UTC, by the operator's authorization. One readiness (5 of
# 5, exit 0, 18:07:33), exactly one `preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry` (exit 0, 587 lines,
# written 18:08:07), exactly one `run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry
# --expect-arm=armed-storage-rca-c333d09c`. `fastboot boot` only, nothing flashed, no `POWER_CONTROL
# 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word, no byte of the medium. The neighbour `33e80afe` was absent
# from BOTH `fastboot devices` and `adb devices` at fire time (0 occurrences) and no stray runner, gate
# or watcher was running. The runner's own line: the device came back 28 s after the send; the interval
# from the gate log's mtime to the captured log's is 74 s. Capture
# `out/stage90/captures/rung19-rca-20260926-180922-last_kmsg.txt`, 630,486 B, sha256
# 990983a9477eb556a05fbf50e61f68f7a2625f8a8e3d3de03785e7af0a6c8944, ARCHIVED BY HAND (the runner does
# not archive - 730 section 6). The three press logs are under the job tmp and are named here so their
# shas are checkable: readiness (23 lines, 01b4e68b593d67655710eca11fe7a24812b93f8bece44737ad993dd3fad1175b),
# gate (587 lines, 3a16875d40d60cbe88e496b9bf93d8aa0924c2c00e1f12c74b5f8cd6bb7599e9), combined run log
# (1095 lines, 8f74372adc27dfd474af790e84b90f0ebacaac4c742997cfeb2242a05ae870e8). THE ARM IS SPENT.
#
# THE ANSWER: CMD3 WAS ACCEPTED AND STARTED, AND IT NEVER FINISHED. `_rca_word = _rca_word_read =
# 0x031a` and `_rca_arg = _rca_arg_wrote = 0x00010000` - BOTH READ BACK OUT OF `COMMAND 0x0E` and
# `ARGUMENT 0x08`, so the ladder's first non-zero argument and its first INDEX bit are on the bus and
# in the block - with `_rca_op = 3`, `_rca_flags = 0x15`, `_rca_sent = 1`, `_rca_gated = 0`. AND THEN:
# `_rca_complete = 0`, `_rca_err = 0`, `_rca_timeout = 1`, `_rca_status_any = 0` with `_rca_any_polls
# = 0` over `_rca_polls = 0x004da400` (5,088,256 samples) and `_rca_ticks = 0x015f92d7` (23,040,727 =
# 1.2000 s at 19,200,000 Hz). 726's sixth hypothesis - this controller completes a command and does
# not set its interrupt-status register - measured a third time.
#
# AND THREE THINGS ARE NEW, WHICH IS WHY THIS PRESS CHANGED THE QUESTION:
#  (1) `_rca_inhibit_seen = 0x400` - ALL 1024 SAMPLES - with `_rca_inhibit_last = 0x01f80001`, BIT 0
#      STILL SET, and `_rca_ps_after = 0x01f80001` the same word read at the far end. THE FIRST
#      OBSERVATION IN THIS LADDER OF A COMMAND STILL IN PROGRESS AT THE SAMPLING WINDOW'S END. The
#      contrast is in the same capture: `_rb_present = 0x01f80000`, `_cmd_ps_before = 0x01f80000` and
#      `_cmd_ps_after = 0x01f80000` - three PRESENT_STATE reads with bit 0 clear, one of them bracketing
#      the only command that ever completed.
#  (2) `_rca_resp_pre = 0x40ff8080` -> `_rca_resp = 0x40ff8080` -> `_rca_resp_post = 0x00000000`, so
#      `_rca_resp_moved = 1` and `_rca_resp_is_arg = 0`: THE REGISTER MOVED, AND IT MOVED TO ZERO. An
#      SDHCI host clears RESPONSE when it begins a response-expecting command; the response registers
#      were CLEARED and nothing was written into them. The contrast is also in the same log - CMD2 left
#      the register at CMD1's word (`_cid_resp0 = 0x40ff8080`), and CMD3 did not.
#  (3) The R1 decode is a decode of THAT STALE WORD: `_rca_state = 0`, `_rca_ready = 0`,
#      **`_rca_illegal = 1` is bit 22 of `0x40ff8080`**, which is CMD1's own OCR-shaped answer - not
#      'the card rejected CMD3'.
#
# THE FOUR COMMANDS' OWN INHIBIT READINGS, SIDE BY SIDE IN ONE LOG (740 section 6's question made
# answerable rather than asked): CMD0 (`0x0000`) `_inhibit_seen = 0x219`/537, `_inhibit_last` bit 0
# CLEAR, `_complete = 1`, `_status_any = 1`, poll ended at `_cmd0_ticks = 0x0000132a` (4,906 ticks,
# 0.26 ms) - the only command whose inhibit CLEARED, and the only one asking for no response at all;
# CMD1 (`0x0102`) and CMD2 (`0x0209`) never showed inhibit at all (`_inhibit_seen = 0` both); CMD3
# (`0x031a`) held it for the whole window. ONE CELL IS ABSENT RATHER THAN SMOOTHED OVER: `_cid_inhibit_after`
# does not exist in this image - CMD2's body publishes the pair and not the third cell - so the table's
# CMD2 row has a hole that is a property of the arm and not of the run.
#
# THE WINDOW'S CELLS AS PRE-REGISTERED: `_rca_ena_wrote = 1` (the gate's own value, not a constant),
# `_rca_ena_held = 0x8001`, `_rca_sig_enable = 0` READ AND NEVER WRITTEN, `_rca_wrote_back = 0`,
# `_rca_status_post = 0`, `_rca_readback = 0x8000` (the PARTIAL restore). TWO stores, both to INT_ENABLE
# 0x34. AND THE PRESS IS ON THE ARM'S OWN RECORD-NOT-PROMISE TERM: bit 16 of `_rca_ps_after` is
# `SDHCI_CARD_PRESENT` in this tree's vendor header
# (external/android_kernel_xiaomi_cancro/drivers/mmc/host/sdhci.h:71) and it reads CLEAR - as it does in
# every reading this ladder has ever taken (`_rb_present` and every `_inhibit_last` baseline carry the
# same bits 19-24 and a clear bit 16) - so this press adds NOTHING about the card-detect line and must
# not be read as if it did. What rung 19 added to PRESENT_STATE is BIT 0 and nothing else.
#
# THE RUNGS BELOW ARE INHERITED UNCHANGED: all 17 `_rb_*` cells reproduce 740, with **`_rb_resp_zero = 1`**
# - the cell this whole rung was built on - and `_rb_int_enable = 0`; 736's four-place exclusion holds in
# the bytes (`_cmd_int_enable = 0`, `_cmd_gate_kind = 0`, `_cmd_refused = 0`, `_cmd_sent = 2`); **there is
# NO `_quiet_*` KEY AT ALL**, so rung 15's body (compiled for the ladder value 15 and no other) is not in
# this image; `_cid_word = _cid_word_read = 0x00000209`, `_cid_resp0 = 0x40ff8080`, `_cmd1_resp =
# 0x40ff8080` with `_cmd1_resp_read = 1`; 730's pair `_int_status_before = 0x00000000` ->
# `_int_status_after = 0x00000001`; `_ena_host_version = 0x00001102`. THE PRE-REGISTERED SPI 123 -> INTID
# 155 HAZARD DID NOT OCCUR: **there is NO `_irq_other_*` key in this capture**, on a third arm in a row.
# THE ENDING IS UNMOVED: `_seam_lr = 0x800492dc`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `_seam_sp = 0x80557ec8`,
# `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`, `_sleh_storm = 9` with episodes_seen = 9, `_slot_post_calls`
# published 1,2,3,4 (a running counter), and the kernel's own `No errors detected` (1 occurrence).
#
# THE RUNNER'S CRITERION BLOCK, READ EXACTLY - AND A CORRECTION TO THE FIRST COUNT, BECAUSE THE COUNT WAS
# WRONG: the block is **7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2 UNREAD** in each of its two appearances. The FAIL is the known
# storage-arm shape criterion (`seam_sp = 0x80557ec8 is not sleh_sp-8`), and the two UNREAD lines are the
# SLOT_NULL switch (`STAGE90_XNU_SLOT_NULL=1` writes a count and no words) - an absence produced by a
# switch in the image and not an agreement. NOTHING IN THE BLOCK IS A VERDICT ABOUT CMD3. **The block
# appears TWICE in the run log**: once inside the gate's `== payload output ==` section, which reads THE
# LOG THOSE INSTRUCTIONS NAME AS IT STANDS AT GATE TIME - at gate time that was the PREVIOUS run's
# capture - and once in the runner's own `== reading the log this run captured ==` section over this
# run's capture. The two blocks are identical except for two lines, and those two lines are what tells
# them apart: `xnu_live_post_elapsed`/`xnu_live_post_t0` read `0x06ea65db`/`0x07c78b7a` in the first
# (RUNG 18'S OWN VALUES, checkable in /tmp/cancro-last_kmsg.txt.prev.5) and `0x06eb64fd`/`0x0b558bf2` in
# the second. **Counting the verdict LINES gives 14 PASS / 2 FAIL; counting the CRITERIA in the block the
# runner produced for THIS run gives 7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2 UNREAD.** The first form was quoted as "16 PASS /
# 2 FAIL" at one point in this step's own notes, which is wrong twice - it double-counts the block AND
# reads one criterion as two failures. A predicate the tool prints once per artefact it was applied to
# must be counted PER ARTEFACT and not per line. **Recorded as m751.**
#
# WHAT THE NEXT RUNG IS, AND IT IS A SINGLE VARIABLE RATHER THAN A PLAN: the one command that ever
# completed is the one that asked for NO response (CMD0, `RESP_NONE`, word `0x0000`), so the next rung is
# THE SAME OPCODE AND THE SAME ARGUMENT WITH A FLAG WORD CARRYING NO RESPONSE DEMAND - `RESP_NONE` on
# opcode 3, word `0x0300`, argument `0x00010000` - through the same one-bit window, publishing the same
# triplet. If the block completes THAT, the demand for a response is what stalls it and the subject
# becomes the response path; if it does not, the opcode or the block's own state is the subject, and the
# census moves to `SLOT_INT_STATUS 0xFC` and `CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC`. Either way the failure mode is a
# diagnosis: the 1.2 s bound is inside `st_send_command` (measured four times now) and the ending is
# unmoved. TWO THINGS MUST NOT BE READ INTO THE PRESS: this rung is a CONTROLLER question and not the
# driver's next statement (`mmc.c` would say CMD9 SEND_CSD, and arming a further response-expecting
# command on a block whose command line has been held for 1.2 s would stack the same unknown on itself),
# and `_rca_ps_after`'s clear CARD_PRESENT bit is NOT evidence about a card.
#
# NO FIRER IS ARMED, no press is owed, and the press path is the two commands readiness prints, run by
# hand once each.
#
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").

# ================================================================================================
# 743: A NEW ARM IS ARMED, AND IT IS ONE CONSTANT DIFFERENT FROM THE ARM 742 SPENT.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-b7e5a18f` - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=19`, ordinal rung 20, and the eight hex of its
# name is `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 prefix (the rule every `armed-storage-*` set in this record
# follows). It is 742's arm's whole act - the same opcode, the same argument `0x00010000`, the same
# one-bit `INT_ENABLE 0x34` window, the same five registers read at the same widths in the same
# program order, the same gate (`st_all_send_cid`'s returned `cid_sent`) - with **ONE CONSTANT MOVED**:
# the flag word goes from `MMC_RSP_R1` to `MMC_RSP_NONE`, so the command word goes from `0x031A` to
# **`0x0300`** and the demand for a response is GONE from a command that is otherwise identical.
#
# **THE REASON IS 742's ANSWER, WHICH IS AN ANSWER AND NOT A DOUBT.** 742 pressed rung 19 and read the
# driver's own CMD3 as: TAKEN (`_rca_word = _rca_word_read = 0x031a`, `_rca_arg = _rca_arg_wrote =
# 0x00010000` - the block holds the word the driver wrote), STARTED (`_rca_inhibit_seen = 0x400`, bit 0
# of `CMD_INHIBIT` in ALL 1024 samples of the poll), and NEVER FINISHED (`_rca_complete = 0`,
# `_rca_err = 0`, `_rca_timeout = 1`, `_rca_status_any = 0` across 5,088,256 polls) (778: this read 5,081,600) - with the response
# register EMPTIED across it (`_rca_resp_pre = _rca_resp = 0x40ff8080` -> `_rca_resp_post = 0x00000000`,
# `_rca_resp_moved = 1`). The one command this ladder has ever driven to a COMPLETION is CMD0, the one
# that asks for NOTHING BACK. So 742 section 8 named the next rung as a single variable rather than a
# plan: **the same opcode and the same argument with the flag word carrying no response demand.**
#
# **WHAT THE COMMAND IS.** opcode `MMC_SET_RELATIVE_ADDR` 3 (`mmc.h:32`, unchanged), argument
# `card->rca << 16` with `card->rca = 1` (unchanged, `0x00010000`), flags `MMC_RSP_NONE`, which is
# `core.h:50`'s literal **zero** - the ABSENCE of `MMC_RSP_PRESENT` - so under `sdhci_cmd_to_flags`
# (`sdhci.c:1131-1143`) every other term it adds is conditioned away and the word is **`0x0300`**:
# rung 19's `0x031A` minus `RESP_SHORT`, `CRC` and **`INDEX`** together. **The ladder's first `INDEX`
# bit does not survive this rung**, and that is the whole of the difference. All of it is
# `_Static_assert`ed in the source (`ST_SDHCI_CMD_WORD(ST_CMD_OP_SET_RELATIVE_ADDR, ST_MMC_RSP_NONE)
# == 0x0300u` and `ST_MMC_RSP_NONE == 0u`), because the build's clause asserts that body's ACCESSES and
# never the value it stores.
#
# **THE ANSWER IS A THREEWAY, WHICH IS WHY THIS RUNG IS WORTH A PRESS.** `_nrsp_complete = 1` with
# `_nrsp_status_any != 0` says the response DEMAND is what stalled CMD3, and the subject becomes the
# response path (`RESPONSE 0x10`, `SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE`, and the `INT_ENABLE` bit this ladder has carried
# since rung 13). `_nrsp_inhibit_seen > 0` with `_nrsp_complete = 0` says a no-response command stalls
# the SAME way, so the demand is EXONERATED and the subject is the opcode or the block's own state - and
# then the census moves to the two registers 726 section 3 named and no rung has yet read with a
# completion to report, `SLOT_INT_STATUS 0xFC` and `CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC`. `_nrsp_inhibit_seen = 0`
# with `_nrsp_word_read = 0x0300` says the block never started it at all.
#
# **AND THE SECOND READING IS THE RESPONSE REGISTER, TAKEN THREE TIMES.** The body reads `RESPONSE 0x10`
# through the DRIVER'S OWN WORD-0 DERIVATION (`(readl(RESPONSE + 0x1C) << 8) | readb(RESPONSE + 0x1B)`,
# `sdhci.c:1164-1172`) immediately BEFORE the window, inside the command object's own `resp`, and again
# immediately AFTER the publishes - so `_nrsp_resp_pre`, `_nrsp_resp` and `_nrsp_resp_post` are ONE
# ARITHMETIC AT THREE TIMES and the register is not collapsed into a boolean the way rung 19's
# `_rca_resp_moved` alone was (742 section 2 records that as that rung's pre-registration defect). With
# the demand removed, nothing should clear the register during this command, so `_nrsp_resp_post` should
# STILL HOLD CMD1's word `0x40ff8080` - which rungs 17 and 18 both measured there. **If the R1 command
# emptied it and the no-response command does not, the CLEARING is tied to the response demand**, and
# that is 742 section 1's reading CONFIRMED rather than inferred from one press.
#
# **`_nrsp_resp` MUST BE READ WITH CARE AND THE ARM SAYS SO IN A COMMENT.** The command asked for no
# response, so `rsp_present` is zero and that word is a reading of the REGISTER and NOT this command's
# answer - which is exactly why the three R1-decode cells rung 19 publishes (`_state`, `_ready`,
# `_illegal`) are ABSENT from this body. A word decoded here would be CMD1's OCR wearing a card-status
# shape, which is 738's stale-word trap in its cheapest form. **The R1 fields belong to rung 19's log
# and to no other.**
#
# **THE CONTRAST THE PAIR BUYS.** Every rung since 12 publishes its command through `ST_CMD_PUBLISH`,
# and this command goes through the driver's own `st_send_command` exactly as CMD0, CMD1, CMD2 and rung
# 19's CMD3 do - so one log will hold FOUR commands' own inhibit readings plus a FIFTH row that is the
# same opcode as the fourth with the demand removed: `_cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219` (537 of 1024) with
# `_cmd0_complete = 1`, `_rca_inhibit_seen = 0x400` (all 1024) with `_rca_complete = 0`, and this
# rung's. If the new row looks like CMD0's, the demand is the stall; if it looks like rung 19's, the
# opcode is.
#
# **RUNG 19's BODY IS EXCLUDED FROM THIS IMAGE, AND IT IS ASSERTED TWICE.** Both bodies send the same
# opcode with the same argument, so an image carrying both would put two response-demand variants of one
# command on the same bus in one boot and the two cells a press is spent for would answer each other's
# question. The source compiles `st_set_relative_addr` for the value 18 and NO OTHER (`#if ... == 18`
# where rung 19 had `>= 18`) and its call site the same; `xnu_entry_743`'s exclusion clause reads the
# SYMBOL out of the linked image and refuses if it is there. **Measured on this arm: `st_set_relative_addr`
# is not in the image.** The second assertion is the entry image's own seam - see the falsification
# paragraph below, where the two were measured to fire in that order.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT IS RUNG 19's AND THE BUILD ASSERTS THAT IT IS, LITERALLY.** TWO stores, both to
# `INT_ENABLE 0x34`: the one-bit window `(read | SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE)` immediately before the command is
# put on the bus, and its restore on ONE unconditional line after the publishes, writing back the value
# the gate read and not a constant. `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` is READ AND NEVER WRITTEN, at every rung - it
# is the register whose conjunction with `INT_ENABLE` raises this block's SPI 123 -> intid 155, a line
# nobody here owns. No new command class, no data path, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no
# `core_mem` word, and **no byte of the medium**. The restore is PARTIAL and that is a measurement, not
# a bug (bit 15 rides along on any write to `0x34`): expected `_nrsp_ena_held = 0x00008001`,
# `_nrsp_readback = 0x00008000`. The failure mode is a DIAGNOSIS and not a lost device: the 1.2 s
# command bound is inside `st_send_command`, and a delivery on that line ends the run at the dispatcher
# as `_irq_other_count = 1` / `iat = 155`, an ending this image already reads and survives (709 ended
# that way on intid 170).
#
# **`xnu_entry_743`'s `_want` STRINGS ARE RUNG 19'S, CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER, AND THAT IS THE
# ONE-VARIABLE CLAIM MADE STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN NARRATED.** `st_cmd3_noresp`'s device access SET is
# `f982491b:ldrb f982491c:ldr f9824924:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824934:ldr f9824934:str f9824938:ldr`; its
# COUNTS are `f982491b:ldrb=2 f982491c:ldr=2 f9824924:ldr=1 f9824930:ldr=1 f9824934:ldr=2
# f9824934:str=2 f9824938:ldr=1`; its STORES are exactly `f9824934:str` (two, both to `0x34`, nothing
# else anywhere); its PROGRAM ORDER, distinct, is `f982491c:ldr f982491b:ldrb f9824934:str f9824934:ldr
# f9824938:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824924:ldr` - the freshness baseline FIRST, before any store; its image
# side is EMPTY; `st_cmd_path` calls it exactly once, after `st_all_send_cid` and after the command
# path's last `bl <st_send_command>`; and the four command call sites are `[st_cmd_path 2,
# st_all_send_cid 1, st_cmd3_noresp 1]`. **If the flag word this rung changes had moved a single device
# access, a width or an ordering, that clause would refuse.** What it does NOT assert is the opcode,
# the argument, the flags and the word: a body's accesses and its `bl` sites carry no immediate, so
# `0x0300` is carried by the two `_Static_assert`s and by the run's own `_nrsp_op`, `_nrsp_flags`,
# `_nrsp_arg` and `_nrsp_word` cells.
#
# **THIRTEEN FALSIFICATIONS, AND TWO OF THEM WERE REFUSED BY A DIFFERENT CLAUSE - BOTH OF WHICH IS THE
# FINDING.** Every perturbation rebuilt the entry image under the arm's own switch set, and every edit
# asserted `t.count(old) == 1` before it edited (m748; the rung-19 batch had to be re-run for want of
# it). Against `st_cmd3_noresp`: the `0x1b` byte read made a 32-bit `ldr` -> **SET**,
# `NODECL-f982-2328:ldr`; the post half taken from the pre half -> **COUNTS**, `f982491b:ldrb=1
# f982491c:ldr=1`; the window never written back -> **COUNTS**, `f9824934:str=1`; a W1C store into
# `INT_STATUS 0x30` -> **SET**, `f9824930:str`; a store to `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` -> **SET**,
# `f9824938:str`; a fifth `st_send_command` call -> the **four-call-site** clause `[2, 1, 2]`; the call
# site duplicated -> the **one-call-site** clause; the call site removed -> the **compiler**
# (`entry_storage.c:3141:48: error: 'st_cmd3_noresp' defined but not used [-Werror=unused-function]`).
# **THE BASELINE DUPLICATED BELOW THE WINDOW'S STORE** was refused by **COUNTS**
# (`f982491b:ldrb=3 f982491c:ldr=3`), not by PROGRAM ORDER, because that edit ADDED a third reading
# rather than moving the first and the COUNTS clause sits before the ORDER clause in the same check -
# so the ORDER clause's own subject was never built. **A LIVE CALL INSERTED ABOVE CMD2** was refused by
# the **one-call-site** clause for the same reason (it added a second call rather than relocating the
# gated one), and a cell that MOVED the gated block would be folded away by the compiler first (741
# section 7's cell 7) - so **the ORDER clause is reached by no perturbation in this table**, and that is
# recorded rather than dressed up. It is not decoration: it runs and passes on the real image.
#
# **THE EXCLUSION WAS REFUSED BY AN EARLIER CLAUSE FIRST, AND THE SECOND CELL NAMES BOTH.** Widening
# rung 19's guard back to `>= 18` puts a second CMD3 body into the image and **the entry image's own
# layout moves with it**: the exit's `bl <__wrap_FlushPoU_Dcache>` - the seam every rung since 9 has
# been built on - moved from `0x800492d8` to `0x8004a2d8`, exactly `0x1000`, and **`xnu_entry_535`**
# refused because its return address no longer equals `entry_trace.c`'s `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR`. That
# clause sits at `build_entry.sh:29102` and `xnu_entry_743`'s exclusion at `:31351`, so **the exclusion
# CANNOT be the first thing to answer this perturbation, and it did not**. The second cell set the seam
# constant to the address the first cell's own build printed, and **the exclusion fired**: *"st_set
# relative_addr IS in the linked image while STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=19 ... an image carrying both puts
# two variants of one command on the same bus in one boot and each reading becomes unattributable to its
# own flag word."* **Two clauses guard this rung's exclusion and the stronger one is the seam**, and the
# same measurement says something about the rung: rung 19's arm and this arm have entry images of **the
# same size to the byte (5,552,764)** with the seam at the **same address**, because rung 20's body
# occupies rung 19's bytes - which is what a one-constant change should look like.
# **AND ONE CELL IS NOT A REFUSAL, DELIBERATELY**: the flags changed back to `MMC_RSP_R1` **BUILDS**,
# which is the honest control and the clause's own stated limit - the disagreement it creates lives
# between the run's log and the document, not in the build. A table in which every cell refused would be
# a table testing its own expectation.
#
# **THE BUILDS WERE RUN AND THIS IS THE DISCLOSURE.** The entry image was built for the FINAL source
# TWICE - the build carrying `xnu_entry_743` and a second clean build after the falsification batch
# restored the source, both giving **`b7e5a18f35ebfa7a7f1d1b411bac405ecdbb7490875ebf17c6126f93be5efc5d`,
# 5,552,764 B, `cmp`-measured** - and thirteen times more carrying the perturbations above, each of
# which the build REFUSED except the deliberate one. The payload was built **ONCE**, with
# `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'`, and **its `stage90-build-config.txt` is
# BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pressed rung-19 arm's (`6c2b6038...`, `cmp`-measured)** - so the owed config
# comparison of the 732 block holds for this arm on the FIRST build, and `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1` is at
# line 5 of it. **The trap is still not a refusal**: `./scripts/build.sh` takes its variant switches
# from that variable and its default leaves the switch at `0u`, where every `armed-storage-*` arm
# carries `1`; a check that compares a fresh payload's config against the arm's record line in
# `make check` is still OWED and is still not in this commit.
#
# **THE ARM'S OWN MEASUREMENTS.** Entry image `b7e5a18f...`, 5,552,764 B, `.text` **5,329,120**;
# `platform_cache_idle_exit` at `0x800492d4` (size `0x6c`), its `bl <__wrap_FlushPoU_Dcache>` at
# **`0x800492d8` returning to `0x800492dc`** = `entry_trace.c:2688`'s `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` (re-derived
# read-only from the fresh disassembly; four wrapped call sites in the image, at `0x80048d08`,
# `0x80049284`, `0x800492d8` and `0x800493bc`); **`st_set_relative_addr` IS NOT IN THE IMAGE** - the
# exclusion, as a property of the bytes; `st_cmd3_noresp` at **`0x8000df54`, size `0x29c`** (668 B);
# `st_all_send_cid` at `0x8000dcb4`, size `0x2a0`; `st_cmd_path` at `0x8000e1f0`, size `0x788`;
# `st_send_command` at `0x8000cff8`, size `0x314`; `st_resp_before` at `0x8000d7c8`, size `0x17c`.
# `tools/check_arm_variant_pair.sh` agrees. **The `.text` comparison with rung 19's arm is the rung in
# one number**: rung 19's `st_set_relative_addr` was `0x2d8` (728 B) and this rung's `st_cmd3_noresp` is
# `0x29c` (668 B) - the same body with three published cells removed - and the two images' `.bin` sizes
# are equal, so the change is a change and not a shift.
#
# **THE ARM IS ARMED AND NOT PRESSED** and nothing is owed. Readiness **5 of 5** (flags
# `--allow-xnu-entry`, `--expect-arm=armed-storage-b7e5a18f`), `tools/rehearse_revert_set.sh`
# **38 ok / 0 failed**, `tools/rehearse_live_path.sh` **39 ok / 0 failed, exit 0** (the runner's own
# state machine against stubs - no device), the park and the export VERIFIED (11 files each), and
# `make check` exit 0. **THE PRESS IS THE OPERATOR'S**, and the paths carry the `scripts/` prefix from
# the repo root:
#   sudo -n adb devices   # 4a2fe00b; the neighbour 33e80afe must be ABSENT from BOTH lists
#   ./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry
#   ./scripts/run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-b7e5a18f
# **No firer is armed**, and the press path is those two commands run by hand once each (730 section 6:
# the launcher's bytes are kept nowhere).
#
# **TWO THINGS MUST NOT BE READ INTO THE PRESS.** This rung is a CONTROLLER question and not the
# driver's next statement - `mmc.c` would say CMD9 `SEND_CSD`, and arming a further response-expecting
# command on a block whose command line rung 19 held for 1.2 s would stack the same unknown on itself.
# And `_nrsp_ps_after`'s clear `CARD_PRESENT` bit (bit 16 of `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` in this tree's vendor
# header, `sdhci.h:71`) is a property of what this image has done to the block and **NOT evidence about
# a card**: it reads clear in every reading this ladder has ever taken.
#
# The goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not observed reaching userland, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld (the clause is conditioned on "if the OS can already get in").
