# ================================================================================================
# 744: THE OWED BUILD-CONFIG CHECK IS CLOSED, AND IT IS A PROPERTY RATHER THAN THE `cmp` THIS RECORD ASKED FOR.
# ================================================================================================
# `tools/check_payload_config_entry.sh`, run from `make check`, refuses when the payload switch record in
# `out/stage90/` does not carry the XNU-entry switch the way this record's arms do. The debt was opened by
# 732 and repeated by 739, 741 and 743 - four steps that each ended with the same sentence: *a one-line
# `cmp` of a fresh payload's config against the arm's own record line in `make check` is OWED.*
#
# **THE `cmp` THIS RECORD ASKED FOR COULD NOT HAVE FIRED, AND THAT IS WHY THE CHECK IS A PROPERTY.** The
# mistake happens *before* the new arm is recorded - the payload is built, and the switch is wrong in that
# build - so at the moment the check is needed there is no record line for the new arm to compare against.
# The case this record actually documents is 741's: the first payload build was made without
# `-DSTAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'`, it was caught by `cmp` **against the parked PREDECESSOR
# arm**, and the arm's own line was written afterwards from the good build. So the four steps were asking
# for an instrument that is silent exactly when it is needed. What is checked instead is a property: the
# live config's own VALUE must be the value this record's arms carry.
#
# **WHY NO HASH IN THIS TREE COULD SEE IT, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT.** 741's own account, above: *"that
# payload's own record said `#define STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 0u`, its embedded blob was the right entry image, and
# every hash it takes part in matched."* Both builds are internally consistent - the manifest, `SHA256SUMS`,
# the entry blob, the gate's content check - so the config file is the ONLY record of which switches the
# build was given, and it is written by the build it is about. That is
# [[mi4-one-value-two-definitions]] in its purest form, and it is why the check reads the VALUE and not a
# digest of the file.
#
# **WHAT IT CHECKS.** (1) the live config exists and is non-empty, else exit 2 - a check that could not read
# its subject has not passed; (2) it names `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY` EXACTLY ONCE, else exit 1 - an absent key and
# a doubled key are the two ways a value stops being readable (m720), and a `sed` over a file that names it
# twice prints both into one variable silently; (3) its value equals the value EVERY parked arm carries,
# else exit 1 - **derived from the 29 parks on disk and not asserted as a constant**, because a `1` written
# into the tool would be a value nothing in this record could contradict (m719); (4) at least one park is
# readable, else exit 2 - **zero parks is not unanimity**, which is the shape that would let this pass on a
# tree with no arms at all. A park whose config does not name the key once is SKIPPED AND COUNTED and the
# count is printed in both the pass and the refusal line, this record's treatment of a frozen oddity
# (`tools/check_set_name_rule.sh` names `frozen-574` as a label and does not refuse it).
#
# **IT IS DERIVED, AND THE DERIVATION IS UNANIMOUS TODAY, MEASURED**: all 29 parks under
# `out/stage90/frozen/` carry `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1`. 28 of them carry the SAME payload config
# (`6c2b6038...`); the one that differs is `armed-selftest-wdog-ef0361a2` (`7d54be1e...`), and the
# difference is one line - `STAGE90_HW_WATCHDOG_SELFTEST 1` against `0u`. So **a check asserting all
# configs equal would have refused a correct arm**, and the unanimity this one rests on is on the ONE key
# whose default the build leaves off.
#
# **FALSIFIED IN NINE CELLS BEFORE IT SHIPPED**, all against scratch trees named by the tool's own first
# argument so nothing under `out/` was touched: live `0u` against parks on `1` -> exit 1, naming both values
# and printing the rebuild command; parks disagreeing (`1` and `0u`) -> exit 1, *"an ambiguous baseline is
# not a baseline"*; the key absent from the live config -> exit 1, "0 time(s)"; the key doubled -> exit 1,
# "2 time(s)"; no parks -> exit 2; the live config absent -> exit 2; only malformed parks -> exit 2; a
# malformed park beside a good one -> **skipped, named, exit 0**; and the honest case -> exit 0 with what it
# read printed. `make check` is exit 0 with it in the list, and the live tree's own reading is
# `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1` against 29 parks.
#
# **AND IT IS NOT THE ONLY NET, WHICH IS SAID HERE SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR ONE.** The gate's own clause
# (`preflight_boot_check.sh:1699-1719`, readiness row 3) refuses `--allow-xnu-entry` against an image built
# with the switch off, and that net works - it is a READINESS net, so it fires only when someone chooses to
# run readiness. This one fires on every `make check`, which is every step. Neither of them makes the
# mistake impossible: `./scripts/build.sh` still defaults `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY` to `0u`, and the durable fix
# for that is the build's own default and not a check on its output.

# ================================================================================================
# 745: THE OS IS OBSERVED REACHING USER MODE, AND THE SENTENCE THIS RECORD REPEATS IS WRONG IN ONE CLAUSE.
# ================================================================================================
# **NO DEVICE ACTION AND NO BUILD.** This block is a reading of the capture 742's press already archived
# (`out/stage90/captures/rung19-rca-20260926-180922-last_kmsg.txt`) plus the arm's own ELF and sources.
# `out/` is untouched; rung 20 remains ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.
#
# EVERY BLOCK IN THIS RECORD ENDS WITH *"the goal is still NOT met: no storage, no filesystem, the OS not
# observed reaching userland"*. **The first two clauses are true. The third is FALSE AS WRITTEN**, and it
# has been false since 520: the OS reaches user mode, a user process runs a complete syscall cycle, and a
# driver answers a read made from it. Measured in 742's capture, in the runner's OWN goal criterion (*user
# mode reached, and a driver answering*): `BSD root: md0, major 2, minor 0`;
# `mini4: the OS starts the process at 0x10e0`; `mini4: the AST is done -- pid 1's thread is at 0x10e0 for
# user mode (sp 0x101efc)`; `_open_seq=0x1 _open_error=0x0 _open_fd=0x0` (the driver's open answered);
# `_open_seq=0x2 _open_error=0x2` (the control open answered the ENOENT devfs returns for a name it has no
# node for); `_read_nbytes=0x4 _read_ret_lo=0x4` with **`_read_word_before=0x00102000` ->
# `_read_word_after=0xfeedface`** (a driver's `uiomove64` moved `MH_MAGIC` into a user page);
# `_getpid_value=0x1`; **`_exit_pid=0x2 _exit_rval=0x3`** (a SECOND process exited) and
# `_wait_pid=0x2 _wait_status=0x300` (pid 1 reaped it); `_ast_calls=0x2 _ast_done_seq=0x2
# _ast_sp=0x00101efc` (the kernel took a user thread's AST and put it back). The idle path was then entered
# **60,037 time(s)** and `platform_cache_idle_enter` entered once and RETURNED.
#
# **AND WHAT ENDS THE RUN IS THE ARM'S OWN CLOCK, NOT THE OS - verified instruction for instruction.** The
# capture's panic is `panic(cpu 0 caller 0x80457668): kernel abort type 4: fault_type=0x3,
# fault_addr=0xfa0065c`, and every address in it resolves into the run's deliberate ending:
# `pc: 0x8047e488` = **`entry_seam_end_run`**, `lr: 0x8047e5bc` = **`entry_post_clock`**,
# `caller 0x80457668` = `sleh_abort`, `sp: 0x80557ec8` = the seam's own stack. The disassembly agrees with
# the dump register for register:
#     8047e5b8: bl  8047e47c <entry_seam_end_run>   (entry_post_clock's call; lr becomes 0x8047e5bc)
#     8047e47c: movw r3, #21761  ; 0x5501            )
#     8047e480: mov  r2, #262144000 ; 0xfa00000      ) r2 = 0x0fa00000 = MSM_IMEM_BASE_PHYS
#     8047e484: movt r3, #30822  ; 0x7866            ) r3 = 0x78665501 = STAGE90_ENTRY_RESET_REASON_NORMAL
#     8047e488: str  r3, [r2, #1628] ; 0x65c         <- far 0x0fa0065c, fsr 0x00000805
# so the dump's `r2: 0x0fa00000  r3: 0x78665501  far: 0x0fa0065c  fsr: 0x00000805` matches exactly, and
# `fsr` bit 11 says **WRITE** with bits[3:0] = 5 = **translation fault, section** - the `0x0fa` megabyte is
# not mapped in the context the entry image's reset tail runs in. The address is
# `STAGE90_ENTRY_RESET_REASON_ADDR` (`entry_reset.h:33`) and the value is
# `STAGE90_ENTRY_RESET_REASON_NORMAL` (`entry_reset.h:35`): **the store that faults is the one that writes
# "the boot got to its normal end", into an unmapped megabyte.** That store and its sibling at
# `0xfc4ab000` are already this project's record ([[mi4-the-run-ends-at-entry-epilogue]]); what is new is
# that the CALLER here is the arm's own deadline clock.
#
# **AND THE DEADLINE CLOSES ARITHMETICALLY.** `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000` (115,200,000) at
# `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800` (19,200,000 Hz) is **6.0000 s**; `_post_elapsed` climbs
# 0 -> 1,947,393 (0.1014 s) -> 38,717,341 (2.0165 s) -> **116,090,109 (6.0464 s)**, and 6.0464 >= 6.0000
# **crosses**; `_post_end_calls = 0x00000006` is published on the pass that ends the run. **The two OTHER
# ending switches read `_seam_end_run = 0x00000000` and `_seam_post_end_run = 0x00000000`** - which is how
# the deadline is known to be the instrument responsible rather than inferred. So the sequence is: boot ->
# user mode -> the fixture's syscalls -> ~60,000 idle passes -> the arm's own 6-second deadline ->
# `entry_seam_end_run` -> the unmapped `RESTART_REASON` store -> `sleh_abort` -> panic -> `MACH Reboot`.
# **THE `kernel abort type 4` THAT READS LIKE A CRASH IS THE INSTRUMENT.**
#
# **IT IS STILL NOT THE GOAL, AND THE REASON IS A DIFFERENT CLAUSE.** The driver that answers is
# `memdev.c`, which `entry_trace.c:1726` names as what serves `open("/dev/rmd0")`, and the root is
# `md0` - **a RAM disk.** So *no storage, no filesystem* stands exactly as written. And `/sbin/launchd`
# **FAILED** (`load_init_program: failed loading /usr/local/sbin/launchd.development: errno 2`), so pid 1
# is **this project's own fixture** (`stage90_fixture.macho`, 1,744 B), not the OS's init - the honest
# sentence is *the kernel reaches user mode and runs a user process*, and the OS is not usable.
#
# **THE RECORD USES A DIFFERENT DEFINITION AND NEVER WROTE THE DIFFERENCE DOWN WHERE THE GOAL IS SCORED.**
# The runner's own goal block calls all of the above **"a FLOOR, not progress"**: *all of it is in 520 and
# 533 - the whole userland phase happens before the death - so a run has to LOSE these readings to be a
# regression, and having them is not evidence that the boot got further than any boot has*; and at its
# foot, *"this criterion does not decide whether the machine stayed up ... the ceiling is the arm's own
# clause, and the run that matters is the one whose log has both."* So the project's goal is **user mode
# AND the machine stays up**, and **it is the SECOND half that is unmet and not the first.** An operator
# reading *"the OS not observed reaching userland"* will look for a failure in the wrong place. **The
# precise sentence is: user mode is reached and a driver answers, in every capture since 520; what has
# never been observed is the machine staying up, and the reason is below.**
#
# **AND THAT HALF IS A DESIGN QUESTION, NOT A RUNG.** Every run is ended by one of TWO instruments and
# neither of them is the OS: (1) **the entry-side deadline** (`STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_TICKS`, which this arm
# carries as 115200000 = 6.0000 s and which this block measures FIRING); (2) **the payload's own hardware
# watchdog**, armed before the jump and - per this record's 606 clause - the net that caps a run of this
# arm at **28 s** (25 s bark + 3 s bite gap) *on a successful run too*. **And the second exists because the
# operator's standing constraint requires it**: 「一定要保证不要让设备彻底死机或者变砖」 is implemented, in
# this tree, by the payload arming a watchdog it cannot later disarm. Observing *"the OS stays up"* needs
# both off, and the one that would have to come off is **the exact net that makes the no-brick property
# true.** That is the one place in this project where the goal and the safety constraint are in direct
# tension, and this block is the first place it is stated in one sentence. **The two sides are separately
# switchable** (`STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_TICKS` / `SEAM_END_RUN` / `POST_END_RUN` on the entry side, the
# watchdog's own arm/disarm on the payload side), so a future arm with **the entry deadline off and the
# hardware watchdog on** would buy the longest window the constraint allows - it would still not read
# *"the OS stays up"*, because the watchdog still ends it at 28 s, but it would read how far past the
# deadline the boot gets. **NO SUCH ARM EXISTS AND THIS BLOCK ARMS NOTHING.**
#
# NO PRESS IS OWED, NO FIRER IS ARMED, and the press path is the two commands readiness prints, run by
# hand once each. Rung 20 (`armed-storage-b7e5a18f`) is ARMED AND NOT PRESSED, and the ladder's own next
# step is unmoved.
#
# The goal is still NOT met: **no storage, no filesystem, and the machine never observed staying up** -
# which is a sharper and a truer sentence than the one this record has been repeating. TWRP-to-storage
# stays withheld.

# ================================================================================================
# 746: THE RUNG-20 PRESS - THE RESPONSE DEMAND WAS THE STALL.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-b7e5a18f` (`STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=19`, ordinal rung 20, park
# `out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-b7e5a18f`), the driver's own CMD3 `SET_RELATIVE_ADDR` with
# `MMC_RSP_NONE` in place of `MMC_RSP_R1`, so the command word is `0x0300` where rung 19's was `0x031A`.
# **ONE PRESS, SPENT BY HAND UNDER THE OPERATOR'S OWN AUTHORIZATION.** Readiness 5 of 5 exit 0 (flags
# `--allow-xnu-entry`, set `armed-storage-b7e5a18f`, found by hashing the live `stage90-qcdt.img`
# `86c9e5d7…`); **exactly one** `./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry` (exit 0, 596 lines);
# **exactly one** `./scripts/run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-b7e5a18f`.
# Fired 2026-09-27 01:21:21 UTC, returned 01:22:35 UTC: **RUNNER EXIT=0**, 74 s, the device back 28 s after
# the send (seen via adb). `fastboot boot` only, **nothing flashed**, no firer armed, no stray runner, gate
# or watcher, and the neighbour `33e80afe` absent from BOTH `fastboot devices` and `adb devices` at fire
# time (0 occurrences). Capture `out/stage90/captures/rung20-nrsp-20260927-012235-last_kmsg.txt`,
# **630,462 B**, sha256 `9f4473862d313ccc3ae16dd7af7e2da3eae9f14b79199c56f55f5ba6e3f606a5`, archived by hand
# with its run log and its gate log (the runner does not archive - 730 section 6).
#
# **IT ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE FIRST BRANCH OF 743 SECTION 7'S THREEWAY: THE RESPONSE DEMAND WAS THE
# STALL.** `_nrsp_complete = 0x00000001` beside `_nrsp_status_any = 0x00000001`, with `_nrsp_err = 0` and
# `_nrsp_timeout = 0`. The block took the driver's own word (`_nrsp_word = _nrsp_word_read = 0x0300`, read
# back out of `COMMAND 0x0E`), `CMD_INHIBIT` rose on the read after the store (`_nrsp_inhibit_after = 1`),
# stayed up for 538 of the poll's 539 samples (`_nrsp_inhibit_seen = 0x21a`) and was **CLEAR at the last
# one** (`_nrsp_inhibit_last = 0x01f80000`, where rung 19's was `0x01f80001`), and the whole command ran
# `_nrsp_ticks = 0x1327` = **4,903 ticks = 0.255 ms** against rung 19's `0x015f92d7` = 23,040,727 ticks =
# **1.2000 s**, the full bound.
#
# **AND THE LADDER NOW HOLDS ONE TABLE IT COULD NOT BUILD UNTIL THIS RUNG EXISTED** - five commands' own
# inhibit and completion readings, four in this capture and the fifth (rung 19's CMD3) in 742's. **AND THE
# SPLIT IS THREE-WAY, NOT TWO-WAY - THE CORRECTION THIS BLOCK'S HEADLINE NEEDS**, added after the table was
# first written by reading the same capture again: the block **STARTED** CMD0 and this CMD3, **STARTED AND
# STUCK** on rung 19's CMD3 R1, and **NEVER TOOK CMD1 AND CMD2 AT ALL** - `_cmd1_inhibit_after = 0` with
# `_cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0` over **5,091,328** samples while `_cmd1_word_read = 0x0102` proves the word
# reached the register, and `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0` over 5,090,304 with `_cid_word_read = 0x0209` for CMD2. **778: 5,088,256 is rung 18's own CMD1 count and 5,088,000 is no capture's count at all - this block's `this capture` is rung 20's, whose CMD1 and CMD2 counts are 5,091,328 and 5,090,304.**
# **Identical in all three of the last three presses** - rungs 18, 19 and 20 carry the same three CMD1
# values. So `inhibit_seen = 0` is not *no inhibit was observed* but **the block declined the command**, and
# the sampler demonstrably catches this bit: CMD0's was seen **537 times in 538 samples**. **The headline
# stands** (rungs 19 and 20 are a matched pair differing in ONE flag word and their outcomes differ), but its
# reach is narrower than the sentence - **the ladder cannot yet say that a response demand makes the block
# REFUSE a command, because two of the three response commands were never taken and the only
# response-demanding command it ever took is CMD3.** CMD1 and CMD2 were declined with the SAME enable window
# open as CMD0 (rung 17 opens one around CMD2: `_cid_ena_wrote = 1`, `_cid_ena_held = 0x8001`), so the window
# is not the discriminator. **Two one-constant moves follow, and NEITHER IS DESIGNED HERE and neither is
# armed**: (1) **opcode 1 or 2 with `MMC_RSP_NONE`** - the same opcode and the same argument as a command that
# was never taken, with the flag word changed to the value that demonstrably starts - which asks whether the
# FLAG WORD is the refusal; (2) **CMD3 with `RESP_SHORT` alone, word `0x0302`** - exactly CMD1's flag shape on
# the opcode that demonstrably starts - which asks whether the SHAPE is. A note so the table is not read as
# complete: **CMD2's `_inhibit_after` does not exist** (`st_all_send_cid` publishes the pair and not the third
# cell), so its "never started" row rests on one cell less than CMD1's - a slightly weaker reading, said
# rather than footnoted.
#
#   command                   word      inhibit_seen  inhibit_last   polls      ticks        complete status_any
#   CMD0 GO_IDLE_STATE        0x0000    0x219 (537)   0x01f80000     0x21a      0x1326           1          1
#   CMD1 SEND_OP_COND  (R3)   0x0102    0               0x00f80000     0x4db000   0x015f9067       0          0
#   CMD2 ALL_SEND_CID  (R2)   0x0209    0               0x01f80000     0x4dac00   0x015f9ceb       0          0
#   CMD3 rung 19       (R1)   0x031a    0x400 (1024)    0x01f80001     0x4da400   0x015f92d7       0          0
#   CMD3 rung 20       (NONE) 0x0300    0x21a (538)     0x01f80000     0x21b      0x1327           1          1
#
# **The only two rows with a completion are the only two rows whose flags are zero.** That is a property of
# the PAIR and of neither rung alone, which is why the rung was worth a press. **And it retires 726
# section 2's sixth hypothesis** (*"this controller completes a command and does not set its
# interrupt-status register"*): `INT_STATUS` bit 0 DOES latch for a completing command, through the same
# window on the same block in the same image - what rungs 13, 14 and 15 read was a block whose commands
# never finished.
#
# **THE ONE PREDICTION THAT WAS REFUTED, AND THE REFUTATION IS A DEFECT IN THE PRE-REGISTRATION.** 743
# section 7 predicted that with the demand removed nothing should clear `RESPONSE 0x10`, so
# `_nrsp_resp_post` should still hold `0x40ff8080`. **It reads `0x00000000`**, exactly as rung 19's did -
# so **the clearing is NOT tied to the response demand**, and 742 section 1's reading cannot be inferred
# from this pair. **And the three readings 743 section 7 called "one arithmetic at three times, the
# driver's own `(readl(RESPONSE + 0x1C) << 8) | readb(RESPONSE + 0x1B)`" are TWO DIFFERENT WORDS**:
# `_nrsp_resp_pre` and `_nrsp_resp_post` are that derivation (`st_cmd3_noresp`'s `before`/`after` pair),
# while `_nrsp_resp` is a bare `readl(RESPONSE + 0x10)` read inside `st_send_command` - word 0 - and the
# middle reading agreeing with the first is CMD1's `0x40ff8080` still sitting in word 0 while CMD2's
# leftover still sat in words 2 and 3. **SO THIS RECORD DOES NOT SAY THE RESPONSE REGISTER WAS CLEARED**,
# because the pair published does not license it. The discriminating reading is owed and it is cheap: **the
# four raw `RESPONSE` words at their four offsets, at the three moments** - the shape rung 17's and rung
# 18's bodies already publish (`_cid_raw0..3`, `_rb_raw0..3`) and this arm's CMD3 body does not. Recorded as
# an instance of the project's most-repeated class (`mi4-one-value-two-definitions`): one quantity - what
# the RESPONSE block holds after CMD3 - with **two readings**, and a pre-registration that wrote them as
# one. Its cost is not a wrong cell; it is that a three-time comparison compared two different words and
# the number that came back agreed with the middle one for a reason nobody chose.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT HELD, AND EVERY CELL OF IT IS IN THE LOG.** Two stores, both to `INT_ENABLE 0x34`:
# the one-bit window `(read | SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE)` before the command (`_nrsp_ena_wrote = 0x00000001`,
# `_nrsp_ena_held = 0x00008001` - the partial readback rung 14 measured, bit 15 set by a write and not
# cleared by one) and its restore on ONE unconditional line after the publishes (`_nrsp_wrote_back =
# 0x00000000`, `_nrsp_readback = 0x00008000`). **`SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` READ AND NEVER WRITTEN**
# (`_nrsp_sig_enable = 0`), which is what keeps this block's SPI 123 -> intid 155 off a line nobody here
# owns. No data path, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word, no byte of the medium - and
# **0 `xnu_live_irq_other_*` keys anywhere in the capture**, so the pre-registered delivery did not occur,
# the fourth arm in a row.
#
# **THE ENDING IS 690's CLOCK AND IT DID NOT MOVE**: `_seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000`,
# `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800` (19,200,000 Hz), `_post_elapsed` climbing 0 -> 0x001db763 -> 0x024e1686 ->
# **0x06e94a31** (= 116,083,761 ticks = 6.0460 s, crossing the 6.0000 s deadline), **`_post_end_calls =
# 0x00000006`**, with `_seam_end_run = 0` and `_seam_post_end_run = 0`, so the deadline is the one that
# fired. `_seam_lr = 0x800492dc` unmoved, `_seam_sp = 0x80557ec8`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`,
# `_sleh_storm = 0x9`. The runner's criterion block is the known storage-arm shape - **7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2
# UNREAD counted per criterion** (the `seam_sp = 0x80557ec8 is not sleh_sp-8` FAIL and the two `SLOT_NULL`
# UNREADs) - and it is printed TWICE in the run log, so counting LINES reads one criterion as two failures
# (m751).
#
# **THE NEXT SUBJECT IS THE RESPONSE PATH, and two readings are owed before any new command class.**
# (1) **A 48-bit demand that gets no reply never completes AND never times out**: rung 19's CMD3 ran
# 5,088,256 polls with `_rca_err = 0`, the WHOLE `INT_STATUS` register zero (`_rca_status_any = 0`), and
# per 726's census the block's own `TIMEOUT` bit never set either - which is not the SDHCI timeout
# behaviour and not the card's, but this block waiting on a CMD-line reply nothing on the other end is
# sending. (2) **Which words move** - the four raw `RESPONSE` words at the four offsets at the three
# moments, the reading 743 section 7 thought it was publishing. **And a third thing that is a READING and
# not a verdict**: `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` reads `0x01f80000` at ALL TWELVE moments this capture published it,
# with bit 16 (`SDHCI_CARD_PRESENT`) clear - **and this record does NOT read that as "no card"**, because
# the arm's own source says at `entry_storage.c:218` that the bit *"must NOT be read as 'no card' here"*.
# A bit that cannot be read either way needs a second reading beside it, not a verdict.
#
# **THIS BLOCK ARMS NOTHING.** Rung 20 is pressed and spent; no firer is armed; **no press is owed**; and
# the press path remains the two commands readiness prints, run by hand once each (730 section 6 - the
# launcher's bytes are kept nowhere).
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). The goal's floor - *user mode reached, and a driver answering* - is met and no further.
# **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**

# ================================================================================================
# RUNG 21 ARMED: THE SAME DRIVER'S CMD3 WITH ITS RESPONSE DEMAND PUT BACK AND ITS `INDEX` BIT TAKEN OUT.
# ================================================================================================
# `armed-storage-46fe6737` - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20`, ordinal rung 21, and the eight hex of its
# name is `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 prefix (the rule every `armed-storage-*` set in this record
# follows). It is rung 20's whole act - the same opcode 3, the same argument `0x00010000`, the same
# one-bit `INT_ENABLE 0x34` window, the same registers read at the same widths, the same gate
# (`st_all_send_cid`'s returned `cid_sent`) - with **ONE FLAG BIT MOVED**: `MMC_RSP_NONE` (`0x00`) to
# `ST_MMC_RSP_R1_NOIDX` (`ST_MMC_RSP_PRESENT | ST_MMC_RSP_CRC`, `0x05`), so the command word goes
# `0x0300` -> **`0x030A`**. **It sits ONE BIT from each arm of a measured pair**: rung 19's `0x031A`
# with the `INDEX` bit gone, and rung 20's `0x0300` with the response demand put back.
#
# **THE RULE THIS RUNG TESTS, AND IT IS THE FIRST RULE THAT FITS ALL FIVE OF THE LADDER'S WORDS.**
#
#   `0x0000`  started, completed     the ladder's first completion
#   `0x0300`  started, completed     0.255 ms
#   `0x031A`  started, never finished
#   `0x0102`  NEVER STARTED
#   `0x0209`  NEVER STARTED
#
# **A word that asks for a response (`ST_MMC_RSP_PRESENT`) and carries no `INDEX` bit is DECLINED.**
# `0x0300` is the only word here that asks for nothing and the only OTHER word that completes; `0x031A`
# asks and carries `INDEX`, so it is taken and never finishes; `0x0102` and `0x0209` ask and carry NO
# `INDEX`, and NEITHER WAS EVER STARTED in all three of the last three presses
# (`_cmd1_word_read = 0x00000102` with `_cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0` over 5,088,256 samples, identically in
# rung 18's, rung 19's and rung 20's captures). 746 section 4b named two one-constant moves to test it;
# THIS IS THE SECOND OF THEM, and the FIRST was refuted in advance by rung 20 (`0x0300` is exactly that
# shape on opcode 3 and it started), which is why the rule's exclusion is down to the `INDEX` bit.
#
# **THE ANSWER IS A THREEWAY, AND EACH BRANCH NAMES THE NEXT ACT.** `_nidx_inhibit_seen = 0` with
# `_nidx_word_read = 0x030A` CONFIRMS the rule and explains CMD1 and CMD2 in the same stroke, and the
# subject becomes the block's own sequencer rather than a flag word. `_nidx_inhibit_seen > 0` with
# `_nidx_complete = 0` KILLS the rule's `INDEX` half and puts the stall back on the response itself,
# withdrawing rung 20's exoneration of the demand for want of a second sample. `_nidx_complete = 1`
# kills the rule outright - a response-demanding command can finish on this block.
#
# **AND IT CARRIES m756'S REPAIR, DECLARED AND READ-ONLY.** 743 section 7 wrote rung 20's three response
# readings as ONE arithmetic at three times, `(readl(RESPONSE + 0x1C) << 8) | readb(RESPONSE + 0x1B)`,
# and it was NOT one: `st_send_command` reads `readl(RESPONSE 0x10)` - WORD 0, the word a 48-bit
# response actually lives in - while rung 19's and rung 20's bodies took their `before`/`after` pair
# through the 136-bit formula whose top word is `+0x1C`. So `0x40ff8080 -> 0x00000000` compared word 0
# against words 2 and 3, and the two agreed at the first moment only because CMD1's word was still in
# word 0 while CMD2's leftover still sat in words 2/3. **THIS BODY'S PAIR IS `readl(RESPONSE 0x10)` AT
# BOTH MOMENTS**, the same expression `st_send_command` reads, **and the four raw RESPONSE words are
# published at both moments this body owns** (`_nidx_raw_pre0..3`, `_nidx_raw_post0..3`), word-3-first
# in rung 18's own order, plus `_nidx_resp_moved` and `_nidx_resp_is_arg`. **What is deliberately NOT
# done**: the middle moment's four raw words are not published, because that reading lives in
# `st_send_command` - the one body every command in this ladder shares - and four new offsets there is
# a change this arm does not need to make its own point. The R1 decode is BACK for the reason rung 20
# left it out: this command DOES demand a 48-bit response, so `_nidx_resp` is this command's to read,
# with rung 19's own caveat that a field is a decode of whatever the register holds.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT HELD AS A SET A BUILD READS.** TWO stores, both to `INT_ENABLE 0x34`: the
# one-bit window set immediately before the command and its ONE unconditional restore after the
# publishes. `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` READ and NEVER WRITTEN, which keeps this block's SPI 123 -> intid 155
# off a line nobody here owns. No data path, no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word,
# no `INT_STATUS 0x30` write and no byte of the medium. **The failure mode is a diagnosis and not a
# lost device**: the 1.2 s bound is inside `st_send_command`, the ending is unmoved, and a delivery on
# the shared SPI 123 line ends the run at the dispatcher as `_irq_other_count = 1` / `iat = 155` - an
# ending this image already reads and survives (709 ended that way on intid 170).
#
# **WHAT THE BUILD HOLDS IT TO, AND WHAT THE BUILD'S OWN FIRST DRAFT GOT WRONG.** The new clause is
# `xnu_entry_746` in `src/entry/build_entry.sh`, guarded `-ge 20`, and it asserts the access set, the
# COUNT of every access (each of the four RESPONSE words exactly 2 - m756's repair as a number), the
# store set exactly `f9824934:str`, the distinct program order, an EMPTY image side, the call site's
# position after `st_all_send_cid` and after the command path's last `st_send_command`, the four
# command call sites `[2, 1, 1]`, and the exclusion of BOTH `st_cmd3_noresp` and `st_set_relative_addr`.
# **THE FIRST BUILD OF THIS RUNG REFUSED** - `FAIL: st_cmd3_noresp is not in the linked image while
# STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20` - because rung 20's own clause was guarded `-ge 19` and so demanded
# rung 20's symbol in a rung-21 image; it is `-eq 19` now, which is a property rather than a spelling.
#
# **AND THE CLAUSE GAINED A SPLIT, BECAUSE A FALSIFICATION CELL BUILT.** The distinct-order assertion
# cannot see a re-ordering of an access that REPEATS, and this rung repeats twice (four RESPONSE words
# at two moments, `0x34` stored twice): a cell that moved the window's restore to the body's LAST line
# came back `rc=0 BUILT-OK`. The body is now cut at its own `bl <st_send_command>` and two sets are
# asserted - the head exactly `f982491c:ldr f9824918:ldr f9824914:ldr f9824910:ldr f9824934:str
# f9824934:ldr f9824938:ldr`, and the whole body with the head's device accesses deleted exactly
# `f982491c:ldr f9824918:ldr f9824914:ldr f9824910:ldr f9824934:str f9824930:ldr f9824934:ldr
# f9824924:ldr` - so the restore must be the tail's FIRST store, ahead of `INT_STATUS 0x30` and
# `PRESENT_STATE 0x24`. **The pair is deliberately not head/tail**: `classify_body` resolves a base
# register only from a `movw`/`movt` it has itself seen, so a bare tail half reports every access as
# `UNK:`; the materialisation is in the head. THREE of the clause's own refusal messages were
# over-claims and are corrected: a removed call site is refused by the COMPILER first
# (`-Werror=unused-function`), not by the symbol check; and the exclusion clause is a SECOND net - both
# exclusion cells, one widening ALL FOUR of rung 20's guards, were refused by the SEAM IDENTIFICATION
# (`STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR is 0x800492dc`), because a body added to that translation unit moves the exit
# path. The falsification battery is 16 cells plus 2, in `/tmp/vprfix/falsify21.sh` and
# `/tmp/vprfix/falsify21_extra.sh`; the results are tabulated in experiment-747 section 8b.
#
# **THE ARM'S OWN NUMBERS.** entry bin `46fe6737…`, 5552764 B - **the seam constant did NOT move**
# (`STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR (0x800492dc`), and the entry bin's SIZE is byte-identical to the pressed rung-20
# arm's, so the image grew nowhere. `st_cmd3_noidx` at `0x8000df54`, size `0x338`; `st_cmd3_noresp` and
# `st_set_relative_addr` BOTH ABSENT (0 occurrences each); 44 `xnu_live_storage_nidx_*` keys in the body
# plus the two `_nidx_gated*` cells in `st_cmd_path` = 46 strings in the image; the encoded command in
# the disassembly is `mov r2, #5`, `mov r1, #65536`, `mov r0, #3`, `bl 8000cff8 <st_send_command>`; and
# the owed config comparison HOLDS - the payload's `stage90-build-config.txt` is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the
# pressed rung-20 arm's (`cmp` 6c2b6038), so no payload switch moved and the entry config differs in
# exactly two lines (the entry bin's hash and `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE 19 -> 20`).
#
# **THIS BLOCK ARMS A PRESS THAT HAS NOT BEEN AUTHORIZED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the
# press path is the two commands readiness prints, run by hand once each (730 section 6). When it is
# authorized: `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` first, then EXACTLY ONE
# `./scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh <printed flags>` and EXACTLY ONE
# `./scripts/run_and_capture.sh <printed flags> --expect-arm=<printed set>`. `fastboot boot` only,
# NEVER flash; the neighbour `33e80afe` absent from BOTH device lists before firing.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**
#
# **AND THE ENTRY IMAGE WAS REBUILT ONCE AFTER THE PAYLOAD, SO ONE MEMBER'S HASH MOVED AND THE OTHERS DID
# NOT.** `make check` refused this arm's first committed state with SIXTEEN unescaped backticks inside four
# of `xnu_entry_746`'s own refusal messages - **m730's class**: an unescaped backtick in a double-quoted
# shell string is a COMMAND SUBSTITUTION, so the refusal fires and its explanation has a hole, and a
# successful report prints a sentence with a word missing. The fix is escaped backticks, and
# `build_entry.sh` **IS one of the entry image's 23 recorded sources**, so the tree in front of the gate
# stopped being the tree the image was built from. The entry build was re-run: the **entry bin did NOT
# move** (`46fe6737`, 5552764 B - `build_entry.sh` is not compiled into the image, so nothing it does can
# move a byte of it) and neither did any other member; only `xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt` moved, and the
# `role=` on its line records why. **The general rule, and it is the one this arm is the first to hit:
# a check that is green only because nobody ran it is not green - `make check` is part of arming, and a
# source recorded by content makes every edit to the BUILDER a reason to rebuild.**
#

# 750 (2026-09-27): THE STORE CENSUS'S WIDTH CLAUSE FIRES - AND A WIDENED STORE STILL DOES NOT REACH
# IT. **THE OWED ITEM CARRIED BY EVERY BLOCK FROM 701 ONWARD IS HEREBY HALF CLOSED AND HALF
# EXPLAINED.** No arm was built, moved or pressed: this block records a measurement about a CHECK.
# ================================================================================================
#
# The item, in the words it has been carried in since 2026-09-25 (the rung-4 block above, and then
# 701/703/705/706/708/709/710/711/712/713/714/717/720 and every "STILL OWED" list since):
#
#     the width clause of the store census still never observed to fire on a WIDENED DEVICE store
#
# and the reason always printed beside it - every mutation that widened a store was refused by the AMB
# clause first, because a mutated store changes GCC's register allocation.
#
# THE MEASUREMENT IS `docs/experiments/experiment-750-the-width-clause-fires-and-a-widened-store-still-does-not-reach-it.md`,
# driven by `/tmp/vprfix/falsifyW.sh`: two cells on ONE site - `entry_storage.c:1228`,
# `entry_storage_probe`'s second CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C store (`sdhci.c:1305`) - in opposite directions.
#
#   * W2, the store NARROWED (`st_write16` -> `st_write8`): **THE WIDTH CLAUSE FIRES.**
#       hc_mem's window is [strb strb strh strb] against the record's strb strh strh strb, through
#       the same four base registers [sl r9 r3 r7]. The OFFSET assertion is the clause immediately
#       above it in the source AND DID NOT FIRE, so the offsets were still 47 44 44 41 and ONLY THE
#       WIDTH MOVED. **This refutes the item's own words** ("a wider store to 0x2F that still
#       materializes the same base pair is not a body this source produces") by measurement: the
#       clause is reachable, and the pair offset+mnemonic is two independent readings, not one.
#
#   * W1, the same store WIDENED (`st_write16` -> `st_write32`): **still not reached**, and the
#       mechanism is now exact rather than anecdotal. core_mem's window lists
#       [120 0 120 120 268 268 2348 ] and 0xf9824000 + 0x92C IS 0xf982492C = hc_mem + 0x2C - THE SAME
#       ABSOLUTE ADDRESS. Unmutated the store is `strh rX,[r10,#0x2C]` with r10 = 0xf9824900; widened
#       it is `str rX,[r6,#0x92C]` with r6 = 0xf9824000. **The census attributes a store to a window by
#       the BASE REGISTER GCC chose, not by the address the store computes**, so a perturbation that
#       reallocates registers MOVES A STORE BETWEEN WINDOWS and the wrong window's rule refuses -
#       true, correct, and silent about width. Recorded as m761; it is m718's shape surviving 718's
#       repair, because 718 changed how a base SET is REPORTED and not how a store is ATTRIBUTED.
#
# THE PRECISE REPLACEMENT SENTENCE, and it is narrower than the item it retires: **the store census's
# width clause is reachable and fires on a store NARROWED at a 4-aligned register; a store WIDENED at
# the same register is refused by the NEIGHBOURING window's offset clause first. Both are refusals, so
# the image was never at risk - what was unmeasured was WHICH NET was carrying the claim.**
#
# THE TREE WAS RESTORED AND RE-VERIFIED, NOT TRUSTED, because a battery that builds writes through
# out/ and a build that FAILS leaves the mutated image there while xnu_arm_entry-config.txt still
# describes the last image that passed (the hazard the rung-4 block above measured). The battery ends
# with a clean rebuild on the restored source, and every member was re-checked against this record:
# xnu_arm_entry.bin 46fe6737..., .elf f44d7fe5..., config b67c31df..., sources 5d565d31..., qcdt
# 3f11e29b..., and src/entry/entry_storage.c 38fb069c... byte-identical to the rung-21 armed base the
# battery started from. `git status --porcelain` empty; BOTH parks verify (11 files, 6 manifest checks
# each, in tree and in the export directory). The battery refuses up front if the live source is not
# the armed source, so it cannot measure a tree nobody armed.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: `out/` holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21), ARMED AND
# NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands readiness
# prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent from
# BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**

# 752 (2026-09-27): 747 SECTION 5'S BRANCH 2 HAS THE SAME DEFECT AS BRANCH 1 - AND ITS TWO
# PRODUCERS ARE A COMMAND **WITH** AN `INDEX` BIT AND A COMMAND **WITHOUT** ONE. No arm was built,
# moved or pressed: this block records a measurement about a BRANCH TABLE, and it changes the READING
# of the press this record already holds armed.
# ================================================================================================
#
# 749 fixed 747 section 5's BRANCH 1 (its condition was satisfied by two shapes). BRANCH 2 has the
# same defect, and this is what 751's tool found when it was pointed at the whole archive instead of
# one capture - a new `--branch-audit` mode that reports, per branch, HOW MANY DISTINCT PRODUCER
# SHAPES satisfy the branch's condition, and exits 1 when any branch has more than one:
#
#   B1  the block DECLINED the command          -> 2 shapes  (749's finding, reproduced by a check)
#   B2  the block TOOK it and never finished    -> 2 shapes  (THIS FINDING)
#   B3  the word STARTED and COMPLETED          -> 1 shape   (clean)
#
# B2's condition is `_nidx_inhibit_seen > 0` with `_nidx_complete = 0`, and the archive holds TWO rows
# that satisfy it which are NOT the same event:
#
#   * rung 19's CMD3-R1 (`_rca_`): word 0x031A, _rca_inhibit_seen = 0x400 (ALL 1024 samples),
#     _rca_inhibit_last = 0x01f80001 - **the inhibit bit STILL SET at the last sample**: taken and
#     STUCK, and the driver's own `sdhci_send_command` would still be waiting on it.
#
#   * rung 13's and rung 14's CMD0 (`_cmd0_`): word 0x0000, _cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x21b / 0x219,
#     _cmd0_inhibit_last = 0x01f80000 - **the bit CLEAR**: the block raised CMD_INHIBIT, held it for
#     about 126 us, RELEASED it, and latched nothing - no completion, no error, not one bit of the
#     whole INT_STATUS register, across 5,088,256 polls and the arm's own 1.2 s bound.
#
# **AND 0x0000 IS A WORD WITH NO RESPONSE DEMAND AND NO `INDEX` BIT AT ALL** (`rsp_present = 0` in both
# logs; flags 0x00 = RESP_NONE, no CRC, no INDEX). So if rung 21's log lands in B2 with inhibit_last
# bit 0 CLEAR, 747's sentence - "the rule's INDEX half is KILLED, 0x031A and 0x030A behave alike" -
# would be printed about a shape the ladder has ALREADY measured on a command carrying neither of the
# bits the branch is about. The CLEAR member's subject is 730's `INT_ENABLE` mask, not the flag word.
#
# THE CONFOUND IS UNAVOIDABLE, BECAUSE THREE RUNGS DIFFER IN ONE CELL. Rung 13's, rung 14's and rung
# 15's CMD0 drive the SAME word 0x0000 with the SAME _cmd0_inhibit_last = 0x01f80000 and (14 against
# 15) the SAME _cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219 - and `complete` is 0, 0, 1 with status_any 0, 0, 1. Rungs
# 13/14 are B2 and rung 15 is B3 **on an identical command word**. The record attributes the difference
# to the `INT_ENABLE` window (730's press: _int_status_after = 1 against _int_status_before = 0 with
# one store between them), so **`complete` is a function of the ARM'S OWN WINDOW as well as of the
# command** - and a table reading `complete = 0` as a statement about the command's flags is reading a
# cell the arm itself also decides.
#
# THE CORRECTED READING OF B2, a refinement and not a retraction:
#
#   seen > 0, complete = 0, **bit 0 of inhibit_last SET**   -> STILL STUCK (rung 19's shape). The
#       rule's INDEX half is genuinely in question.
#   seen > 0, complete = 0, **bit 0 CLEAR**                 -> RELEASED, NOTHING LATCHED (rungs 13/14's
#       shape). This is the mask subject, and it has been measured on a word with no INDEX bit.
#   seen > 0, complete = 1                                  -> COMPLETED (branch 3, one shape).
#
# **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS BIT 0 OF `_inhibit_last`, ALREADY PUBLISHED** - so NO REBUILD, and the parked
# arm stays valid. It is the second time in two experiments that a bit sitting unused inside a word the
# record had ALREADY printed was the whole of the missing reading (bit 24 of PRESENT_STATE 0x24 in 749).
#
# B3 IS CLEAN and is checked: one shape, six rows, all with inhibit_seen > 0. One note, recorded
# because a reader comparing B2's two shapes with B3's one should know the six rows behind it: **all six
# are commands that ask for nothing back** - CMD0 five times (0x0000) and rung 20's CMD3-no-response
# (0x0300) once - so B3's verdict ("a response-demanding command can finish on this block") is correct
# as a new reading and has simply never been produced. That is the branch rung 21 exists to reach.
#
# WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It does not move the arm, the payload, `out/`, or the entry image; nothing here
# is compiled. It does not say B2's verdict is wrong - rung 19's member of it is a real, well-measured
# stuck command and the INDEX question is genuinely raised by it. **What is wrong is the MERGE: two
# shapes, one name, for the THIRD time in three experiments** (746 section 4b's CMD1/CMD2, 749's branch
# 1, this branch 2). See m762 and m760.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: `out/` holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21), ARMED AND
# NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands readiness
# prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent from
# BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**

# 753 - READ OUT OF THE ARCHIVE AND THE SOURCE; NO DEVICE ACTION, NO BUILD, out/ UNTOUCHED.

# WHAT CHANGED IN THE RECORD. **Nothing in this file's arms, gates or flags.** 753 is a correction
# to a landed document (748 section 4) and one more elimination from rung 21's candidate set; it
# adds no set=, moves no arm, and compiles nothing. See
# docs/experiments/experiment-753-the-key-read-after-the-power-byte-reads-bus-on.md.
#
# THE DEFECT. 748 section 4 dismissed CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC with "the archive has read it after
# the power byte on fifteen boots, all zero". The COUNT is exact and the KEY is the one it named
# (_reg_pwrctl_status_after, fifteen captures) - **the MOMENT is not.** That key is emitted at the
# end of st_driver_reset (entry_storage.c:784, function at :719, rung >= 4), and the probe calls
# st_driver_reset() FIRST (:4063), before st_pwr_irq_arm() :4100, st_power_set() :4113 and
# st_pwr_wait() :4127 - so on EVERY rung it is read before the block is asked anything. The key
# read either side of the byte is a DIFFERENT one, _pwr_status_before / _pwr_status_after
# (:1374 / :1388, the st_write8 at :1384 between them), and it reads:
#
#     _pwr_status_before = 0x00000000   ->  store POWER_CONTROL 0x29  ->  _pwr_status_after = 0x00000002
#
# **twelve captures, one value each**, 0x02 = ST_CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON (rung7, rung8, rung9, rung12..rung20).
# This is the m732 shape again: a cell that stands for a register has to be asked WHEN it was read.
#
# AND THE BLOCK ANSWERS. Over the ELEVEN captures carrying the rung-8 client: _pwr_irq_status =
# _pwr_irq_status32 = 0x00000002 (the rung's own two-widths self-check, passing on eleven boots),
# _pwr_irq_ctl_before = 0x00 -> _pwr_irq_ctl_after = 0x01 (vendor's read either side of the ack store,
# :1573-1576). The wait agrees on nine consecutive rungs (_pwr_wait_timeout = 0, _pwr_wait_ctl_after
# = 0x01), and the TIMING is now a number: _pwr_irq_at - _pwr_wait_t0 = 1033, 1059, 1055, 1045, 1049,
# 1044, 1048, 1053, 1053 on rungs 12-20 - **nine boots within 26 ticks (2.5%) of ~1,050.**
#
# RUNG 9 IS THE SOLE OUTLIER AT 1922347 (1,831x) AND THE SAME CAPTURE SAYS WHY. _pwr_wait_cpsr =
# 0x80000093 (bit 7, I, SET) against rung 12/20's 0x80000013 (clear); the handler entered 1,153 ticks
# after the poll gave up against a bound of 1,920,000 - it lost by 0.06% of its own bound.
# **So _pwr_wait_timeout = 1 on rung 9 is a property of the WAITER, not of the block**, and the record
# has been quoting it beside the block. (The direct measurement of the block's assertion instant on
# rung 9 is NOT in the archive - that capture's _pwr_irq_at is stamped at the handler's entry, after
# the unmask. The inference is labelled as such in the document.)
#
# WHAT IT DOES TO RUNG 21. 748 section 4's VERDICT survives and its REASON does not: 0xDC is still not
# worth a rung, now for the stronger reason that the key it quoted is read before the block is asked.
# And it removes one more explanation for CMD1/CMD2's silence - a block holding an unanswered power
# request would show 0x02 with no ctl_after = 1, and the archive has never shown that pair.
#
# **THE CAVEAT, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED.** The handler FOLDS the vendor's two regulator branches to
# the success direction (:1551, "there is no regulator call here to fail"), so _pwr_irq_ctl_after = 0x01
# is THIS IMAGE's acknowledgement, not a PMIC's. What is proved is that the block's handshake ran to
# completion and was answered here; **what is NOT proved is that the eMMC's supplies are physically up**,
# because nothing in this image talks to the PMIC. The elimination is among the explanations this ladder
# can distinguish, and it is stated that way on purpose.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: out/ holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21),
# ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands
# readiness prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent
# from BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**

# ================================================================================================
# 754 - HOST-SIDE ONLY: ONE NEW TOOL, ONE MAKEFILE CLAUSE. NO DEVICE ACTION, NO BUILD, out/ UNTOUCHED.
# ================================================================================================
# WHAT CHANGED. `tools/read_storage_key_order.py` (new) and one clause in `make check` that runs its
# `--selftest`. **Nothing in this file's arms, gates or flags changed**; no set= was added (still 330)
# and no arm moved. See docs/experiments/experiment-754-the-moment-of-a-cell-is-now-a-lookup.md.
#
# THE DEFECT CLASS IT MAKES MECHANICAL. Two landed findings are the same question asked of the same
# file, and both were found BY HAND, one press apart:
#   * m732 - 723 section 3 read the rung-3/rung-4 `_reg_power_control*` cells for a moment that
#     belongs to rung 7, which is the rung that writes POWER_CONTROL 0x29.
#   * m763 - 748 section 4 read `_reg_pwrctl_status_after` for a moment that belongs to the power
#     byte, while the key is published at the END OF `st_driver_reset` (`:784`), which the probe
#     calls FIRST (`:4063`).
# Both are answerable from `entry_storage.c`'s own call order and neither number was in any document.
#
# THE TOOL. For any `xnu_live_storage_*` key it prints the function that publishes it, the chain of
# calls from `entry_storage_probe` that reaches it, and the preprocessor guard on each hop; `--after`
# refuses a "read after X" claim the call order does not support. The rung defaults to the value the
# image in `out/` was built at (from out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry-config.txt), and a `--rung` outside the
# domain parsed from the file's own `#error` guard is REFUSED - `--rung 21` prints the two-spellings
# note (the record counts ordinal rungs; the ladder's "rung 21" IS value 20). A cell published from an
# interrupt client is reported as an INTERVAL, never given a point.
#
# THE AUDIT, at rung 20 (the value the armed arm carries): **516 keys** (462 literals + 54 generated by
# two macros), **31 functions**, **418 POINT**, **15 INTERVAL** (all in `st_pwr_irq`), **83 DEAD at
# rung 20**, **0 UNKNOWN**. The 83 are the families whose guards are `== 18` (rca, 38), `== 19` (nrsp,
# 35) and `== 15` (quiet, 10) - the three arms whose bodies were REPLACED rather than added - so a
# sentence quoting `_nrsp_complete` against a rung-20 capture is quoting a cell that cannot exist in
# that image. `_nidx_*` (rung 21's own family) is live, guard `>= 20`.
#
# **THE TOOL WAS WRONG FOUR TIMES WHILE IT WAS WRITTEN, AND EACH ONE IS MEASURED** - which is why its
# self-test is a clause of `make check`:
#   (a) the key pattern matched only the FIRST string literal, so `ST_LIVE("xnu_live_storage_cmd" tag
#       "_ps_before")` produced the phantom key `xnu_live_storage_cmd`. Half a name, a key that never was.
#   (b) **14 of the file's 31 definitions were parsed as `__attribute__`** - the anchored matcher found
#       `__attribute__` inside `static __attribute__((noinline, noclone)) void\nst_send_command(...)` -
#       so `st_send_command`, `st_pwr_wait`, `st_resp_before` and `st_cmd_path` had no reachable edges
#       and the stage table showed SEVEN OF ITS ELEVEN STAGES.
#   (c) the first draft cleared its signature accumulator on every blank depth-0 line and parsed ZERO
#       functions while still printing "0 function(s) parsed, 463 distinct key(s) published".
#   (d) the macro-invocation argument text was read out of the copy whose string bodies are blanked, so
#       **all 27 generated keys collapse** - the CMD0 and CMD1 censuses merge into one family with a
#       space where the tag belongs, 489 keys instead of 516, and every `_cmd1_*` lookup REFUSES.
#       **That is m760's exact class - one key, two producers, one name - inside the tool built to
#       catch it.**
# None of the four raised an exception and two printed an internally consistent table.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: out/ holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21),
# ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands
# readiness prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent
# from BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**

# ================================================================================================
# 755 - READ OUT OF THE ARCHIVE AND THE SOURCE; NO DEVICE ACTION, NO BUILD, out/ UNTOUCHED.
# ================================================================================================
# WHAT CHANGED IN THE RECORD. **Nothing in this file's arms, gates or flags.** No set= was added
# (still 330) and no arm moved. 755 re-derives a landed conclusion from a second clock, corrects a
# landed document's rung spelling, and puts a number on what the storage probe costs the OS. Plus the
# one live-code fix: `tools/read_storage_key_order.py`'s headers now print BOTH rung spellings.
# See docs/experiments/experiment-755-the-os-poll-is-late-by-exactly-the-probes-blocking-commands.md.
#
# THE FINDING. 503's `_poll_ticks` is, by its own source comment, "the wall time the kernel kept this
# thread parked" (entry_stubs.c:4561, comment at :4523) - WALL TIME, not the timeout. Across nine
# captures the third poll (the first asking 2000 ms) returns late by exactly the probe's full-bound
# command polls minus the 2000 ms the OS asked for:
#
#   rung6/8/9/16   probe has NO full-bound poll            p3 = 2.0077..2.0093   (+7.7..+9.3 ms)
#   rung12/13/14   ONE  (cmd0 1.2001)                      p3 = 2.0076..2.0093   (fits in the window)
#   rung15         ONE  (cmd1 1.2001)                      p3 = 2.0090
#   rung17         TWO  (cmd1 1.2000 + cid 1.2001 = 2.4001) p3 = 2.4270          (+427.0 ms)
#   rung18         TWO  (2.4003)                            p3 = 2.4220          (+422.0 ms)
#   rung19         THREE(cmd1+cid+rca = 3.6003)             p3 = 3.6241          (+1624.1 ms)
#   rung20         TWO  (2.4002)                            p3 = 2.4249          (+424.9 ms)
#
# Each excess agrees with the probe's own published total to a residual of 21.6 - 26.8 ms (the tail
# after the last command).
#
# **THE INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION OF 746.** rung 19 -> rung 20 moved ONE constant (RSP_SHORT -> NONE);
# _rca_ticks 1.2000 s became _nrsp_ticks 0.0003 s; the probe's full-bound total fell 3.6003 -> 2.4002 s;
# and **the OS's poll lateness fell 1624.1 -> 424.9 ms - 1199.2 ms measured against 1200.1 ms of probe
# time removed, agreeing to 0.9 ms.** That is 746's headline reached from a clock the ladder does not
# own. It also confirms the rung-value-to-ordinal mapping four times over from the guards alone
# (cmd0_ticks >= 11 first at rung12; cid_ticks >= 16 first at rung17; rca == 18 at rung19;
# nrsp == 19 at rung20).
#
# TWO MORE CONSTANTS. The poll-to-poll handoff (p4.before - p3.after) is **1.505 - 1.529 ms across
# twelve boots on nine rungs (a 1.6% spread)**. The 5 ms/40 ms pair's DIFFERENCE is **37.69 - 38.57 ms
# for a 35 ms difference in the ask - constant to 0.88 ms across thirteen boots**, so 503's own
# falsifier passes for that pair; and it FAILS for the 2000/2000 pair on rungs 17-20 only (same ask,
# durations differing by 411-1609 ms) - the falsifier fired and the storage probe is the explanation,
# not the kernel's timer.
#
# **A CORRECTION TO 754 SECTION 4.** Its sentence "a reader holding a rung-20 capture and a sentence
# quoting `_nrsp_complete` is quoting a cell that cannot exist in that image" is WRONG: _nrsp_complete's
# guard is `== 19`, and the capture named `rung20-nrsp` is the ORDINAL rung 20 = value 19, where that
# body is exactly the one that ran. The tool's report had used the switch-VALUE spelling (20 = the
# armed arm, where nrsp is indeed dead). The cause was the tool's own header carrying ONE spelling;
# fixed rather than annotated - every header now prints `# rung 20 (switch VALUE; the record's ordinal
# rung 21)` and `--rung 21` still refuses with the note that the ladder's "rung 21" IS value 20.
# `--selftest` is unmoved and `make check` still exits 0. **This is the fifth instance in five
# experiments of the name-with-two-readings family, and the first that landed inside a tool whose own
# refusal message warns about it** - the warning was in the refusal path, the header was not.
#
# A READING FOR THE SECOND STRATEGIC DECISION. `entry_post_clock` (entry_trace.c:2358) is a HARD STOP
# (end_now -> entry_seam_end_run()), and at the buzzer the OS is HEALTHY: /dev/rmd0 opened (error 0),
# /dev/nosuch refused (error 2, 503's control pair), a child reaped (_wait_status = 0x300), four polls
# plus _poll_over = 1, in a repeating poll(nfds=0, 2000 ms) loop whose _poll_retval = 0 says it returns
# normally every time - and the gap to the deadline is **4.0249 s = exactly 2.000 x a 2000 ms poll**.
# So the "stays up" arm would test FOR HOW LONG an idle OS with a 2-second sleep loop keeps returning,
# not whether the OS survives.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: out/ holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21),
# ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands
# readiness prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent
# from BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and the machine never observed
# STAYING UP (745). **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**
#
# 756 - READ OUT OF THE VENDOR TREE AND THE PAYLOAD SOURCE; NO DEVICE ACTION, NO BUILD, out/ UNTOUCHED.
# The vendor's bring-up disables the DLL before its FIRST command and this ladder has never touched
# that register. See
# docs/experiments/experiment-756-the-vendors-bring-up-disables-the-dll-and-the-ladder-never-has.md.
#
# THE DIFF IS A DIFF, NOT A MEASUREMENT. Rungs 2, 6 and 7 transcribe sdhci_msm_init's mode sequence,
# sdhci_msm_set_clock and mmc_power_up's PASS A - **and all three are faithful transcriptions of
# functions that do not contain the write in question.** The write is sdhci_msm_set_uhs_signaling
# (sdhci-msm.c:2537-2600), reached by the MMC core through sdhci_set_ios -> sdhci_do_set_ios
# (sdhci.c:1784-1785; .set_ios at :2384; sdhci_msm_ops at sdhci-msm.c:2653) DURING POWER-UP, BEFORE
# THE FIRST COMMAND. At host->clock <= CORE_FREQ_100MHZ (:164 = 100 MHz; this ladder is at 400 kHz,
# rung 6) it does TWO things: ctrl_2 &= ~SDHCI_CTRL_UHS_MASK (net 0 = SDR12 for MMC_TIMING_LEGACY,
# because no arm of the seven-arm else-if chain at :2547-2560 matches LEGACY), and it sets
# **CORE_DLL_RST (1<<30) and CORE_DLL_PDN (1<<29) in CORE_DLL_CONFIG 0x100** (:2578, :2583) - "make
# sure DLL is disabled when not required" - then writes HOST_CONTROL2 unconditionally at :2597.
# The vendor's own comment is the point: "the feedback clock must be provided and DLL must not be
# used so that tuning can be skipped" - a statement about HOW THE CONTROLLER SAMPLES, and
# msm_host->calibration_done = false is set with it.
#
# WHY THIS IS A CANDIDATE FOR THE STALL. The ladder's own five-command table (746, 749) said the
# failure is confined to RECEIVING: CMD0 and the rung-20 CMD3 complete in 0.255-0.30 ms while every
# command that must receive a response is TAKEN (the word is in the register, CMD_INHIBIT rises on
# the read after the store) and then never completes, with _status_any = 0 across the whole
# INT_STATUS register over 5,088,256 polls and 1.200 s. **A receive path that cannot sample has
# exactly this signature, and a command that asks for nothing back cannot see it.**
#
# THE GAP IS STRUCTURAL, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT WORTH A NOTE. If sdhci_msm_set_clock contained the
# same DLL writes the ladder would have them at rung 6 by transcription and the observation would
# collapse. It does not: sdhci_msm_set_clock (:2402-2535, read in full) writes CORE_VENDOR_SPEC and
# the standard clock registers and calls clk_set_rate, and **CORE_DLL_CONFIG appears nowhere in it**.
# The DLL is the one low-clock setup step that lives on the function this ladder did not port. And
# the only other DLL code - msm_init_cm_dll (:577-630, with msm_cm_dll_set_freq :549,
# msm_config_cm_dll_phase :379, msm_dll_poll_ck_out_en :351, sdhci_msm_cdclp533_calibration :661) -
# **ENABLES** the DLL and is reachable only from sdhci_msm_execute_tuning (:792, via :820 and :846),
# i.e. the TUNING path. So this block has exactly two vendor statements about the DLL at 400 kHz -
# disable it on set_ios, enable it on tuning - and it is on the first one.
#
# SDHCI_HOST_CONTROL2 0x3E IS THE SECOND REGISTER THE LADDER HAS NEVER READ OR WRITTEN, and for it
# the answer is genuinely UNKNOWN rather than probably fine. The argument that rung 4's RESET_ALL
# already clears its bits[2:0] is a SPEC read against ONE STORE with no cell - and it is undercut by
# the vendor's own code: sdhci_reset (sdhci.c:229-283) writes ONLY SDHCI_SOFTWARE_RESET 0x2F and
# HOST_CONTROL2 appears nowhere in it, while the vendor writes the field explicitly on every
# set_ios. A register a driver always writes explicitly is one it does not trust a reset to set.
# CORE_DLL_CONFIG is at 0x100, far outside the register file Software Reset for All is defined over,
# and set_uhs_signaling's own comment says the DLL "needs to be restored and CDCLP533 recalibrated
# when the clock frequency is set back to 400MHz" - a reset that left it sane would make the flag
# and the comment unnecessary. So BOTH offsets go into the one arm, and each has three outcomes.
#
# THE CENSUS SKIPS BOTH AND THE CENSUS IS THE INSTRUMENT THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT THEM. Rung 3
# (st_standard_census, entry_storage.c:612-674) reads TEN values: eight register-file offsets
# (0x2F 0x29 0x28 0x2C 0xFC 0x24 0x44 0x48) and two MSM-core ones (0xE0 0xE8) - and it STOPS at 0x44,
# skipping 0x3E between 0x2C and it. `grep -n 'HOST_CONTROL2|0x3E|host_control2|DLL_CONFIG|dll'`
# over entry_storage.c RETURNS NOTHING (exit 1), so neither register has a cell in any capture. The
# file is otherwise careful about exactly this - ST_SDHCI_HOST_CONTROL 0x28's own comment is "a BYTE,
# and at an offset no 32-bit access reaches" (:204) - and 0x40 and 0x44 are two separate constants
# (:200-201) of which the census reads the second, so offsets have been chosen one register per press.
#
# THE NEGATIVE HALF, WHICH IS WORTH AS MUCH. Every register sdhci-msm.c writes falls into four
# families and three are implemented AND MEASURED: the mode/core-reset sequence (rung 2; _mode_w0_read
# 0x0 -> _mode_w1_read 0x1 -> _mode_w2_read 0x2001, _mode_bit_after = 1 on ALL 13 captures); the
# power-IRQ handshake (rungs 8/9; 753); and the clock plus its vendor register (rung 6, whose own
# #error text names the two CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C read-modify-writes). Only the DLL/CDC/calibration
# family has no cell of any kind.
#
# AND SDHCI_TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E IS RETIRED FOR THIS LADDER - same shape as 748 section 2, and it
# deserves to stop being proposed. sdhci.c touches it EXACTLY TWICE: read at :115 inside
# sdhci_dumpregs (the debug dump, exactly like SLOT_INT_STATUS 0xFC), and written at :828, guarded by
# :826's `if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY))`. **Not one of the ladder's six commands carries
# MMC_RSP_BUSY (1 << 3, include/linux/mmc/core.h:31)** - the words are R2 0x07, R1 0x15, NONE 0x00,
# R1_NOIDX 0x05, PRESENT 0x01 - and ST_SDHCI_CMD_RESP_SHORT_BUSY 0x03 exists in the payload only as a
# macro and a _Static_assert. So on the COMMAND path this register is dead for the same reason
# SLOT_INT_STATUS is, and it goes live the moment the driver has a DATA path, which is where the
# storage driver the goal needs will first need it. A note for a future rung, NOT a proposal for this.
#
# THE DISCRIMINATOR IS TWO READS IN ONE ARM, AND IT IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION. Read hc_mem + 0x3E and
# hc_mem + 0x100 (0xf982493E, 0xf9824A00). hc_mem 0xf9824900 >> 20 = 0xf98; both offsets are in that
# same megabyte, i.e. the one every register this image already reads lives in, so the
# addr >> 20 interlock (692's lesson) is satisfied BEFORE the arm is written rather than argued after.
# Both are inside the declared window too, and the window is the PRO one: msm8974.dtsi:503 declares
# `reg = <0xf9824900 0x11c>` and msm8974pro.dtsi:1765-1766 OVERRIDES `&sdhc_1` to **0x1a0**, which is
# the applicable one for cancro (MSM8974Pro-AC). No new mapping, NO STORE, so no mechanism risk at
# all - which matters because the ladder is mid-flight and out/ is parked. A cheaper half-step exists
# if the operator prefers it (the same two reads with NO command in the arm). Neither is armed here.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY. It does not say the DLL is the cause: four facts are measured and the causal
# link is named as a hypothesis. It does not say HOST_CONTROL2 is set wrong: both directions of that
# argument are given with their exact strength and the question goes into the arm instead of into
# prose. It does not re-open 753. It does not move the arm, the payload, out/, or the entry image:
# **RUNG 21 STAYS ARMED AND NOT PRESSED, and it tests the INDEX bit, not the sampling clock, so this
# finding does not change what rung 21 will read.** No rung is designed or armed by this document.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: out/ holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry rung 21),
# ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands
# readiness prints, run by hand once each. `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe
# absent from BOTH lists before firing, and no press may be spent without the operator's authorization.
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**
#
# 758 - **THE PRESS WAS SPENT.** 2026-09-27, one press, WITH THE OPERATOR'S AUTHORIZATION, on
# `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20, entry ordinal rung 21). `fastboot boot` only,
# NEVER flash; neighbour 33e80afe absent from BOTH the adb and fastboot lists before firing; exactly one
# gate (`--allow-xnu-entry`, exit 0) and exactly one runner (`--allow-xnu-entry
# --expect-arm=armed-storage-46fe6737`). **EXIT 0 - returned and captured, the device back 29 s after the
# send.** Capture out/stage90/captures/rung21-noidx-20260927-last_kmsg.txt, 639,147 B, sha256
# 25512586ae087d17210b33debe6fb1d5217d2e21681ab137b90be3ca745a385b. See
# docs/experiments/experiment-758-the-rung-21-press-the-index-rule-is-refuted.md.
#
# READINESS REFUSED FIRST, AND THAT IS PART OF THIS RECORD. `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` returned 1 of 5
# on the armed arm: 747 ARMED THIS ARM AND NEVER WROTE ITS NARRATION - the branch chain in that file stopped
# at `wst == 19`, so value 20 fell to the else arm's sentence and the row would have printed 693's name.
# That is the 698 defect exactly (a branch that enumerates the values it knows mis-names the next one in
# silence), caught by the clause written for it. Commit 89525c6 wrote the arm sentence and the
# consequence paragraph; readiness then went 5 of 5, exit 0. **Two names the first draft nearly carried
# were ABSENT from the image and were removed before landing** - `_rca_gated` and `_nrsp_gated`, whose
# bodies are compiled out at this value - measured with arm-none-eabi-strings; the row's own name check
# reports 53 names and 0 globs read against the ELF's keys.
#
# **THE ANSWER: THE INDEX RULE IS REFUTED.** The arm asked whether a word that demands a response and
# carries no INDEX bit is DECLINED. It is not. 0x030A was ACCEPTED AND STARTED - _nidx_inhibit_seen =
# 0x00000400 (ALL 1024 samples), _nidx_inhibit_after = 1, _nidx_inhibit_last bit 0 STILL SET - and never
# finished: _nidx_complete = 0, _nidx_status_any = 0 over _nidx_any_polls = 0, _nidx_timeout = 1,
# _nidx_ticks = 0x015f91be = 1.20002 s. The word was read back out of the block (_nidx_word =
# _nidx_word_read = 0x0000030a), so this is not a store that failed to land.
#
# **AND 0x030A IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM rung 19's 0x031A IN EVERY STOPPING CELL.** One bit apart on the
# bus (INDEX), and: inhibit_seen 0x400 both; inhibit_last 0x01f80001 both (bit 0 set at the last sample);
# complete 0 both; status_any 0 both; any_polls 0 both; ticks 1.20002 s against 1.20004 s - **20 us apart
# on a 1.2 s quantity (1.7 ppm)**. A block that could tell the two words apart would not produce that.
#
# SO THE RUNG-19/20 PAIR'S COMPETING EXPLANATION IS DEAD AND 746's HEADLINE SURVIVES, NARROWER. Across all
# six words on the record - 0x0000 (no demand, completes 0.2553 ms), 0x0300 (no demand, completes
# 0.2554 ms), 0x031A (demand, starts, stalls), 0x030A (demand, starts, stalls), 0x0102 and 0x0209 (demand,
# NEVER STARTED) - the variable that tracks the outcome is RESP_PRESENT alone, and it is now the only one
# standing. The arm's own three-way resolved against its own published prediction: 747's SECOND branch
# fired verbatim.
#
# THE WINDOW AND THE ENDING ARE UNMOVED: _nidx_ena_wrote = 0x00000001, _nidx_ena_held = 0x00008001,
# _nidx_readback = 0x00008000, _nidx_wrote_back = 0, _nidx_sig_enable = 0 (read and never written),
# _nidx_status_post = 0; _seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879, _seam_post_end_ticks = 0x06ddd000, _post_end_calls = 6,
# _sleh_storm = 9 - every one the rung-20 press's value. **The declared failure mode did not fire**, and it
# had been declared in advance so its absence is a reading rather than a relief.
#
# **AND 746's REPAIR EARNED ITS KEEP ON ITS FIRST PRESS.** The arm's pair is one readl(RESPONSE 0x10) at
# both moments (rungs 19 and 20 used the 136-bit formula whose top word is a different offset - m756) and
# it publishes the four raw words at BOTH moments so a reader can check the claim. Checked:
#   _rca_resp  0x40ff8080 -> 0x00000000   (rung 19)
#   _nrsp_resp 0x40ff8080 -> 0x00000000   (rung 20)
#   _nidx_resp 0x00000000 -> 0x40ff8080   (THIS PRESS)
# and this press's raw words: pre 0x1C=0x0040ff80 0x18=0x80000000 0x14=0 0x10=0; post 0x1C=0 0x18=0 0x14=0
# 0x10=0x40ff8080. SAME VALUE, SAME REGISTER, OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS on arms one flag bit apart, with the
# byte pattern one word up before this command. **What moved the register is UNNAMED and nothing answered**
# (_nidx_resp_is_arg = 0); _nidx_state/_nidx_ready/_nidx_illegal are a decode of 0x40ff8080 - the word the
# ladder has read since 733 - and 738's stale-word trap is exactly that shape, which is why the arm
# published the raw word beside the decode.
#
# **THE QUESTION THIS PRESS OPENED, RECORDED AND NOT ANSWERED.** Four words in ONE capture split into two
# behaviours - {0x0000, 0x030A} started, {0x0102, 0x0209} never started - and the OPCODE column is the only
# field that splits them cleanly ({0,3} against {1,2}); CRC_CHECK splits it wrong on 0x0209; RESP_TYPE
# splits it wrong on 0x0209; ARGUMENT splits it wrong on 0x0102; and CMD_INDEX - the field the refuted rule
# was about - does not split it at all. **A clean split is not an explanation and the record says the
# ladder does not know which.** Two facts bound the search and both are measured: (a) the CMD2 gate at
# entry_storage.c:3742 (`c1.sent != 0u && (c1.resp & ST_MMC_CARD_BUSY) == 0u`) PASSED - _cid_gated = 0,
# _cid_sent = 1, _cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080 with bit 31 clear - so CMD2 was not skipped by the ladder; and
# (b) _cmd1_any_polls = 0 and _cid_any_polls = 0, so neither word's poll saw one non-zero INT_STATUS
# sample - the same never-started the rung-20 press measured, reproduced.
#
# **NO ARM IS DESIGNED OR ARMED BY THIS STEP, AND out/ STILL HOLDS THE ARM THIS PRESS SPENT
# (`armed-storage-46fe6737`, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20). No firer is armed and none is needed; the press
# path is the two commands readiness prints, run by hand once each. Any further press is the operator's
# decision - and a new rung costs ONE PRESS and the arm in `out/` its REBUILDABILITY, not the payload and
# not this park: 666 section 5 measured that the payload build DOES reproduce (the 48 bytes 408 read as
# nondeterminism are the STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY switch), so what a source change costs is that a past arm can no
# longer be produced from the tree - its park is bytes on disk, outside the build path, and reverting to it
# is a `cp`. Corrected by 760; this block said the opposite before that step.**
#
# The goal is still **NOT met**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount. The block starts a command and never
# completes one. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**
#
# 759 - READ OUT OF THE RUNG-21 CAPTURE AND THE PAYLOAD SOURCE; NO DEVICE ACTION, NO PRESS, NO BUILD,
# out/ UNTOUCHED. **NO ARM MOVED.** Three explanations for 758's four-row split are refuted by cells the
# same capture already carries. See
# docs/experiments/experiment-759-three-explanations-for-the-four-row-split-are-refuted.md.
#
# (1) **THE ENABLE WINDOW DOES NOT PREDICT `started`.** CMD2 and CMD3 carry the IDENTICAL five window
# cells - _cid_ena_wrote = 1, _cid_ena_held = 0x8001, _cid_readback = 0x8000, _cid_sig_enable = 0, and
# the same four plus _nidx_ena_wrote for CMD3 - and CMD2 never started while CMD3 did. CMD0 ran with
# rung 14's window OPEN (_int_enable_wrote = 1, _int_enable_held = 0x8001) and started; CMD1 ran with
# that window restored (_int_enable_readback = 0x8000, bit 0 clear) and did not. **An open window is
# present in ONE of the two failing rows and BOTH succeeding rows; a closed window in the other failing
# row. The window takes two values and the outcome takes both values under each.**
#
# **AND THIS REFUTES THE CODEBASE'S OWN COMMENT**, which is why it is worth a record block.
# entry_storage.c:3833-3836 says a second command in one boot "would meet the driver's own inhibit guard
# inside st_send_command and either wait out its 10 ms bound or be refused". That is a PREDICTION,
# written before this ladder had ever sent four commands in one boot; the rung-21 press is the first
# boot that can test it, and it does not hold. The guard itself was not reached: _cmd1_inhibit_before =
# 0x00000000, _cmd1_inhibit_polls = 1, _cmd1_inhibit_ticks = 8, _cmd1_inhibit_timeout = 0, so the
# `return` at :2263 that would leave sent = 0 was not taken. The comment is NOT edited - it did its job.
# **Note the cell asymmetry and do not smooth it over**: st_all_send_cid and st_cmd3_noidx are their own
# bodies and NEITHER publishes `inhibit_before` or `ps_before` (0 occurrences of _cid_inhibit_before,
# _cid_ps_before, _cid_inhibit_after in the capture), so for those two rows the evidence is
# `_*_sent = 1` and the word read back, and that is stated rather than assumed.
#
# (2) **st_send_command HAS NO PER-OPCODE BRANCH.** Read in full: it clears its result, reads
# PRESENT_STATE (:2239), runs the inhibit loop (:2246-2260), clears the latch (:2267-2270), writes
# ARGUMENT (:2277), builds the word with ST_SDHCI_CMD_WORD(opcode, mmc_flags) (:2299), stores it 16-bit
# to COMMAND 0x0E (:2301), sets r->sent = 1u (:2302), reads word and inhibit back (:2322-2323). **The
# only use of `opcode` anywhere in the function is the macro argument at :2299.** So CMD0 and CMD1 take
# identical code with SEVEN identical cells - ps_before 0x01f80000, inhibit_before 0, stale 0,
# clear_wrote 0, clear_after 0, arg 0, sent 1, word = word_read - **and the two cells that differ are
# the answer**: _cmd0_inhibit_after = 1 / _cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x219 against _cmd1_inhibit_after = 0 /
# _cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0. Nothing between the store and the block is visible to this image.
#
# (3) **ORDER ALONE DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT.** Started: the 1st and the 4th. Never started: the 2nd and the
# 3rd. A block that stopped accepting after its first command would not start the fourth, and one wedged
# by a stalled command (rung 19's CMD3 stalled 1.2 s) would not start on the next one. Not monotonic.
#
# **AND THE ABSENCES ARE READINGS, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSERTED** (749's and 760's discipline).
# _cmd0_polls = 0x0000021a = 538 with _cmd0_any_polls = 538 (a completion was seen); _cmd1_polls =
# 0x004da000 = 5,087,232 with _cmd1_any_polls = 0; _cid_polls = 0x004da400 = 5,088,256 with 0;  **778: the
# decimals here were the row below's, one command over - the hexes are rung 21's own.**
# _nidx_polls = 0x004da000 with 0. **5,088,256 samples is not zero samples** - `any_polls` is the cell
# that distinguishes "ran and saw nothing" from "did not run", and the three commands ran the same-size
# 1.2 s bound.
#
# **WHAT IS LEFT.** The split {0x0000, 0x0300, 0x030A, 0x031A} against {0x0102, 0x0209} separates by
# OPCODE {0,3} against {1,2} and by NO other bit-field of the COMMAND register: RESP_TYPE_SELECT fails
# on 0x0102 (type 10, did not start; 0x030A type 10, did); CRC_CHECK fails (0x0102 off and 0x0209 on
# both did not start); CMD_INDEX is set only on 0x031A; CMD_TYPE is 00 on all six; the argument fails
# (0x0000 is 0 and started, 0x0102 is 0 and did not); and any numeric threshold is the opcode statement
# in another spelling, opcode being bits 13:8. **AND AN OPCODE IS NOT A MECHANISM** - nothing in the
# payload branches on it and nothing in the register's field definitions makes 1 and 2 a family. Three
# alternatives are measured away; no cause is named and no rung is designed.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED: out/ still holds `armed-storage-46fe6737` (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20), the arm 758's
# press spent.** No firer is armed and none is needed; the press path is the two commands readiness
# prints, run by hand once each. **A NEW RUNG COSTS ONE PRESS AND THE ARM IN `out/` ITS REBUILDABILITY,

# the build path, and reverting to it is a `cp`. Corrected by 763; 760 corrected the same sentence
# earlier in this block and left this second occurrence, which is why 763's census counts occurrences
# rather than sites.** `fastboot boot` only, NEVER flash, and no press may be spent without the operator's
