# 761 - READ OUT OF THE ARCHIVED CAPTURES AND THE VENDORED KERNEL TREE; NO DEVICE ACTION, NO PRESS, NO GATE,
# NO RUNNER, NO BUILD, out/ UNTOUCHED. **NO ARM MOVED.** Two landed claims read back against their own
# evidence: 759 section 5's "only the opcode separates them" FAILS, and 756's gap list PASSES.
#
# (a) 759 section 5's class {0x0102, 0x0209} is TWO of the archive's own four shapes - tools/
# read_storage_commands.py, run over every archived capture (39 in out/stage90/captures/, 9 carrying a command
# body), names them: TAKEN -> COMPLETED (0x0000, 0x0300), LINE MOVED INSIDE THE WINDOW (0x0102),
# LINE NEVER MOVED (0x0209), TAKEN -> STUCK AT THE BOUND (0x031A, 0x030A). 749 section 3 already measured the
# cell that separates the merged pair: inhibit_last 0x00f80000 vs 0x01f80000 - bit 24, the CMD line, LOW vs
# HIGH - and RESPONSE 0x00000000 -> 0x40ff8080 against unmoved. The opcode claim fails under either reading of
# "started": merged, the class is a label whose members a published cell separates; unmerged, the
# not-started class is {0x0209} ALONE and a class of one separates nothing.
#
# (b) AND THE MERGE CHANGED THE ANSWER. Under the split a measured shape supports - the two
# TAKEN -> COMPLETED words against the four that did not complete - the only field of the COMMAND register
# that separates them is BIT 1, RESP_PRESENT (0,0 against 1,1,1,1). RESP_TYPE_SELECT's low bit, CRC_CHECK,
# CMD_INDEX and CMD_TYPE all fail, and the OPCODE fails too (3 is in both sets). 759 section 5 listed those
# fields and omitted RESP_PRESENT, because its merged split made 0x0102 and 0x030A one class, both
# RESP_TYPE = 10. **746's headline survives and is strengthened: RESP_PRESENT is the unique separating bit of
# the word across all six commands. 758's INDEX refutation is untouched.**
#
# (c) 756's GAP LIST, ENUMERATED RATHER THAN CITED, AND IT PASSES. Every register the vendor's power-up path
# writes at the ladder's clock: CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C (MCLK select <- DFLT, HC_SELECT_IN cleared,
# sdhci-msm.c:2496-2513, plus CLK_PWRSAVE :2415-2440) - IMPLEMENTED by rung 6; CORE_DLL_CONFIG 0x100
# |CORE_DLL_RST (:2578-2580) then |CORE_DLL_PDN (:2583-2585) - NOT touched (0 occurrences in
# src/entry/entry_storage.c); HOST_CONTROL2 0x3E (:2597) - NOT touched (0). Reachability checked, which 756
# asserted: sdhci.c:1784-1785 sits inside `if (host->version >= SDHCI_SPEC_300) {` (:1729) with NO timing
# test, and power-up is MMC_TIMING_LEGACY at host->clock 0 or 400 kHz - both <= CORE_FREQ_100MHZ (:164). And
# CORE_DLL_CONFIG has FIVE writers in the vendor tree with exactly ONE reachable before the first command:
# msm_config_cm_dll_phase (:392-423, tuning), msm_init_cm_dll (:571-687, ENABLES the DLL, execute_tuning
# only), sdhci_msm_toggle_cdr (:2216/:2220, called only from sdhci.c:1007-1011, the DATA path), and
# sdhci_msm_set_uhs_signaling (:2578-2585, every set_ios, and it DISABLES the DLL). **So the candidate set
# for "a write the vendor makes that the ladder does not" is exactly two writes, and both turn something OFF.**
#
# (d) THE CONTROLLER'S IDENTITY, SETTLED FROM THE DT: msm8974.dtsi:24 `sdhc1 = &sdhc_1; /* SDC1 eMMC slot */`,
# :500 `qcom,bus-width = <8>`, msm8974pro.dtsi:1765 `reg = <0xf9824900 0x1a0>` - and 0xf9824900 IS
# ST_HC_MEM_BASE. So xnu_live_storage_reg_card_present = 0x00000000 is the quirk sdhci-msm.c:2896 names
# (SDHCI_QUIRK_BROKEN_CARD_DETECTION) and NOT an empty socket; "the ladder has been probing an empty SD slot"
# is refuted.
#
# **NO ARM IS DESIGNED OR ARMED BY THIS STEP, AND out/ STILL HOLDS THE ARM 758's PRESS SPENT
# (`armed-storage-46fe6737`, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=20). No firer is armed and none is needed. Any further

# 762 (2026-09-27): THE RUNG-22 ARM IS BUILT AND PARKED - THE VENDOR'S DLL AND HOST_CONTROL2
# CENSUS, THREE READS AND NO STORE, AND THE FIRST ARM THIS LADDER HAS BUILT SINCE 747.
# **NO PRESS IS SPENT BY THIS BLOCK AND NO FIRER IS ARMED.**

#
# THE ARM: `armed-storage-c3007c37`, identified by the sha256 prefix of its own `xnu_arm_entry.bin`
# (`c3007c37891a4d38...`). Switch VALUE 21, which this record counts as its ORDINAL rung 22 - the two
# spellings are one apart (755 section 7) - `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=21`.
#
# WHAT IT ADDS: three reads in a new body `st_dll_census`, at the position rung 18's `st_resp_before`
# takes - immediately BEFORE `st_cmd_path()`, after rung 9's wait, i.e. at the one moment the vendor's
# own bring-up has finished and no command has ever been on this bus. Pre-registration is 756 section
# 6; the alternative space was closed by 761 section 5.
#
#   HOST_CONTROL2 0x3E   as a HALFWORD (`f982493e:ldrh`) - sdhci.h:79; the register
#                        `sdhci_msm_set_uhs_signaling` writes UNCONDITIONALLY at sdhci-msm.c:2597, and
#                        the only thing in this image that could have cleared its UHS field is rung 4's
#                        SDHCI_RESET_ALL.
#   CORE_DLL_CONFIG 0x100 as a WORD (`f9824a00:ldr`) - sdhci-msm.c:80. The vendor's
#                        `sdhci_msm_set_uhs_signaling` sets `CORE_DLL_RST` (1<<30, :2578) and
#                        `CORE_DLL_PDN` (1<<29, :2583) here at `host->clock <= CORE_FREQ_100MHZ`
#                        (`:2567`; 100 MHz, `:164`) with the vendor's own comment "the feedback clock
#                        must be provided and DLL must not be used so that tuning can be skipped".
#   CORE_DLL_STATUS 0x108 as a WORD (`f9824a08:ldr`) - sdhci-msm.c:89, whose bit 7 is CORE_DLL_LOCK.
#
# **THE WINDOW IS `hc_mem` AND NOT `core_mem`, AND THAT IS THE ONE ERROR THAT WOULD MAKE THESE READINGS
# MEAN SOMETHING ELSE.** The vendor reaches both registers as `host->ioaddr + CORE_DLL_CONFIG`, and
# `host->ioaddr` is `sdhci_pltfm_init`'s FIRST memory resource - `hc_mem` (`msm8974.dtsi:500`,
# `reg-names = "hc_mem", "core_mem"`). The DT settles it: `msm8974pro.dtsi:1765` widens `&sdhc_1` from
# the base node's `0x11c` to `0x1a0`, and `0x100`/`0x108` are beyond `0x11c` - the window was widened to
# cover exactly this pair. `core_mem + 0x100` is a different register file one window away, and both
# windows share their high half `0xf982`, so a reading taken there would publish under these names with
# every cell in the log agreeing with the record.
#
# **THE THREE-WAY, PRE-REGISTERED (756 section 6), AND EACH WAY NAMES THE NEXT ACT:**
#   `_dll_rst` AND `_dll_pdn` BOTH 1 -> the DLL is already reset and powered down by something that ran
#     before this image; **the 756 section 3 hypothesis is DEAD and nothing further is owed here**.
#   EITHER BIT 0 -> the block is running with an undischarged DLL at 400 kHz against the vendor's own
#     comment; **doing what the vendor does - set both bits - becomes the cheapest untried thing on
#     this path**, and that would be the NEXT rung (a store, and therefore its own pre-registration).
#   `_dll_ctrl2_uhs = 0` -> 756 section 4's spec argument holds and rung 4's RESET_ALL did reach
#     HOST_CONTROL2; nonzero -> the reset does NOT clear that field on this IP and the second half of
#     the vendor's write becomes live too.
#
# **SAFETY, AS A PROPERTY OF THE BYTES AND NOT A SENTENCE**: `build_entry.sh`'s clause for this rung
# asserts `st_dll_census`'s device store set is EMPTY and its three addresses are each accessed exactly
# once (`f982493e:ldrh=1 f9824a00:ldr=1 f9824a08:ldr=1`), that its image-side access set is empty, that
# it is called EXACTLY ONCE from `entry_storage_probe`, and that its call line precedes `st_cmd_path`'s.
# **The empty store set is the whole safety argument for reading above the command gate**, the same one
# 739 wrote for rung 18: 736's press cost a press because rung 15's store to `INT_ENABLE 0x34` sits
# above that gate and bit 15 rides with any write to that register and no write clears it, so the gate
# read `0x00008000` and NO COMMAND WENT ON THE BUS. **No new window and no new mapping**: all three
# addresses are in the megabyte `0xf98` this image already installs and reads, and 692's `addr >> 20`
# interlock is satisfied by construction.
#
# **AND THE RUNG-21 COMMAND IS STILL SENT, DELIBERATELY.** Rung 21's call site is guarded `>= 20` in
# `src/entry/entry_storage.c`, and this rung changes no guard on a rung that has already been built,
# armed and pressed. So a value-21 image carries BOTH the census and the `0x030A` CMD3: the log carries
# the three DLL readings taken BEFORE the first command beside a second sample of the stall. **That is
# recorded rather than edited away**, and it is why this rung is purely additive - no existing rung's
# guard, body or reading was moved to build it.
#
# **THE BUILD, AND WHAT IT COST**: `src/entry/build_entry.sh` refused the first two invocations on
# purpose - its own arm-change gate, which requires `STAGE90_ENTRY_ARM_CHANGE=1` and printed the switch
# set the parked arm needed, catching TWO switches this step had wrong (`STAGE90_XNU_ISTACK_SEPARATE`
# defaults to 1 and `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_TICKS` to 0; the pressed arm carries 0 and 115200000). The
# third invocation also refused, on this rung's own new clause - `st_dll_census` was INLINED because the
# body was declared plain `static`, and every other body this clause family asserts carries
# `__attribute__((noinline, noclone))` (m731: `noinline` alone does not stop GCC cloning). With both
# attributes the entry build exits 0 printing this rung's clause, and the payload build follows with
# `-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1`.
#
# WHAT WAS NOT DONE: **no press, no firer, no device action of any kind.** The previous arm
# (`armed-storage-46fe6737`, value 20) is still parked and its bytes are untouched; its park is what

# --allow-xnu-entry`, EXIT 0, 590 lines) and EXACTLY ONE runner (`scripts/run_and_capture.sh
# --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-c3007c37`), both with the flags readiness printed,
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` 5 of 5 / EXIT 0 beforehand. Capture archived BY HAND (the runner does
# NOT archive): `out/stage90/captures/rung22-dllcensus-20260927-080441-last_kmsg.txt`, 631,439 B, sha256
# `5ee96f58ee9b5082b399fc77f7113b61c2b79ccb64eb5b46860377eeaf882b1d`, with `-gate.log`, `-run.log` and
# `-press.log` companions.
#
# THE ANSWER - THE FIRST BRANCH OF 756 SECTION 6's PRE-REGISTERED THREE-WAY:
#   `_dll_config` = 0x60006400  ->  `_dll_rst` = 1 (bit 30 SET) and `_dll_pdn` = 1 (bit 29 SET), with
#                                    `_dll_en` = 0 (bit 16), `_dll_ckout` = 0 (bit 18), and the whole
#                                    tail below bit 16 reading 0x6400.
#   `_dll_status` = 0x00000000  ->  `_dll_lock` = 0 (bit 7), consistent with a reset, powered-down DLL.
#   `_dll_ctrl2`  = 0x00000000  ->  `_dll_ctrl2_uhs` = 0 - the SECOND half of 756 section 4 answered in
#                                    the same press: rung 4's SDHCI_RESET_ALL DID reach HOST_CONTROL2 and
#                                    the spec argument holds, so the vendor's ctrl_2 write at
#                                    sdhci-msm.c:2597 is NOT live as a missing act.
#   `_dll_loads` published 1,2,3 and `_dll_done` = 3 - A COMPLETE RUN of all three reads.
#
# **THE DLL IS ALREADY RESET AND ALREADY POWERED DOWN BEFORE THIS IMAGE'S FIRST INSTRUCTION**, so 756
# section 3's hypothesis - the block running with an undischarged DLL at 400 kHz against the vendor's own
# comment - IS DEAD, and **BOTH of the two writes 761 section 5 closed the candidate space at are closed
# at ZERO**. There is no "something the vendor disabled and the ladder left on" left to find on this
# path, and the pre-registration's own branch says NOTHING further is owed here. A negative result, bought
# with three reads, that removes a hypothesis completely rather than narrowing it.
#
# AND THE RUNG-21 COMMAND REPRODUCED ITS OWN STALL, CELL FOR CELL (the arm was purely additive by design:
# rung 21's call site is guarded `>= 20`, so a value-21 image sends 0x030A as well):
#   `_nidx_word` = `_nidx_word_read` = 0x0000030a;  `_nidx_inhibit_seen` = 0x00000400 (all 1024 samples);
#   `_nidx_inhibit_after` = 1, `_nidx_inhibit_last` = 0x01f80001;  `_nidx_complete` = 0;
#   `_nidx_status_any` = 0 over `_nidx_any_polls` = 0;  `_nidx_timeout` = 1;
#   `_nidx_ticks` = 0x015f95a7 (1.20002 s) against 758's 0x015f91be;  `_nidx_polls` = 0x004db800
#   (5,093,376) against 758's 0x004da000 (5,087,232);  `_nidx_ena_held` = 0x8001,
#   `_nidx_readback` = 0x8000, `_nidx_sig_enable` = 0.  TWO PRESSES, TWO ARMS, TWO DAYS, ONE STALL.
#
# **THE SAFETY CONTRACT HELD CELL BY CELL**: `_gate` = 1, `_gate_read` = 1, `_gated_out` = 0,
# `_loads` = 6, `_writes` published 0-4 (rung 21's own set, UNCHANGED - the census added NO store at all,
# which the build clause asserted before the press), `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` read and never written, no
# `_irq_other_*` key, nothing flashed. The ending is unmoved: `_post_end_calls` = 0x6,
# `_seam_sctlr` = 0x30c57879, `_seam_post_end_ticks` = 0x06ddd000, `_sleh_storm` = 9.
#
# WHAT REMAINS, STATED EXACTLY: every register the ladder has measured is now consistent with a
# CORRECTLY CONFIGURED controller - SDHCI mode (`_mode_bit_after` = 1), powered at 3.3 V (`_pwr_after` =
# 0x0b), clock on and stable (`_clk_set_cc_after` = 0xe045), DLL reset and down, HOST_CONTROL2 clean,
# card-detect the DT's own quirk (`_reg_card_present` = 0 on an 8-bit eMMC node). **The anomaly is no
# longer "something is configured wrong". It is that a controller in a correct state, given a
# response-demanding command, holds CMD_INHIBIT for a full 1.2 s and sets NO BIT OF `INT_STATUS` AT ALL -
# not the completion, and not the driver's own TIMEOUT.** A block whose response never arrives should
# raise `SDHCI_INT_TIMEOUT`; `_status_any` = 0 over 5,093,376 polls says it does not. Three candidates
# remain and NONE has been tested: (1) THE CARD IS NOT POWERED - the eMMC's VCC/VCCQ come from the PMIC
# and NO RUNG IN THIS LADDER HAS EVER TOUCHED THE PMIC, which is the only candidate explaining both
# halves; (2) the response timeout is not armed on this IP for these `RESP_TYPE` values; (3) the block is
# not actually issuing onto the bus. **Candidate 1 is the one the ladder has never had a cell for,
# because the PMIC is a different block. Naming it does not make it true and NO RUNG IS DESIGNED from
# this document.**
#
# **NO FIRER IS ARMED AND NO PRESS IS OWED.** `out/` still holds `armed-storage-c3007c37` - now PRESSED -
# and its park `out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-c3007c37` is intact (11 members). The press path is the
# two commands readiness prints, run by hand once each; no press may be spent without the operator's
# authorization.
#
# The goal is **STILL NOT MET**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount. The SDHCI storage driver does not
# exist - the block starts a response-demanding command and never completes one - so **TWRP-to-storage
# stays withheld.** What this press buys is that **the next reader does not spend a press on the DLL.**

# ================================================================================================
# 765 (2026-09-27): THE INTERRUPT-ENABLE CENSUS - THE VENDOR ENABLES ELEVEN STATUS BITS AND THE LADDER
# ENABLES ONE, SO "THE BLOCK SAID NOTHING" IS A MASKED READING. **NO ARM MOVED, NO PRESS SPENT, NO PRESS
# OWED, out/ UNTOUCHED.** Host-side only; the vendor tree read at line numbers, the ladder's own store
# census, and the rung-22 capture's cells.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE VENDOR'S SET: `sdhci_init` (sdhci.c:284-296), called once from `sdhci_add_host` at `:3589`, ends in
# `sdhci_clear_set_irqs(host, SDHCI_INT_ALL_MASK, <11 bits>)`, and `sdhci_clear_set_irqs` (sdhci.c:181-189)
# writes that ONE value to BOTH `INT_ENABLE 0x34` AND `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` as 32-bit words - so the
# error-enable halves at 0x36/0x3A are written too. `SDHCI_INT_ALL_MASK` is `(unsigned int)-1`
# (sdhci.h:152), so the clear is total, and the eleven bits are `0x01FF0003`, INCLUDING
# `SDHCI_INT_TIMEOUT 0x00010000` (sdhci.h:130), `SDHCI_INT_CRC 0x00020000`, `SDHCI_INT_END_BIT 0x00040000`
# and `SDHCI_INT_INDEX 0x00080000` - the four `SDHCI_INT_CMD_MASK` (sdhci.h:144-147) groups as THIS
# COMMAND'S OWN error set. `sdhci_msm_ops` (sdhci-msm.c:2653-2664) overrides neither `.irq` nor
# `.set_timeout` and never writes 0x34/0x38, so that call is the WHOLE of the vendor's enable programming.
#
# THE LADDER'S SET, ENUMERATED: every write to 0x34 in src/entry/entry_storage.c is `0`, `1`,
# `int_enable | RESPONSE`, or a restore of the value read back - **NEVER AN ERROR BIT** - and the write
# count to `SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` is **0**. Measured in the rung-22 capture: `_int_enable_before =
# 0x00008000`, `_int_enable_held = 0x00008001`, `_int_enable_readback = 0x00008000`, `_nidx_sig_enable = 0`
# - against the vendor's `0x01FF0003`.
#
# AND `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` IS A REGISTER NEITHER SIDE WRITES ON THIS PATH: the ladder has zero
# occurrences, and the vendor writes it only in `sdhci_prepare_data` (sdhci.c:827-828) inside
# `if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY))` - and every command this ladder has sent is data-less and
# none carries `RSP_BUSY`.
#
# SO `_status_any = 0` IS A MASKED READING, AND 726's SECOND REFUTATION DOES NOT HOLD AS STATED. 726
# refuted *wrong bit* with *"refuted by construction, `status_any` covers the whole register"* - and the
# poll DOES cover the whole register OF THE READ (st_read32 at entry_storage.c:2296, :2299, :2361, :2603,
# :2621, so 0x30-0x33 includes the error half where SDHCI_INT_TIMEOUT is bit 16). AND THE COVERAGE IS
# EXACT, which is what makes this worth stating carefully: the recorded cell is the RAW, UNMASKED word
# (`if (r->status_after != 0u && r->status_any == 0u) r->status_any = r->status_after;` at
# entry_storage.c:2362-2365), so `_status_any` is the first non-zero reading of EVERY bit of the
# register; the mask appears only in the poll's BREAK condition (:2389) and the pre-send staleness gate
# (:2300). So there is no masking defect in the EXTRACTOR - the register really was read whole five
# million times and really did read zero. **BUT 730 MEASURED ON
# THIS VERY BLOCK that a status bit appears only while its enable stands** - the completion was there all
# along and INT_ENABLE was the mask - with 733's rule and 738's *necessary and not sufficient* the same
# finding from two more presses. If the latch is enable-gated, a poll over a register whose error-enable
# half is zero cannot see an error bit however many samples it takes, and the ladder has NEVER ONCE set an
# error enable. The correct statement is narrower than 726's and stronger than "nothing was latched": no
# bit was ever seen in INT_STATUS, and its error half has had its enable at zero for every one of those
# samples - so the absence is a reading about the MASK as much as about the block, and it CANNOT
# DISTINGUISH "the controller had nothing to report" from "the controller's error reporting was switched
# off". `_status_any = 0` is cited in five documents as *the block said nothing*; it measures *no enabled
# bit was latched*. Plus an observation left OPEN: 0x34 reads 0x00008000 (bit 15, SDHCI_INT_ERROR) both
# before the ladder's first write and after a write of 0.
#
# AND 764 SECTION 4's FIRST CANDIDATE IS REFUTED BY THE VENDOR'S OWN DT. 764 named *"the card is not
# powered ... no rung has ever touched the PMIC"* as the most likely remaining candidate.
# `msm8974-mtp.dtsi:384-410` gives `&sdhc_1` `vdd-supply = <&pm8941_l20>`, `vdd-io-supply = <&pm8941_s3>`
# AND `qcom,vdd-always-on` / `qcom,vdd-io-always-on`; `sdhci_msm_dt_get_vreg_info` parses
# `"qcom,%s-always-on"` into `vreg->is_always_on` (sdhci-msm.c:1072-1074) and `sdhci_msm_vreg_disable`'s
# own comment is **"Never disable regulator marked as always_on"** (:1795-1796), the always-on branch only
# demoting it to LPM (:1813-1817). And `sdhci.c:3583` asks for `regulator_get(..., "vmmc")` - the con_id
# `vmmc`, where the DT supplies `vdd-supply` - so it takes IS_ERR, prints "no vmmc regulator found" and
# sets `vmmc = NULL`, making every `mmc_regulator_set_ocr` call (:1625-1626, :1667-1669, :1688-1689, all
# guarded by `host->vmmc &&`) a NO-OP. **The vendor does not turn the card's power on either.** **(780: THIS SENTENCE IS FALSE AS WRITTEN AND ITS REASON DOES NOT APPLY TO THIS BOARD. `host->vmmc` is `sdhci.c`'s OWN rail handle; `sdhci-msm.c` - the driver that binds `sdhc_1` - has its own vreg table from the same node's `vdd-supply`/`vdd-io-supply` and its own path ENABLES both rails: `probe:2826` -> `vreg_init:1873` -> `vreg_reset:1865` `setup_vreg(pdata,1,true)` -> `vreg_enable:1765` `regulator_enable` with NO `is_always_on` test. The early return this block also leans on is keyed on `vreg_data == NULL` (`:1833`), which `populate_pdata:1397` makes UNREACHABLE here - it allocates `vreg_data` unconditionally, before parsing, and both `-supply` phandles exist - and `qcom,vdd-always-on`'s ONLY behavioural effect is in the RELEASE path (`vreg_disable:1796` / `:1813`), where it demotes to LPM instead of disabling. So `always_on` means *enable it and never release it*, the opposite of *skip the regulator call*. THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES ON A STRONGER READING - the vendor's own bring-up is what leaves the rails enabled and the flag is what keeps them so - and 764 section 3's candidate 1 is thereby WEAKENED and still UNMEASURED. See 780.)** Withdrawn
# at the strength of the evidence: no board file here supplies `vmmc` to `sdhc_1` and the platform's own
# marks both rails always-on - which refutes "the ladder skipped a power act the vendor performs", not
# that the rails are up on the physical device (the Mi 4's own board dts is not in this tree).
#
# THE PRE-REGISTRATION FOR THE NEXT ARM (NO RUNG IS BUILT FROM THIS WITHOUT THE OPERATOR'S AUTHORIZATION):
# in the rung-21/22 window's position with the rung-21 command still sent, write FIVE BITS to `INT_ENABLE
# 0x34` - RESPONSE | TIMEOUT | CRC | END_BIT | INDEX - with **`SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38` HELD AT ZERO** so no
# line can rise and no delivery can end the run, then read `INT_STATUS 0x30` whole and `TIMEOUT_CONTROL
# 0x2E` as a byte, then undo both stores with both readbacks published. SAFE BECAUSE 730 MEASURED IT: that
# press wrote INT_ENABLE, took no command on the bus and took no interrupt (`_post_end_calls = 7`, no
# `_irq_other_*`). THE ANSWER SPACE, EACH BRANCH NAMING THE NEXT ACT:
#   0x00010000 (TIMEOUT) latched -> THE CARD DID NOT ANSWER AND THE CONTROLLER KNEW IT: the stall is the
#     CARD's silence and the whole rungs-13-22 reading is refuted in one bit; the subject becomes the bus
#     width, the CMD/DAT pads and the board's `qcom,pad-*` settings.
#   0x00020000 / 0x00080000 / 0x00040000 -> the card answered and the word was malformed; subject =
#     sampling and bus width.
#   NOTHING, WITH THE ENABLES UP -> the STRONG form of the negative: a command taken, started and held
#     1.2 s never armed a response timeout; subject = the block's own command path.
#   BIT 15 ONLY -> the sticky bit is a property of the IP and the observation closes with no news.
# The cells that ride along, at the width they are actually new: `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` read as a byte
# (GENUINELY NEW - never read by this ladder, and left at reset on the vendor's own path for every
# data-less command, so a non-zero value would be a finding), and PRESENT_STATE 0x24 BIT 24 - THE CMD
# LINE'S LEVEL - which is ONE NEW SAMPLE AND NOT A NEW CELL, corrected in the document in place: the bit IS
# already published through `_..._inhibit_last`, the whole PRESENT_STATE word at the poll's last sample
# (`_nidx_inhibit_last = 0x01f80001` has bit 24 SET, as do 758's and 742's `0x01f80001` and 730's
# `0x01f80000` - the line HIGH at the end on all of them). What no rung has is bit 24 at any moment other
# than "last" - the question 749 section 3's table asks is whether the line ever goes LOW during a
# response-demanding word - so the new cell is the COUNT OF SAMPLES IN WHICH BIT 24 WAS LOW over the first
# 1024, beside the bit-0 count `inhibit_seen` already keeps.
#
# New instance m773: COVERAGE OF A READ READ AS ENABLEMENT OF A LATCH - a read's WIDTH offered as proof
# that an absence is real; the test is to name the enable, the mask and the mode a bit needs to appear.
#
# **NO ARM MOVED, NO PRESS SPENT, NO PRESS OWED.** `out/` still holds the PRESSED rung-22 arm
# `armed-storage-c3007c37`; its park is intact (11 members). The press path is the two commands readiness
# prints, run by hand once each. The goal is **STILL NOT MET**: no storage, no filesystem, no mount, and
# **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.** What this step buys is that the next press is spent on a question the
# ladder has never asked, instead of on a fourth sample of a masked one.


# ================================================================================================
# 766 (2026-09-27): **THE RUNG-23 ARM - THE WINDOW S ENABLE WORD WIDENED FROM ONE BIT TO THE FIVE
# 765 section 4 PRE-REGISTERED, BUILT AND PARKED. NO PRESS SPENT, NO PRESS OWED, NO FIRER ARMED.**
# ================================================================================================
#
# WHY THIS RUNG EXISTS. 730 pressed the rule on THIS block - a status bit is latched only while its
# enable stands (the completion was there all along and INT_ENABLE was the mask) - and 765 section 1
# measured the distance: the vendor own sdhci_init (sdhci.c:284-296, called once from sdhci_add_host
# at :3589) writes ELEVEN status bits, 0x01FF0003, into INT_ENABLE 0x34 AND SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38, while
# this ladder has enabled exactly ONE bit in its whole history. So the ladder headline negative -
# _nidx_status_any = 0 over 5,093,376 polls at rung 22 - is a statement about a MASK as much as about
# the block, and 765 section 2 is its narrowest true form. 765 section 3 then REFUTED 764 section 4
# candidate 1 from the vendor own DT (msm8974-mtp.dtsi:384-410 marks both eMMC rails
# qcom,vdd-always-on, and sdhci.c:3583 asks for con_id vmmc which the DT does not supply, so
# host->vmmc = NULL and every mmc_regulator_set_ocr call is a no-op). The press that separates
# the two readings is this rung.
#
# WHAT IT CHANGES, AND IT IS ONE CONSTANT: st_cmd3_noidx writes ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE_CMD =
# 0x000F0001 = RESPONSE | TIMEOUT | CRC | END_BIT | INDEX into INT_ENABLE 0x34 in place of the lone
# SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE, at the SAME call site, in the SAME position, with the SAME opcode (3), the
# SAME argument (0x00010000) and the SAME flag word (0x030A). FIVE bits and not the vendor eleven:
# AUTO_CMD_ERR (0x01000000) and the DATA_*/BUS_POWER half (0x00F00000) are excluded because neither
# is a fact about the card, and the exclusions are carried by SIX _Static_asserts in
# src/entry/entry_storage.c because NO BUILD CLAUSE CAN READ A VALUE - a body memory accesses and
# its bl sites carry no immediate.
#
# AND TWO NEW READS, BOTH 765 section 4 PRE-REGISTERED: _nidx_status_pre is INT_STATUS 0x30 read
# BETWEEN the enable store and its readback, with the wider mask in place and no command of this
# arm on the bus; _nidx_tout_ctl is TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E read as a BYTE after the restore, the
# register no rung has ever read (the vendor writes it only from sdhci_prepare_data, sdhci.c:827-828,
# under if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY)) - so a NON-ZERO value here would be a finding, and
# 0x00 is the field LONGEST setting rather than the absence of a timeout).
#
# THE PRIMARY ANSWER NEEDS NO NEW MACHINERY. st_send_command poll publishes the RAW, UNMASKED
# INT_STATUS word it first saw non-zero in (entry_storage.c:2362-2365), over 5.09 million readings.
# The same poll that has always read zero is the poll that will read 0x00010000 if the card is
# silent and the block knows it. THE FOUR-ROW ANSWER SPACE, each row naming the next act:
#   0x00010000 (TIMEOUT) latched -> THE CARD DID NOT ANSWER AND THE CONTROLLER KNEW IT. One bit
#     refutes the whole rungs-13-22 reading; the subject becomes the card and the bus - bus width,
#     the CMD/DAT pads, the clock at the card pins, the board file qcom,pad-* settings.
#   0x00020000 / 0x00040000 / 0x00080000 (CRC / END_BIT / INDEX) -> the card ANSWERED and the word
#     was malformed; subject = sampling and bus width.
#   NOTHING, WITH THE ENABLES UP -> the STRONG form of the negative: a command taken, started and
#     held 1.2 s that never armed its own response timeout; subject = the block own command path,
#     TIMEOUT_CONTROL value, CLOCK_CONTROL divider at the moment of the command, and whether
#     PRESENT_STATE bit 24 ever moves for a response-demanding word.
#   BIT 15 ONLY, or the same 0x00008000-shaped value as before -> the sticky bit is a property of
#     the IP and not a report.
#
# SAFETY, AS A PROPERTY OF THE BYTES AND NOT A SENTENCE. The line rises on the CONJUNCTION of
# INT_ENABLE and SIGNAL_ENABLE; SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is READ AND NEVER WRITTEN at this rung, and 730
# press wrote INT_ENABLE on this block, took no command and took no interrupt (_post_end_calls = 7,
# no _irq_other_* key). Rungs 17, 19, 20, 21 and 22 have each written that register with the signal
# enable at zero and none took a delivery. build_entry.sh holds this body store set to EXACTLY
# [f9824934:str] - TWO stores, both to INT_ENABLE 0x34, and nothing else anywhere.
#
# THE BUILD. The first invocation refused on STAGE90_ENTRY_TRACE (the pressed arm carries 1); the
# second refused on STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_POC; the third FAILED the compiler on one of the six new
# _Static_asserts - (ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE_CMD & ST_SDHCI_INT_CMD_ERR) == ST_SDHCI_INT_CMD_ERR, which
# is FALSE by construction because ST_SDHCI_INT_CMD_ERR is 0x010F0000 and CARRIES AUTO_CMD_ERR that
# this window excludes; the literal 0x000F0000 replaced it. The fourth FAILED the clause this step
# had just made value-dependent, on NODECL-... for nine of ten accesses: the declared-address list
# had been turned into a variable and passed UNQUOTED, and classify_body takes its second POSITIONAL
# argument alone - so declset held one address and every other access was refused. `bash -n` cannot
# see it; the clause the step was writing is what caught it (m774). With the argument quoted the
# entry build exits 0 printing xnu_entry_766 and the payload build follows with
# STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS=-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1 (NOT STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1 - the earlier bare form left the
# config at 0u and would have produced an image that never jumps into XNU).
#
# AND THIS IS THE STEP THAT PAID 763 section 5 SITE: src/entry/build_entry.sh comment on 408. It had
# said a payload rebuild does not reproduce; 408 48 bytes are kernel_size 6,015,356 WITH
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1 against 6,015,308 without it - a SWITCH and not a nondeterminism - and the
# correct reason is that the switch DEFAULT is OFF, so a careless ./build.sh makes an image that
# never jumps into XNU and overwrites the armed bytes in out/.
#
# **NO PRESS, NO FIRER, NO DEVICE ACTION OF ANY KIND.** out/ now holds THIS arm, ARMED AND NOT
# PRESSED; the previously pressed arm armed-storage-c3007c37 is still parked and intact. Spending
# this press is the operator decision.

# 767: THE RUNG-24 ARM - `armed-storage-94de2ede`, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=23.
# Built 2026-09-27 host-side only: NO device action, NO press, NO gate against a
# device, NO runner. It is 766's rung-23 window PLUS ONE NEW CELL, and the cell
# costs no device access at all: the poll that already reads PRESENT_STATE 0x24
# once per iteration into `inhibit_last` now also counts the samples in which bit
# 24 (the CMD line's own LEVEL) is LOW. `_nidx_cmdlow_seen = 0` over the same
# first 1024 samples `_nidx_inhibit_seen` counts is the reading that separates
# 764 section 4's candidate 3 (the block never drove the bus) from candidate 1
# (the block drove and the card stayed silent) - the one question no arm in this
# ladder has ever been able to ask, because `_nidx_inhibit_last` is the LAST
# sample and an idle line and a finished transmission both read HIGH.
#
# **`out/` NOW HOLDS THIS ARM, AND `armed-storage-3a92aa52` (rung 23) IS NO
# LONGER IN `out/`** - its park is intact and unchanged (11 members, every one
# cmp-identical to what 766 parked), so rung 23 can still be re-armed with
# `tools/verify_revert_set.sh out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-3a92aa52 --set=armed-storage-3a92aa52`.
# NO PRESS IS SPENT BY THIS STEP AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# What did NOT move, measured on the new artifacts rather than asserted:
# `entry_epilogue` is still 0x80004948 and STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR is still
# 0x800492dc (the build's own seam clause, exit 0); `xnu_arm_entry.bin` is
# 5552764 bytes, unchanged, the growth having fitted the padding; and the
# payload switch set is BYTE-IDENTICAL to rung 23's (6c2b6038...), which is the
# trap 763 section 5 and 766 section 6 paid for.
#
# `st_send_command`'s device set is UNCHANGED BY THIS RUNG, and that was
# MEASURED rather than argued: the classifier reports
# `f9824924:ldr f9824930:ldr f9824930:str f9824908:str f982490e:strh
#  f982490e:ldrh f9824910:ldr` and an image-side set of exactly
# `UNK:ldr UNK:str` - the same pair rung 11's clause names. So the census added
# no register, no width and no new symbol. **A relaxation of that clause was
# drafted, and REMOVED after this measurement showed it was not needed**: it
# would have weakened a load-bearing check for a reason that was not true.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 768: THE RUNG-24 PRESS - `armed-storage-94de2ede` WAS PRESSED AND IT ANSWERED.
# ONE PRESS SPENT, WITH THE OPERATOR'S AUTHORIZATION, 2026-09-27 09:05:22-09:06:36
# UTC (74 s), EXIT 0, returned and captured. One `fastboot boot` only, never
# flash; neighbour `33e80afe` absent from BOTH `adb devices` and `fastboot devices`
# at fire time (`4a2fe00b` present as `device`); exactly one gate
# (`--allow-xnu-entry`, exit 0, 600 lines) and exactly one runner
# (`--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-94de2ede`), both with the flags
# readiness printed; readiness 5 of 5 beforehand. Capture archived by hand
# (the runner does not archive) as
# `out/stage90/captures/rung24-cmdline-20260927-090636-last_kmsg.txt`, 631,581 B,
# sha256 053818944ec8009f55e1ebeded90c82b45cdaa24861e2fe6e9d4013d130c506e, with
# its `-gate.log` and `-run.log` companions.
#
# **THE ANSWER IS ROW 1 OF THE PRE-REGISTERED FOUR-ROW TABLE.** `_nidx_status_any
# = 0x00018000` = `SDHCI_INT_ERROR` (bit 15) + `SDHCI_INT_TIMEOUT` (bit 16),
# `_nidx_err = 0x00010000`, `_nidx_complete = 0`, `_nidx_timeout = 0` (a break at
# poll 1789, not the 1.2 s budget), `_nidx_ticks = 0x31e3` = 12,771 ticks =
# **665.2 us** - THE CARD DID NOT ANSWER AND THE CONTROLLER KNEW IT.
#
# **AND THE 665 us IS THE TIMEOUT'S OWN SIGNATURE**: `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` read
# 0, value 0 = 2^13 TMCLK cycles, and 8192 / 665.2 us = 12.31 MHz against this
# platform's 12.288 MHz - **666.7 us predicted, 665.2 us measured**.
#
# **AND THE CMD LINE MOVED**: `_nidx_cmdlow_seen = 0x187` = 391 of the first 1024
# samples, so 764 section 4's candidate 3 (the block never put anything on the
# bus) is RETIRED; candidate 2 (the response timeout not armed) is retired by the
# 665 us itself.
#
# **THE LARGER RESULT IS IN THE SAME LOG FROM RUNG 11**: `_cmd0_complete = 1` and
# `_cmd0_status_any = 0x00000001` (`SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE`) at poll 537 in 255.4 us.
# A no-response command completes and says so; a response-demanding one is
# issued, drives the line, and arms and FIRES its own timeout. **THE RUNGS-13-22
# HEADLINE - "this controller runs a command and never sets its status" - IS
# FALSE**, and it was false because the error-enable half of `INT_ENABLE 0x34`
# was zero. 765's mask finding is confirmed one cell deeper:
# `_nidx_status_pre = 0x00018000` (INT_STATUS read between the enable store and
# its readback) shows a timeout summary LATCHED AND INVISIBLE since an earlier
# command. Two producers - named as m739's shape - and the poll settles the fresh
# latch independently: `_nidx_clear_after = 0`, then a break at poll 1789.
#
# Safety, cell by cell: `_nidx_sig_enable = 0` (SIGNAL_ENABLE never written) and
# NO `_irq_other_*` key anywhere in the log - no delivery reached the dispatcher;
# `_nidx_ena_held = 0x000f8001`, `_nidx_readback = 0x00008000`; all
# `persistent_write_attempted` and `failure_mask` zero; ending unmoved
# (`_post_end_calls = 6`, `_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `_sleh_storm = 9`).
#
# **WHAT IS LEFT.** The controller is exonerated. The candidate space is the
# card's power and the clock at its pins - the two things a register read of the
# controller cannot see. Host-side, three obvious suspicions are CLOSED: the
# 1.8 V power byte is correct (`_pwr_wrote = 0x0b` = `SDHCI_POWER_180|ON`, from
# CAPABILITIES 0x742dc8b2's VDD_180-only, and the board's
# `qcom,vdd-io-voltage-level = <1800000 1800000>` agrees); the `qcom,pad-*`
# settings are drive strength and pull and NOT a function mux; and HOST_CONTROL's
# bus width cannot matter for data-less commands.
#
# **m776 - a field's DIRECTION stated backwards in a live instruction.**
# `TIMEOUT_CONTROL`'s `0x00` was called the field's LONGEST setting in 765
# section 4 and carried into 766 and into BOTH the rung-23 and rung-24 readiness
# narrations. It is the SHORTEST (value v selects 2^(13+v) TMCLK cycles), and
# this press measured it. The correction in `src/entry/entry_storage.c` is OWED,
# not paid: that file is one of the entry image's 23 recorded sources, so editing
# its `#error` text makes the gate refuse the arm this press just spent (763
# section 5's measured cost). It is corrected in the experiment document and in
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`, which is not an entry source.
#
# **RUNG 23's PARK IS NOW SUPERSEDED AND UNSPENT.** `armed-storage-3a92aa52`'s
# question is answered by this press, and its park stays intact and verified.
# NO PRESS IS OWED AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED; `out/` still holds this arm, pressed.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 769: THE SDC1 PADS - HOST-SIDE ONLY. NO DEVICE ACTION, NO PRESS, NO ARM BUILT,
# NO GATE AGAINST A DEVICE, NO RUNNER, NO FIRER. `out/` is untouched: the live
# arm is still 768's spent `armed-storage-94de2ede`, and rung 23's
# `armed-storage-3a92aa52` remains a superseded, unspent park. Nothing in this
# block was measured on hardware; every claim carries its `file:line`.
#
# **768 SECTION 5's *FUNCTION MUX* IS RETIRED BY CONSTRUCTION.** MSM8974 routes
# SDC1 through DEDICATED PADS, not GPIOs: `sdhci-msm.c:267-270` documents the
# distinction and `sdhci-msm.c:1266` picks the pad branch because `sdhc_1` has no
# `gpios` property; `sdhci_msm_setup_pad` (`sdhci-msm.c:964-988`) calls only
# `msm_tlmm_set_hdrive`/`msm_tlmm_set_pull`, both landing in `MSM_TLMM_BASE`
# (phys 0xFD510000, `msm_iomap-8974.h:34-35`) at `SDC1_HDRV_PULL_CTL = 0x2044`
# -> **0xFD512044**. Every field of that register is drive strength or pull
# (`gpio-msm-common.c:59-88`); there is no function-select field to write. The
# function-select writers that exist in this tree are applied only to the
# GPIO-routed SDC3/SDC4.
#
# **THE VENDOR WRITES THE PADS FROM INSIDE THE POWER ISR.** `sdhci_msm_pwr_irq`'s
# `CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON` branch (`sdhci-msm.c:2015-2032`) does
# `sdhci_msm_setup_vreg` -> `sdhci_msm_setup_pins` -> `sdhci_msm_set_vdd_io_vol`.
# Rung 7 wrote the power byte and rung 8 registered the handler, but the ladder's
# handler acks the latch rather than performing the vendor's BUS_ON work, so
# **the pads' drive and pull have been attempted by no rung.**
#
# **THE PRE-REGISTERED EXPECTED VALUE.** `msm8974pro-ac-pm8941-mtp-v5.dts:25-29`
# gives `&sdhc_1` `qcom,pad-pull-on = <0x0 0x3 0x3 0x1>` and
# `qcom,pad-drv-on = <0x4 0x4 0x4>`; through `msm_tlmm_set_field`'s
# `reg &= ~(mask<<off); reg |= (val&mask)<<off;` (`gpio-msm-common.c:481-496`)
# that evaluates field by field to **0x00009F24**. The field that is the
# mechanism is `PULL SDC1_CMD = pull-up`: MMC's CMD line is OPEN-DRAIN during
# identification and returns HIGH only through this pull, which on this SoC is
# the pad register rather than a discrete resistor. A board left at reset reads
# 0x00000000 - no pull-up on CMD, no pull-up on DATA.
#
# **NEXT ARM PRE-REGISTERED, NOT BUILT.** Install the 0xFD5 megabyte through
# `entry_mmio_section` (693's design; 0xFD512044 >> 20 = 0xFD5 and no other
# device lives there, so 692's `addr >> 20` interlock is satisfied by
# construction), then ONE 32-bit read, ZERO stores, published raw as `_pad_raw`
# beside seven field decodes plus `_pad_map` and `_pad_writes = 0`.
# ROW 1: `0x00009F24` -> the pads are configured, 768 section 5's other half is
# retired, and the subject moves to the PMIC rails (`sdhci_msm_setup_vreg`, a bus
# the ladder cannot reach from `hc_mem`).
# ROW 2: `0x00000000`, or any field at odds with the DT -> the vendor act was
# never applied on this path, and the next rung is a single read-modify-write of
# one register whose section is already installed.
#
# **THREE CANDIDATES RETIRED AND ONE CONTRADICTION FOUND IN 768.**
# (i) `HOST_CONTROL2 0x3E` bit 3 `VDD_180` = 0 is NOT a defect:
# `sdhci_msm_set_uhs_signaling` read-modify-writes only `UHS_MASK`
# (`sdhci-msm.c:2544-2546`, `:2597`), and the only writer of `VDD_180` is
# `sdhci_do_start_signal_voltage_switch` (`sdhci.c:2015`), called on timing
# switches and not at 400 kHz init. The vendor's own HOST_CONTROL2 is 0 at the
# first command too.
# (ii) 768 section 1's mechanism attribution is a coincidence read as causation:
# `SDHCI_INT_TIMEOUT` (`sdhci.h:130`) is the COMMAND timeout, and `sdhci.c`
# writes `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` only at `:115` (a dump) and `:828` (inside
# `if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY))` at `:826`). No ladder command
# carries either, so the register is at its RESET value and *unset* and *set to
# longest* are indistinguishable when the field is 0. The 665.2 us against
# 2^13 @ 12.288 MHz = 666.7 us is still a real reading - and a positive
# measurement that TMCLK runs - but the attribution is not.
# (iii) 768 section 3's "the RESPONSE register was read and never changed" is
# refuted by the arm's own cells: `_nidx_resp_pre = 0` -> `_nidx_resp` =
# `_nidx_resp_post` = 0x40ff8080 with `_nidx_resp_moved = 1`, and the pre-words
# at +12/+8 (0x0040ff80 / 0x80000000) went to zero. All three reads are
# `ST_SDHCI_RESPONSE 0x10`, so the change is about ONE register.
#
# **AND THE CONTRADICTION IS SETTLED HOST-SIDE, WITH NO PRESS.** Rung 20's
# archived capture carries a CMD3 with `_nrsp_flags = 0x00000000`
# (`MMC_RSP_NONE`) and `_nrsp_resp_moved = 1` (0x40ff8080 -> 0): **a command that
# demanded nothing moves the register.** So the movement is the block's own and
# **RESPONSE is retired as a cell about the card in BOTH directions** - rung 19
# 0x40ff8080->0, rung 20 0x40ff8080->0, rung 24 0->0x40ff8080, the direction
# tracking the field's prior content and not what the command asked for.
#
# **A CENSUS CLAIM RECORDED AS WRONG SO IT IS NOT CARRIED.** A reading of the
# vendor bring-up pass produced "the vendor writes 0x29 = 0x0E | 0x01 = 0x0F, VDD
# 3.3 V". It is false: `sdhci_set_power` switches on `1 << power` where `power =
# ios->vdd` is a BIT INDEX (`sdhci.c:1312-1318`); `ocr_avail = MMC_VDD_165_195`
# because `CAPABILITIES 0x742dc8b2` has CAN_VDD_330/CAN_VDD_300 clear and
# CAN_VDD_180 set (`sdhci.h:195-197`; `sdhci.c:3434/3447/3460`); `fls(ocr_avail)
# - 1 = 7` -> `SDHCI_POWER_180 = 0x0A` -> **0x0B**, which is what the ladder
# writes. The wrong claim came from reading the storage assignment
# `sdhci.c:1339 host->pwr = pwr` without evaluating the switch above it - m737's
# shape: a claim about a REGISTER that is really a claim about a MOMENT.
#
# **NO PRESS SPENT, NO ARM BUILT, NO PRESS OWED AND NONE AUTHORIZED.** The goal
# is still NOT met - no storage, no filesystem, no mount - and TWRP-to-storage
# stays withheld.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 770: THE RUNG-25 ARM - `armed-storage-f72e9f18`, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=24.
# Built 2026-09-27 host-side only: NO device action, NO press, NO gate against a
# device, NO runner, NO firer. It is 769 section 6's pre-registration made real,
# and it is the smallest arm this ladder has ever carried: **ONE READ, ONE NEW
# MEGABYTE, ZERO STORES.**
#
# WHAT IT IS. `st_pad_census` installs the 0xFD5 megabyte through
# `entry_mmio_section` (693's design; 692's press is why the install comes
# before the dereference - an uninstalled megabyte faults with fsr = 0x5), then
# takes ONE 32-bit read of `0xFD512044` - `TLMM + 0x2044`, the SDC1
# dedicated-pad drive/pull register, in a SECOND device's megabyte that no rung
# in this ladder has ever read or written. It publishes the word raw as
# `xnu_live_storage_pad_raw` beside seven field decodes (clk/cmd/data drive at
# bits 6/3/0, clk/cmd/data/rclk pull at 13/11/9/15), the derived expectation
# `xnu_live_storage_pad_expect = 0x00009F24`, a one-bit `_pad_match`, the
# `entry_mmio_section` outcome (`_pad_map`, `_pad_slot_before`, `_pad_desc`) and
# **`xnu_live_storage_pad_writes = 0`, a count that never leaves zero.**
#
# THE EXPECTATION IS DERIVED AND NOT TYPED. `qcom,pad-pull-on = <0x0 0x3 0x3 0x1>`
# and `qcom,pad-drv-on = <0x4 0x4 0x4>` (`msm8974pro-ac-pm8941-mtp-v5.dts:25-29`)
# through `msm_tlmm_set_field`'s shift-and-mask (hdrive width 3, pull width 2,
# `gpio-msm-common.c:481-496`) evaluate to 0x00009F24, and six `_Static_assert`s
# in `entry_storage.c` fix the address (== 0xfd512044), its megabyte
# (`>> 20 == 0xFD5`), its alignment, the seven masks' disjointness (their sum
# equals their OR) and that the expectation ANDs with the CMD-pull mask - so the
# value the arm compares against is computed from the DT arrays on every build
# rather than pasted from this paragraph.
#
# THE TWO-ROW ANSWER, PRE-REGISTERED IN 769 SECTION 6 AND UNCHANGED:
# ROW 1: `_pad_raw = 0x00009F24` -> THE PADS ARE CONFIGURED. 768 section 5's
#        other half is retired, `PULL SDC1_CMD` is present, and the subject moves
#        to the PMIC rails - `sdhci_msm_setup_vreg` (`sdhci-msm.c:2019`), a bus
#        the ladder cannot reach from `hc_mem`.
# ROW 2: `_pad_raw = 0x00000000`, or any field at odds with the DT -> THE VENDOR
#        ACT WAS NEVER APPLIED ON THIS PATH: no pull-up on CMD, no pull-up on
#        DATA, drive strength at minimum. The next rung is then a single
#        read-modify-write of ONE register in a megabyte whose section this arm
#        has already installed.
# ONE CAVEAT THE CELL CANNOT CARRY, recorded with it: the read is taken at the
# arm's own moment, which is BEFORE the command path, so row 1 is a reading about
# the pads' STATE and not a proof that this image applied them - the boot chain
# may have left exactly the same values. Only row 2 carries an act.
#
# **THE SEAM CONSTANT MOVED A FOURTH TIME AND THE BUILD REFUSED IT.**
# `st_pad_census` and its call site grew `entry_storage.o` past the entry group's
# remaining alignment slack, so the whole kernel text run moved by one page and
# the exit's `bl FlushPoU_Dcache` is now at **0x8004a2d8** returning to
# **0x8004a2dc** (`0x800492d8` -> `0x800492dc` on 724's arm, which is the value
# every arm from 724 to 768 carried). The build refused with its own clause -
# `the exit's call to FlushPoU_Dcache is at 2147787480 and returns to 2147787484,
# while entry_trace.c's STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR is 0x800492dc` - and after the refusal
# BOTH copies of the address were moved: `entry_trace.c:2688`'s
# `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` and `scripts/run_and_capture.sh:315`'s
# `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL`, which `preflight_boot_check.sh` compares against the live
# ELF ([[mi4-one-value-two-definitions]]). **The mechanism is the one 696, 708 and
# 724 measured: the move is a page, and it is a property of the entry group's SIZE
# and not of what a rung does.** `platform_cache_idle_enter` is now
# 0x8004a238..0x8004a2d4 and the entry's window is [0x8004a240, 0x8004a33c).
#
# **A REFUSED ENTRY BUILD LEAVES `out/` IN A STATE WORTH NAMING.** The entry
# build writes `xnu_arm_entry.bin` BEFORE its clause family runs, so the refused
# rung-25 build left a new entry bin (`594d539ae41f28da…`) sitting beside the
# rung-24 arm's own config record (`94de2ede…`, 08:54) - a pair the record does
# not describe and the gate's blob clause refuses. The live ARM was never in
# doubt (`stage90-qcdt.img` was untouched, still 94de2ede's), but **the lesson is
# that a refused entry build must be finished or re-armed from a park before any
# press**: `out/` is not left as it was.
#
# WHAT DID NOT MOVE, MEASURED ON THE NEW ARTIFACTS RATHER THAN ASSERTED:
# `xnu_arm_entry.bin` is 5552764 bytes, unchanged - the growth fitted the padding
# while the page boundary inside it was still crossed. The payload's switch set is
# BYTE-IDENTICAL to rung 24's and rung 23's (`6c2b6038...`, `stage90-build-config.txt`,
# 682 bytes, with `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1` at line 5), which is the trap 763 section 5
# paid for and `tools/check_payload_config_entry.sh` now refuses. The build's own
# rung-25 clause reports the body's device set as `[fd512044:ldr ]` with counts
# `[fd512044:ldr=1 ]` and an EMPTY image side, in program order, called once from
# `entry_storage_probe` before `st_cmd_path` - so the arm's safety reading is the
# build's reading and not this paragraph's.
#
# **`out/` NOW HOLDS THIS ARM, ARMED AND NOT PRESSED.** Rung 24's

# 771 - THE FOUR COMMANDS READ AS ONE TABLE, AND TWO LIVE EXPLANATIONS REFUTED. NO ARM IS BUILT BY THIS
# STEP AND NOTHING IN `out/` MOVED: the live arm is still 770's armed-storage-f72e9f18
# (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=24), ARMED AND NOT PRESSED, and rung 23's armed-storage-3a92aa52 stays a
# superseded, unspent park. This block exists because the step CHANGED THE GATE and the change has to be
# findable from the record rather than from a commit subject:
#
#   tools/verify_press_ready.sh - the rung-24 narration. Its sentence **THE RUNGS-13-22 HEADLINE IS
#   FALSE**: ... *it did not, because the error-enable half of `INT_ENABLE 0x34` was zero* is WITHDRAWN,
#   because the SAME capture the sentence cites carries the refutation: `INT_ENABLE` was `0x00000001` -
#   bit for bit the same word, written by two different windows - when CMD0 was issued (`_ena_wrote`) and
#   when CMD2 was (`_cid_ena_wrote`), and CMD0 completed and latched `INT_RESPONSE` while CMD2 latched
#   NOTHING across `_cid_polls` = 5,088,256 polls. The enable word separates those two rows in NEITHER
#   direction, so it is not the reason and the sentence now says so. `_nidx_status_pre = 0x00018000` is
#   still a real reading and is NOT withdrawn: what is withdrawn is the attribution, because
#   `_cid_status_post = 0` was read immediately before it and the only write between the two is the enable
#   store itself - so whether that word arrived during CMD2 or is the store's own effect on this block is
#   UNSEPARATED, and 771 names the two reads that would separate it. The frontier sentence is also
#   NARROWED from the same log: the CLOCK is not a candidate (`clk_set_max_clk = 0x16E36000` = 384 MHz
#   against `clk_set_real_div` = 0x3C0 = 960 is 400,000 Hz EXACTLY, and `cmd2_clock_control = 0x0000E047`
#   carries `SD_CLOCK_CARD_EN`), so the remaining subject is the PADS AND THE RAILS and not the clock
#   register.
#
#   docs/experiments/experiment-771-the-command-table-read-as-one-table.md - the reading itself, with the
#   four-row table (CMD0 / CMD1 / CMD2 / CMD3 against word, response demand, the enable word written before
#   it, `inhibit_after`, `inhibit_seen`, `word_read`, `status_any`, polls, ticks, the poll's own end and its
#   outcome) and the second refutation: 737's window rationale at entry_storage.c:3024-3031 is refuted AS
#   A PREDICTION, because CMD2 was sent inside that window and `_cid_status_any = 0`, `_cid_complete = 0`,
#   `_cid_err = 0` - `_cid_inhibit_seen = 0` says why, the block never raised `CMD_INHIBIT` so there was no
#   completion for any enable to deliver. The window is NOT removed (a pressed arm's evidence); the
#   sentence that says what it BUYS is corrected, and so is the same paragraph's `_cmd1_resp` reading,
#   which 769 section 5.3/7 already retired in both directions.
#
#   WHAT THE TABLE DOES SUPPORT IS ONE CELL: `CMD_INHIBIT`. `_inhibit_seen` is 536 / 0 / 0 / 1024 for
#   CMD0 / CMD1 / CMD2 / CMD3 against completed / nothing / nothing / TIMEOUT at 665.2 us, and
#   `inhibit_after` - the same bit one instruction after the store - agrees on the three rows that publish
#   it (1 / 0 / not published / 1). THE MISSING CELL IS MEASURED AND THROWN AWAY: `cid_inhibit_after` is
#   filled into the result struct by every command and printed by no publish block, so it is absent from
#   EVERY capture in the archive - and publishing it costs NO DEVICE ACCESS AT ALL.
#
#   TWO READS PRE-REGISTERED HERE AND NOT BUILT (a rung carrying them is host-side work; spending it is
#   the operator's decision, and rung 25's pad read is ahead of them): one `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` read AFTER
#   the poll loop ends (outside it, so the poll's own traffic is unchanged) separates *the block held the
#   command and dropped it* from *it never loaded it*; and one `INT_STATUS 0x30` read immediately BEFORE
#   and one immediately AFTER the enable store separates the two surviving readings of `0x00018000`.
#
#   TWO NUMBERS IN THE SAME 737 SENTENCE - AND 771's CORRECTION OF THEM IS ITSELF WRONG IN BOTH HALVES
#   (778). The sentence writes `_cmd1_polls` as `0x004db000` with the decimal 5,088,000 - quoted here
#   SPLIT so that this note is not itself a false equality claim, which is the one thing the new check in
#   `make check` cannot tell from a defect - and it is about the press whose
#   capture carries a `_cmd1_polls` cell: rung 15's and rung 20's both read `0x004DB000`, whose decimal is
#   5,091,328 - so the DECIMAL is the only thing wrong there and the hexadecimal was never mis-attributed.
#   771 section 5 called the hexadecimal "CMD2's `cid_polls`" and gave its own value for it as `0x004DB000`
#   = 5,088,256; both are false, and it got there by comparing a sentence about one press against rung 24's
#   capture, where `0x004DB000` IS CMD2's count. The two counts SWAP between presses (rung 20 CMD1
#   `0x4DB000` / CMD2 `0x4DAC00`; rung 24 CMD1 `0x4DAC00` / CMD2 `0x4DB000`), which is why a remembered pair
#   reads right against one capture and wrong against the next. The repair is one decimal, not a hex; and
#   `ena_host_version = 0x00001102` says this block reports SDHCI specification 2.00, while the sentence
#   that introduced the cell argues from "a 3.00-or-later block" - so any argument in this ladder that
#   rests on what the specification says about latching has to name the specification the block says it is.
#
#
#   AND TWO THINGS THIS STEP MEASURED THAT THE NEXT BUILD SHOULD CARRY. (1) THE POLL LOOP'S EXIT IS A
#   MULTIPLE OF 1,024. `entry_storage.c`'s poll is nested - an inner block of `ST_CMD_DONE_INNER` = 1024
#   iterations that increments `polls` and leaves through `cmd_done` on a mask bit, and an outer step that
#   reads the clock and breaks on `ST_CMD_DONE_TICK_BUDGET`. A command that latched NOTHING can only leave
#   by the second, on a multiple of 1,024; and the seven distinct large counts in the ten archived rung
#   captures are exactly the seven multiples of 1,024 in [0x004DA000, 0x004DB800]. So a decimal in that
#   band that is not a multiple of 1,024 is not a reading at all, and three were quoted as readings:
#   5,088,000 and 5,088,768 and 5,081,600, none of them on the lattice. (2) FOUR `5,088,000`s REMAIN AND
#   THEY ARE ALL IN ONE ENTRY SOURCE - `entry_storage.c:116` twice, `:3054` and `:3861` - owed for the COST
#   reason and not a lane reason, because a comment there moves the entry image's own source manifest and
#   the gate would then refuse the arm a press is waiting on. Fourteen other sites were corrected in place
#   this step, and `tools/check_count_citations.py` now refuses any count quoted in two bases that
#   disagree, so the next one of these is caught at `make check` instead of fourteen steps later.
#
#   **AND 778's OWN `make check exit 0` WAS MEASURED IN A STATE THE COMMIT DESTROYS (779a).** The block
#   above records the check's reading as *73 counts, 0 disagreeing in any scope*. That reading was taken
#   while `tools/check_count_citations.py` was still UNTRACKED, and the check scans `git ls-files` - so the
#   file that was being verified was itself excluded from the verification, and so was the document that
#   quotes the defects. Committing them made both visible and `make check` went RED on master at `9a0291e`:
#   three of the check's own ten `SELFTEST` cells ARE the defect they test, so the check refused itself.
#   It is the same collision 778 documents twice in this record and once in the Makefile - a citation of a
#   defect and the defect are the same characters - and it is repaired the same way: the three cells are now
#   ONE string in the source split across two adjacent literals, so they still test the real string at
#   runtime and no line of the file contains the bad pair. The honest count after the repair is **108 counts,
#   0 disagreeing in the refusing scope, 11 in the historical record** - and the 11 are the 778 document's
#   own deliberate quotations of the bad pairs, in the scope that reports and does not refuse.
#
#
# 779: THE PAD EXPECTATION IS ONE OF THE DEVICE'S FOUR MTP BOARDS' WORD, AND THE DEVICE'S OWN DEVICE TREE IS
# ON DISK. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no firer, nothing built, and `out/` is byte-identical
# before and after - the armed rung-28 arm (armed-storage-d5d98738) is unspent. The gate was run ONCE,
# host-side, because this step edits its narration and a narration edit is verified by running the gate:
# readiness 5 of 5, exit 0, under the arm's own flags.
#
#   WHAT 776's GUARD COMPARES AGAINST, AND WHAT THE SOURCE SAYS IT IS. `ST_TLMM_SDC1_EXPECT` = `0x00009F24`,
#   and `entry_storage.c:302-303` calls `msm8974pro-ac-pm8941-mtp-v5.dts` *the Mi 4's own board file*. Both
#   halves are wrong and neither is visible from the source: the vendor checkout holds NO CANCRO OR XIAOMI
#   DEVICE TREE AT ALL (381 dts files, every one a Qualcomm reference board - MTP, CDP, FLUID, LIQUID - and
#   V5 is one of FOUR MSM8974PRO-AC MTP variants), and `.gitignore:12` excludes `external/` under a comment
#   that calls those checkouts large and reproducible - so the citation names a file no clone can resolve.
#
#   THE DEVICE'S OWN TREE IS THE dt.img THE IMAGE PACKS. `scripts/build.sh:130` sets
#   QCDT_DT=.../boot-unpacked/dt.img, the Mi 4's own partition backup: 2521088 bytes, sha256
#   c8faf487909b668fcebddfb2dcbd793bfb94d0411286c1f5c588bc0015120efe, a QCDT v2 table - A LIST OF
#   ALTERNATIVES, one of which the bootloader selects ON THE DEVICE. Six FDT trees, five with an
#   `sdhci@f9824900` node, and they declare TWO pad words:
#     #0 Qualcomm MSM 8974Pro-AC + PMA8084 MTP   board-id 8 1     pull [0,3,3,1] drv [7,4,4] -> 0x00009FE4
#     #1 Qualcomm MSM 8974Pro-AC MTP             board-id 8 256   pull [0,3,3,1] drv [4,4,4] -> 0x00009F24
#     #2 Qualcomm MSM 8974Pro-AC MTP V4          board-id 8 1024  pull [0,3,3,1] drv [4,4,4] -> 0x00009F24
#     #3 Qualcomm MSM 8974Pro-AC MTP V5          board-id 8 1280  pull [0,3,3,1] drv [4,4,4] -> 0x00009F24
#     #4 Qualcomm MSM SAMARIUM RUMI              (no SDC1 node)
#     #5 Qualcomm MSM 8974Pro-AA/AB MTP          board-id 8 0     pull [0,3,3,1] drv [4,4,4] -> 0x00009F24
#   The two words differ in ONE MASKED FIELD OF ONE REGISTER: CLK hdrive 7 against 4. WHICH TREE THE
#   BOOTLOADER SELECTS IS NOT ESTABLISHED ANYWHERE IN THIS TREE, and 779 does not establish it.
#
#   AND THE FORK'S SECOND ROW IS NARROWER THAN IT READS - corrected in the gate's own narration for this arm,
#   in both places it is printed, BEFORE any press. `_pad_write_skipped = 0` means *the register did not hold
#   0x9F24*, NOT *the vendor's act was never applied*: a register holding 0x9FE4 is a CONFIGURED pad -
#   declared by another tree on this same device - that the arm would re-configure to V5's word, and
#   `_pad_after_match = 1` is then not the statement that the fix landed on an unconfigured pad. The fork is
#   `raw == 0x9F24` versus `raw != 0x9F24`; `_pad_raw` is the reading and it is taken unconditionally. The
#   narration's other over-claim is corrected in the same edit: it called the constant "the value this
#   board's own arrays produce", and it is a value SOME tree on this board produces.
#
#   AND THE RAIL CAVEAT IS ANSWERED AT ITS PROVENANCE WITHOUT BEING CLOSED. Every tree that carries the node
#   declares `qcom,vdd-always-on` and `qcom,vdd-io-always-on`, so the sentence at 5646 - "not that the rails
#   are up on the physical device (the Mi 4's own board dts is not in this tree)" - no longer rests on a
#   missing file: the device's own device tree IS in this tree, in the boot backup. It is still NOT a
#   measurement, and no capture in the archive contains a PMIC, SPMI or regulator reading of any kind.
#
#   THE REPAIR IS A DERIVATION AND A TRACKED READING, BECAUSE THE INPUT CANNOT BE TRACKED. Three artifacts:
#   `tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py` parses any QCDT blob, walks each tree, re-derives the word through the
#   kernel's own shift and mask (`msm_tlmm_set_field`, gpio-msm-common.c:481-496; hdrive width 3 at 6/3/0,
#   pull width 2 at 13/11/9/15, DT arrays indexed from the enum base) and PICKS NO TREE;
#   `records/sdc1-pad-candidates.txt` is the TRACKED reading - every candidate word, the model and board-id
#   of each tree declaring it, and the sha256 of the dt.img it was read from - which is what converts a
#   gitignored derivation into something a clone holds; and `tools/check_sdc1_pad_expectation.py`, in
#   `make check` with a 9-cell `--selftest` on its own line, reads the RECORD and refuses a constant that
#   matches NO candidate. **ITS SCOPE IS DELIBERATELY NARROWER THAN "is the constant right"**: it catches a
#   constant no board on this device implies and it CANNOT catch one the wrong board implies, because the
#   record holds two words and the choice is made on the device - so a record holding both PASSES and
#   PRINTS the ambiguity, and the fact lives in the output of every `make check`. Four falsification cells
#   were run against scratch records (only the other word -> refuse; no candidate line -> refuse; no
#   input/sha header -> refuse; both -> pass and print).
#
#   AND make check WAS RED ON MASTER AT 9a0291e (this is the 779a commit, which landed first). The cause is
#   778's own class a third time: `check_count_citations.py` scans `git ls-files`, so while that file was
#   untracked its ten SELFTEST cells were invisible to it - and three of those cells ARE the defect they
#   test - so committing the file made the check refuse itself with exit 2. 778's recorded green was
#   therefore measured in a state the commit destroys. Repaired 778's own way: the three cells are now ONE
#   string in the source split across two adjacent literals, so the cell still tests the real string at
#   runtime and no line of the file contains the bad pair.
#
# OWED, named rather than inferred: `entry_storage.c:302-303`'s mis-citation itself (COST reason - an entry
# source moves the image's recorded source manifest and the gate would refuse the arm a press is waiting on;
# the fix is to name the tree the word came from and cite `records/sdc1-pad-candidates.txt` for the
# candidates); and the SUBSTANTIVE repair - make the arm compare against the SET of candidate words and
# publish which member matched - which is a BUILD, PRE-REGISTERED HERE AND NOT BUILT, because the arm is
# armed and unspent and moving `out/` is the operator's decision. Also unchanged from 775-778: the
# `rung_para` correction for values 12..23, the seam-address class, `run_and_capture.sh`'s
# `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL`, the 737 window paragraph, and the four `5,088,000`s in `entry_storage.c`.

#
# 783: THE MASK THE LADDER FOUND WAS LIFTED AT ONE OF THREE WINDOWS - CMD1's and CMD2's still run with
# the one-bit enable. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate against a device, no runner, no
# firer, nothing built, and `out/` is untouched - the armed rung-28 arm (armed-storage-d5d98738,
# 93026ca1... / d5d98738..., equal to its own set lines) is unspent. This is a reading of the
# REPOSITORY'S OWN SOURCE and not of a gitignored input.
#
#   THE SENTENCE THIS FOLLOWS. 765 section 1 measured that the vendor's own `sdhci_init` writes ELEVEN
#   bits (0x01FF0003) into INT_ENABLE 0x34 and drew: *"if the latch is enable-gated, a poll over a
#   register whose error-enable half is zero cannot see an error bit however many samples it takes, and
#   the ladder has NEVER ONCE set an error enable."* Rung 23 acted on it and the rung-24 press confirmed
#   the rule (the CMD3 row that had read status_any = 0 over 5,088,256 polls now reads 0x00018000, break
#   at poll 1789, 665.2 us). **WHAT HAS NEVER BEEN WRITTEN IS THE SCOPE.**
#
#   THE WINDOW CENSUS, now computed on every `make check` by tools/report_int_enable_windows.py:
#     4317  st_cmd_path          CMD0, CMD1, CMD2  int_enable | ST_SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE  -> 0x00000001
#     3110  st_all_send_cid      CMD2              int_enable | ST_SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE  -> 0x00000001
#     3981  st_cmd3_noidx        CMD3              ena (built :3976 under #if >= 22)    -> 0x000f0001
#   and the tool's own summary: the FIVE-BIT word is built at ONE line and reaches ONE store, all in
#   st_cmd3_noidx; the ONE-BIT word is written at every other command window. (st_set_relative_addr and
#   st_cmd3_noresp also write the one-bit word; both are rungs the armed arm does not contain, and both
#   are in the census so the table is the whole reading.)
#
#   WHAT IT MEANS FOR THE ARMED PRESS. In the rung-28 arm CMD1's and CMD2's windows still run with
#   INT_ENABLE = 0x00000001, so their _status_any = 0 over the full 1.2 s is STILL THE MASKED READING
#   765 described - on this arm the mask was lifted for the LAST command and left standing for the two
#   that run FIRST. CMD1 is SEND_OP_COND, the command an eMMC must answer for the ladder to move at all.
#   THE PRESS WOULD ANSWER THE PAD QUESTION AND THE CMD-LINE COUNTS AND LEAVE THE CMD1 TIMEOUT QUESTION
#   WHERE RUNG 21 LEFT IT --- not a reason not to spend it (row 1 of the pad fork closes a candidate on
#   its own), but the reason to know before the press that the arm asks two questions and not three.
#
#   AND THE CMD1 QUESTION IS NOT ALREADY ANSWERABLE FROM THE ARCHIVE, cell by cell: _cmd1_status_any = 0
#   is a masked read; _cid_stale (INT_STATUS at CMD2's entrance, after CMD1's poll gave up) reads
#   0x00000000, and on this block a bit latches only while its enable stands, so a zero there is
#   CONSISTENT WITH A TIMEOUT THAT COULD NOT LATCH and separates nothing; and _nidx_status_pre =
#   0x00018000 is read after CMD2's window too and does not reach back to CMD1. So NO PRESS OF THE ARMED
#   ARM CAN ANSWER IT.
#
#   THE FIX IS ONE CONSTANT WIDE IN THE SAME SENSE RUNG 23 WAS: st_cmd_path's store and st_all_send_cid's
#   store would each take int_enable | ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE_CMD under a new #if threshold, and then ONE
#   press answers the pad question, the CMD-line counts, the CMD3 row AND the CMD1/CMD2 latch question
#   together. It is a BUILD, which moves out/ and supersedes the armed arm unspent, so it is the
#   operator's decision and NOT this step's. Both shapes are one press.
#
#   LANDED AS A CHECK AND NOT A SENTENCE: tools/report_int_enable_windows.py prints the assignments that
#   BUILD a word (with the #if threshold guarding each) and every store to 0x34 with what it writes,
#   resolved BY NAME through the assignment and never by line number; make check runs it and its
#   --selftest, so THE SCOPE OF EVERY WIDENING IS PRINTED ON EVERY CHECK. Its ONE REFUSAL is a defect and
#   not a choice: a store whose value is a bare literal, because a literal there is one value with two
#   definitions. Four selftest cells pin it.
#
#   AND ONE DEFECT OF THIS TOOL'S OWN, found by running it and recorded because it is the project's
#   oldest class: the first version classified a store by SUBSTRING ('int_enable' in expr), and
#   `int_enable | (uint32_t)ST_SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE` CONTAINS `int_enable` - so every command window was
#   called a passthrough and the census printed ZERO five-bit windows: a tool built to report the scope of
#   a widening, reporting that the widening had not happened. The repair is per-OPERAND (split on `|`,
#   strip casts, ask what each operand is). A reading that is a property of the instrument - 778's count
#   defect and 779's board constant, a third time.
#
# NO set= line follows this block: no arm was built, no park was made, and the sets above are unchanged.

# 784: THE EMMC'S TWO RAILS ARE RPM RESOURCES, AND WHICH PMIC CARRIES THEM IS THE TREE'S BUSINESS.
# HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate against a device, no runner, no firer, nothing
# built, and `out/` is untouched - the armed rung-28 arm (armed-storage-d5d98738, 93026ca1... /
# d5d98738..., equal to its own set lines) is unspent. The input is the device's own dt.img, which is
# .gitignore'd, so the reading is landed as a TRACKED record as in 779/781/782.
#
#   THE CANDIDATE. The safety gate's own notes carry it as *"the eMMC's VCC/VCCQ come from the PMIC and
#   no rung in this ladder has ever touched the PMIC"* - NAMED, NOT ESTABLISHED. 780 weakened the
#   record's reason for closing it (the vendor's sdhci-msm DOES enable both rails through
#   regulator_enable; always_on gates only the RELEASE path) and did not close it.
#
#   THE RAILS, resolved through the collector node's own phandles, in every tree that has the node:
#     vdd-supply     /soc/qcom,rpm-smd/rpm-regulator-ldoa20/regulator-l20
#                    name 8941_l20 on trees #1/#2/#3/#5, name 8084_l20 on tree #0
#                    init-uv 2950000 (0x2d0370)  qcom,set 3  min = max  always-on NONE
#     vdd-io-supply  /soc/qcom,rpm-smd/rpm-regulator-smpa3/regulator-s3  (trees #1/#2/#3/#5)
#                    /soc/qcom,rpm-smd/rpm-regulator-smpa4/regulator-s4  (tree #0)
#                    name 8941_s3 / 8084_s4     init-uv 1800000 (0x1b7740)  qcom,set 3  always-on NONE
#   sdhci@f9824900 declares exactly these two supplies and no third.
#
#   THE PATH IS INVARIANT AND THE NAME IS NOT - ONE PATH, TWO DEFINITIONS. regulator-l20 is the SAME
#   path in all five trees and carries TWO names, and the vdd-io rail moves between smpa3/s3 and
#   smpa4/s4 as well. So the rail's identity is a property of the tree the bootloader selects, which
#   is 781's hardware subtype one level down: tree #0's arbiter declares pm8941@2/@3 and pma8084@0/@1
#   and its rails are named for the PMA8084; the other four declare pm8941@0/@1 and pm8841@4/@5 and
#   their rails are named for the PM8941.
#
#   AND THE RAIL'S NAME AND ITS CHIP'S NODE NAME DO NOT CONTAIN EACH OTHER IN THE DIRECTION A SUBSTRING
#   TEST WOULD NEED: rail 8941_l20, child qcom,pm8941@0 - 8941 IS a substring; rail 8084_l20, children
#   qcom,pma8084@0/@1 - 8084 is a SUFFIX of pma8084 and not a substring of its front. So the rule the
#   check states is *the token is the last four characters of a declared SPMI child's name*, and it is
#   a rule because a rail named with no `_` has no token at all.
#
#   THE RAILS ARE NOT ALWAYS-ON, AND THE FILE MARKS OTHER RAILS ALWAYS-ON - COMPUTED. Across the six
#   trees there are 42 separate regulator-always-on / regulator-boot-on declarations (vph_pwr_vreg, l2,
#   l3, l12, l18, l22, lvs1, the disp_* pair, spi_eth_phy_vreg) and **0 of the 42 lands on a rail the
#   ladder's controller declares**. So the device's own tree USES the always-on vocabulary and does not
#   use it for LDO20 or S3/S4 - qcom,vdd-always-on on the collector node is the CONSUMER-side flag the
#   vendor driver reads on its release path (780), NOT the RPM holding the rail up. This CORRECTS an
#   inference 781's record drew from the same two properties. The rails' runtime state is a REQUEST,
#   and the only requester 780 could name is sdhci-msm's vreg_enable, which this payload does not
#   execute because it is not the Linux driver.
#
#   THE AP'S DECLARED ROUTES, AND WHY NO STORE CAN POWER THE CARD. The same file declares BOTH families
#   this SoC has and puts the eMMC's two in the RPM one:
#     RPM resource      /soc/qcom,rpm-smd/... - compatible qcom,rpm-regulator-smd and **NO `reg` at
#                       all** - reached through /soc/qcom,smem@fa00000 with qcom,smd-rpm
#                       (compatible qcom,smd, edge 0xf) and a node whose rpm-channel-name is
#                       **rpm_requests** with rpm-channel-type 0xf.
#     SPMI-direct reg   /soc/qcom,spmi@fc4c0000/qcom,<pmic>@n/regulator@XXXX - 78 to 126 of them per
#                       tree - through the arbiter /soc/qcom,spmi@fc4c0000 (qcom,spmi-pmic-arb,
#                       reg <0xfc4cf000 0x1000 0xfc4cb000 0x1000 0xfc4ca000 0x1000>, reg-names
#                       core/intr/cnfg).
#   So there is NO ADDRESS whose store is the rail: the AP asks for it BY NAMING A CHANNEL. A rung that
#   powered the rails would be an SMD/RPM client, not a register poke - which is why the rung-28 arm's
#   pad question needs no PMIC and is the cheap one.
#
#   AND THE LADDER HAS NEVER TOUCHED ANY OF IT, MEASURED: grep over src/entry/entry_storage.c finds the
#   regulator/PMIC/SPMI vocabulary twice, both times inside the two comments carrying 779's wrong
#   msm8974pro-ac-pm8941-mtp-v5.dts citation (:300, :3550). No store to the arbiter, no RPM request, no
#   LDO/SMPS register. ONE QUALIFICATION, because it is a real touch: the reset path writes PS_HOLD at
#   0xfc4ab000 (src/entry/entry_reset.h:37) in entry_epilogue on every returning run, and 0xfc4ab000
#   sits in the `0xFC4` megabyte where 0xfc4004c0 already faulted on 692's press - the entry source says
#   so itself (:50-52). The precise statement is: the ladder has never touched a REGULATOR; it does
#   write one PMIC line, in a megabyte that is not uniformly mapped.
#
#   AND THE RPM FIRMWARE IS NOT THE DIFFERENTIATOR EITHER. rpm.img (1048576 bytes, sha256 18402f25...)
#   carries BOTH /rpm/pmic/target/pm8x41 and /rpm/pmic/target/pma8084, plus PMIC_ARB, the rail-name
#   fragments (ldoa, smpa, vsa, ldoa4K), the channel name rpm_requests, and the RPM's own format string
#   `%s (Enabled: 0/1)`. One image serves both boards, and the tree the bootloader selects decides.
#
#   LANDED AS THREE ARTIFACTS. tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py gained `--rails`: the phandle-resolved reading,
#   the always-on contrast COMPUTED, the arbiter and its children, the RPM channel, and - because a
#   producer that can write what its own checker refuses is the m749 class - THE VERDICT THE TRACKED
#   RECORD'S CHECK WILL REACH, printed at read time on the same rule object. records/
#   sdc1-pad-candidates.txt carries `# rail`, `# spmi` and `# rpm` lines (children are `;`-separated
#   because a PMIC node name is itself `qcom,pm8941@2`, so a comma-separated list of them is not
#   separable). tools/check_sdc1_pad_expectation.py gained a FOURTH REFUSAL: the record's rails must
#   attribute to PMICs the record's own `# spmi` lines declare - a rail named for a chip the same record
#   does not declare is 779's defect one level down, a citation to another board standing where a
#   reading of this one should be.
#
#   FALSIFIED, NOT ASSERTED. Six cells pin rail_census_verdict as a pure function (the device's own
#   reading passes; 8084_l20 against a record declaring only the PM8941 refuses and the converse; a rail
#   with no token refuses; no rail lines refuses; rails with no declared chip refuses) and eight more pin
#   rail_verdict's token rule, including the case a SUBSTRING TEST WOULD GET WRONG: 8084_l20 against
#   qcom,pma8084@0 passes, 8084_s4 against qcom,pm8941@0 refuses. tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py --selftest
#   grew from 11 cells to 26 (8 rail attributions, 1 token rule, 10 against the device's own file,
#   including the computed 0-of-42). make check exit 0.
#
#   WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY. It does not say the rails are up, and it does not say they are down - no
#   capture in this archive holds a PMIC, SPMI, RPM or rail reading of any kind. It does not change the
#   arm. It does not name which tree the bootloader selects, so it does not name which PMIC is on this
#   board - only that the device's own file declares both candidates. And it does not propose a rung.
#   Two cheap readings are NAMED and not designed: /soc/qcom,rpm-log@fc19dc00 (reg <0xfc19dc00 0x4000>,
#   and the RPM's own format string is `%s (Enabled: 0/1)`) and /soc/qcom,rpm-master-stats@fc428150
#   (reg <0xfc428150 0x3200>) - each in a megabyte this image's tables do not cover, so each carries
#   the same interlock cost and 692's rule applies unchanged.
#
