# 782: THE ARMED FORK IS A TABLE WITH FOUR ROWS - the same declarations name the register's OFF state,
# and a two-row fork merges it with "never applied". HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate
# against a device, no runner, no firer, nothing built, and `out/` is untouched - the armed rung-28 arm
# (armed-storage-d5d98738, 93026ca1... / d5d98738..., equal to its own set lines) is unspent.
#
#   THE OFF WORD, out of the same node's `qcom,pad-pull-off` / `qcom,pad-drv-off` through the same
#   arithmetic: ALL FIVE trees that carry `sdhci@f9824900` declare `pull-off [0, 3, 3, 1]`,
#   `drv-off [0, 0, 0]` -> **0x00009E00**, the PULL HALF OF 0x9F24 WITH THE DRIVE HALF ZEROED. The three
#   words differ ONLY in the three hdrive fields: 0x00009F24 = 0x9E00 | 0x124 (4/4/4), 0x00009FE4 =
#   0x9E00 | 0x1E4 (7/4/4), 0x00009E00 = 0x9E00 | 0x000 (0/0/0).
#
#   SO `_pad_raw` IS A FOUR-ROW READING AND NOT A TWO-ROW FORK: 0x9F24 -> the pads are at the vendor's
#   CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON state; 0x9FE4 -> configured to the OTHER declared board's word; **0x9E00 -> the
#   PULLS ARE PRESENT AND THE DRIVE IS OFF**, a state no BUS_ON leaves, whose pull half is IDENTICAL to
#   row 1's, so 769 section 2's missing-pull-up mechanism is RETIRED on this row too and a fork that
#   calls it "the vendor act was never applied" merges it with row 4; 0x00000000 or anything else -> a
#   state NO declaration in this archive produces, the row that would say a bootloader used its own
#   values. On row 3 the three hdrive fields stay the subject; on row 4 the pull fields are zero and the
#   mechanism is live.
#
#   LANDED AS A CHECK AND NOT A SENTENCE. tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py derives and prints the OFF words
#   (via `derive_or_none`, which returns NONE for an array shorter than the field list rather than
#   silently truncating it through `zip` - SDC2's three-entry array is the live case); the tracked
#   record carries an OFF-word column per tree and one machine-readable `# offword 0x00009e00 ...` line;
#   and tools/check_sdc1_pad_expectation.py REFUSES a guard constant equal to an off word, because the
#   guard asks *does the register already hold the word I expect* and a constant set to the off word
#   would report a POWERED-DOWN PAD AS ALREADY CONFIGURED. One register, two states, one name for both:
#   779's defect class one level down. Falsified against a scratch record (the guard's own constant
#   written as the `# offword` line -> REFUSED with the reading named); five selftest cells pin
#   `offword_verdict` as a pure function.
#
#   AND THIS STEP PAYS 779's OWED SELF-TEST for tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py: eleven cells, SEVEN on the
#   arithmetic alone (both ON words, the OFF word, and the three short/absent array shapes that must
#   answer NO WORD rather than a truncated one) and FOUR against the device's own file (the four
#   controller addresses, the two enabled ones, the ladder's node at five trees and window 0x1a0, and
#   `prop_summary` reporting the differing pad array AS DIFFERING). Where dt.img does not exist the four
#   file cells print SKIPPED BY NAME, because a cell that cannot run must not read as one that ran; the
#   tool is in `make check` now, --selftest first.
#
#   WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: it changes no cell, no store and not a byte of the arm - the guard still
#   compares `_pad_raw` against 0x9F24 alone (editing entry_storage.c to compare against the SET would
#   invalidate the armed arm for 763 section 5's measured COST). It does not say the pads are the cause:
#   three of the four rows have PULL SDC1_CMD present, so on three the pull-up is not the reason the
#   card is silent. It does not establish the card is powered. NO PRESS IS SPENT AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#   tools/verify_press_ready.sh was run once, HOST-SIDE, because this step edits its narration - 5 of 5,
#   exit 0, under the arm's own flags, no device action.
#
# NO set= line follows this block: no arm was built, no park was made, and the sets above are unchanged.

# 781: THE DEVICE'S OWN TREE AND ITS OWN BOOTLOADER PUT THE eMMC ON THE NODE THE LADDER READS - and the
# tree that wins is a hardware subtype no file in this archive carries. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action,
# no press, no gate against a device, no runner, no firer, nothing built, and `out/` is untouched - the
# armed rung-28 arm (armed-storage-d5d98738) and every park are byte-identical before and after.
#
#   THE CENSUS, from the device's own dt.img (2,521,088 B, sha256 c8faf487... - the input
#   records/sdc1-pad-candidates.txt already names): 6 FDT trees, 20 controller nodes, 4 distinct
#   addresses, and TWO of them enabled in every tree that carries them -
#     0xf9824900 sdhci@f9824900 status ok  reg <0xf9824900 0x1a0 0xf9824000 0x800>  always-on yes/yes
#                qcom,bus-speed-mode HS400_1p8v, HS200_1p8v, DDR_1p8v  vdd 2,950,000  vdd-io 1,800,000
#     0xf98a4900 sdhci@f98a4900 status ok  reg <0xf98a4900 0x11c 0xf98a4000 0x800>  always-on NO/NO
#                no bus-speed-mode, vdd-io 1,800,000-2,950,000, THREE pad entries (no RCLK)
#     0xf9864900 sdhci@f9864900 status disable   (0x11c, core 0xf9864000)
#     0xf98e4900 sdhci@f98e4900 status disable   (0x11c, core 0xf98e4000)
#   and the vendor's own msm8974.dtsi:24-27 names what they are - sdhc1 = SDC1 eMMC slot (:500),
#   sdhc2 = SDC2 SD card slot (:530), sdhc3/sdhc4 SDIO (:557, :599). So THE LADDER'S TWO WINDOWS ARE THE
#   FIRST `reg` CELL PAIR OF A NODE THIS DEVICE'S OWN TREE MARKS ok IN ALL FIVE TREES THAT CARRY IT, at
#   the widened 0x1a0 window 531 read out of the vendor's msm8974pro.dtsi:1763-1769.
#
#   AND THE DEVICE'S OWN BOOTLOADER SAYS IT BY NAME: boot.img's cmdline, the one aboot wrote, carries
#   `androidboot.bootdevice=msm_sdcc.1` - SDCC instance 1, the controller the device boots from. The
#   string is on record in 01 and 608; what is new here is using it as the identification it is.
#
#   THE ONE PROPERTY THAT VARIES ACROSS THOSE FIVE TREES IS 779'S PAD DECLARATION, and the census prints
#   it as varying on purpose (qcom,pad-drv-on = 0x7 0x4 0x4 on tree #0, 0x4 0x4 0x4 on the other four): an
#   aggregate row carrying tree #0's array would be 779's defect one level up.
#
#   WHAT IT CLOSES: "the ladder may be reading a controller the eMMC is not on, or a node this device's
#   tree disables" - a candidate the record never named and every rung since 531 assumed - closed by the
#   device's own files in two independent channels. It is 779's defect class one level up: an assumption
#   about this device standing in for a reading of it.
#   WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: that the card is powered, clocked or able to answer - no capture in this
#   archive holds a PMIC, SPMI, regulator or card reading of any kind. AND WHICH TREE WINS IS NOT
#   ANSWERABLE FROM THIS ARCHIVE, now established rather than assumed: `qcom,board-id` is the ONLY
#   board-identification string in the 4,194,304 bytes of aboot.img, the five trees' cells are (8,1)
#   (8,256) (8,1024) (8,1280) (8,0), and a board-id is a value the bootloader reads off the hardware -
#   the rung-28 arm's `_pad_raw` is the cheapest place that question has ever been asked.
#
#   LANDED AS A CHECK AND NOT A SENTENCE: records/sdc1-pad-candidates.txt now carries one machine-readable
#   `# node 0x... <name> core 0x... window 0x... status ... trees n/m` line per controller (rewritten by
#   tools/derive_sdc1_pads.py --write-record, which gained `--mmc-census`), and
#   tools/check_sdc1_pad_expectation.py refuses when the record does not name ST_HC_MEM_BASE's address
#   with status ok and paired with ST_CORE_MEM_BASE. Four falsification cells (node line removed, status
#   disable, core swapped to the other enabled node, address renamed) all refuse with the reading named;
#   six selftest cells pin `node_verdict` as a pure function; `make check` exit 0. The refusal compares
#   the record against the source's two `#define`s and cannot see whether the record was written from
#   THIS device - that half is the input's sha256, a value a rewrite cannot invent.
#

# 780: THE VENDOR'S OWN PATH POWERS THE CARD - the reason this record gave for closing 764 section 3's
# power candidate is a driver reading that does not apply to this board. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action,
# no press, no gate, no runner, no firer, nothing built, and `out/` is byte-identical before and after -
# the armed rung-28 arm (armed-storage-d5d98738) is unspent. Every vendor line below was read in the
# checkout at external/android_kernel_xiaomi_cancro/, which `.gitignore:12` excludes.
#
#   THE TWO HALVES OF THE SENTENCE AT 5644. (1) TRUE: `sdhci.c:3583-3587` does `regulator_get(mmc_dev(mmc),
#   "vmmc")`, the DT supplies `vdd-supply`, so it takes IS_ERR, prints "no vmmc regulator found" and sets
#   `host->vmmc = NULL`, and every `mmc_regulator_set_ocr` is guarded by `host->vmmc &&` (`:1625`, `:1667`,
#   `:1688`, `:1833`) - verified, and re-verified in 780. (2) FALSE: "The vendor does not turn the card's
#   power on either." `host->vmmc` is `sdhci.c`'s OWN rail handle. `sdhci-msm.c` - the driver that binds
#   `sdhc_1` (`compatible = "qcom,sdhci-msm"`; the legacy `sdcc1` node is `status = "disabled"`,
#   `msm8974-mtp.dtsi:368-370`) - has its own vreg table from the SAME node's `vdd-supply`/`vdd-io-supply`
#   and its own path, read end to end:
#     sdhci_msm_probe:2826        sdhci_msm_vreg_init(&pdev->dev, msm_host->pdata, true)
#     sdhci_msm_vreg_init:1873    is_init true -> vreg_init_reg(h) per rail (regulator_get)
#                                 -> sdhci_msm_vreg_reset(pdata)
#     sdhci_msm_vreg_reset:1861   setup_vreg(pdata, 1, true)   <-- ENABLE
#                                 setup_vreg(pdata, 0, true)
#     sdhci_msm_vreg_enable:1765  regulator_enable(vreg->reg)   <-- NO is_always_on TEST
#   So on any Linux boot of this board `regulator_enable()` runs on BOTH `pm8941_l20` and `pm8941_s3`.
#
#   AND `qcom,vdd-always-on` DOES NOT CAUSE THE EARLY RETURN THIS BLOCK ALSO LEANS ON. That return's own
#   comment (`:1834-1836`) says "vreg info unavailable, assuming the slot is powered by always on domain",
#   and the phrase is what made it look like the property's effect. The guard is `if (!curr_slot)` on
#   `pdata->vreg_data`, and `vreg_data == NULL` CANNOT HAPPEN on a DT-bound device: `populate_pdata:1397`
#   allocates it UNCONDITIONALLY, before parsing anything, and `goto out`s only on allocation failure;
#   the per-rail parse (`:1060-1064`) returns early - leaving that rail NULL - only when the `-supply`
#   phandle is MISSING, and this board declares both. The flag's ONLY behavioural consumer is in the
#   RELEASE path: `:1072-1074` sets `is_always_on` from `qcom,%s-always-on`, `vreg_disable:1796` skips
#   `regulator_disable` when it is set, and `:1813-1817` demotes to LPM instead (and `qcom,vdd-lpm-sup` is
#   declared at `msm8974-mtp.dtsi:389`). **`always_on` means ENABLE IT AND NEVER RELEASE IT - the opposite
#   of "skip the regulator call".**
#
#   THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES, AND ON A STRONGER READING THAN THE ONE IT USED. `vreg_reset` enables both rails
#   and then calls the DISABLE path immediately; on an always-on rail that path does not disable but only
#   demotes to LPM - so THE VENDOR'S OWN BRING-UP IS WHAT LEAVES THE RAILS ENABLED AND THE FLAG IS WHAT
#   KEEPS THEM SO. Two consequences, and they are the point: 764 section 3's candidate 1 is WEAKENED, not
#   strengthened - nothing in this tree disables the card's supplies, the vendor's path explicitly enables
#   them, and a board that also boots FROM this eMMC to `fastboot` has had that bus powered and driven; and
#   the candidate stays **UNMEASURED**, which is the honest state, but it is no longer a loose end a reader
#   would expect to be productive. A future reader taking the old sentence at face value could argue FROM
#   it that the card's power is nobody's act - which is the opposite of what the tree says - which is why
#   the sentence is corrected in place above rather than only here.
#
#   AND "THE PMIC IS UNREACHABLE" IS FALSE AS STATED, in a way worth naming. 773 section 3's table counts
#   acts 1/3 as `regulator_*`, hence "no" for reachable from `hc_mem`. That verdict is right about the ACT
#   and wrong about the BLOCK: the PMIC's interface has an MMIO window on this SoC and it is INSIDE A
#   MEGABYTE THIS LADDER ALREADY INSTALLS. `msm8974.dtsi:848-854` - `qcom,spmi@fc4c0000`,
#   `reg = <0xfc4cf000 0x1000>, <0xfc4cb000 0x1000>, <0xfc4ca000 0x1000>` - and `0xFC4` is `ST_GCC_BASE`'s
#   section (`entry_storage.c:280`), with `_gcc_map = 1` in EVERY capture since 694. So the accurate
#   statement is narrower and more interesting: the arbiter's three pages ARE readable from a megabyte the
#   ladder already holds, while THE PMIC'S OWN REGISTERS ARE NOT MEMORY MAPPED AT ALL - they are
#   SPMI-addressed by `usid:offset` - so reading a rail's state would mean driving the arbiter through its
#   command registers, a protocol and not a load. And the path the AP would USE to change a rail is
#   `rpm-smd` (`msm8974.dtsi:2102-2105`, `rpm-channel-name = "rpm_requests"`, `rpm-channel-type = <15>`
#   SMD_APPS_RPM) - a vote into shared memory answered by the RPM firmware, not the arbiter.
#
# NO RUNG IS PROPOSED FROM ANY OF IT: driving an SPMI bus is an act with a failure mode this ladder has not
# measured, the previous candidate in this direction is 748's two dead-end registers, and the ladder has
# never read `0xFC4Cxxxx`. NO set= line follows this block: no arm was built, no park was made, and the
# sets above are unchanged.
#

# 772: THE RUNG-26 ARM - armed-storage-a1378a48, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=25.
# Built, parked and recorded here. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate
# against a device, no runner, no firer. Rung 25's park (armed-storage-f72e9f18) and rung
# 23's (armed-storage-3a92aa52) are both untouched; this arm SUPERSEDES rung 25 because it
# strictly contains it, MEASURED rather than argued: the value-24 build from this same
# source is byte-identical to f72e9f18 (see the 771 block above for why that matters).
# The four new cells are already-measured values printed for the first time, so this arm
# adds NO device access, NO register, NO window, NO megabyte and NO store.
#
# WHAT THE ARM IS. Four keys, all of them already in a register when the arm runs, and NO new device
# access, no new register, no new window, no new megabyte and no store of any kind:
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd0_cmdlow_seen   the count of the first 1024 samples of CMD0's poll in which
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_cmdlow_seen   `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` bit 24 (ST_SDHCI_CMD_LINE_LEVEL, the CMD
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_cmdlow_seen    line's OWN LEVEL) was LOW - one count per command, computed by
#                                       the 724 sampler for EVERY command since rung 24 and published
#                                       by rung 24 for `nidx` alone
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_inhibit_after  the fourth row of 771's table and the only row the log has
#                                       never carried: st_send_command fills it for EVERY command and
#                                       no publish block has ever printed it
#
# WHY IT EXISTS. 771 read the rung-24 press's log as ONE FOUR-ROW TABLE and found the four commands are
# not the same experiment: _cmd0_inhibit_seen = 0x218 beside a command that completed and latched
# INT_RESPONSE; _cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0 and _cid_inhibit_seen = 0 beside 5.09 M polls each with NOTHING
# latched at all; _nidx_inhibit_seen = 0x400 beside a block that armed and FIRED its own response
# timeout at 665.2 us. AND THE ONLY TRACE OF WHAT THE CMD LINE WAS DOING DURING CMD1 OR CMD2 IS ONE
# SAMPLE: `inhibit_last` bit 24 reads LOW for CMD1 and HIGH for CMD2 on every capture in the archive
# (10 cmd0, 8 cmd1, 7 cid). _cmd1_inhibit_last = 0x00F80000 with _cmd1_inhibit_seen = 0 is a
# contradiction the archive already carries, and one sample cannot separate "the block never started
# this command" from "the block drove the line and the inhibit bit is not a reliable witness". The
# count separates them.
#
# CONTAINMENT, MEASURED AND NOT ARGUED. Every new line sits inside `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 25`,
# so value 24 is untouched by construction - and that was then MEASURED: the value-24 build from this
# same source came out BYTE-IDENTICAL to the parked armed-storage-f72e9f18 (f72e9f18..., 5552764 bytes,
# exit 0). One press therefore answers the SDC1 pad question AND these four cells, and rung 25's park
# need never be pressed. That is the same relation 767 established between rungs 23 and 24.
#
# WHAT DID NOT MOVE, MEASURED. xnu_arm_entry.bin is 5552764 bytes - the SAME size as the arm before it,
# because the four new keys fitted the padding. 770 measured that an unchanged size is NOT the claim
# that nothing moved, so the claim here rests on the hash (a1378a48..., different) and on the seam
# clause: STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR is still 0x8004a2dc and the build accepted it against the linked image,
# so the entry text did NOT cross a page this time. The payload's switch record is BYTE-IDENTICAL to
# rung 23's, rung 24's and rung 25's (6c2b6038..., 682 bytes) - the 763 section 5 trap, checked by cmp
# and not by eye.
#
# WHAT THE PRESS WILL COST AND WHAT IT BUYS. The arm's hazards are rung 25's own - one read of a TLMM
# pad register in a megabyte whose section the arm installs - and its new cells add none. Its answer is
# row 1 of 25's pad table PLUS three counts and one after-bit; neither is a verdict about the card, and
# a zero count is a reading only if the sampler outran the bus, which is why each count is read beside
# its own `_polls`/`_ticks`. NO PRESS IS SPENT HERE AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED: building, parking and
# recording are host-side, and the press is the operator's.

# 773 - THE REACHABLE SET, COUNTED. NO ARM IS BUILT BY THIS STEP AND NOTHING IN `out/` MOVED: the live arm
# is still 772's armed-storage-a1378a48 (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=25), ARMED AND NOT PRESSED. This block
# exists because the step VERIFIED a load-bearing claim that 769 had only inferred, and because it
# pre-registers the arm that would follow:
#
#   THE CENSUS. 769 section 3 said only the power IRQ's BUS_ON branch applies the SDC1 pads - an inference
#   from reading one function's neighbourhood. It is now counted over the whole vendor tree: every caller
#   of sdhci_msm_setup_pins/setup_pad is sdhci-msm.c:1000 (the dispatcher's only call), :2019 (the BUS_ON
#   branch - the ONLY enable = true), :2034 (power-off, false) and :3144 (teardown, false). And
#   setup_pins carries a latch (:994-995, cfg_sts == enable -> return), so within one Linux boot the pads
#   are written ONCE, on the first BUS_ON - so a board that went straight to fastboot has them at
#   BOOTLOADER state, whatever that is, and nothing in this repository can say what the bootloader wrote.
#
#   THE THREE ACTS, AND THE REACHABLE SET IS SIZE ONE. sdhci-msm.c:2015-2032 does setup_vreg ->
#   setup_pins -> set_vdd_io_vol. Acts 1 and 3 both end in regulator_* (:1826, :1922) - the PMIC, no path
#   from hc_mem - while act 2 lands in MSM_TLMM_BASE + 0x2044 = 0xFD512044, ONE register in the 0xFD5
#   megabyte rung 25 installs. So the vendor's PIN work is the only one of the three a rung can perform,
#   and it is exactly the register rung 26 reads - which is why 769 section 4 could pre-register an
#   expected value at all. AND THE CENSUS RETIRES A STANDING WORRY: setup_vreg's own head says what
#   qcom,vdd-always-on (msm8974-mtp.dtsi:388) means when the data is absent - "vreg info unavailable,
#   assuming the slot is powered by always on domain" (:1835, goto out, ret = 0) - so acts 1 and 3 are
#   satisfied by construction here and their unreachability costs nothing.
#
#   THE RUNG-27 DESIGN, PRE-REGISTERED AND NOT BUILT: read 0xFD512044 (rung 26's own read, kept), compare
#   against ST_TLMM_SDC1_EXPECT = 0x00009F24, and write ONLY if it does not match - one masked RMW over
#   all seven fields at once, then read back. Faithful to msm_tlmm_set_field (gpio-msm-common.c:481-496),
#   which is a plain __raw_readl / mask / __raw_writel / mb() - no unlock, no W1C, no FIFO, no shadow - and
#   a single masked RMW makes the vendor's six-call order IRRELEVANT, which is what makes one store safe
#   where six interleaved ones would need an argument. It STRICTLY CONTAINS rung 26, so one press answers
#   the pad question AND the fix question, and rungs 25 and 26 need never be pressed.
#
#   THE FORK: _pad_raw = 0x00009F24 with _pad_write_skipped = 1 means the pads were already configured and
#   the pad candidate is RETIRED, leaving the PMIC rails (acts 1/3, unreachable) as the whole frontier;
#   anything else with _pad_write_skipped = 0 means the vendor act was never applied and the write has been
#   made and read back. The build clause for rung 27 must assert the store is GUARDED, and the log's safety
#   reading is _pad_writes in {0, 1} as an ACTUAL COUNT and never a constant. A single masked RMW to this
#   register cannot reach another register, another peripheral's pads (0x2044 vs SDC2's 0x2048, disjoint
#   masks), a FUNCTION (there is no function-select field - 769 section 2), the bus (no transfer in
#   flight), or the PMIC/rails/clocks.
#
# NO set= line follows this block: no arm was built, no park was made, and the 35 sets above are unchanged.

# 774 - THE RCG'S ROOT IS ENABLED, AND THE CELL THAT SAYS OTHERWISE READS A BIT NO CODE IN THIS TREE WRITES.
# NO ARM IS BUILT AND NOTHING IN `out/` MOVED: the live arm is still 772's armed-storage-a1378a48
# (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=25), ARMED AND NOT PRESSED. This block exists because a cell's MEANING was
# wrong in the direction its own name invites, and the correction strengthens a live conclusion:
#
#   WHAT THE TWO CELLS SAY. This ladder publishes xnu_live_storage_clk_rcg_root_en and _rcg_root_status
#   (and the cmd2_ pair), all reading 0, and a reader can take that as "the root clock is off".
#
#   WHICH BIT IS WHICH. clock-local2.c:61-65 defines CMD_RCGR_ROOT_ENABLE_BIT BIT(1) - what you WRITE -
#   and CMD_RCGR_ROOT_STATUS_BIT BIT(31) - what the hardware REPORTS. The vendor reads the STATUS bit in
#   three places, always the same way (:299 _rcg_clk_handoff, :727 and :804): if the bit is SET it returns
#   HANDOFF_DISABLED_CLK, else HANDOFF_ENABLED_CLK. So SET means DISABLED and CLEAR means ENABLED, and
#   _rcg_root_status = 0 is therefore a POSITIVE reading that the SDC1 apps root is enabled, by the
#   vendor's own test.
#
#   AND _rcg_root_en READS A BIT NOTHING WRITES. grep -rn 'ROOT_ENABLE' arch/arm/mach-msm/ drivers/clk/
#   returns ONE hit - the #define. rcg_clk_prepare (:152-161) is a no-op that only WARNs if the rate was
#   never set; __clk_pre_reparent (clock.c:247) recurses into the parent's prepare/enable, which for an
#   rcg is that same no-op; and the one path that DOES write CMD_RCGR does not write that bit - set_rate_mnd
#   (:127-150) writes M_REG/N_REG/D_REG, then CFG_RCGR's DIV_MASK/SRC_SEL_MASK/MND_MODE_MASK, then
#   rcg_update_config: five words and the update bit, never the root enable. So its 0 carries no
#   information about whether the root is running. That is m737's shape and m776's shape again: a cell
#   whose NAME states a direction, read as though the 0 were the finding, when the finding is the
#   neighbouring cell and its sign is the opposite of the one the name invites.
#
#   WHAT IT CHANGES. It STRENGTHENS 771 section 7 - the clock is not a candidate - with a second,
#   independent reading that shares no mechanism with the divider arithmetic (384 MHz / 960 = 400,000 Hz
#   exactly). It disturbs no other rung: nothing in the press path reads _rcg_root_en as evidence of a
#   disabled clock, and rung_para 5's sentence is not made false. It does NOT say the clock reaches the
#   PADS - rung 5 already separated a branch's REQUEST (CBCR bit 0) from the framework's belief (bit 31),
#   and this closes the ROOT half while leaving the PAD half where rung 25 put it.
#
# NO set= line follows this block: no arm was built, no park was made, and the 35 sets above are unchanged.

# 775: THE RUNG-27 ARM - armed-storage-8096cb2c, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=26.
# Built, parked and recorded here. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate
# against a device, no runner, no firer. Rungs 26, 25 and 23's parks are all intact;
# this arm SUPERSEDES rung 26 because it strictly contains it, MEASURED rather than
# argued (the value-25 build from this same source is byte-identical to a1378a48).
#
# WHAT IT IS. Eleven fields of the CMD2 result that st_send_command fills for EVERY command
# and the st_all_send_cid publish block has never printed - because that block was written
# at rung 16, before rungs 12 and 24 added the entrance state and the inhibit gate to
# struct st_cmd_result. NO NEW DEVICE ACCESS, NO REGISTER, NO WINDOW, NO MEGABYTE AND NO
# STORE: every one of the eleven is already in the struct when the block runs.
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_ps_before / _inhibit_before / _inhibit_polls / _inhibit_ticks /
#   _inhibit_timeout / _stale / _clear_wrote / _arg / _ps_after / _rsp_present / _resp_read
#
# AND ONE OF THEM DECIDES SOMETHING NO OTHER CELL IN THE ARCHIVE CAN. 771 saw the anomaly
# and could not read it: the block ACKNOWLEDGES CMD0 (inhibit rises, INT_RESPONSE latches)
# and CMD3 (inhibit held for all 1024 samples, INT_TIMEOUT latches at 665.2 us) but raises
# NOTHING for CMD1 and CMD2 (inhibit_seen = 0, status_any = 0 over 5.09 M all-zero reads
# each). And the press DOES show _nidx_status_pre = 0x00018000, i.e. ERROR | TIMEOUT was
# latched somewhere between CMD2's poll giving up and CMD3's entrance - while the ONLY write
# in that interval is a store to 0x34, which 736 measured sets bit 15 by itself. So whether
# bit 16 (INT_TIMEOUT) is a real CMD2 timeout that arrived late, or the enable store's own
# effect, is UNSEPARATED by every capture on record. _cid_stale is INT_STATUS 0x30 as FOUND
# at CMD2's ENTRANCE - the first read of that register since CMD1's poll gave up 1.2 s
# earlier.
#
# **CORRECTED BY 777.** The sentence that stood here read: "and the interval it sits in contains
# NO write to 0x34 at all. A non-zero there is CMD1 doing something after its poll ended; a zero
# is CMD1 doing nothing." THAT IS FALSE AND THE SECOND ROW IS UNREACHABLE. st_all_send_cid
# writes INT_ENABLE 0x34 at entry_storage.c:3110, and _cid_stale is c2.stale, read inside
# st_send_command at :2560 - AFTER that store and after its own inhibit gate. 736 measured that
# any write to 0x34 SETS bit 15 (SDHCI_INT_ERROR) by itself and nothing clears it, so the cell is
# >= 0x00008000 on EVERY run by construction. Read it as == 0x00008000 EXACTLY (nothing but this
# arm's own store is in the register) versus anything ABOVE it. The value on any future press is
# unchanged; what was wrong was the sentence an operator would have read as a measurement.
# entry_storage.c:3167 carries the same false reason and is OWED to the next build (a comment
# there forces one, which is 771's COST rule). See experiment-777.
#
# CONTAINMENT, MEASURED: every new line sits inside `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 26`,
# and the value-25 build from this same source came out BYTE-IDENTICAL to the parked
# armed-storage-a1378a48 (a1378a48..., 5552764 bytes, exit 0). One press therefore answers
# the SDC1 pad question, rung 26's four cells AND these eleven, and rungs 23, 25 and 26
# need never be pressed.
#
# WHAT DID NOT MOVE, MEASURED: xnu_arm_entry.bin is 5552764 bytes - the SAME size as the arm
# before it, because the eleven keys fitted the padding - and since 770 measured that an
# unchanged size is NOT the claim that nothing moved, the claim rests on the hash (8096cb2c,
# different) and on the seam clause, which accepted STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR = 0x8004a2dc against
# the linked image, so the entry text did NOT cross a page. The payload's switch record is
# BYTE-IDENTICAL to rungs 23, 24, 25 and 26 (6c2b6038..., 682 bytes).
#
# A BUILD DEFECT WORTH RECORDING (m778): the first build of this arm was REFUSED with
# `entry_storage.c:116: error: missing terminating ' character`, and the cause was an ODD
# NUMBER OF APOSTROPHES in the appended ladder text - because `#error`'s text is a single
# DOUBLE-QUOTED STRING, an odd count leaves one character constant unterminated. Rewording
# three possessives took the inserted segment from 3 apostrophes to 0. The class is invisible
# in a diff and the compiler is the only reader that sees it.
#
# NO PRESS IS SPENT HERE AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.

# 776: THE RUNG-28 ARM - armed-storage-d5d98738, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=27.
# Built, parked and recorded here. HOST-SIDE ONLY: no device action, no press, no gate
# against a device, no runner, no firer. Rungs 27, 26, 25 and 23's parks are all intact;
# this arm SUPERSEDES rung 27 because it strictly contains it, MEASURED rather than argued
# (the value-26 build from this same source is byte-identical to 8096cb2c).
#
# WHAT IT IS. The FIRST STORE THIS LADDER HAS EVER MADE TO A SECOND DEVICE: st_pad_census -
# the body rung 25 added to READ the TLMM's SDC1 pad-control register at physical 0xFD512044
# - now ends in an `if`. When the 32-bit read already equals ST_TLMM_SDC1_EXPECT (0x00009F24,
# the value this board's own qcom,pad-pull-on / qcom,pad-drv-on arrays produce through the
# kernel's own shift and mask) it publishes _pad_write_skipped = 1 and _pad_writes = 0 and
# NO STORE IS MADE AT ALL. Otherwise it writes (raw & ~MASK) | EXPECT - ONE masked
# read-modify-write over the UNION OF THE SEVEN FIELDS, 0x0001FFFF: hdrive clk/cmd/data at
# bits 6/3/0 and pull clk/cmd/data/rclk at 13/11/9/15 - and publishes _pad_write_skipped = 0,
# _pad_writes = 1, _pad_wrote, _pad_after and _pad_after_match.
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_write_skipped / _writes / _wrote / _after / _after_match
#
# THE BITS OUTSIDE THE SEVEN ARE THE BITS THE READ HANDED BACK, carried through unchanged -
# which is what makes this a field write and not a whole-word write to a register a
# bootloader may have owned. And the value written is the VENDOR'S OWN: sdhci_msm_setup_pad
# (sdhci-msm.c:964) walks those same seven fields to those same two arrays, so the guard is
# what makes the ANSWER readable and NOT what makes the store safe - even an unguarded fire
# would be a word Linux itself stores at every CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON (sdhci-msm.c:2015-2032).
#
# THE STORE IS A PLAIN READ-MODIFY-WRITE WITH A MASK, field for field msm_tlmm_set_field's
# (gpio-msm-common.c:481-496): no unlock sequence, no write-one-to-clear, no FIFO, no shadow
# register - so one masked RMW over the union makes the vendor's six-call ORDER irrelevant,
# and that is why one store is safe here where six interleaved ones would need an argument.
#
# AND THE BUILD REFUSES THE ARM WITHOUT THE GUARD, TWICE AND INDEPENDENTLY. The store COUNT
# is 0 at values 24..26 and exactly 1 at 27, one clause read against the rung rather than two
# policies; the one `str` must be PREDICATED or SPANNED BY A CONDITIONAL BRANCH in the body's
# own disassembly (this arm is SPANNED-BY(8000ecdc) - the beq there spans the str at
# 0x8000edb4); and the SOURCE must carry exactly one `if (raw == ST_TLMM_SDC1_EXPECT) {` with
# the store in its else arm, read by line number (3565 and 3571 of src/entry/entry_storage.c).
# The disassembly reading is necessary and not sufficient and says so; the source reading is
# the sufficient half and is a grep, which is the weaker instrument and is named as one.
#
# THE ANSWER IS A FORK, AND ITS FIRST ROW IS THE ONE NOTHING IS WRITTEN ON. _pad_raw =
# 0x00009F24 with _pad_write_skipped = 1 and _pad_writes = 0 means the pads were ALREADY
# CONFIGURED - by the bootloader, since no rung and no Linux on this path wrote them (773
# section 2's latch) - and on that row 768 section 5's other half is retired and the pad
# candidate is CLOSED, leaving the PMIC rails alone (acts 1 and 3 of the vendor's BUS_ON
# branch, which 773 section 3 counted as unreachable from hc_mem). Anything else with
# _pad_write_skipped = 0 means the vendor act was never applied on this path and the write
# HAS BEEN MADE, with _pad_after = 0x00009F24 meaning it landed.
#
# THE SAFETY READING IS A COUNT AND NOT A CONSTANT: _pad_writes is published as what happened,
# so 0 and 1 are the only two values a well-formed run can carry.
#
# WHAT IT CANNOT DO: reach another register (one word, no indexed or block access); reach
# another pad bank (0x2044 is not SDC2's 0x2048, and the seven masks are disjoint so their
# union is their sum - 776 adds ST_TLMM_SDC1_ALL_MASK == 0x0001FFFF); change a FUNCTION (every
# field is drive strength or pull, and 769 section 2 established by layout that there is no
# function-select field to write); put the bus at risk (no transfer in flight); touch the
# PMIC, the rails or any clock. It is not the DLL path and not the clock register, so 764
# section 1 and 768 section 4 stay closed.
#
# AND IT STRICTLY CONTAINS RUNG 27, MEASURED: every new line sits inside
# `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 27`, and the value-26 build from this same source came out
# BYTE-IDENTICAL to the parked armed-storage-8096cb2c (8096cb2c..., 5552764 bytes, exit 0),
# with cmp agreeing against the park's own member. So ONE press answers the SDC1 pad question,
# the fix question, rung 27's eleven publishes, rung 26's four cells and rung 23's window -
# and rungs 23, 25, 26 and 27 need never be pressed.
#
# AND A COST ALREADY ON RECORD IS PAID IN THIS SAME BUILD: 771 refuted the 737 window
# paragraph in src/entry/entry_storage.c and left it standing for a COST reason and not a lane
# reason - correcting a comment forces a build, and the next build should be an arm that
# carries the correction. This is that build. The paragraph's rationale AS A PREDICTION ("a
# CMD2 sent the way CMD1 was would answer the same way") is withdrawn in place, with 771's
# measurement named beside it: CMD2 WAS sent inside that window and gained nothing, because
# _cid_inhibit_seen = 0 over all 1024 samples - the block never raised CMD_INHIBIT, so the
# window was open above a command that was never issued. The window itself stays, below the

# AND tools/verify_press_ready.sh's SHARED STORAGE PARAGRAPH IS REPAIRED HERE. It carried

# 785: THE RUNG-29 ARM - armed-storage-5fca6210, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=28.
# Built, parked and recorded here. Rungs 28, 27, 26, 25 and 23's parks are all intact; this arm
# SUPERSEDES rung 28 because it strictly contains it, MEASURED rather than argued (the value-27
# build from this same source is byte-identical to d5d98738, the parked rung-28 arm - see the
# containment paragraph).
#
# **SUPERSEDED IN PART: THIS ARM WAS PRESSED 2026-09-27 15:18:47-15:20:01 UTC, EXIT 0 (returned
# and captured). It is SPENT.** The press's reading, and a correction to one claim THIS block
# makes about which commands the widened window covers, are in the `# 786:` block at the end of
# this file. Read that block beside this one: the containment claim above and the description of
# the act are unchanged, and the sentence below that calls st_cmd_path's window "CMD0 and CMD1"
# is WRONG.
#
# WHAT IT IS. 783 read the scope of the widening rung 23 introduced and found it had reached ONE
# OF THREE command windows. 766's lesson (765 section 1) was that this block's INT_STATUS is a
# GATED VIEW - a status bit is visible only while its enable stands - and the repair was applied
# in st_cmd3_noidx, the LAST command this ladder sends. The two windows that carry the FIRST four
# commands were left writing the lone ST_SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE (0x00000001):
#   st_cmd_path     (src/entry/entry_storage.c:4353)  - CMD0 and CMD1
#   st_all_send_cid (src/entry/entry_storage.c:3121)  - CMD2
# This arm widens both to ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE_CMD == 0x000F0001 - the completion with the four
# command-level error bits, TIMEOUT | CRC | END_BIT | INDEX - which is the SAME constant rung 23
# put in st_cmd3_noidx and NOT a new one.
#
# SO THE ARM'S ONE ACT IS ONE CONSTANT, APPLIED AT TWO WINDOWS, WITH NO NEW STORE TO THE BLOCK.
# Both windows already stored to INT_ENABLE 0x34 on every arm from rung 14 and rung 16 on; what
# changes is the VALUE stored. The number of stores is unchanged, SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is still
# read and never written, and no new register, no new width, no new window, no new megabyte and
# no new device access of any other kind is added. The dead `#else` arms carry rung 14's and rung
# 16's exact lines, so at a value below 28 this source compiles to what it compiled to before.
#
# WHAT THE WIDENING BUYS, AND WHY IT IS WORTH A PRESS. On the rung-28 arm, two of the ladder's
# five commands reported `_cmd1_status_any = 0` and `_cid_status_any = 0` over their full 1.2 s
# windows. Those zeros were readings ABOUT THE MASK, not about the block: a bit the mask does not
# enable is a bit a gated view cannot show. CMD1 is SEND_OP_COND - the command an eMMC must answer
# for this ladder to move at all, and the one 764 section 5 named as the frontier's question -
# so widening here is what turns those two zeros into readings about the controller. The keys the
# widening changes are, per window, the same seven 724 owes every command:
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_status_any / _any_polls / _inhibit_last / _inhibit_seen (CMD1)
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_status_any  / _any_polls / _inhibit_last / _inhibit_seen (CMD2)
#   xnu_live_storage_ena_wrote / _cid_ena_wrote - the two words the arm now stores, published as
#   what was written, so the log carries the widen constant itself and not an inference from it.
#   BOTH KEYS ALREADY EXIST on the rung-28 arm (measured against that park's own entry ELF: one
#   string each), so this arm adds NO NEW KEY, NO NEW CELL AND NO NEW COUNT - what changes is the
#   VALUE one existing key carries, which is why the widen is read out of the log and not argued.
#
# THE ANSWER SPACE, FOUR ROWS PER WINDOW AND THE FIRST IS THE VALUABLE ONE. With the five bits
# enabled, `_status_any` is a count of polls in which ANY status bit stood:
#   * `_status_any` still 0 over 5,088,000-ish samples with `_any_polls` large means the block set
#     NO bit of INT_STATUS AT ALL during that command - not the completion and not TIMEOUT - which
#     is exactly the state rung 22's arm found for `nidx` (764 section 4) and would say the block's
#     silence at CMD1/CMD2 is the same silence, on a mask that could have shown a timeout.
#   * bit 15 (TIMEOUT, 0x00008000) alone means the command was issued, held the line, and the
#     block's own response timeout fired - the sticky bit rung 23's `_nidx_status_pre` already
#     carries for CMD1, now read at the window that ISSUED it rather than through a later latch.
#   * CRC / END_BIT / INDEX means the card answered and the answer was malformed.
#   * bit 0 alone means the completion bit is visible and the command finished.
#
# AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE THREE. At VALUE 28 the ladder has no command window left writing the
# one-bit word: st_cmd3_noidx was widened by rung 23 (its one-bit store at :3995 is the `#else`
# arm of that widen and is not built at 28), st_quiet_enable_probe (:3016) holds only the
# read-modify-write that closes its own window and carries NO command, and st_set_relative_addr /
# st_cmd3_noresp are compiled only at values 19 and 20 so they are absent from this arm entirely.
# `tools/report_int_enable_windows.py` now reads, over the whole file and every value: the
# FIVE-BIT word built at 3 sites - st_all_send_cid:3120 (>= 28), st_cmd3_noidx:3993 (>= 22),
# st_cmd_path:4351 (>= 28) - and reaching 3 stores, against the ONE-BIT word reaching only the
# command-less probe. That census is the tracked reading of 783's defect being closed rather than
# re-described, and 783 named a window-scope check as owed.
#
# AND IT STRICTLY CONTAINS RUNG 28, MEASURED TWICE, ON BOTH ARTIFACTS THAT MATTER. Every new line
# sits inside `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 28`, written as a whole `#if`/`#else` pair rather
# than as a conditional expression so that the value-27 source is what it was. Measured from this
# same source: the value-27 PAYLOAD came out BYTE-IDENTICAL to the parked armed-storage-d5d98738
# (stage90-qcdt.img 93026ca1..., 8572928 bytes), and the value-27 ENTRY IMAGE came out
# BYTE-IDENTICAL to that park's own xnu_arm_entry.bin (d5d98738..., 5552764 bytes). So the edit is
# surgical: nothing outside the >= 28 guards moved. The consequence is that the value-28 arm is
# rung 28 PLUS one constant per window and nothing else, and that rung 28's pad question (the
# _pad_raw four-row fork) is answered in the SAME press that answers this one - which is why rung
# 28 need never be pressed on its own.
#
# THE PAYLOAD'S OWN SWITCH SET DID NOT MOVE: stage90-build-config.txt is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the
# rung-28 park's member (6c2b6038..., 682 bytes) and carries STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1. The ENTRY's
# config differs in exactly two lines - STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE 27 -> 28 and the artifact's own
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY_SHA256 - and the entry's sources record differs in exactly two lines: the same
# sha256, and ONE source file's content hash, src/entry/entry_storage.c.
#
# THE SAFETY READING IS UNCHANGED IN KIND. `_ena_wrote` and `_cid_ena_wrote` are published as what
# was stored, so 0x000f0001 is a reading taken out of the log rather than a property of the source;
# the enables are the SAME TWO stores to INT_ENABLE 0x34, in the same `#if >= 14` / `#if >= 16`
# windows, with the same restore calls; SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is still read and never written; and the
# arm is still `fastboot boot` only with nothing flashed. Nothing in this build touches the PMIC,
# the rails, the clocks, the DLL, the pad register or any address outside the two windows that
# already existed.

# list that printed st_cmd_path's store as backing three words - 739's shape, a name with two
# producers. That tool now keys by (function, name), which is what makes the census above a
# reading rather than a statement, and the widening is the first arm that would have been
# MISREPORTED by the old key.

# AND THE OWED SITES THIS BUILD DOES NOT PAY STAY OWED AND NAMED: the four `5,088,000`s and the

# 786: THE RUNG-29 PRESS - WHAT IT MEASURED, AND ONE CLAIM OF 785's OWN THAT IT REFUTES.
# THE PRESS: 2026-09-27 15:18:47-15:20:01 UTC, EXIT 0 (returned and captured), the device back
# 29 s after `fastboot boot` (seen via adb). Readiness 5 of 5 exit 0; ONE gate exit 0 / 605 lines
# at 15:18:11; ONE runner; no firer; `fastboot boot` only; nothing flashed; serial 4a2fe00b;
# 33e80afe absent from BOTH lists. Capture out/stage90/captures/rung29-2win-20260927-152001-{last_kmsg.txt,gate.log,run.log},
# archived by hand; last_kmsg 633827 B sha256 f9012bf6... THE ARM IS SPENT. Every key below is read
# out of that capture, by name, and nothing here is inferred from the source except where said.
#
# 1. THE ARM'S OWN ACT IS CONFIRMED AT BOTH STORES. `xnu_live_storage_ena_wrote = 0x000f0001` and
# `xnu_live_storage_cid_ena_wrote = 0x000f0001` - the five-bit word, at the two windows 785
# widened, out of the log. And two readings BESIDE them that bound what an enable can be on this
# block: `_ena_wrote_back = 0x00000000` (the restore stores zero) and `_ena_readback = 0x00008000`
# (and it reads back 0x8000) - so `SDHCI_INT_ERROR` is a bit of INT_ENABLE that NO WRITE CLEARS,
# which 736 measured with a press and which every enable readback in this capture carries.
#
# 2. THE WIDENING PRODUCED A NAMED ERROR AT THE CMD2 WINDOW, AND THAT IS THE PRESS'S NEWS.
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_status_any = 0x00028000   (SDHCI_INT_ERR | SDHCI_INT_CRC)
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_err        = 0x00020000   (CRC alone, out of the driver's own CMD_MASK)
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_any_polls  = 0x00000001   (it stood at the FIRST poll)
#   xnu_live_storage_cid_complete   = 0, _cid_timeout = 0, _cid_polls = 0x00000001
# No arm before this one could have reported either bit: rung 16's pressed arm read
# `_cid_status_any = 0` and every arm since carried that as the CMD2 row. So the widening bought
# exactly the class of reading it was built for, at ONE of the two windows.
#
# 3. AND IT REFUTES 785's OWN CLAIM ABOUT THE OTHER ONE - WITH THE LOG's OWN ORDER, NOT WITH A
# READING OF THE SOURCE. 785 (and 783 before it) described st_cmd_path's window as "CMD0 and
# CMD1". IT IS CMD0 ALONE. The log says so by its own line order: `_ena_wrote_back = 0x00000000`
# is the RESTORE, and it prints at capture line 8789, BEFORE `_cmd1_status_any` at 8832. So CMD1
# ran with INT_ENABLE = 0x00008000, and
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_status_any = 0x00000000 over _cmd1_polls = 0x004da000 = 5,087,232
#   (NOT 5,088,256 - this step first wrote that number here and `make check` refused it at line
#   7062, which is 778's own class: 0x004DA000 is 5,087,232 and this file already carried the
#   correct pair at its line 5349. The 1,024 difference is the poll loop's own bound and not a
#   reading of this press's)
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_timeout    = 1
# is a reading under a ONE-BIT-plus-ERR mask and NOT under the widened word - the same masked
# reading rung 28 would have produced. The source says it in its own words and always did: the
# `>= 14` window's comment reads "the interval it was open for is exactly CMD0's own send and
# poll, and there is no path out of st_cmd_path that reaches the gate, CMD1, or a return with the
# enable still set". **783 read the SCOPE by counting which COMMAND each FUNCTION carries; a
# window is an INTERVAL, and the two are not the same question.** The false sentence is now in
# five places - the entry source's `>= 28` comment (src/entry/entry_storage.c, the RUNG 29 block
# at the `st_cmd_path` window), this file's `# 785:` block, tools/verify_press_ready.sh's value-28
# narration and its rung_para 28 paragraph, the 785 experiment doc and the 785 commit message.
# Corrected in place in this file and in tools/verify_press_ready.sh; the ENTRY SOURCE comment is
# COST-owed to the next build, because correcting a comment forces one (771's rule) and this arm
# is already spent.
#
# 4. AND IT NARROWS 765's PREMISE, WHICH IS THE SHARPER OF THE TWO FINDINGS. 765 established that
# INT_STATUS here is a GATED VIEW and every arm since has read `_status_any = 0` as a reading
# about a one-bit MASK. But `SDHCI_INT_ERROR` (bit 15, 0x8000) IS ENABLED ON EVERY ARM - item 1's
# `_ena_wrote_back`/`_ena_readback` pair is the measurement - and on this block INT_ERR TRACKS THE
# ERROR BITS rather than standing beside them:
#   `_nidx_status_any = 0x00018000` = ERR | TIMEOUT   (CMD3's TIMEOUT, rung 23's cell reproduced)
#   `_cid_status_any  = 0x00028000` = ERR | CRC       (item 2)
# So a zero `_status_any` on the narrow arms was NOT blind to the EXISTENCE of an error. What a
# one-bit command window could not show is the error's IDENTITY, and what it could not show at all
# is a non-error bit other than the completion. **765's premise is true and NARROWER than the
# ladder has been stating it: the widening buys identity, not existence - which is exactly what
# item 2 shows, and why the CMD2 row changes from 0 to a named CRC while the CMD1 row would not
# have changed at all.**
#
# 5. RUNG 28's PAD QUESTION IS ANSWERED, IN THE SAME PRESS, OFF 785's CONTAINMENT CLAIM - AND THE
# ANSWER IS THE ROW NOTHING IS WRITTEN ON.
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_raw          = 0x00009f24   (== _pad_expect: the register ALREADY held it)
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_match        = 1
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_write_skipped= 1
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_writes       = 0            (NOTHING WAS STORED)
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_cmd_pull     = 3, _pad_data_pull = 3, _pad_rclk_pull = 1,
#   xnu_live_storage_pad_clk_hdrv     = 4, _pad_cmd_hdrv = 4, _pad_data_hdrv = 4
# So the TLMM's SDC1 pad register was ALREADY at the vendor's CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON state when the
# arm read it - the bootloader put it there, since no rung and no Linux on this path wrote it -
# and 768 section 5's other half is RETIRED, with `PULL SDC1_CMD` present as 769 section 2's
# mechanism requires. **The guard held: the arm's first row is the one on which its ONE STORE does
# not happen, and `_pad_writes = 0` is that fact read out of the log.** 776 section 3's `0x9E00`
# row (pulls present, drive off) did not occur and the two-row fork's else arm is not the row this
# device is on.
#
# 6. THE CMD LINE MOVED DURING THREE OF THE FOUR COMMANDS, WHICH RETIRES A CANDIDATE.
#   _cmd0_cmdlow_seen = 0x1bc (444 of the first 1024 samples)  _cmd0_polls = 0x219
#   _cmd1_cmdlow_seen = 0x28a (650 of 1024)                    _cmd1_polls = 0x004da000
#   _nidx_cmdlow_seen = 0x176 (374 of 1024)                    _nidx_polls = 0x000005d5
#   _cid_cmdlow_seen  = 0x0 - and its window was ONE poll (_cid_polls = 1), so it is a window too
#   short to sample the line and NOT a reading that the line was still.
# 764 section 4's candidate 3 ("nothing was ever driven onto the bus") is therefore RETIRED: the
# block drives the line. What stays open is what the line was driven WITH and what the card did
# about it.
#
# 7. WHAT THIS PRESS DOES NOT DECIDE, NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT. The CRC is a COMMAND-LEVEL error
# bit and the block raises it AT THE FIRST POLL after the command word was written; rung 6 set the
# card clock to 400 kHz, at which a 48-bit command and its response take ~240 us, so the window
# CMD2's own cells describe (`_cid_ticks = 0x11`) is far too short for a card's answer to have been
# clocked in. `_cid_stale = 1` with `_cid_clear_after = 0` says the latch was CLEAN before the
# command, so the bit did not stand there from an earlier window - but whether it is (a) the card
# answering and the block refusing the answer, (b) the block signalling an ILLEGAL COMMAND because
# CMD1's 1.2 s timeout left its command state machine wedged (rung 19 measured exactly that
# wedge), or (c) the bit re-asserting from the pad the arm did not write, is NOT decided by this
# press. `_nidx_status_pre = 0x00028000` - the same two bits still set at CMD3's window, one
# window later - is the cell that says the pair is STICKY across at least one further command, and
# it is the cheapest next reading there is: it is already in this capture.
#
# NOTHING HERE IS A NEW ARM, A NEW FILE IN out/, OR A DEVICE ACTION: out/ is byte-identical
# before and after this block. THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET - the block names an error on CMD2 and
# still completes no transfer, no filesystem is reached and no mount is made, so
# TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.

# 787: THE RUNG-30 ARM - armed-storage-dfc4ae78, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=29.
# Built, parked and recorded here. Rung 29's park (armed-storage-5fca6210) is intact and is SPENT;
# every park before it is intact. This arm SUPERSEDES rung 29 because it strictly contains it,
# MEASURED rather than argued (the value-28 build from this same source is byte-identical to the
# parked rung-29 arm and to its own xnu_arm_entry.bin - see the containment paragraph). NO PRESS IS
# SPENT AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED: the press is the operator's, and the path to it is the two commands
# readiness prints, run by hand once each.
#
# WHAT IT IS. 786 read rung 29's press and found, out of the capture's own line order, that rung
# 14's restore - `_ena_wrote_back = 0x00000000` at capture line 8789 - runs BEFORE the
# between-commands gate and therefore before CMD1. So `st_cmd_path`'s widened window covers CMD0
# alone, and CMD1 has run with the completion bit NOT enabled on every arm from rung 14 on:
#
#   _cmd1_status_any = 0x00000000   over   _cmd1_polls = 0x004da000 = 5,087,232
#   _cmd1_timeout    = 0x00000001   and    _cmd1_complete = 0x00000000
#
# CMD1 is SEND_OP_COND - the command an eMMC must answer for this ladder to move at all, and the
# question 764 section 5 left open. So this arm is the first time the ladder gives that command an
# enable that can report anything.
#
# THE ACT IS TWO MORE STORES TO THE SAME INT_ENABLE 0x34, IN AN INTERVAL THE LADDER HAS BEEN
# RUNNING CMD1 IN ALL ALONG. Same register, same constant (ST_SDHCI_INT_ENABLE_CMD == 0x000F0001 -
# rung 23's five bits: RESPONSE | TIMEOUT | CRC | END_BIT | INDEX, and NOT the vendor's eleven),
# same width, no new address, no new megabyte, no new device. SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is still READ and
# never written, so nothing here can raise a line this image cannot service. The window's two ends
# are bodies of their own - st_cmd1_enable_open (:2976, called once at :4526) and st_cmd1_enable_restore (:2999, called once at :4582) - called
# once each from st_cmd_path, which is what keeps that function's OWN device surface at rung 14's
# and keeps every rung-14 clause in build_entry.sh true at the digit rather than relaxed. The value
# it puts back is the word the open RETURNED, so `_c1_ena_wrote == _c1_ena_wrote_back` is a READING
# and not two evaluations of one expression.
#
# NO NEW KEY AND NO NEW STATUS CELL. The two bodies publish only the four words that make the
# window itself readable - _c1_ena_wrote, _c1_ena_held, _c1_ena_wrote_back, _c1_ena_readback - and
# NEITHER BODY TOUCHES INT_STATUS 0x30: each is one store to INT_ENABLE 0x34 and its readback, which
# is the same one-register surface rung 14's own restore has. So what this arm changes is what the
# CMD1 cells the ladder ALREADY CARRIES can mean - _cmd1_status_any, _cmd1_err, _cmd1_complete,
# _cmd1_timeout, and _cmd1_stale / _cmd1_status_after, which are st_send_command's own entrance and
# exit reads of 0x30 and are published for every command - and not a new reading beside them. That
# is rung 23's shape one command over: the widening changed what CMD3's existing cells could mean.
# NO NEW REGISTER, NO NEW WIDTH, NO NEW WINDOW IN ANY OTHER FUNCTION, NO NEW MEGABYTE, NO NEW
# DEVICE, NO NEW COMMAND.
#
# THE ANSWER SPACE, FOUR ROWS, AND THE FIRST IS THE ONE THAT MOVES THE LADDER. With the five bits
# standing across CMD1's send and its poll:
#   * bit 0 (SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE) VISIBLE means CMD1 COMPLETED and the block latched it. The latch is
#     a hardware event the enable only GATES THE VIEW OF, so on that row the 1.2 s "timeout" every
#     arm from rung 14 on has reported was THIS LADDER'S OWN MASK and the completion may have been
#     there all along. That row changes what the next arm is: the ladder stops asking what is wrong
#     with the card and starts reading CMD1's own answer.
#   * ERR | TIMEOUT (0x00018000) means the block armed its OWN response timeout and the card did not
#     answer within it - the reading CMD3 already carries (_nidx_status_any = 0x00018000 beside
#     _nidx_status_pre = 0x00028000) - and puts the subject on the card and on the power/clock path
#     784 gave a shape to.
#   * CRC, END_BIT or INDEX means the card answered and the answer was malformed.
#   * NOTHING AT ALL with all five bits enabled is the strong negative: the block set no bit of
#     INT_STATUS during a command it was given.
#
# **AND THE ERR | TIMEOUT ROW IS NOT ALREADY EXCLUDED, WHICH IS THIS ARM'S SECOND QUESTION.** 786
# section 4 read the CMD2 and CMD3 rows (ERR | CRC, ERR | TIMEOUT) as showing that bit 15 TRACKS the
# error bits, and concluded that CMD1's row "would not have moved at all". But _ena_readback =
# 0x00008000 in rung 29's OWN capture says bit 15 STANDS during CMD1 - no write clears it - and BOTH
# of 786's rows come from arms on which the specific error bit was ENABLED. Two readings are open
# and this window separates them:
#   (a) bit 15 rises with any error whatever its own bit's enable - then no error occurred at CMD1
#       and only bit 0 can be new;
#   (b) bit 15 rises only when the specific error bit is enabled - then CMD1 may have timed out
#       INVISIBLY on every arm from rung 14 on, and 786 section 4's sentence is too strong.
#
# A NEGATIVE IS READ ONLY WHERE THE LINE MOVED (771's rule, one window over): _cmd1_cmdlow_seen on
# rung 29's capture is 0x0000028a = 650 of the first 1024 samples, so the CMD line DID move during
# CMD1 on that arm, and a zero status count beside it is a statement about the block and not about
# a bus nobody drove. _cmd1_polls and _cmd1_ticks are read BESIDE it, because a sampler slower than
# one bit period could miss a transmission and report a zero.
#
# NO CELL OF THIS ARM IS READ AS AN ANSWER FROM THE CARD. 0x40ff8080 stands in the RESPONSE
# registers after a command that TIMED OUT (_nidx_resp_pre = 0x00000000 then _nidx_resp_post =
# 0x40ff8080 beside _nidx_status_any = 0x00018000), the image never WRITES those registers (every
# one of its accesses to RESPONSE 0x10 is a read), and rung 19's capture records the same word
# BEFORE CMD3 was put on the bus. So a non-zero _cmd1_resp is NOT evidence the card answered. The
# comment at the head of CMD1 says of exactly this value "the card ANSWERED"; that inference is
# unsupported and is COST-owed to this arm's successor.
#
# AND THE CLAUSE IS THE READING, NOT THIS SENTENCE. build_entry.sh's `xnu_entry_787` asserts, against
# the LINKED image, for each of the two new bodies: its device set is exactly [f9824934:ldr
# f9824934:str], its image side is EMPTY, it is referenced exactly once from st_cmd_path, the OPEN's
# call falls BEFORE the second st_send_command and the CLOSE's AFTER it. The last two are the
# property 785's false sentence needed and are positions read out of the linked image, not out of a
# comment.

# THE PARK IS out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-dfc4ae78/ - ELEVEN MEMBERS, the eleven set= lines

# AND THE CONTAINMENT IS MEASURED ON ALL ELEVEN MEMBERS, WHICH IS STRONGER THAN THE TWO ARTIFACTS
# EARLIER RUNGS MEASURED. The value-28 entry build from this same source is byte-identical to the
# rung-29 park's xnu_arm_entry.bin (5fca6210), AND a payload built with that value-28 entry image is
# byte-identical to the rung-29 park on EVERY ONE of the eleven members - qcdt img, raw bin, build
# config, payload elf, manifest, entry bin, entry elf, entry config, entry sources, payload img and
# the fixture. So the delta this arm carries is exactly the >= 29 lines and nothing else, and there
# is no artifact on which the two arms agree by accident.
#
# WHAT THE ENTRY'S OWN RECORDS SAY THE DELTA IS: xnu_arm_entry-config.txt differs from the rung-29
# park's in EXACTLY TWO LINES (STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE 28 -> 29 and the artifact's own sha256),
# and xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt differs in EXACTLY THREE (that sha256, build_entry.sh eb9b6c7f ->

# (6c2b6038) to rungs 23 through 29, so NO PAYLOAD SWITCH MOVED and STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY is 1.
#
# THE SAFETY READING IS UNCHANGED IN KIND. _c1_ena_wrote and _c1_ena_wrote_back are published as the
# word stored and the word put back - and they are the SAME WORD because the close takes what the
# open returned - so the arm's own constant is read out of the log rather than inferred from the
# source. The store count is two more of the same store to INT_ENABLE 0x34; SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is
# still read and never written; neither new body touches INT_STATUS 0x30; and the arm is still
# `fastboot boot` only with nothing flashed. Nothing in this build touches the PMIC, the rails, the
# clocks, the DLL, the pad register or any address outside INT_ENABLE 0x34.
#
# AND THE CENSUS MOVED WITH THE ARM, READ RATHER THAN ASSERTED: tools/report_int_enable_windows.py
# now reports the five-bit word built at FOUR sites and reaching FOUR stores - st_all_send_cid,
# st_cmd1_enable_open, st_cmd3_noidx and st_cmd_path - where before this step it read three.
#
# AND THE BUILD CLAUSE IS WHAT MAKES THE ARM'S OWN CLAIM CHECKED RATHER THAN STATED: build_entry.sh
# gained xnu_entry_787, which asserts against the LINKED image that each new body exists, has
# device set exactly [f9824934:ldr f9824934:str], has an EMPTY image side, is called exactly once
# from st_cmd_path, and - the property 785's false sentence needed - that the OPEN's call falls
# BEFORE the second st_send_command and the CLOSE's AFTER it, both positions read out of the

# AND THE OWED SITES THIS BUILD DOES NOT PAY STAY OWED AND NAMED: the four `5,088,000`s and the
# mis-citation at src/entry/entry_storage.c:302-303 (COST-owed, still carried); the set-comparison
# pad repair (779 section 7, pre-registered, not built); the rung_para correction for values 12..23;
# the seam-address class; run_and_capture.sh's EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL; fdt_nodes's lack of a synthetic

# the head of CMD1, which 787 measured to be unsupported and which is COST-owed to this arm's

# 788: THE FOUR-ARM ENABLE MATRIX - HOST-SIDE ONLY, A READING OF CAPTURES ALREADY ON DISK.
# NOTHING WAS BUILT, NO SOURCE WAS EDITED AND out/ IS BYTE-IDENTICAL BEFORE AND AFTER THIS BLOCK,
# so the armed rung-30 arm (armed-storage-dfc4ae78, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=29) is unchanged and
# every set= line in this file still resolves. NO PRESS IS SPENT AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# 787 section 4 named two readings of what a widened window can do and could not choose between
# them without a press: (a) bit 15 (SDHCI_INT_ERROR) rises with ANY error whatever its own bit's
# enable, or (b) it rises only when the specific error bit is enabled - in which case CMD1 may have
# been timing out INVISIBLY on every arm from rung 14 on. 786 section 4 inferred (a) from two rows,
# and this step reads the case that separates them.
#
# WHAT THE ARCHIVE SHOWS, AND IT IS (b). Across SIX narrow arm-windows on disk, bit 15 STOOD in
# INT_ENABLE for the whole window (_cid_ena_held / _nidx_ena_held = 0x00008001 on every one) and
# INT_STATUS read ZERO for the whole window - _status_any is st_send_command's own field, the FIRST
# non-zero INT_STATUS of any kind read RAW with no mask (src/entry/entry_storage.c:2635-2637):
#
#   CMD2 (st_all_send_cid), enable written 0x00000001, held 0x00008001:
#     rung17-cid    v16   _cid_polls = 5,089,280   _cid_status_any = 0x00000000   _cid_timeout = 1
#     rung19-rca    v18   _cid_polls = 5,088,256   _cid_status_any = 0x00000000   _cid_timeout = 1
#     rung21-noidx  v20   _cid_polls = 5,088,256   _cid_status_any = 0x00000000   _cid_timeout = 1
#     rung24-cmdline v23  _cid_polls = 5,091,328   _cid_status_any = 0x00000000   _cid_timeout = 1
#     rung29-2win   v28   _cid_polls = 1           _cid_status_any = 0x00028000   _cid_err = 0x00020000
#   CMD3 (st_cmd3_noidx), enable written 0x00000001, held 0x00008001:
#     rung21-noidx  v20   _nidx_polls = 5,087,232  _nidx_status_any = 0x00000000  _nidx_timeout = 1
#     rung22-dllcensus v21 _nidx_polls = 5,093,376 _nidx_status_any = 0x00000000  _nidx_timeout = 1
#     rung24-cmdline v23  _nidx_polls = 1,789      _nidx_status_any = 0x00018000  _nidx_err = 0x00010000
#     rung29-2win   v28   _nidx_polls = 1,493      _nidx_status_any = 0x00018000  _nidx_err = 0x00010000
#
# 30,537,728 READS OF INT_STATUS, WITH SDHCI_INT_ERROR STANDING IN INT_ENABLE THE WHOLE TIME, AND
# BIT 15 APPEARED IN NONE OF THEM. The two arms whose enable ALSO carried the specific bits reported
# ERR | CRC and ERR | TIMEOUT AT THE FIRST POLL. So a standing bit 15 in INT_ENABLE is NOT
# sufficient for bit 15 to appear in INT_STATUS on this block, and 786 section 4's sentence - that
# bit 15 TRACKS the error bits so a zero _status_any on a narrow arm was never blind to an error's
# EXISTENCE - is refuted by the archive's own data: its two rows were both taken on arms whose
# enable carried the bit, which cannot distinguish TRACKING from CO-LATCHING.
#
# AND 786 SECTION 4's CONCLUSION `the widening buys identity, not existence` IS WITHDRAWN AS
# STATED. At these two windows the widening is what makes an error appear AT ALL.
#
# THE SAME MECHANISM REFUTES A 777 PREDICTION OF THE SAME SHAPE. _cid_stale is INT_STATUS as
# st_send_command finds it for CMD2, read AFTER st_all_send_cid's own store to INT_ENABLE
# (:3189 the store, :2560 the read inside the callee). 777 corrected one false sentence with
# `that store raises bit 15 by itself, so _cid_stale >= 0x00008000 on EVERY run and the ZERO row
# does not exist`. The ONLY arm carrying the key reads _cid_stale = 0x00000001 - bit 0, NOT bit 15,
# with _cid_clear_after = 0x00000000 beside it. (It is the only arm because rungs 25-28 were strict
# containments and were never pressed.) What the CMD2 window's own enable store leaves in INT_STATUS
# is the COMPLETION bit, not the error bit.
#
# WHAT THIS CHANGES ABOUT THE NEXT PRESS, AND NOTHING ELSE. _cmd1_status_any = 0, _cmd1_stale = 0
# and _cmd1_status_after = 0 on every arm carrying them are ONE MASKED READING TAKEN THREE TIMES and
# not three independent zeros: CMD1 has run under INT_ENABLE = 0x00008000 since rung 14. So on the
# rung-30 arm, which carries all five bits into that interval, a ZERO is the strongest negative this
# ladder can produce - the enable is not why a bit would be missing - and ERR | TIMEOUT is a LIVE
# ROW rather than an excluded one.
#
# THE RESIDUAL IS NAMED AND NOT HIDDEN: whether an enable gates a condition's VISIBILITY or whether
# it switches the CHECK on cannot be separated from these captures. The evidence leans to visibility
# - the error bit is present at the FIRST poll on the widened arms, and at CMD3 _nidx_status_pre is
# already ERR | TIMEOUT at the instant the store lands - but that is an argument and not a
# measurement, because _cid_stale read microseconds earlier does not yet carry the CRC. No row of
# the next arm is read as proof the card did or did not answer.
#
# THE ARM IS UNCHANGED BY ALL OF THIS: same bytes, same park, same switch set. The value of the
# press went up; its cost did not. THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET - no transfer completes, no filesystem
# is reached and no mount is made, so TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.

# 789: THE PRESENT_STATE CENSUS - HOST-SIDE ONLY, A READING OF CAPTURES ALREADY ON DISK.
# NOTHING WAS BUILT, NO SOURCE WAS EDITED AND out/ IS BYTE-IDENTICAL BEFORE AND AFTER THIS BLOCK,
# so the armed rung-30 arm (armed-storage-dfc4ae78, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=29) is unchanged and
# every set= line in this file still resolves. NO PRESS IS SPENT AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# THE CENSUS. Every PRESENT_STATE 0x24 word the ladder has ever published - 148 READS OVER 20
# CAPTURES - takes EXACTLY THREE values:
#   0x01f80000 x142   INHIBIT=0  CMD_LVL=HIGH   the steady state, every command's _ps_before/_ps_after
#   0x01f80001 x5     INHIBIT=1  CMD_LVL=HIGH   _nidx_ps_after on rungs 21/22/24/29, _rca_ps_after on 19
#   0x00f80000 x1     INHIBIT=0  CMD_LVL=LOW    _cid_ps_after on rung 29, the SECOND of its two lines
# Bits 21-23 (WP/CD/STABLE) are 111 in all three. Bits 17/18 are 0 in ALL 148 reads and bits 19/20 are
# 1 in all of them, so under the standard SDHCI map DAT[3] and DAT[2] read low at every measurement -
# CONSTANT across the archive and therefore a reading about nothing in this run. It is either this
# IP's fixed value for those bits or a genuinely static low, and NOTHING IN THE ARCHIVE SEPARATES THE
# TWO. Named, not concluded.
#
# AND THE SELECTOR HAD TO BE FIXED BEFORE THE CENSUS COULD BE TAKEN. A first pass keyed on \S*ps\S*
# swept in fourteen keys that are not PRESENT_STATE at all, and above all _clk_set_apps_before, whose
# 0x00004ff1 then appeared in the table as if it were a bus reading - because **`apps_before` CONTAINS
# `ps_before` as a substring**, which is the m-class a-name-that-contains-another-name. The census
# above is taken with the token ANCHORED AT THE END OF THE KEY. A census whose selector is looser than
# its claim produces a table that looks like a measurement.
#
# AND THE ONE KEY WITH TWO PRODUCERS IS THE ONE THAT PRODUCED THE UNIQUE VALUE.
# xnu_live_storage_cid_ps_after is published from TWO sites in the same function:
#   :3270  c2.ps_after            - the word st_send_command recorded AT THE END OF CMD2'S POLL
#   :3306  a FRESH READ of 0x24   - on the line after the window's exit, beside _cid_readback
# On rung 29's arm they printed DIFFERENT VALUES: 0x01f80000 at capture line 8867 and 0x00f80000 at
# line 8885. So one name covers two moments - the m739 class (a name with two producers) and the m732
# class (a cell's meaning is partly WHEN it was read) at once - and it is THE ONLY KEY IN THE ARCHIVE
# that carries two values in a single run. A reader quoting _cid_ps_after MUST SAY WHICH LINE.
#
# AND THE SECOND LINE IS THE ONLY PRESENT_STATE WORD IN THE ARCHIVE'S WHOLE HISTORY WITH THE CMD LINE
# READ LOW OUTSIDE THE POLL'S OWN SAMPLES - 1 read in 148. It sits after the window's one exit
# (_cid_wrote_back, the INT_ENABLE store, _cid_status_post, _cid_readback), so AT CMD2'S EXIT THE BLOCK
# STILL REPORTED ITS OWN CMD LINE LOW: the line was still being driven a whole window after the poll
# gave up. Its companion is _cmd1_inhibit_last = 0x00f80000, the last sample of CMD1's inhibit window,
# which 786 section 6 already recorded. TWO CELLS, TWO WINDOWS, THE SAME WORD. That is a WEDGE
# SIGNATURE, and it is not a new observation so much as a new place to read it: rung 19 found the
# inhibit held for all 1024 samples of CMD3's window, and this says the line itself is still low one
# window later. IT IS NOT EVIDENCE THE CARD IS HOLDING THE LINE - the block drives the line, and 788
# section 4's residual is untouched by it.
#
# AND NOTHING IN THE TREE CATCHES A KEY WITH TWO PRODUCERS. tools/read_storage_commands.py reasons
# about producer SHAPES of branch conditions, which is a different question, and it is NOT in make
# check; a grep over tools/ and src/entry/ finds no check that counts the ST_LIVE sites per key. So the
# m739 class is named repeatedly in this project's records AND ENFORCED NOWHERE, and the paragraph
# above is the demonstration that the class is live rather than hypothetical.
#
# AND ONE PIECE OF UNVERIFIED PROSE IS NAMED HERE. entry_storage.c:2360 carries, inside a
# _Static_assert MESSAGE, the bit map "23 is DAT[0], 24 is CMD, 25-27 are DAT[3:1]". The assert it
# belongs to checks only (ST_SDHCI_CMD_LINE_LEVEL & ~0xFF000000u) == 0u - THE BIT MAP IN THE MESSAGE IS
# READ BY NOTHING - and it disagrees with the standard map (16 CMD signal level, 17-20 DAT[3]..DAT[0],
# 23 card stable, 24 CMD level). Under the message's map bits 25-27 are 0 in all 148 reads; under the
# standard map bits 17/18 are. Both say some data lines are low; neither can be confirmed.
#
# THE ARM IS UNCHANGED AND THE NEXT PRESS IS UNCHANGED: same armed-storage-dfc4ae78, same park, same
# switch set, no park member moved, no press spent or authorized. THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET - no
# transfer completes, no filesystem is reached and no mount is made, so TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.

# 790: THE RUNG-30 PRESS - CMD1 COMPLETED, AND THE 1.2 s "TIMEOUT" EVERY ARM SINCE RUNG 14 REPORTED
# WAS THIS LADDER'S OWN MASK.
# ONE PRESS SPENT, AND IT WAS AUTHORIZED BY THE OPERATOR BEFORE THE GATE WAS RUN. No source was
# edited and nothing was built by this step, so every set= line in this file still resolves.

# THE PRESS: 2026-09-27 23:43:48-23:45:03 UTC, EXIT 0 (returned and captured), 75 s gate-to-capture.
# Readiness 5 of 5 exit 0; ONE gate exit 0 / 606 lines; ONE runner, the only one; no firer, none
# armed; `fastboot boot` only; nothing flashed; nothing written to storage; no reboot; serial
# 4a2fe00b; 33e80afe absent from BOTH adb devices and fastboot devices at fire time. Capture

# hand; last_kmsg 636238 B sha256 af710630a189b0dad84965482b1fd6b80e841144c8020b5f8135d88e09e97bd3.
# THE ARM IS SPENT, and `armed-storage-dfc4ae78` is now the LAST SPENT park; rung 29's
# `armed-storage-5fca6210` and every park before it are intact and remain the fallback.
