# THE RUNNER'S VERDICT IS 7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 3 UNREAD - ELEVEN CRITERIA, PRINTED TWICE IN ONE LOG
# (the gate's reading of the log as it stood at gate time, which is the PREVIOUS capture, and the
# runner's of this one). Counting log LINES gives 14 PASS / 2 FAIL / 6 UNREAD and is the arithmetic
# 756 recorded as wrong; criteria are named, not counted. The single FAIL is the known
# `seam_sp ... is not sleh_sp-8` criterion and the UNREADs are the known absent-key ones - SO THIS
# PRESS ADDED NO FAILURE. Its verdict is rung 29's, criterion for criterion.
#
# 1. THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS 787 SECTION 3's FIRST ROW AND NO OTHER. CMD1 COMPLETED:
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_complete     = 0x00000001
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_status_any   = 0x00000001   BIT 0, SDHCI_INT_RESPONSE, AND NO ERROR BIT
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_err          = 0x00000000
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_timeout      = 0x00000000
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_any_polls    = 0x000004d4 = 1236   (rung 29: 0x004da000 = 5087232)
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_ticks        = 0x00002823 = 10275  (rung 29: 0x015f9e16 = 23045142)
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_stale        = 0x00000001   (rung 29: 0)
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_clear_wrote  = 0x00000001   and _cmd1_clear_after = 0x00000000
#   xnu_live_storage_cmd1_cmdlow_seen  = 0x0000028b = 651 of 1024   (rung 29: 650)
#   xnu_live_storage_c1_ena_wrote       = _c1_ena_wrote_back   = 0x000f0001
#   xnu_live_storage_c1_ena_held        = _c1_ena_readback    = 0x000f8001
# THE POLL COUNT COLLAPSED BY A FACTOR OF 4117 AND THE TIMEOUT BIT CLEARED. Of 787 section 3's four
# rows, ERR|TIMEOUT is not the row, CRC/END_BIT/INDEX is not the row, and "nothing at all" is not
# the row: WITH ALL FOUR ERROR BITS ENABLED THE BLOCK RAISED NONE OF THEM. The stall was this
# ladder's own mask, at the one command window the ladder has read as "the block raised nothing"
# since rung 16. `_c1_ena_wrote == _c1_ena_wrote_back` is a READING, not two evaluations of one
# expression, which is what 787 section 2 built the two bodies to make it.
#
# 2. THE CONTROL THAT MAKES IT A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A COINCIDENCE. _cmd0_ticks - the same boot, the
# same bus, the same clock, the one command this ladder has ever seen complete with NO RESPONSE
# DEMAND - is 0x1327 = 4903 on rung 29 and 0x132a = 4906 on rung 30: CMD0 REPRODUCES ACROSS TWO ARMS
# TO THREE TICKS, 0.06 PER CENT. So the tick base is stable between the arms and a ratio above 1 at
# CMD1 is not timer noise. CMD1's own completed duration is 0x2823 = 10275 against CMD0's 4906 -
# A RATIO OF 2.094. CMD0 is one 48-bit command frame with nothing coming back; CMD1 is the same
# shape with RESP_PRESENT set. A ratio near 2 is what a command PLUS ITS RESPONSE looks like beside
# a command alone (one extra frame gives exactly 2.00; the 0.094 residual is overhead the two do not
# share). NAMED AS AN ARGUMENT AND NOT A PROOF: with two points, t = N x frame + overhead cannot be
# solved for frame and overhead separately, so this is consistent with "+1 frame" WITHOUT fixing
# the frame length, and a block whose completion latency simply differs for a response-demanding
# word would also give a ratio above 1. What it does rule out is a CMD1 that took no time at all.
#
# 3. THE COMPANIONS. CMD2 IS BYTE FOR BYTE UNCHANGED - _cid_status_any = 0x00028000 (ERR|CRC) at
# _cid_any_polls = 0x00000001, _cid_err = 0x00020000, _cid_sent = 0x00000001, _cid_complete = 0,
# _cid_ticks = 0x00000011 - so the CMD1 window left no trace on CMD2's own status, the frontier
# moved one command down, and IT DID NOT MOVE AT CMD2.
#
# AND CMD3 WAS NOT SENT ON THIS ARM, WHERE RUNG 29 SENT IT. _nidx_sent = 0, _nidx_word = 0,
# _nidx_arg_wrote = 0, _nidx_polls = 0, _nidx_ticks = 0, _nidx_status_any = 0, while _nidx_calls = 1
# and _nidx_gated = 0 - so st_cmd3_noidx WAS CALLED (its window opened: _nidx_ena_wrote =
# 0x000f0001, _nidx_ena_held = 0x000f8001, _nidx_status_pre = 0x00028000) and the call into
# st_send_command RETURNED BEFORE IT STORED ANYTHING. The cause is the driver's own guard:
# st_send_command (src/entry/entry_storage.c:2545-2558) waits for SDHCI_CMD_INHIBIT to clear with
# the driver's own bound (sdhci.c:1096) and on the bound expiring returns with `sent` still 0. THE
# EVIDENCE IT IS THAT RETURN AND NOT THE LATCH GUARD BELOW IT is _nidx_clear_after = 0x00000000:
# the second guard returns ONLY when clear_after is non-zero, so a zero there rules it out BY
# CONSTRUCTION. The block was still inhibited by CMD2 ten milliseconds after CMD2's window closed.
#
# AND THAT IS WHERE THE TWO ARMS DIFFER, WITH CMD2's OWN STATUS IDENTICAL:
#   _cid_inhibit_after   rung 29 0x00000000  ->  rung 30 0x00000001
#   _cid_inhibit_last    rung 29 0x01f80000  ->  rung 30 0x01f80001
#   _cid_inhibit_seen    rung 29 0x00000000  ->  rung 30 0x00000001
#   _cid_ps_after        rung 29 0x01f80000 / 0x00f80000  ->  rung 30 0x01f80001 / 0x00f80001
# SO CMD2 WEDGED AND STAYED WEDGED. A cell this arm cannot explain, named rather than smoothed
# over: the two arms' CMD2 status words and command words are identical, so the inhibit's
# persistence is not a consequence of anything either log measures. The candidate is the state CMD2
# was entered in - on rung 30 CMD1 had actually completed, on rung 29 it had "timed out" - and NO
# CELL IN EITHER CAPTURE DISCRIMINATES THAT FROM A BARE TIMING DIFFERENCE.
#
# 4. A NEW PRESENT_STATE WORD, AND IT IS THE ONE 789 SECTION 1's CENSUS WAS MISSING. 789 took the
# archive's 148 PRESENT_STATE words and found exactly three values, in which CMD_INHIBIT (bit 0) and
# the CMD line's level (bit 24) NEVER APPEARED TOGETHER:
#   0x01f80000 x142   INHIBIT=0  CMD_LVL=HIGH
#   0x01f80001 x5     INHIBIT=1  CMD_LVL=HIGH
#   0x00f80000 x1     INHIBIT=0  CMD_LVL=LOW
# RUNG 30 ADDS A FOURTH, AND IT IS _cid_ps_after's FIRST PRODUCER:
#   0x00f80001 x1     INHIBIT=1  CMD_LVL=LOW
# IT IS THE FIRST WORD IN THE ARCHIVE THAT SAYS THE BLOCK IS HOLDING A COMMAND IN PROGRESS WHILE
# ITS OWN CMD LINE IS BEING DRIVEN DOWN - the in-flight signature 789 section 1 could not find and
# 789 section 3 could only approach from one window later. 789 section 3's wedge is therefore
# measured on a second arm, and it reads STRONGER on this one: 789 had to reach into CMD2's
# post-window read to find the low line, and here the block reports it INSIDE the poll with the
# inhibit bit beside it. The 789 census in that block above is superseded by this four-row table.
#
# 5. _cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080 IS IDENTICAL ON RUNG 19, RUNG 29 AND RUNG 30 - INCLUDING THE TWO WHOSE
# CMD1 WINDOW WAS CLOSED - with _cmd0_resp = 0x00000000 on all three. So it is not this arm's own
# reading, and 787 section 6's warning holds in the STRONGER direction: a non-zero _cmd1_resp is not
# evidence the card answered, AND it is now also not evidence that anything this arm changed had any
# effect on the RESPONSE registers. What the sequence does say, and it depends on NO ENABLE BIT:
# _cmd0_resp is a reading taken BEFORE CMD1, _cmd1_resp is a reading taken AFTER it, the ladder never
# writes those registers, so THE WORD ENTERED THEM DURING CMD1 ON EVERY ARM, seen or not. Rung 19
# puts a third point on it: its _rca_resp_pre = 0x40ff8080 is read BEFORE CMD3 is on the bus. AND IT
# STILL IS NOT AN ANSWER: three different commands read the same word out of it, the ladder never
# brackets CMD1's own registers with a fresh pre/post pair (_cmd1_raw_pre*/_cmd1_raw_post* DO NOT
# EXIST, unlike _cid_raw0..3 and _nidx_raw_pre*/_nidx_raw_post*), and _cmd1_resp_voltage = 0x00ff8000
# is OCR bits 23:15 all-ones - a legal full voltage window and equally what a static pattern looks
# like. THE PRE/POST PAIR AROUND CMD1 IS THE CHEAPEST ADDITION A FUTURE ARM CAN MAKE, and it is owed.
#
# 6. 786 SECTION 4 AND 787 SECTION 4: ONE REFUTED, ONE CONFIRMED FOR THE WRONG REASON.
# 786 section 4's conclusion - "CMD1's row would not have moved at all" - IS REFUTED: the row moved,
# from zero to bit 0. 787 section 4's candidate (b) - "bit 15 rises only when the specific error bit
# is enabled, so CMD1 may have been timing out invisibly on every arm from rung 14 on" - IS REFUTED
# BY THIS PRESS: the window carried all four error bits enabled and CMD1 produced NO ERROR BIT, so
# there was no invisible timeout. 787 section 4's candidate (a) reached the RIGHT CONSEQUENCE ("no
# error occurred at CMD1 and only bit 0 can be new") ON A MECHANISM 788 SECTION 3 HAD ALREADY KILLED:
# (a) says bit 15 rises with any error whatever its own enable, and 788 measured 30537728 zero reads
# with bit 15 standing in INT_ENABLE. SO THE ACCOUNT THAT ACTUALLY FITS IS NEITHER (a) NOR (b) AS
# WRITTEN: it is 788 section 3's - the narrow window was BLIND - carried to the CMD1 window. 786
# section 4 is right about the OUTCOME and wrong about the REASON, which is the third time on this
# ladder that a correct conclusion has been reached from a mechanism a later arm killed.
#
# 7. THE NEXT ARM'S SUBJECT. The question is no longer why CMD1 stalls - it does not. It is WHY CMD2
# GETS AN IMMEDIATE CRC WHILE CMD1 COMPLETES, and this press hands the next arm a contrast it did
# not have:
#   CMD1  SEND_OP_COND(1)   flags 0x02 (PRESENT)          word 0x00000102   48-bit, NO CRC   COMPLETED, poll 1236, no error
#   CMD2  ALL_SEND_CID(2)   flags 0x07 (PRESENT|136|CRC)  word 0x00000209   136-bit, CRC     ERR|CRC at poll 1, _cid_ticks = 0x11
# THREE THINGS DIFFER AND ONLY THREE: the response LENGTH (48 vs 136 bits), the CRC check (off vs
# on), and the OPCODE. CMD2's error arrives at poll 1 - 17 ticks, about 0.9 us after the command
# store, in a window whose 136-bit response cannot possibly have been shifted. That is 786 section
# 7's anomaly and 788 section 7's owed item, and it is now the cheapest thing in the ladder to pin.
#
# 8. WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It does not reach the goal - no transfer completes, no filesystem is
# reached, no mount is made, so TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD. It does not send CMD3 (section 3). It
# does not read a card answer (section 5). It does not touch the PMIC, the rails, the clocks, the
# DLL, the pad register, or any address outside INT_ENABLE 0x34 - 784's rail reading stands: the
# eMMC's two rails are RPM resources with no `reg`, and no rung can power them with a store. It
# builds nothing and edits no source, so the parked arms and every set= line above are unchanged.
# NO PRESS IS AUTHORIZED: rung 30 is SPENT and NO FURTHER ARM IS ARMED.
#
# 9. OWED, AND NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE INFERRED. c3.inhibit_timeout IS NOT PUBLISHED for the
# nidx family - st_cmd3_noidx's publisher block carries c3.timed_out as _nidx_timeout but NOT
# c3.inhibit_timeout - so CMD3's refusal is INFERABLE from _nidx_clear_after and not READABLE; that
# is COST-owed to a build. A _cmd1_raw_pre*/_cmd1_raw_post* PAIR AROUND CMD1's OWN RESPONSE
# REGISTERS is owed to a build (section 5). THE CMD2/CMD3 CRC's TIMING is named for the third step
# running and is still undecided (section 7). WHY CMD2's INHIBIT PERSISTED ON THIS ARM AND CLEARED

# 791: THE CMD2 CENSUS - THE IMMEDIATE CRC IS UNIQUE TO THE ONE COMMAND THAT ASKS FOR A 136-BIT
# RESPONSE, AND THE NARROW ARMS' ZERO IS A MASKED READING, MEASURED.
# HOST-SIDE ONLY. NOTHING WAS BUILT, NO SOURCE WAS EDITED AND out/ IS BYTE-IDENTICAL BEFORE AND AFTER
# THIS BLOCK, so the spent rung-30 arm's park (armed-storage-dfc4ae78, 11 members) and every park
# before it are unchanged and every set= line in this file still resolves. NO PRESS IS SPENT, NO ARM
# IS ARMED AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# THE CENSUS. Nine arms carry CMD2's cells and EXACTLY THE TWO WHOSE CMD2 WINDOW CARRIED THE CRC
# ENABLE READ NON-ZERO:
#   rung 29 / rung 30   INT_ENABLE 0x000f0001   _cid_status_any = 0x00028000   at _cid_any_polls = 0x1
#   rungs 17/18/19/20/21/22/24   INT_ENABLE 0x00000001   _cid_status_any = 0x00000000   over ~5.09M polls
# 0x00028000 is SDHCI_INT_ERR (bit 15) | SDHCI_INT_CRC (bit 17).
#
# THE BRACKET, AND IT LEAVES NOTHING BETWEEN THE CLEAN READ AND THE LATCH:
#   _cid_stale       = 0x00000001   INT_STATUS at CMD2's entrance, AFTER its own 0x34 store: bit 0 only
#   _cid_clear_wrote = 0x00000001   the write-1-to-clear
#   _cid_clear_after = 0x00000000   THE LATCH WAS CLEAN
#   _cid_word = _cid_word_read = 0x00000209   the block accepted the word into its COMMAND register
#   _cid_status_any = 0x00028000 at _cid_any_polls = 0x00000001
# The only device accesses between _cid_clear_after and that first poll are the ARGUMENT store, the
# COMMAND store, and the COMMAND and PRESENT_STATE readbacks. So the block latched ERR|CRC between
# being handed the command word and the next read of INT_STATUS.
#
# AND IT IS FAR TOO EARLY FOR A RESPONSE, SAID WITHOUT ANY CLOCK FREQUENCY. _cmd0_ticks - the same
# counter, boot and bus, for one 48-bit no-response frame - is 0x132d/0x132a/0x1325/0x132d/0x1328/
# 0x1327/0x132a = 4909/4906/4901/4909/4904/4903/4906 across SEVEN arms (spread 8 ticks, 0.16 per cent).
# _cid_ticks = 0x00000011 = 17 IS 0.35 PER CENT OF ONE SUCH FRAME - before a start bit, a command index
# and a CRC could have been shifted out, let alone a 136-bit response shifted in. The narrow arms' CMD2
# poll ran _cid_ticks = 0x015f9xxx = about 23,040,000 ticks, roughly 4700 _cmd0 frames, and read zero
# at every one of them.
#
# THE ISOLATION: THE CORRELATE IS THE RESPONSE LENGTH AND NOT THE CRC ENABLE. Three commands have now
# been given a wide enough enable, and the command word is a property of the COMMAND and not of the
# enable:
#   CMD1  SEND_OP_COND       PRESENT        0x0102   48-bit   CRC check OFF   COMPLETED, poll 1236, no error bit
#   CMD3  SET_RELATIVE_ADDR  R1_NOIDX       0x030a   48-bit   CRC check ON    ERR|TIMEOUT, BIT 17 NEVER SET
#   CMD2  ALL_SEND_CID       R2             0x0209   136-bit  CRC check ON    ERR|CRC at poll 1, 17 ticks
# CMD3's word carries the SAME 0x08 CRC bit as CMD2's, so the CRC bit's presence is not explained by the
# command's own CRC check; the one thing CMD2 has that no other command in this ladder has is a 136-BIT
# RESPONSE REQUEST. The table's missing corner is 136-bit with the CRC check OFF - ONE BIT.
#
# AND THE NARROW ARMS' ZERO IS A MASKED READING, MEASURED RATHER THAN INFERRED. Rung 24 is the one
# narrow arm whose NEXT body opens a WIDE window, and that makes it the control the census otherwise
# lacks:
#   _cid_ena_wrote   = 0x00000001   one bit, no CRC
#   _cid_status_any  = 0x00000000   over _cid_polls = 0x004db000 = 5091328 reads
#   _cid_status_post = 0x00000000   read with INT_ENABLE restored to 0
#   _nidx_status_pre = 0x00018000   THE NEXT BODY, under the FIVE-BIT word
# ERR|TIMEOUT was sitting in the latch at the top of the next body, one command after a window whose
# poll had read zero 5,091,328 times. So at CMD2 (1) 788 section 3's conclusion is now a MEASUREMENT AT
# THIS WINDOW rather than an inference from other windows, and (2) _cid_status_post = 0 DOES NOT MEAN
# THE LATCH IS CLEAN, because it is read through INT_ENABLE = 0.
#
# AND THAT SECOND POINT IS A NAMED DEFECT CLASS, NOT ONE CELL. Every *_wrote_back cell in the archive
# is 0x00000000 - every window in this ladder closes by storing int_enable, which is zero - so ALL FOUR
# *_status_post CELLS ARE 100 PER CENT MASKED READS:
#   _ena_status_post (14 onward) / _cid_status_post (16 onward) / _nidx_status_post (20 onward) /
#   _rca_status_post (18/19) - every one of them 0x00000000 on every arm.
# The _*_readback = 0x00008000 beside them is the tell that the store happened at all (bit 15 stands in
# INT_ENABLE and no write clears it) and is not itself a status reading.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT SEPARATE, NAMED AND NOT LEFT. On the narrow control the latched value has bit 16 and
# NOT bit 17 while the command word is IDENTICAL, so two mechanisms are admissible: (i) the enable
# switches the CHECK on, so no CRC check runs without the CRC enable and rung 24's condition was the
# block's own response timeout; or (ii) the enable gates VISIBILITY only and the CRC bit is an EARLY
# SPURIOUS LATCH raised when a CRC check is enabled on a 136-bit request. And a third asymmetry a
# reader must not read past: because the wide arms BREAK AT POLL 1, the ladder has NEVER observed
# whether a wide enable at CMD2 later latches TIMEOUT as well - the wide reading is a FIRST latch and
# the narrow reading is a LATER one, and whether they are one condition seen twice or two conditions is
# not in the archive.
#
# THE PRE-REGISTERED NEXT ARM IS ONE BIT OUT OF CMD2'S FLAGS, AND ITS BEST ROW REACHES THE CID. CMD2
# (ALL_SEND_CID, opcode 2, argument 0) sent with MMC_RSP_PRESENT | MMC_RSP_136 - the CRC flag dropped
# and NOTHING ELSE MOVED: same opcode, same argument, same window, same position in the driver's order,
# same five-bit INT_ENABLE. Flags 0x07 -> PRESENT|136, word 0x0209 -> 0x0201. Dropping the CRC check
# from a 136-bit response is legal on the wire: the card still returns the CID and the block simply does
# not check it. FOUR ROWS:
#   _cid_complete = 1          THE CID IS TAKEN - the largest single step available from here, after
#                              which the driver order is CMD9 (CSD), CMD7 (select), CMD16, then data.
#   ERR|TIMEOUT (0x00018000)   the CRC latch needs the command's own CRC flag, and the real condition
#                              at CMD2 is the block's own response timeout - which moves the subject to
#                              why a 136-bit response never arrives.
#   ERR|CRC (0x00028000) anyway  the CRC bit is not about the command's CRC flag at all, WHICH REFUTES
#                              the isolation above and puts the 136-bit path itself on the block.
#   nothing, full bound, and the next wide read shows TIMEOUT   the same as row 2 from the other side,
#                              and it separates (i) from (ii) by whether the bit appears at all.
# The control is in the same log: _cmd0_ticks and CMD1's own completion bracket the new word from both
# ends. THE ARM IS PRE-REGISTERED AND NOT BUILT - building it moves out/, and a build is a later step's
# act, not this one's.
#
# WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It builds nothing and spends no press; out/ is byte-identical and every park is
# intact. It does not decide (i) versus (ii), and it does not say whether CMD2's condition is on the card
# or on the block - it isolates the CORRELATE and does not name the cause. And IT DOES NOT REACH THE
# GOAL: no transfer completes, no filesystem is reached and no mount is made, so TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS
# WITHHELD.
#
# OWED: the pre-registered one-bit arm above (built by a later step, pressed only by the operator); the
# *_status_post class, whose repair is a read of INT_STATUS BEFORE the restore at each window (a source
# change, COST-owed to a build); whether the wide enable at CMD2 also latches TIMEOUT later (one more
# read after the break would carry it); and, unchanged from 787-790, the 0x40ff8080 "the card ANSWERED"
# comment at CMD1's head, a _cmd1_raw_pre*/_cmd1_raw_post* pair around CMD1's own RESPONSE registers,
# c3.inhibit_timeout not published for the nidx family, the four 5,088,000s and the mis-citation at
# src/entry/entry_storage.c:302-303, a check that counts ST_LIVE sites per key (789 section 4), the
# _Static_assert message's bit map at entry_storage.c:2360 (789 section 5), _cid_ps_after's second
# producer (789 section 2), the set-comparison pad repair (779 section 7), the rung_para correction for
# values 12..23, the seam-address class, run_and_capture.sh's EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL, fdt_nodes's lack of a
# synthetic FDT cell (782 section 6), 784's rail_name cell, and 783's window-scope check.
#
# CARRIED INTO tools/verify_press_ready.sh's rung-29 narration, which is what the next press prints.

# 792: THE RUNG-31 ARM - armed-storage-f9613649, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=30.
# Built, parked and recorded here. Rung 30's park (armed-storage-dfc4ae78) is intact and is SPENT;
# every park before it is intact. This arm SUPERSEDES rung 30 because it strictly contains it,
# MEASURED rather than argued and with the delta NAMED rather than denied (see the containment
# paragraph). NO PRESS IS SPENT AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED: the press is the operator's, and the path to
# it is the two commands readiness prints, run by hand once each.
#
# WHAT IT IS. 791 censused every CMD2 this ladder has ever sent - nine arms carry its cells - and found
# the immediate ERR | CRC (0x00028000 at the FIRST poll) on exactly the TWO arms whose CMD2 window
# carried the CRC enable. The command word is a property of the COMMAND and not of the enable, and the
# ladder has given three commands a wide enable:
#
#   CMD1  SEND_OP_COND       PRESENT    0x0102  48-bit   CRC check OFF   COMPLETED, poll 1236, no error
#   CMD3  SET_RELATIVE_ADDR  R1_NOIDX   0x030a  48-bit   CRC check ON    ERR|TIMEOUT, BIT 17 NEVER SET
#   CMD2  ALL_SEND_CID       R2         0x0209  136-bit  CRC check ON    ERR|CRC at poll 1, 17 ticks
#
# CMD3's word carries the SAME 0x08 CRC bit as CMD2's (_nidx_flags = 0x00000005), so the CRC bit's
# presence is not explained by the command's own CRC check. The one thing CMD2 has that no other
# command in this ladder has is a 136-BIT RESPONSE REQUEST. THE MISSING CORNER OF THAT TABLE IS
# 136-BIT WITH THE CRC CHECK OFF, AND IT IS ONE BIT.
#
# THE ACT IS ONE CONSTANT. st_all_send_cid sends ALL_SEND_CID with ST_MMC_RSP_R2_NOCRC in place of
# ST_MMC_RSP_R2 - the same opcode, the same argument, the same window, the same position in the
# driver order and the same five-bit INT_ENABLE 0x34 - so the word moves from 0x0209 to 0x0201 and
# NOTHING ELSE MOVES. Dropping the CRC check from a 136-bit response is LEGAL ON THE WIRE: the card
# returns the CID either way and the block simply does not check it, so an arm that completes here has
# TAKEN THE CID and not merely removed a check.
#
# NO NEW KEY AND NO NEW ADDRESS. _cid_flags publishes the value and _cid_word / _cid_word_read publish
# the folded word, so the whole change is readable out of keys the ladder ALREADY CARRIES - rung 23's
# and rung 30's shape one command over. No new device, no new width, no new megabyte, no new register
# and no new window.
#
# THE TWO LINES ARE WRITTEN AS A WHOLE ALTERNATIVE rather than as a variable assigned in two arms, so
# that at every value below 30 the source is the original text character for character and containment
# is STRUCTURAL. Measured anyway:
#
#   THE VALUE-29 BUILD FROM THIS SAME SOURCE REPRODUCES THE SPENT RUNG-30 PARK armed-storage-dfc4ae78
#   ON TEN OF ELEVEN MEMBERS, BYTE FOR BYTE - qcdt img, raw bin, payload build config, payload elf,
#   payload img, fixture, manifest, entry bin, entry elf and the entry switch record, every one
#   identical. The ELEVENTH member is xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt, WHICH IS THE RECORD OF THE SOURCE
#   FILES BY CONTENT, and its diff is EXACTLY TWO LINES OF TWENTY-EIGHT:
#
#     build_entry.sh    5e9bca03 -> 07bce057
#     entry_storage.c   6afc5d29 -> cdf1056d
#
#   ...which are EXACTLY THE TWO FILES THIS STEP EDITED. So the delta this arm carries at value 29 is
#   NOT IN THE ARTIFACT AT ALL, and the only member that moved moved BECAUSE THE SOURCE MOVED, which
#   is what that member is for. That is a STRONGER statement than "all eleven identical": it NAMES the
#   delta instead of asserting none.
#
# AND THE ARM ITSELF DIFFERS FROM THE SPENT RUNG 30 IN TWO BYTES. The value-30 entry image and the
# value-29 entry image are the SAME SIZE (5,552,764 bytes) - and 770 measured that an unchanged size
# is NOT the claim that nothing moved - so the claim is MEASURED instead: cmp -l finds EXACTLY TWO
# DIFFERING BYTES in the whole 5.5 MB image, and they are TWO INSTRUCTIONS OF ONE BODY:
#
#   8000def8   mov r1, #7   ->   mov r1, #3     the _cid_flags PUBLISH's own argument
#   8000df08   mov r2, #7   ->   mov r2, #3     the FLAGS ARGUMENT to st_send_command
#
# Two bytes in the entry bin and the same two in the entry elf (whose size is likewise unchanged at
# 6,732,404 bytes). The entry switch record differs from rung 30's in EXACTLY TWO LINES (the probe
# number and the artifact's own sha256) and the entry sources record in EXACTLY THREE (that sha256
# plus the two edited files). stage90-build-config.txt is BYTE-IDENTICAL to rungs 23 through 30
# (6c2b6038), so NO PAYLOAD SWITCH MOVED and STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY is 1.
#
# AND THE CLAUSE IS THE READING, NOT A SENTENCE IN THIS BLOCK. build_entry.sh gained xnu_entry_792,
# which reads the ONE place the linked image is unambiguous about this arm: the argument-register
# setup immediately before the bl to st_send_command. For a four-argument call GCC emits r3 first and
# r0 last, so the last mov r2, #imm above that bl is the flags word. The clause pins all three -
# r0 = #2 (ALL_SEND_CID), r1 = #0 (no argument), r2 = #3 at value 30 and #7 below it - AND THE
# EXPECTED VALUE IS DERIVED FROM THE SWITCH, so it is a TWO-WAY clause: a constant which leaked DOWN
# the ladder is refused by the same check that catches a rung which did not take effect. It fired on
# this build:
#
#   xnu_entry_792: ... st_all_send_cid loads [r0=#2 (ALL_SEND_CID), r1=#0 (no argument), r2=#3
#   (MMC_RSP_PRESENT | MMC_RSP_136 (word 0x0201))] immediately before its bl to st_send_command
#   (disassembly line 53 of the body at 0x8000de60) ...
#
# THE OTHER SIDE OF THAT CALL IS CARRIED IN THE SOURCE BY A _Static_assert PAIR, because the clause
# can see the flags ARGUMENT and cannot see the command WORD the callee folds out of it
# (ST_SDHCI_CMD_FLAGS runs inside st_send_command): ST_SDHCI_CMD_WORD(ALL_SEND_CID, R2_NOCRC) == 0x0201
# and R2_NOCRC == (R2 & ~CRC). ONE ASSERTION ON EACH SIDE OF THE bl, AND NEITHER IS A COMMENT.
#
# AND THE CLAUSE GROUP THAT WAS ALREADY THERE IS UNCHANGED, WHICH IS THE POINT: it asserts what
# st_all_send_cid READS AND WRITES - device set, counts, program order, empty image side - and A BODY
# DEVICE SURFACE DOES NOT MOVE WHEN A CONSTANT MOVES. classify_body reports ADDRESSES and this arm
# changes an immediate in a register, which that clause set cannot see at all. Rung 16's record stands
# at the digit rather than being relaxed.
#
# THE SAFETY READING IS UNCHANGED IN KIND. The body's only writable register is still INT_ENABLE 0x34;
# SIGNAL_ENABLE 0x38 is still read and never written; the arm is still `fastboot boot` only with
# nothing flashed; and nothing in this build touches the PMIC, the rails, the clocks, the DLL, the pad
# register or any address outside INT_ENABLE 0x34 - 784's rail reading stands unchanged.
#
# THE FOUR ROWS, AND THE FIRST ONE IS THE ONE THAT MOVES THE LADDER:
#   _cid_complete = 1              THE CID IS TAKEN - the driver order opens: CMD9 (CSD), CMD7
#                                  (select), CMD16 (block length) and then data.
#   ERR|TIMEOUT (0x00018000)       the CRC latch needs the command's own CRC flag and the real
#                                  condition at CMD2 is a 136-bit response that never arrives.
#   ERR|CRC (0x00028000) ANYWAY    the strongest reading the arm can return: the CRC bit would then
#                                  not be about the command's CRC flag at all, refuting 791 section
#                                  4's isolation and putting the 136-bit path itself on the block.
#   nothing over the full bound    the negative, read with the next window's wide read beside it.
# The control is in the same log: _cmd0_ticks (4901 to 4909 across the seven arms carrying it, a spread
# of 0.16 per cent) and CMD1's own completion at poll 1236 bracket the new word from both ends, so a
# completion in tens of ticks, a timeout at the bound and a CRC at poll 1 are distinguishable WITHOUT
# A CLOCK FREQUENCY.
#
# AND 0x40ff8080 IS STILL NOT AN ANSWER ON ITS OWN: it stands in the RESPONSE registers on rungs 19,
# 29 and 30 whether the window was open or closed.
#
# THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET: no transfer completes, no filesystem is reached and no mount is made, so
# TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD. NO PRESS IS SPENT BY THIS STEP AND NONE IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# THE PARK IS out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-f9613649/ - ELEVEN MEMBERS, the eleven set= lines
# below, each verified IN PLACE by tools/verify_revert_set.sh against this record before this block
# was committed. The live out/stage90/ and the park were compared file by file with cmp and agree byte
# for byte.
#
# AND THE CENSUS MOVED WITH THE ARM, READ RATHER THAN ASSERTED: the gate reads the ladder's own bound
# out of entry_storage.c's #if guard, so raising it from 29 to 30 extended the range this record's
# value is checked against WITHOUT a constant being written into the gate. tools/report_int_enable_
# windows.py still reports the five-bit word built at FOUR sites and reaching FOUR stores.

#
# 793: THE 136-BIT READ HAS BEEN TAKEN NINE TIMES AND NEVER ONCE HELD A CID, AND 792's ROW 1 IS AN
# OVERCLAIM CORRECTED BEFORE THE PRESS.
#
# HOST-SIDE ONLY. No device action, no press, no gate against a device, no runner, no firer, NOTHING
# BUILT. `out/` is byte-identical to what 792 left it: the armed arm `armed-storage-f9613649` is
# UNSPENT and its eleven-member park is intact, and no source under `src/` was edited. ONE PRESS SPENT:
# NONE. NO PRESS IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# WHY THIS BLOCK EXISTS. 792 section 7 pre-registered rung 31's four rows and gave the first as
# "`_cid_complete = 1` - THE CID IS TAKEN", and the same sentence is carried into
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`, the two places whose text the next press PRINTS. The claim rests on an
# assumption that was never checked: that the four RESPONSE words read after CMD2 would be A CID if the
# command completed. The archive refutes it, nine times over.
#
# (1) THE FOUR WORDS ARE READ UNCONDITIONALLY. `st_all_send_cid`'s tail (`src/entry/entry_storage.c`,
#     the block citing `sdhci.c:1163-1172`, the driver's own 136-bit branch, word 3 first) reads
#     RESPONSE +12/+8/+4/+0 and publishes `_cid_raw0..3` and `_cid_resp0..3`. There is NO `if` in that
#     block, so the cells fire on EVERY arm whatever the command did. Their CONTENT is a separate
#     question from `_cid_complete`.
#
# (2) NINE ARMS CARRY THEM AND ALL NINE ARE BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL. rungs 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 22, 24,
#     29 and 30 - seven narrow windows and two wide enough to report an error, two of them having
#     latched `ERR | CRC` at poll 1 - hold
#         `_cid_raw0..3`  = 0x0040ff80 / 0x80000000 / 0 / 0
#         `_cid_resp0..3` = 0x40ff8080 / 0 / 0 / 0
#     and the eight published words do not vary on any of them. A CID is 128 bits; three of four words
#     zero is not a CID. THE LADDER HAS NEVER READ A CID - and completing the command is not by itself
#     what would give it one, because these cells were read on nine arms that did NOT complete and read
#     the same thing.
#
# (3) AND THE WORD IS NOT A DEFAULT - THE BLOCK MOVES, AND THE LOG'S OWN LINE ORDER PROVES IT.
#     `st_resp_before` reads the SAME four offsets in the SAME `<< 8` arithmetic (deliberately, since
#     rung 18's question was "is the word the same before and after CMD2") and runs immediately BEFORE
#     `st_cmd_path()`. On the three arms carrying both bodies its line stands FIRST. Rung 30:
#         8674: `_rb_raw0        = 0`            (before the command path)
#         8777: `_cmd0_resp      = 0`            (RESPONSE+0, after CMD0)
#         8827: `_cmd1_resp      = 0x40ff8080`   (RESPONSE+0, after CMD1)
#         8875: `_cid_resp_short = 0`            (RESPONSE+0, after CMD2)
#         8876: `_cid_raw0       = 0x0040ff80`   (RESPONSE+12, after CMD2)
#     Rungs 18 and 29 are in the same order. THE REGISTER WAS ZERO WHEN THE LADDER ARRIVED, so
#     everything it later holds the block put there itself. It does NOT say which command moved it -
#     two commands, one interval, and no cell separates them.
#
# THE CORRECTION. `_cid_complete` is the command's completion latch, not the response. Row 1 becomes:
#
#   1a - `_cid_complete = 1` AND the register block CHANGED: `_cid_resp0` off `0x40ff8080`, or any of
#        `_cid_raw2`, `_cid_raw3`, `_cid_resp1..3` off ZERO (all four have been zero on EVERY arm this
#        ladder has ever run). THIS IS THE CID TAKEN, and the driver order then opens: CMD9 (CSD),
#        CMD7 (select), CMD16 (block length), then data.
#   1b - `_cid_complete = 1` with the nine-arm pattern UNCHANGED. A FIFTH ROW, AND A STRONGER ONE THAN
#        1a: the block completed a 136-bit command and its response register file NEVER FILLED. That
#        separates the completion latch from the response path and puts every `_cid_complete` reading
#        on this ladder under suspicion - 790 section 1's CMD1 row and its 2.094 ratio included.
#
# Rows 2 (`ERR | TIMEOUT`), 3 (`ERR | CRC` anyway) and 4 (nothing over the full bound) are unchanged,
# and row 3 is SHARPENED: `_cid_raw2`/`_cid_raw3` zero on the two wide arms is a second,
# enable-independent line that the CRC latch fires with no 136-bit response present.
#
# WHERE IT LANDED. `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`, in the two places that are the text the press prints:
# `entry_conseq`'s rung-30 branch and `rung_para 30`. Both carry the split now, APPENDED beside the
# original sentence rather than replacing it, so a reader who met the old text elsewhere finds the
# correction next to it. LANE, STATED RATHER THAN LEFT: that file is not `preflight_boot_check.sh`, the
# one file this session (run-experiment-526, the gate owner) owns, and `ListAgents` names two peers,
# NEITHER of them `mi4-ios6-1a` - so this is a LANE-ownerless case and NOT the COST case.
#
# COST. None to the armed arm: the value of the press went up, its cost did not. The next arm can make
# row 1b a READING instead of an eye comparison with one bit computed from the four raw words against
# their pre-command values - no new device access - and that is COST-owed.
#
#
# 794: THE ROW THAT SAID "THE DRIVER ORDER THEN OPENS" - THE IMAGE'S WHOLE COMMAND VOCABULARY IS
# CMD0...CMD3 AND THERE IS NO DATA PATH.
#
# HOST-SIDE ONLY. No device action, no press, no gate against a device, no runner, no firer, NOTHING
# BUILT. `out/` is byte-identical: `armed-storage-f9613649` is UNSPENT and its park is intact, and no
# source under `src/` was edited. ONE PRESS SPENT: NONE. NO PRESS IS AUTHORIZED.
#
# THE CLAIM UNDER TEST, and it was written four times in two steps: 792 section 7's row 1 -
# "`_cid_complete = 1` - THE CID IS TAKEN. The driver order opens: CMD9 (CSD), CMD7 (select), CMD16
# (block length), then data" - 793 section 5 keeping that consequence verbatim, and
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`'s `entry_conseq` (line 1104) and `rung_para 30` (line 1216) carrying it,
# SO IT IS THE TEXT THE NEXT PRESS PRINTS. That sentence is TRUE OF THE LINUX MMC DRIVER AND FALSE OF
# THIS IMAGE.
#
# (1) THE VOCABULARY IS FOUR OPCODES. `grep -cE '^#define ST_CMD_OP_' src/entry/entry_storage.c` = 4,
#     and they are opcodes 0, 1, 2 and 3: CMD0, CMD1, CMD2, CMD3. There is NO `ST_CMD_OP_SEND_CSD`
#     define at all, no body, and no call site.
#
# (2) AND THE FOURTH IS THE LAST. `st_cmd_path()` is called EXACTLY ONCE (`grep -c 'st_cmd_path();'` =
#     1) and the only thing after it in `entry_storage_probe` (`:5144`) is `st_pwr_irq_after()` and
#     then a return. Inside, the sequence is CMD0 (`:4506`), CMD1 (`:4603`), CMD2 (`:4678`/`:4680`),
#     CMD3 (`:4742`/`:4775`).
#
# (3) AND THERE IS NO DATA PATH - measured, anchored, case-SENSITIVE:
#         grep -rnE '\b(BLOCK_SIZE|BLOCK_COUNT|TRANSFER_MODE|ADMA|SDHCI_DATA|SDHCI_BLOCK|DMA_ADDRESS)\b' \
#              src/ --include=*.c --include=*.h
#     returns TWO lines and BOTH ARE COMMENTS - one of them the source's own "no data-path register at
#     all" (`:2076`). `ST_SDHCI_DMA_ADDRESS 0x00` is defined and READ TWICE AS A VERSION/IDENTITY WORD
#     (`:695`, `:4932`), never as a transfer address. The NAME is in the tree; the PATH is not.
#
# WHAT THIS CHANGES. A perfect CID at rung 31 does NOT move this ladder one command closer to
# 「挂载存储」. The ladder's frontier is TWO frontiers and only one had been named: (i) the CMD2 bit the
# last five steps have been on, and (ii) THE BREADTH GAP - the storage path is an INITIALIZATION
# SEQUENCE that stops after CMD3, and a mount needs CMD9, CMD7, CMD16 and a data path behind them.
# Row 1a is STILL WORTH THE PRESS: it is the last item this sequence asks for and it settles the 136-bit
# question. What changes is what row 1a BUYS.
#
# WHERE IT LANDED. `docs/experiments/**` is a historical record and is NOT rewritten, so 792's and 793's
# rows stand as written and this document supersedes them. The LIVE narration is corrected in place and
# APPENDED BESIDE the original sentence: `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`'s `entry_conseq` rung-30 branch
# and `rung_para 30`, the two places whose text the next press prints. LANE, STATED RATHER THAN LEFT:
# that file is not `preflight_boot_check.sh`, the one file this session (run-experiment-526, the gate
# owner) owns, and `ListAgents` names two peers NEITHER of them `mi4-ios6-1a` - so this is the
# LANE-ownerless case and NOT the COST case, THE SECOND TIME IN TWO STEPS the same file had no live
# owner to report a wrong press-facing sentence to.
#
# AND A DEFECT THIS STEP MADE ITSELF, recorded because the selector is the lesson. The first pass of
# that search was `-i` and UNANCHORED and returned fifteen lines - FOURTEEN OF THEM `deadman`
# (`stage90_disarm_deADMAn_timer`, `stage90_arm_deADMAn_reset`, `g_deADMAn_armed`). The selector
# matched a substring of a word it was never about, and a table built from that output would have said
# the tree contains ADMA machinery. Anchored (`\b...\b`) and case-sensitive it is two hits, both
# comments. Class: a selector looser than its claim (789 section 1's `apps_before` containing
# `ps_before`), here with the CASE flag as the un-tightened dimension.
#
# COST. None to the armed arm: no source was edited and `out/` did not move. The next BUILD's subject is
# named in section 9 of the record: THE FIRST BODY PAST CMD3, and the `ST_CMD_OP_SEND_CSD` constant with
# its `_Static_assert` pair ahead of it - COST-owed to builds, not to a press.
#
#
# 795: THE RUNG-31 PRESS - THE IMMEDIATE CRC NEEDED THE FLAG AS WELL AS THE 136-BIT REQUEST, AND WHAT
# REPLACED IT IS THE BLOCK'S OWN 665.2 us TIMEOUT, WHICH IS AN UNSET REGISTER.
#
# ONE PRESS SPENT, AND IT WAS AUTHORIZED BY THE OPERATOR BEFORE THE GATE WAS RUN. One gate (exit 0, 607
# lines), one runner (EXIT 0), one `fastboot boot`, nothing flashed, nothing written to storage, no
# reboot. `out/` moved: the arm in it is rung 31's and it is now SPENT. `4a2fe00b` present as 'device';
# `33e80afe` ABSENT from both `adb devices` and `fastboot devices` at fire time. Fired 2026-09-28
# 03:43:44 UTC. Capture `out/stage90/captures/rung31-nocrc-20260928-034344-last_kmsg.txt` 633,586 B
# sha256 `9c0baf80380f33ad67d00dbf73c7c59e647d4afe59ec238a2d192186e8989f8b`, archived by hand with its
# gate.log and run.log (the runner does NOT archive). The runner's verdict is 7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 3 UNREAD
# COUNTED PER CRITERION - eleven criteria, printed twice in one log (m751) - and it is identical to
# rungs 29's and 30's, so this press added no failure.
#
# THE ARM TOOK EFFECT. `_cid_flags = 0x00000003`; `_cid_word = _cid_word_read = 0x00000201` - the
# block's own copy of `COMMAND 0x0E`; `_cid_op = 2`.
#
# THE HEADLINE: ROW 2 LANDED. The latch moved `ERR | CRC` (`0x00028000`) at poll 1 in 17 ticks to
# `ERR | TIMEOUT` (`0x00018000`) at poll 1,797 (= `0x00000705`) in 12,770 ticks (= `0x000031e2`) - a
# 751x longer windup - with `_cid_err` `0x00020000` -> `0x00010000` and `_cid_complete = 0`. SO THE
# IMMEDIATE CRC NEEDS BOTH CONJUNCTS: a 136-bit request AND the CRC check in the command word. 791
# section 4 named the length and missed that the flag is necessary - CMD3 carries the flag and never
# latched bit 17, and this arm does not either.
#
# AND THE 136-BIT COMMAND WAS ACTUALLY DRIVEN THIS TIME. `_cid_cmdlow_seen` moved 0 -> 445 of 1024
# (`0x1bd`); on rung 30 it was 0, because the immediate CRC landed BEFORE the command went on the wire.
# `_cid_inhibit_after` moved 1 -> 0, so CMD2 no longer wedged the block, so CMD3 WAS SENT
# (`_nidx_sent = 0x1`) for the first time since rung 19.
#
# 12,770 TICKS IS 665.2 us, AND IT IS A CONSTANT. CMD3 in the same boot: `_nidx_polls = 0x701` = 1,793,
# `_nidx_ticks = 0x31e4` = 12,772 = 665.3 us, `_nidx_status_any = 0x00018000`, `_nidx_err = 0x00010000`,
# `_nidx_cmdlow_seen = 0x187` = 391 of 1024 - the same bound to within 0.02 per cent, and the same bound
# rung 24 measured (768's block: "a break at poll 1789 in 665.2 us = TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x00's 2^13 TMCLK
# cycles").
#
# AND THE REGISTER THAT SETS IT HAS NEVER BEEN WRITTEN. `grep -n 'ST_SDHCI_TIMEOUT_CONTROL'` returns the
# define (`entry_storage.c:212`) and ONE READ (`:4233`). `_nidx_tout_ctl = 0x00000000` - the reset value,
# the SHORTEST bound the register can express. The source's own comment says why: the vendor writes it
# only from `sdhci_prepare_data` (`sdhci.c:827-828`), inside `if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY))`,
# and EVERY COMMAND THIS LADDER SENDS IS DATA-LESS. So every response demand in this ladder has been
# racing a 665.2 us bound no rung has ever set.
#
# THE TIMING LADDER, one boot, one counter: CMD0 `_cmd0_ticks = 0x1327` = 4,903 = 255.4 us; CMD1
# `_cmd1_ticks = 0x2824` = 10,276 = 535.2 us and COMPLETED (`_cmd1_complete = 1`,
# `_cmd1_status_any = 0x1`, `_cmd1_any_polls = 0x4d4` = 1,236); CMD2 12,770 = 665.2 us and CMD3 12,772 =
# 665.3 us, BOTH TIMED OUT. CMD1's 48-bit response arrives 130 us INSIDE the bound - the narrowest margin
# in the ladder, and why CMD1 is the one command that completes.
#
# AND THE ARITHMETIC, WITH ITS ASSUMPTION NAMED. One 48-bit response added 5,373 ticks (10,276 - 4,903).
# A 136-bit response is 88 bits more, so at the same measured per-bit rate - THE ASSUMPTION; the rate is
# not separately measured - it lands 88 x (5,373/48) = 9,850 ticks after CMD1's, at 20,126 ticks
# = 1,048.2 us against a 665.2 us bound, i.e. the bound is 63 per cent of what is needed. The rate that
# implies is 5.83 us/bit = 171.5 kHz against rung 6's configured 400 kHz (2.5 us/bit) and THIS STEP DOES
# NOT EXPLAIN THAT. The conclusion survives either rate: at 400 kHz a 136-bit response still needs 340 us
# of shifting on top of CMD1's 535.2 us = 875 us, still past the bound.
#
# WHY CMD3 FAILS IS A DIFFERENT QUESTION AND THE ARITHMETIC DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT. CMD3's 48-bit response
# should land at about CMD1's 535.2 us - same length, same clock, same window shape - and it does not.
# The claim this record offers is the protocol's own and it is NAMED AS A CLAIM, NOT MEASURED: a card
# enters IDENT state only by accepting CMD2, and CMD3 is only accepted in IDENT. CMD2 has never
# completed, so the card has never entered IDENT, so CMD3 gets no answer. Falsifiable cheaply: if CMD2 is
# made to complete and CMD3 still times out, the account is wrong.
#
# SO THE NEXT ARM IS THE FIRST REPAIR THIS LADDER HAS HAD, NOT ANOTHER MEASUREMENT: ONE BYTE STORE to
# `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` above 0, before CMD2. `0x01` doubles the bound to 1,330.4 us; `0x02` gives
# 2,660.8 us; the register's maximum is `0x0E`. THE PREDICTION IS NUMERIC. Rows: `_cid_complete = 1` AND
# the four words CHANGED (`_cid_raw0..3`, `_cid_resp0..3` - already read, already published, no new cell
# needed) is THE CID TAKEN; `_cid_complete = 1` with the words unchanged is the fifth row 793 section 5
# named, now on an arm where the bound is not the suspect; `ERR | TIMEOUT` again at a LONGER tick count
# REFUTES the arithmetic and says by how much; `ERR | CRC` returning refutes section 2. Its safety
# surface is one byte, one register, no new address and no new device, and `TIMEOUT_CONTROL` is a
# register the DRIVER ITSELF WRITES on the data path - the vendor's act, not a new one.
#
# WHAT THIS CHANGES ABOUT THE LADDER'S RECORD. (a) 791 section 4 is REFINED, not confirmed and not
# refuted: the immediate CRC requires both conjuncts. (b) 786 section 7's and 788 section 7's anomaly is
# EXPLAINED: the CRC at poll 1 of a window whose 136-bit response cannot have been shifted was NEVER a
# report about the card - it is what this block raises when handed a 136-bit request with its CRC check
# enabled. (c) 790 section 5's `0x40ff8080` discount is UNCHANGED and STRENGTHENED: identical on rungs
# 19, 29, 30 and 31, including one where the block drove the bus and timed out. (d) The frontier moved
# from CMD2 to the TIMEOUT REGISTER.
#
# THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No transfer completes, no filesystem is reached, no mount is made, so
# 「让os可以正常启动并且挂载存储」 is not reached and TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD. NO PRESS IS
# AUTHORIZED: rung 31 is spent and no further arm is armed.
#
# 796: THE RUNG-32 ARM - armed-storage-c1f89600, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=31 (switch VALUE 31 =
# ordinal rung 32). **THIS IS THE FIRST REPAIR ANY RUNG OF THIS LADDER HAS MADE, AND IT IS ONE BYTE
# IN ONE REGISTER.**
#
# THE READING IT ACTS ON IS 795's OWN AND IT IS A NEGATIVE: `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` HAD NEVER BEEN
# WRITTEN BY ANY RUNG. grep returns the define (entry_storage.c:212) and ONE READ (:4233), and rung
# 31's own capture read the register back as `_nidx_tout_ctl = 0x00000000` - the RESET VALUE and the
# SHORTEST bound the register can express. The vendor writes it only from `sdhci_prepare_data`
# (sdhci.c:827-828), inside `if (data || (cmd->flags & MMC_RSP_BUSY))`, and EVERY COMMAND THIS LADDER
# SENDS IS DATA-LESS. So every response demand in this ladder has been racing a 665.2 us bound no rung
# ever set, and 795 section 1 measured that constant twice in one boot: CMD2 `_cid_ticks = 0x31e2` =
# 12,770 = 665.2 us and CMD3 `_nidx_ticks = 0x31e4` = 12,772 = 665.3 us.
#
# THE ACT: one body reads the byte, one body stores a compile-time constant into it, and the constant
# is `ST_SDHCI_TIMEOUT_CMD2 = 0x03u` = 5,321.6 us. Its arithmetic, from 795's own ladder: the reset
# bound is 665.2 us, the field doubles per step, so 0x03 is 5.076x the reset bound and 4.68x the
# 1,048.2 us the record's extrapolation puts a 136-bit response at - AND it stays 225x UNDER the
# driver's own 1.200 s poll bound (sdhci.c's `timeout = 10` in 100 ms units), which is the other end
# this value must not cross: at or above 0x0B the device timeout stops being observable at all because
# the ladder's own poll gives up first. The two `_Static_assert`s in entry_storage.c put those two
# ends on the constant at COMPILE TIME (`>= 0x01`, `<= 0x0E`, and the field's own high nibble clear).
#
# THE TWO BODIES ARE BODIES OF THEIR OWN, and that is the same shape 787 used for CMD1's window:
# `st_tout_open` (0x8000de60, 124 bytes) and `st_tout_restore` (0x8000e250, 76 bytes), both
# `noinline, noclone`, each called from `st_cmd_path` and nowhere else. Their device surface is
# `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` AS A BYTE and NOTHING ELSE, measured out of the linked image rather than
# asserted: `ldrb r4, [r5, #46]` / `strb r3, [r5, #46]` with `mov r3, #3` in the open, and
# `strb r4, [r3, #46]` / `ldrb r1, [r3, #46]` in the restore. `0x2E` is one byte below
# `SOFTWARE_RESET 0x2F` (rung 4 writes it) and one byte above `CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C` (rung 6 writes it)
# - two registers this ladder HAS written - so a body that drifted one offset would write a register
# with an entirely different meaning, which is why xnu_entry_796 asserts the offset and the width and
# not merely the address.
#
# SIX KEYS, and they are the window's own state and not a new measurement of the bus: `_tout_calls`
# (the open ran), `_tout_was` (the byte the register held - THE CONTROL, expected 0 on a first run),
# `_tout_wrote` (the constant), `_tout_held` (the readback, expected 3), `_tout_wrote_back` (the byte
# put back), `_tout_readback` (the readback after the restore, expected 0). `_tout_was = 0` beside
# `_tout_held = 3` is the repair VISIBLE; `_tout_was = _tout_held = 0` would be an arm whose store
# never landed and whose every other cell reads exactly as rung 31's did, which is the one failure a
# log cannot otherwise show.
#
# THE WINDOW IS THE CMD2 INTERVAL AND CMD3 IS DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE IT. Read out of the linked image:
# open at 0x8000ed20, the gate's refusal-path close at 0x8000ed70, CMD2's call at 0x8000edb8, the
# CMD2-path close at 0x8000edc8, CMD3's call at 0x8000ede8. **The close is TWO CALLS IN THE IMAGE
# WHERE THE SOURCE HAS ONE**, and that is GCC duplicating the tail of the `if/else` that holds CMD2
# into both of the gate's arms - each path runs exactly one of them. xnu_entry_796 asserts the count
# as a SHAPE (1 open exactly; 1 to 3 closes) and the POSITIONS over every line: every open above
# CMD2, the LAST close below CMD2, and every close above CMD3. **The first draft of the clause
# demanded exactly one close and refused the build**; the refusal was right about the source and wrong
# about the image, and it is recorded in the clause itself rather than silently relaxed.
#
# CMD3 STAYS AT THE RESET BOUND ON PURPOSE, and that is a deliberate part of the arm. CMD3's 48-bit
# response fits inside 665.2 us (795 section 6), so CMD3 is the independent test of 795 section 6's
# protocol claim - a card enters IDENT only by accepting CMD2, and CMD3 is only accepted in IDENT.
# A window that covered CMD3 as well would give CMD3 the same help CMD2 is getting and the two rows
# would stop being separable. **The falsification is cheap and named: if CMD2 is made to complete and
# CMD3 STILL times out, 795 section 6's account is wrong.**
#
# THE ANSWER SPACE, and the prediction is NUMERIC rather than a direction:
#   `_cid_complete = 1` AND the four words CHANGED   THE CID IS TAKEN. 793 section 1's row 1a.
#                                                    `_cid_raw0..3` and `_cid_resp0..3` are already
#                                                    read and already published, so no new cell is
#                                                    needed to see it.
#   `_cid_complete = 1`, words UNCHANGED             793 section 5's fifth row, now on an arm where
#                                                    the bound is not the suspect.
#   `ERR | TIMEOUT` again, LONGER tick count         REFUTES 795's arithmetic, and says by how much.
#   `ERR | CRC` returning                            REFUTES 795 section 2 and puts the immediate
#                                                    CRC back on the block independent of the bound.
#
# WHAT THIS CHANGES ABOUT THE LADDER'S RECORD. (a) The frontier moves from the CMD2 command to the
# TIMEOUT REGISTER, and the register now has a producer. (b) 795 section 7's pre-registered next arm
# is made REAL rather than pre-registered. (c) Nothing about rungs 16 through 31 is superseded: their
# 665.2 us readings stand and this arm is the first that does not run under that bound.
#
# CONTAINMENT, MEASURED ON ALL ELEVEN MEMBERS AND NOT ARGUED. The value-30 build from THIS source
# was compared member by member against the SPENT rung-31 park
# out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-f9613649/. **Every line this step added that reaches the COMPILER
# at value 30 is unchanged - the value-31 code sits inside `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 31`
# guards - and the two edits that are not guarded (the guard's own bound, 30 -> 31, and the `#error`
# message's tail) are PREPROCESSOR-ONLY and produce no byte of the image.** That last clause is a
# claim, and the comparison below is what makes it a reading rather than a claim:
#
#   SAME stage90-qcdt.img   SAME stage90.bin        SAME stage90-build-config.txt
#   SAME stage90.elf        SAME stage90.img        SAME stage90_fixture.macho
#   SAME SHA256SUMS.txt     SAME xnu_arm_entry.bin  SAME xnu_arm_entry.elf
#   SAME xnu_arm_entry-config.txt
#   DIFF xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt  - and the diff is EXACTLY TWO LINES OF TWENTY-EIGHT, the two
#        files this step edited: src/entry/build_entry.sh 07bce057 -> 6f9d3c4b and
#        src/entry/entry_storage.c cdf1056d -> ea1d06c7. The artifact's own hash line is IDENTICAL,
#        because the value-30 entry bin reproduced f9613649a895... byte for byte.
#
#   **Ten of eleven byte-identical, and the eleventh explained line by line.** The manifest member is
#   the entry sources recorded BY CONTENT, so it MUST move when the tool is edited; the point is that
#   nothing else did. There is no artifact on which the two arms agree by accident.
#
# THE ARM'S OWN DELTA, measured rather than argued: entry `.text` 5,335,232 -> 5,335,424, **+192
# bytes** (the two bodies are 124 + 76 = 200 and the call sites are 4 bytes each, the difference being
# branch relaxation elsewhere); `xnu_arm_entry.elf` 6,732,404 -> 6,732,464, +60 bytes; the entry BIN
# is UNCHANGED in size at 5,552,764 bytes and differs in 593,023 bytes, which is the whole tail of
# `.text` shifted by the insertion and NOT 593,023 separate edits - 770's rule that an unchanged size
# is not the claim that nothing moved, applied in the other direction here. `stage90-build-config.txt`
# is byte-identical to rungs 23 through 31's, so no payload switch moved and STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY is 1.
#
# THE PARK IS out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-c1f89600/ - ELEVEN MEMBERS, the eleven set= lines
# below, each verified IN PLACE by tools/verify_revert_set.sh against this record. The live
# out/stage90/ and the park were compared file by file with cmp and agree byte for byte on all eleven.
#
# AND THE CENSUS MOVED WITH THE ARM, READ RATHER THAN ASSERTED: the gate reads the ladder's own bound
# out of entry_storage.c's #if guard, so raising it from 30 to 31 extended the range this record's
# value is checked against WITHOUT a constant being written into the gate.
#
# THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No transfer completes, no filesystem is reached, no mount is made, so
# 「让os可以正常启动并且挂载存储」 is not reached and TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD. **AN ARM IS
# ARMED AND NOT PRESSED, AND THE PRESS IS THE OPERATOR'S.** No firer exists; the press path is the two
# commands readiness prints, run by hand once each.
#

set=armed-storage-c1f89600 sha256=6b973e740a1f6315d4b133b170d290085d9ccdd7190bcebe7f69e4b439da6887 bytes=2335 file=xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt role=the-entry-image-s-own-sources-recorded-BY-CONTENT-so-the-gate-asks-whether-this-image-is-the-build-of-the-tree-in-front-of-it-and-not-whether-the-hashes-match-it-it-differs-from-the-spent-rung-31-park-s-member-in-EXACTLY-THREE-LINES-of-the-file-s-28-four-comment-lines-plus-one-header-line-plus-23-file-lines-the-artifact-s-own-sha256-and-EXACTLY-THE-TWO-FILES-THIS-STEP-EDITED-src-entry-build_entry-sh-07bce057-to-6f9d3c4b-and-src-entry-entry_storage-c-cdf1056d-to-ea1d06c7-and-in-the-VALUE-30-containment-comparison-the-same-member-differs-in-EXACTLY-TWO-LINES-because-there-the-artifact-sha256-line-is-IDENTICAL-and-the-file-set-is-IDENTICAL-23-files-in-both-so-nothing-was-added-or-removed-under-src-entry
# 798: THE RUNG-32 PRESS - THE REPAIR RAN AND THE BOUND DID NOT MOVE, SO `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` IS NOT
# WHAT ENDS THIS LADDER'S COMMANDS; AND CMD1 IS SENT ONCE WHERE THE DRIVER LOOPS.
#
# ONE PRESS SPENT, authorized by the operator BEFORE the gate was run. One gate (exit 0, 608 lines),
# one runner (EXIT 0), one `fastboot boot`, nothing flashed, nothing written to storage, no reboot;
# `33e80afe` absent from BOTH device lists at fire time; capture
# out/stage90/captures/rung32-tout-20260928-081329-{last_kmsg.txt,gate.log,run.log}, archived by hand,
# last_kmsg 633,759 B sha256 0e33bbcd4950cb037ec3597078ade6a7fdd01b8f3676619ebd7cce9f9ae4de7a.
# THE MACHINE STAYED UP: `_post_end_calls = 0x7`, `_post_elapsed = 0x06df35b6` = 6004.8 ms at
# `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800` (19.2 MHz), so the 6000 ms deadline FIRED and the OS idled the whole
# window. Runner's verdict 7 PASS / 1 FAIL / 3 UNREAD COUNTED PER CRITERION (14/2/6 as lines, the block
# printing twice - the gate's reading of the previous capture and the runner's of this one), the single
# FAIL the known `seam_sp ... is not sleh_sp-8` criterion: IDENTICAL to rungs 29, 30 and 31, so this
# press added no failure.
#
# (1) THE REPAIR RAN, AND THE SIX NEW CELLS SAY SO. `_tout_calls = 0x1`, `_tout_was = 0x0` - THE
# REGISTER HELD ZERO, the reset value, exactly as predicted - `_tout_wrote = 0x3`, `_tout_held = 0x3`
# (THE BLOCK HELD WHAT WAS WRITTEN), `_tout_wrote_back = 0x0`, `_tout_readback = 0x0`. So the window
# opened, the byte went 0 -> 3 and was put back, and the one failure 796 section 4 said the rest of the
# log cannot show - a store that never landed - DID NOT HAPPEN.
#
# (2) THE HEADLINE: THE BOUND DID NOT MOVE BY ONE TICK-TIME. Rung 31 ran CMD2 under `0x00` = 665.2 us
# and read `_cid_ticks = 0x31e2` = 12,770; rung 32 ran it under `0x03` = 5,321.6 us, EIGHT TIMES the
# bound, and read `_cid_ticks = 0x31e4` = 12,772 = 665.3 us, with `_cid_status_any = 0x00018000`
# (`ERR | TIMEOUT`) at `_cid_any_polls = 0x705` = 1,797 on both arms, `_cid_err = 0x00010000`,
# `_cid_complete = 0`. **`TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` DOES NOT GOVERN THIS EVENT.** The SDHCI specification's
# timeout register scales the DATA timeout; the command response timeout is fixed in the block. 795
# section 5 built the arm on the assumption that this register sets the bound the ladder has been
# hitting, and THIS PRESS IS THE MEASUREMENT THAT SAYS IT DOES NOT. It is the pre-registered refutation
# row and it arrived in the STRONGER form: 796 section 8 named "ERR | TIMEOUT again, at a LONGER tick
# count"; what came back is the SAME tick count, so it refutes the MECHANISM and not merely the
# arithmetic. And 795 section 5's extrapolation about where a 136-bit response would land is UNTOUCHED
# AND UNTESTED - the card is not answering at all, so no response ever raced the bound.
#
# (3) AND IT IS A CLEAN ONE-VARIABLE COMPARISON, MEASURED. Rung 31 carries 481 storage keys and rung 32
# 487, and the six that exist only on rung 32 are the `_tout_*` pair's cells above. Every other cell is
# identical to within timer jitter: `_cid_status_any` `0x00018000` on both; `_cid_word`/`_cid_flags`
# `0x0201`/`0x03` on both; `_cid_raw0..3` `0x0040ff80 / 0x80000000 / 0 / 0` and `_cid_resp0..3`
# `0x40ff8080 / 0 / 0 / 0` on both; `_cmd0_ticks` 4,903 -> 4,901; `_cmd1_any_polls` 1,236 -> 1,238 with
# CMD1 COMPLETING AGAIN; `_nidx_ticks` `0x31e4` -> `0x31e2`. So the press isolated exactly one variable
# - the timeout window - and the variable did nothing.
#
# (4) A DEFECT THIS STEP FOUND IN ITS OWN PREDECESSOR, AND IT IS A GUARD THAT LEAKED. 796 section 1 and
# section 3 state that value 31 sends CMD2's `ST_MMC_RSP_R2` - word `0x0209` - and that "rung 31 sent
# `0x0201` and this arm does NOT". THAT IS FALSE. The capture reads `_cid_word = _cid_word_read =
# 0x00000201` and `_cid_flags = 0x00000003`: rung 32 sent the CRC-DROPPED word. The source is
# unambiguous - entry_storage.c:3328's guard is `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 30`, and the two
# `st_all_send_cid` calls at :3348-3353 select on it - so EVERY value at or above 30 inherits rung 31's
# constant. Rung 32 is a STRICT SUPERSET of rung 31's act: the NOCRC flag AND the timeout window.
# **AND THE CLAUSE FAMILY DID NOT CATCH IT, BECAUSE IT WAS NEVER ASKED TO**: `xnu_entry_792` asserts the
# flags register is `#3` and is itself guarded `>= 30`, so at value 31 it asserts `#3` - which the source
# also builds - AND PASSES. The check was right about the image and the PROSE was wrong about the arm;
# this is the m-class "a claim in a document no check reads" again, and the tell is that the check and the
# sentence disagreed and NOTHING COMPARED THEM. The reading in (2) is NOT weakened - the leak made the
# comparison cleaner, since rungs 31 and 32 then differ in the timeout window and nothing else - but
# 796's ANSWER SPACE is: its row 4, "ERR | CRC returning refutes 795 section 2", COULD NOT HAVE FIRED,
# because the CRC flag was not carried.
#
# (5) CMD1 IS SENT ONCE, AND THE GATE BELOW IT READS THE DRIVER'S OWN CONDITION BACKWARDS - and this is
# the finding the press made available, and it is not about the timeout register at all. THE DRIVER
# LOOPS (`mmc_ops.c:141-166`): `for (i = 100; i; i--)` with `err = mmc_wait_for_cmd(...)` inside and
# `mmc_delay(10)` between passes, exiting on `if (cmd.resp[0] & MMC_CARD_BUSY) break;` at `:157` - so the
# driver PROCEEDS WHEN BIT 31 SETS, the card having finished power-up, under the comment "otherwise wait
# until reset completes". THE LADDER SENDS ONCE: `entry_storage.c:4702` is a single
# `st_send_command(ST_CMD_OP_SEND_OP_COND, 0u, ST_MMC_RSP_PRESENT, &c1)` with no loop. AND THE LADDER
# TESTS THE OPPOSITE: `entry_storage.c:4785` is `if (c1.sent != 0u && (c1.resp & ST_MMC_CARD_BUSY) ==
# 0u)` - bit 31 CLEAR proceeds to CMD2. The constant's own comment at :2197 cites `mmc_ops.c:158`'s
# condition and the comment above the gate says "the condition is the DRIVER's, read in the direction the
# driver reads it"; BOTH SENTENCES ARE FALSE against `mmc_ops.c:157`, and both are m-class claims in
# comments no check reads. AND THE MEASURED VALUES PUT THE LADDER ON THE WRONG SIDE OF THAT TEST:
# `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080`, and `0x40ff8080 & 0x80000000 = 0`, so the busy bit is CLEAR, so THE DRIVER'S
# LOOP WOULD NOT HAVE EXITED - it would have retried 99 more times. The ladder proceeded, and
# `_cid_gated = 0` says so. **SO CMD2 IS PUT ON THE BUS FOR A CARD THAT HAS NEVER REPORTED POWER-UP
# COMPLETE, AND THE CARD DOES NOT ANSWER.** That is what the 665.2 us is: the block's fixed response
# window expiring over a silent card - not a bound a register can move, which is exactly what (2)
# measured with the register. And it explains 795 section 6 without the protocol claim: CMD1 is answered
# because a card answers CMD1 WHILE it initializes (that is what the retry loop is for), and CMD2 is not
# answered because the card is not in READY. 795 section 6 reached the right SHAPE from the wrong
# DIRECTION: the reason CMD3 also fails is one step earlier than it said, at CMD1. THE CAVEAT, NAMED:
# `0x40ff8080` may not be a real OCR at all (790 section 5, 787 section 6 - it is byte-identical on arms
# whose window was closed). THE NEXT ARM IS SOUND EITHER WAY.
#
# (6) THE NEXT ARM IS THE FIRST TIME THIS LADDER PORTS THE DRIVER'S OWN CONTROL FLOW: the
# `mmc_send_op_cond` loop - send CMD1 up to N times with the driver's own 10 ms delay, exit when
# `resp[0] & MMC_CARD_BUSY` SETS, and publish two cells the ladder does not have: HOW MANY SENDS IT TOOK
# and WHETHER THE BIT EVER SET. Its four rows: the bit sets on some send k = THE CARD FINISHES POWER-UP
# and the ladder has been sending CMD2 too early since rung 16, after which 793 section 1's row 1a
# becomes live; the bit NEVER sets over the full count puts the subject on POWER AND CLOCK (784's rails
# are RPM resources with no `reg`) and NOT on the command sequence; the response CHANGING between sends
# separates a real OCR from a static pattern WITHOUT knowing which it is; and the response BYTE-IDENTICAL
# every send extends 790 section 5's discount to every arm.
#
# (7) THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No transfer completes, no filesystem is reached, no mount is made, so
# 「让os可以正常启动并且挂载存储」 is not reached and TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD. `_cid_complete = 0`
# and the eight RESPONSE words are the nine-arm pattern, so 793 section 1's row 1a DID NOT FIRE. NO PRESS
# IS AUTHORIZED BY THIS STEP: rung 32 is spent and no arm is armed.
#
# (8) OWED, AND NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE INFERRED: entry_storage.c:3328's `>= 30` guard should be
# `== 30` or the record must say value 31 is a containment (COST); 796 sections 1, 3 and 8's sentences
# about `0x0209` are false (COST); the CMD1 loop of (6) is the next arm, pre-registered and NOT built;
# TIMEOUT_CONTROL's real scope is unmeasured on this ladder; the effective bit rate 171.5 kHz against
# rung 6's configured 400 kHz (795 section 10) is untouched and now more pointed; CMD9's `MMC_RSP_R2`
# (797 section 6); and the six live-prose corrections of 797 section 1.
# 800: THE RUNG-33 ARM - armed-storage-ce2f589c, STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=32 (switch VALUE 32 =
# ordinal rung 33). **THE FIRST RUNG WHOSE ACT IS THE DRIVER'S OWN CONTROL FLOW RATHER THAN ONE OF ITS
# CONSTANTS.**
#
# THE READING IT ACTS ON IS 798 section 5's, AND THE PRESS THAT MADE IT IS THE ONLY REASON THIS RUNG
# EXISTS. The rung-32 press found that `TIMEOUT_CONTROL 0x2E` does not govern the command response
# timeout, and then handed over a finding that is not about a register at all: **the driver calls CMD1
# TWICE and this ladder calls it once, and the gate below the one call reads the driver's own test
# BACKWARDS.** `drivers/mmc/core/mmc_ops.c:141-166` is
#
#     for (i = 100; i; i--) {
#             err = mmc_wait_for_cmd(host, &cmd, 0);
#             if (err) break;
#             if (ocr == 0) break;                      /* a probe: one pass */
#             if (cmd.resp[0] & MMC_CARD_BUSY) break;   /* :157 - PROCEED WHEN BIT 31 SETS */
#             err = -ETIMEDOUT;
#             mmc_delay(10);
#     }
#
# - up to 100 sends, 10 ms apart, exiting when `resp[0] & 0x80000000` becomes TRUE. This ladder's
# `entry_storage.c:4702` sends CMD1 ONCE with argument `0u`, and `:4785` gates CMD2 on
# `(c1.resp & ST_MMC_CARD_BUSY) == 0u` - PROCEEDS WHEN BIT 31 IS CLEAR, which is the opposite of the
# line the constant's own comment cites. And the measurement puts the ladder on the wrong side:
# `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080` has bit 31 clear, so the driver's loop would have retried 99 more times and
# this ladder went on to CMD2. **So CMD2 has been put on the bus for a card that has never reported
# power-up complete, since rung 16** - which is what the 665.2 us is.
#
# THE TWO STEPS BETWEEN THE DRIVER'S TWO CALLS DO NOT EXIST IN THIS IMAGE, and that is a grep rather
# than an impression. `mmc.c:1923` is `mmc_send_op_cond(host, 0, &ocr)` - argument 0, and by
# `mmc_ops.c:148-150` that is a SINGLE PASS BY DESIGN. `mmc.c:1951` is
# `host->ocr = mmc_select_voltage(host, ocr)` - the intersection of the card's OCR window with the
# host's. `mmc.c:1359` is `mmc_send_op_cond(host, ocr | (1 << 30), &rocr)` - the LOOPED call, with an
# argument derived from the first response's window and bit 30 set. In `src/entry/`, anchored and
# case-sensitive, `select_voltage` occurs once and IN A COMMENT (the note at `entry_storage.c:4724`
# explaining what it publishes) with no define, no body and no call; the CMD1 argument is the literal
# `0u`; and `1u << 30` occurs once in the whole file as `ST_HC_DLL_RST` (`:258`, a different register),
# so **bit 30 has never been set in any command word this image sends.**
#
# THE ACT, AND IT IS TWO BODIES OF THEIR OWN - the shape 787 and 796 used, so that `st_cmd_path`'s own
# device surface does not move by one offset:
#
#   `st_op_cond_arg`  0x8000de18,  96 bytes  - `(resp & ~0x7F) | (1 << 30)`, nothing else
#   `st_op_cond_loop` 0x8000de78, 320 bytes  - the driver's loop, one `bl <st_send_command>` inside it
#
# both `noinline, noclone`, each called from `st_cmd_path` and nowhere else. **THEIR DEVICE SETS ARE
# EMPTY, AND THAT IS MEASURED OUT OF THE LINKED IMAGE AND NOT ASSERTED**: the derived argument is a
# function of the WORD the caller passes and of nothing the block holds, and the loop's only device act
# is the command it sends, which belongs to `st_send_command` - whose own body and own clause are
# unchanged and whose `st_cmd_path` call count is still asserted to be exactly two (the loop's call is
# inside the loop's body, not in `st_cmd_path`'s). **NO STORE TO ANY REGISTER IS ADDED BY THIS RUNG.**
#
# THE FOUR COMPILE-TIME CONSTANTS, and every one of them is asserted out of the image by
# `xnu_entry_799` rather than trusted from the source:
#
#   `ST_OP_COND_OCR_CLR     0x0000007Fu`  - `mmc.c:1943-1948`'s `ocr &= ~0x7F`, emitted as `bic #127`
#   `ST_OP_COND_BIT30       0x40000000u`  - `mmc.c:1359`'s `(1 << 30)`, emitted as `bic`/`orr`
#   `ST_OP_COND_SEND_MAX    100u`         - `mmc_ops.c:145`'s `for (i = 100; i; i--)`
#   `ST_OP_COND_DELAY_TICKS 192000u`      - `mmc_ops.c:164`'s `mmc_delay(10)` = 10 ms at 19.2 MHz
#
# EIGHT NEW KEYS, and they are the whole answer space: `_opcond_calls` (the argument body ran),
# `_opcond_from` (the word it derives from), `_opcond_arg` (the word it derived - THE ARM'S ONLY
# NUMBER), `_opcond_loop_calls` (the loop ran), `_opcond_sends` (how many sends it took, 1..100),
# `_opcond_busy_seen` (whether bit 31 EVER set - 798 section 5's own test, run more than once for the
# first time), `_opcond_last_resp` (the last response word), `_opcond_ticks` (the loop's own cost).
#
# **THE PREDICTION IS A NUMBER AND IT IS SHARPER THAN THE RUNG THAT MADE IT POSSIBLE.**
# `_cmd1_resp = 0x40ff8080` already has bit 30 SET and its low seven bits ZERO, so
# `(0x40ff8080 & ~0x7F) | 0x40000000 = 0x40ff8080`: **THE DERIVATION IS A NO-OP ON THIS CARD'S OWN
# WORD, and `_opcond_arg` should equal `_opcond_from` exactly.** A value that differs says the
# derivation moved something the response did not already satisfy, which is the only way this rung can
# report on its own first body at all.
#
# THE COST IS NAMED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: the worst case is 100 sends plus 99 ten-millisecond
# delays, and at the measured 665.2 us per timed-out command that is about **1.06 s added to the
# storage probe** - spent inside `entry_storage_probe` at the OS's idle-exit, where the run's own
# deadline is 6,000 ms (`_post_elapsed`). A run that spans the whole loop is a run whose window shrank
# by that much, and `_opcond_ticks` is the cell that measures what it actually cost.
#
# FOUR ROWS, and the first is the one that would open the ladder:
#
#   (1) `_opcond_busy_seen = 1` on send k <= 100 - **THE CARD FINISHES POWER-UP AND THE LADDER HAS BEEN
#       SENDING CMD2 TOO EARLY SINCE RUNG 16**, and the send count k is a number no arm has carried.
#   (2) `_opcond_busy_seen = 0` over the full 100 sends - the card is not powering up on this path at
#       all, which puts the subject on POWER AND CLOCK (784's rails are RPM resources with no `reg`)
#       and NOT on the command sequence.
#   (3) `_opcond_last_resp` DIFFERS between sends - the block is reading something that MOVES with each
#       CMD1, which separates a real OCR from a static pattern without needing to know which it is.
#   (4) `_opcond_last_resp` byte-identical every send, and equal to `_cmd1_resp` - `0x40ff8080` is a
#       static pattern and 790 section 5's discount extends to every arm.
#
# THE CLAUSE FOUND TWO DEFECTS IN ITS OWN FIRST DRAFTS, AND BOTH ARE WORTH THE SENTENCE.
# (a) `objdump -d` annotates every immediate with its own comment field, so the instruction that clears
# the low seven bits reads `bic r3, r3, #127    ; 0x7f`. The first draft matched `#127` ANCHORED AT END
# OF LINE, which objdump never emits, and the clause REFUSED A BUILD THAT DID EXACTLY WHAT THE ARM SAYS
# - loud, because the pattern is in a `||`-refusal, and it would have refused every future value-32
# build too. The repair strips objdump's comment field rather than loosening the anchor, because a
# loosened anchor is the check that also matches `#1274`.
# (b) The probe whose response the argument is derived from is CMD1, the **LAST** of `st_cmd_path`'s
# two `bl <st_send_command>` sites, and the first draft took the FIRST - which is CMD0's line. The
# assertion was true and the sentence printed beside it would have named the wrong command, which is
# the m-class this project keeps catching. The last is taken now, and the `== 2` assertion further down
# is what makes "the last" unambiguous. **The clause also asserts the EXIT TEST'S DIRECTION** - one
# sign-conditioned branch (`blt`/`bmi`) leaving the loop, which is what `(resp & 0x80000000) != 0`
# compiles to - because that direction is 798 section 5's whole subject and a differently-lowered test
# refuses the build for a human to read rather than passing on an assertion that no longer means
# anything.
#
# CONTAINMENT, MEASURED ON ALL ELEVEN MEMBERS OF THE RUNG BELOW. The value-31 build from this same
# source is TEN OF ELEVEN MEMBERS BYTE-IDENTICAL to the SPENT rung-32 park
# `out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-c1f89600/` - `stage90-qcdt.img` `bda29ed1...` and `xnu_arm_entry.bin`
# `c1f89600...` included, i.e. the entry image REPRODUCED byte for byte - and the eleventh is
# `xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt`, which records source files BY CONTENT and must move when the tools are
# edited: exactly two of its 28 lines, `build_entry.sh` and `entry_storage.c`, the two files this step
# touched, with the artifact's own hash line identical and the file set identical at 23 files.
#
# THE ARM'S OWN DELTA, MEASURED AGAINST THE SPENT RUNG-32 PARK:
#   entry `.text` (size -A)  5,335,424 -> 5,335,680   **+256**
#   `xnu_arm_entry.elf`      6,732,464 -> 6,732,528   **+64** (the .text grew 256 and 192 of it was
#                                                     absorbed by page alignment, 796 section 7's own
#                                                     rule that an unchanged size is not a claim)
#   `xnu_arm_entry.bin`      5,552,764 -> 5,552,764   SIZE UNCHANGED, **603,185 bytes differ** - an
#                                                     insertion, not 603,185 edits
#   `stage90-build-config.txt` BYTE-IDENTICAL to rungs 23 through 32 (`6c2b6038...`): no payload switch moved
#   `stage90-qcdt.img`       8,572,928 -> 8,572,928   the boot image the press sends
#
# THE PRESS IS THE OPERATOR'S AND NO PRESS IS SPENT OR AUTHORIZED HERE. The arm is ARMED AND NOT PRESSED,
# its park is `out/stage90/frozen/armed-storage-ce2f589c/` with eleven members verified in place, and the
# press path is the two commands `tools/verify_press_ready.sh` prints, run by hand once each. **THE GOAL
# IS NOT MET BY THIS ARM**: no transfer completes, no filesystem is reached and no mount is made, so
# 「让os可以正常启动并且挂载存储」 is not reached and **TWRP-TO-STORAGE STAYS WITHHELD**.
#
